Parcamus, inquam, homini; et quia felicitatem perdidit, aeternitatem quoque praecipiamus infelici, ut in neutro sit « quasi unus ex nobis; » nobis est aeterna felicitas, felix aeternitas; sit illi temporalis miseria, vel misera temporalitas; ut tunc illi commodus reformetur aeternitas, cum fuerit recuperata felicitas. In magna ergo ira, magnae misericordiae Dominus recordatus est, hoc ipso quod lignum vitae non concessit misero homini: quippe quem voluit sic esse dissimilem sibi, ut non esset utrobique similis diabolo, id est ne viveret homo, aeque ut diabolus, 97Busque ad ultimum judicium, et aeque ut ille, absque temporali morte, transiret ad aeternum incendium.

VERS. 23.-- « Et emisit eum, » etc. (AUG., de Gen. contra Manich., l. II, c. 22.) In locum sibi congruum; sicut plerumque malus, cum inter bonos vivere coeperit, si in melius mutari noluerit, de bonorum congregatione pellitur pondere pravae consuetudinis suae.

VERS. 24.-- « Ejecitque Adam. » (HIERON. Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.) LXX: « Et ejecit, et habitare fecit contra paradisum voluptatis, et statuit cherubin, et flammeam rhompheam, quae vertitur ad custodiendam ligni vitae viam. » Alius sensus est in Hebraeo; ait enim, « et ejecit Dominus, » Adam scilicet, « et 97Chabitare fecit ante paradisum voluptatis cherubin, et flammeum gladium. » Non quod Adam habitare fecerit contra paradisum voluptatis, sed, illo ejecto, ante fores paradisi cherubin et flammeum posuit gladium, ne quis posset intrare.

« Et collocavit, » etc. (AUG., ut supra. ) Hoc per coelestes potestates etiam in paradiso visibili factum esse credendum est, ut per angelicum ministerium ibi esset quaedam ignea custodia, non tamen frustra, sed quia aliquid de paradiso significat spirituali.

« Collocavit, » etc. (STRAB.) Hoc est per ministerium angelorum igneam custodiam constituit. Flammeus gladius tribulationes, dolores et labores hujus vitae, quibus nos exercemur; vel sententia justae damnationis, quae homini est data, qui ita damnatus 97Dest ut Christi passione redimi posset. Unde gladius versatilis dicitur, quia potest removeri. Remotus est enim Enoch et Eliae, et quotidie removetur fidelibus, de hac vita ad supernam beatitudinem transeuntibus. Nec dicit flammam et gladium, sed « flammeum gladium: » qui enim ad paradisum redeunt, necesse est ut igne Spiritus sancti omnes concupiscentias hujus vitae exurant, et gladio verbi Dei omnia noxia praecidant.

« Et collocavit, » etc. (ISID., in Gen., tom. V.) Cherubin plenitudo scientiae, haec est charitas quae plenitudo legis est divinae. Gladius flammeus, poenae temporales, quae versatiles sunt, quoniam tempora volubilia sunt. Ad arborem igitur vitae non reditur, 98Anisi per cherubin, id est plenitudinem scientiae, id est charitatem: et per gladium versatilem, id est tolerantiam temporalem.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 1-4.-- « But the serpent also was more subtle than all the living creatures, » etc. (AUG., lib. II de Gen. ad litt. c. 2, 3, tom. III.) Others, « most prudent, » said by transference. Certain codices have « most wise, » not in the proper sense, as the wisdom of God, or of the angels, or of the rational soul, is wont to be taken in a good sense, etc., as far as but the power is from God.

« But the serpent, » etc. (STRAB.) So the devil spoke through the serpent, which knew nothing, as he does through the possessed or the frenzied: for whomsoever he seizes, he takes away understanding and reason. But the good Spirit, whomsoever he has filled with grace, makes them seeing and understanding, 91Dwhence the spirit of the prophets is subject to the prophets.

« Who said to the woman. » (AUG., lib. II de Gen. ad litt., c. 30, et ex capp. 7, 8, 6, 10, passim.) The transgression of the woman is inexcusable, because she was mindful of God's precept. Yet forgetfulness, etc., as far as these c. 10: He foresees that there will be good men and evil, and creates them: furnishing himself to the good for their enjoyment, bestowing many things even upon the evil.

« Why hath he commanded, » etc. (STRAB.) Therefore the crafty enemy asks this, and calls to mind God's command, lest, when she had sinned, she should have an excuse as though she had forgotten it.

« Why hath he commanded, » etc. (GREG. Moral., l. XXIII, tom. II.) The enemy of the human race does the same daily: he labours to tear the words 92Aof God out of the hearts of men, and to make his threats light, and to bring them to believe what he falsely promises, for he invites them to temporal things, that he may make light of the eternal punishments which God threatens. For, pledging the glory of the present life, he says: « Taste, and you shall be as gods, » and touch temporal concupiscence, and appear exalted in this world; and when he tries to remove the fear of the divine sentence, he says as it were: « Why hath God commanded you? »

(AUG., lib. II de Gen. ad litt., c. 4, tom. III.) It is asked why God permitted man to be tempted, etc., as far as or earthly, as is the soul of man.

(Ibid., c. 5.) Nor is it to be thought that man would have been cast down, unless there had gone before in him a certain elation to be repressed, so that through the humbling of sin he might know that he had falsely 92Bpresumed concerning himself, and that a nature once made is not well with itself, if it has withdrawn from its maker. For it is commended what a good God is, when it is well for no one to withdraw from him.

(Ibid., c. 18.) This question concerning the blessed life, etc., as far as less than in the life of the holy angels.

(Ibid., c. 16.) It can be thought not without cause that the devil fell from the beginning of time, etc., as far as to whom God did not give foreknowledge concerning themselves?

(Ibid., c. 14.) But how can they be blessed, to whom their own blessedness is uncertain? etc., as far as but on what ground they assert it does not occur to me.

(Ibid., c. 21.) But it must be thought not without cause that from the very beginning of the creature, etc., as far as foreseeing 92Chow great things he would do out of him in his own goodness.

(Ibid., c. 22.) « But the beginning of the thing formed » is said, etc., as far as as evil men.

(Ibid., c. 23.) Whereas, then, it is thought that the devil never stood in the truth, etc., as far as more things agree with his body.

(GREG., hom. 16, in Evang., tom. II.) The devil tempted the first parent in three ways, etc., as far as by delight we are overcome, by consent we are bound.

VERS. 5.--. . . . . « You shall be as gods, » etc. (STRAB.) Mystically. He frames his words with artful guile, so that, if he cannot overthrow them by contempt of obedience, at least in this, that he has corrupted faith and persuaded to idolatry, he may boast himself the victor: but if he has seduced them by contempt of obedience, he may stand 92Dvictor in both.

(AUG., de Gen. contra Manich., l. II, c. 15, tom. III.) The woman, being questioned, answered, etc., as far as for, with none ruling him, God alone is blessed through his own power.

VERS. 6, 7.-- « She saw therefore, » etc. (ISID. ex Aug. ubi supra. ) How did they see, if their eyes were closed? But it was said so that we may understand that those eyes were opened, after they had eaten of the fruit, with which they saw themselves naked and were displeased: that is, the eyes of craftiness, to which simplicity is displeasing. For when a man has fallen from the inmost light of truth, there is nothing whereby pride would please, save by fraudulent pretences. For hence is born hypocrisy, in which those seem very 93Ashrewd who have been able to deceive whom they would.

« She saw therefore, » etc. (GREG., lib. XXI Moral., cap. 2.) Lest we be turned about in slippery thought, we ought not to gaze upon what it is not lawful to desire, etc., as far as the discipline of the outward senses also must be kept.

« And she gave to her husband, who did eat. And the eyes of them both were opened, » etc. (ISID. ex Aug. ubi supra, c. 16, 17.) Of whom it was said: « Then they saw that they were naked, » [Vulg. Gn 3:7 cumque cognovissent se esse nudos, "and when they had known that they were naked"] with perverse eyes, namely, to which nakedness, that is simplicity, seemed a thing to blush at. etc., as far as « My soul is troubled within myself. » (Psal. XLI.)

« Who did eat, » etc. (AUG., de Gen., lib. XI, c. 3, tom. III, col. 429.) Namely to lust 93Bafter one another, etc., as far as which would convict the sinner by the deed, and teach the reader by the writing.

« They sewed together fig leaves. » (ALCUIN., in Gen. t. I.) Because they had lost the glory of simple chastity, they fled for refuge to the twofold itch of lust; whence John I: « When thou wast under the fig tree I saw thee, and I came down, » [Vulg. Jo 1:48 cum esses sub ficu, vidi te, with no et descendi] supply « to deliver thee. »

VERS. 8.-- « And when they had heard, » etc. (AUG., l. II de Gen. ad litt., c. 33.) For at that hour it was fitting that such as they should be visited, because they had fallen away from the light of truth, etc., as far as he may be believed to have appeared to the bodily senses by a local and temporal motion.

« He hid himself, » etc. (AUG., ibid.) When God turns away his face within and man is troubled, things like madness come about, through excessive fear and shame, with a hidden 93Cinstinct also not resting: so that unknowing they did things which should signify something to those who came after, for whose sake these things are written.

« At the afternoon air. » Whence at the ninth hour Christ gave up the spirit (Matth. XXVII). He was crucified at the sixth hour, that man might be restored at the same hour at which he was cast out of paradise.

« In the midst of the tree of paradise. » He hides himself who, turned away from the precept of the Creator, lives by the will of his own error and choice.

VERS. 9.-- « And the Lord God called Adam. » (AUG., ubi supra, c. 34.) By rebuking, that is, not by being ignorant, etc., as far as but now we are not reporting things signified, but defending things done.

VERS. 10-11.-- « And he said: I heard thy voice, » etc. 93D(AUG., ubi supra, c. 34.) Historically. It is probable that a human form was wont to appear to the first men through a fitting creature, etc., as far as and there followed that of which they were ashamed.

« Thy voice, » etc. (ISID., in Gen. tom. V.) Mystically. He answers that, having heard his voice, he had hidden himself, etc., as far as and not to obey the precept of God.

« And God called Adam, » etc. (GREG.) Because he saw him fallen into fault, now under sin, as though hidden from the eyes of truth, because he does not approve the darkness of his error, he is as it were ignorant where the sinner is, and calls him and seeks him, saying: « Adam, where art thou? » [Vulg. Gn 3:9 et dixit ei: Ubi es?, without the vocative Adam] While he calls, he signifies that he is calling him back to penance; while he seeks him, 94Ahe intimates that he knows not sinners justly condemned.

VERS. 12.-- « And Adam said: The woman whom thou gavest, » etc. (AUG.) He does not say, I have sinned: for pride has the deformity of confusion, not the humility of confession. But it was written for this, and the questions were put for this, that they might be written truly and profitably, so that we may know with how great a disease of pride they labour who today try to refer evil to the Creator, and, if they do any good, to attribute it to themselves.

« The woman whom thou gavest, » etc. As though she had been given for this, that she herself should not obey the man, and both of them God.

« The woman whom thou gavest, » etc. (GREG., lib. IV Mor., c. 27, tom. I, col. 661.) In four ways is sin committed in the heart, etc., as far as at the last it is nourished by the seduction of false hope, or by the obstinacy of wretched despair 94B.

VERS. 13.-- « And the Lord God said to the woman, » etc. (AUG.) Neither does she confess the sin, but refers it to another — in an unequal sex, with equal haughtiness. Yet David, born of these, says unto the end of the world: « I said: Lord, be merciful to me, heal my soul, for I have sinned against thee (Psal. XL). » This ought to have been said by those two: but God does not yet regard the necks of sinners: there remained the labours and sorrows of death, and the wearing away of the world, and the grace of God, which comes to help in due time, teaching the afflicted not to presume of themselves.

« The serpent deceived, » etc. As though anyone's persuasion should prevail over God's precept.

« The serpent deceived me, » etc. (AUG., ibid., lib. II, c. 30.) How by these words, etc., as far as since with that 94Cfood he did not see her to be dead.

(AUG., ibid., c. 28.) It is to be noted that the devil was not permitted to tempt the woman, etc., as far as but reason shows that on this account he envied man.

(ALC.) The sin of the angel was left unspoken, that of man laid open, because the one was incurable, the other was cured.

« She saw therefore, » etc. (STRAB.) She is plainly shown to be inexcusable, since it was open to her, under such deliberation, to mark the wiles of the enemy.

VERS. 14.-- « And the Lord God said, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra, c. 36.) This whole sentence is figurative. Where nevertheless it must be believed that all things were said. The serpent is not asked why he did it, because it was not he that did it by his own will and nature, but the devil out of him, and through him, and in him: who already for his impiety and pride had been destined 94Dfor eternal fire. To him, therefore, is referred what is said to the serpent, and it is shown what he will be to the human race.

Mystically. « And the Lord God said to the serpent, » etc. (AUG., de Gen. cont. Manich., lib. II, c. 17.) The serpent, unquestioned, is the first to receive punishment, etc., as far as that he may shut out the very beginning of the evil suggestion.

« Upon thy breast, » etc. (HIERON.) « Belly » the Seventy added, but in the Hebrew there is « breast In the Hebrew it is gahon, which signifies belly and breast. » only, that he might lay open the craft and cunning of his thoughts, that is, that his every step is of wickedness and of fraud.

95A« With breast and belly thou shalt creep. » (GREG., lib. XXI Mor., c. 2.) The evil of lechery is committed either in thought or in deed. For when the enemy is driven out from the accomplishment of the deed, he labours to defile the secret places of thought. He creeps with the belly, when, slippery, through the human members made subject to him, he practises lechery in deed. He crawls with the breast, when he defiles in thought. But because it is through thought that one comes to deeds, he is described as creeping first with the breast and afterwards with the belly.

VERS. 15.-- « I will put enmities between thee and the woman . . . She shall crush thy head, » etc. (GREG. l. I Mor., c. 38.) To crush the serpent's head is to root out the beginnings of his suggestion from the entrance of the heart by the hand of watchful consideration; for when he is caught at the very outset, he contrives to strike the heel, because the man whom he does not strike in the first temptation 95Bhe aims to deceive at the end. But if the heart is corrupted in the temptation, the middle and the end of the action that follows are possessed by the enemy, because he sees the whole tree bearing fruit for him, the tree which he has vitiated at the root with the tooth of his venom.

(HIERON.) « She shall keep thy heel, and thou shalt crush her heel. » [Vulg. Gn 3:15 ipsa conteret caput tuum, et tu insidiaberis calcaneo ejus, "she shall crush thy head, and thou shalt lie in wait for her heel"] It stands better in the Hebrew: « She shall crush thy head, and thou shalt crush her heel, » because both our steps are hampered by the serpent, and the Lord shall crush Satan under her feet.

VERS. 16.-- « To the woman also, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra, c. 37.) These things are better understood figuratively and prophetically, etc., as far as nature will be depraved, and 95Cguilt increased.

« I will multiply thy sorrows, » etc. (AUG., de Gen. contra Manich., l. II, c. 19.) It is manifest that the woman's pains were multiplied, etc., as far as but first there was a struggling with grief against an evil habit.

VERS. 17-18.-- . . . « Cursed is the earth, » etc. (AUG. ibid., c. 38.) That these are the labors of the human race upon earth no one is ignorant, and that they would not be, if the happiness which was in paradise were held fast, stands beyond doubt.

(STRAB.) The earth is cursed, not Adam, so that all men for whose sake these things were written might be terrified, lest, doing the like, they should be struck with a like punishment. But Cain himself, because he added fratricide to the first transgression, is cursed when it is said: « Thou shalt be cursed upon the earth, » 95Detc.

(AUG.) He cursed the earth, not the waters, because man ate of the fruit of the earth against the prohibition, and did not drink of the waters, and in the waters the sin contracted from the fruit of the earth was to be washed away. Land animals too have more of the curse than water creatures, because they live more from the cursed earth. Hence Christ after the resurrection ate of fish, not of a land animal.

« Cursed is the earth in thy work. » (GREG., Moral. lib. IV, c. 5, 6.) God curses the sinning man, etc., as far as and the flame burned up the two captains of fifty.

« In labors, » etc. (RAB.) Thorns were there before, but not so as to bring labor upon man: for him they were, after the sin, 96Afor labor and affliction, as were serpents and whatever things are harmful, harmless before. And it is to be noted that on Adam an outward penalty is laid: « Cursed is the earth in thy work; » but on the woman an inward one, when it is said: « I will multiply thy sorrows, » etc., because, having been seduced, she seduced her husband.

VERS. 19.-- « Dust thou art, » etc. (GREG., ubi supra.) Man was so constituted, etc., as far as old age passes over into death.

VERS. 20.-- « And Adam called the name of his wife Eve, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) These were the words of the first man, since she is the mother of all the living, as though he were rendering the reason of the name imposed by himself, namely why he had called her Eve.

Mystically. (AUG., de Gen. contra Manich., l. II, c. 21.) 96BAfter the sin and the judge's sentence Adam called his wife Eve, etc., as far as it is expressed in the skins which are stripped from dead bodies.

VERS. 21.-- « He made also, » etc. (AUG., de Gen. lib. XI, c. 39.) This too was done for the sake of signification, and in deeds it is asked what was done and what it signifies, and in words what was said and what it signifies.

(STRAB.) They themselves made girdles for themselves, that they might hide their sin: but God made tunics of skin, with which he clothed their whole body, because he justly condemned them both in body and in soul. But it is a foolish question to ask how, or by whose working, the skins were drawn off from the animals: for he who founded all things out of nothing made these 96Chow and in what manner he willed.

VERS. 22.-- « And he said: Behold Adam is as one of us, » etc. (AUG., ibid., c. 41.) The plural number is used on account of the Trinity, etc., as far as as in the Church men are wont to be removed from the visible sacraments by the Church's discipline.

(AUG., ubi supra, c. 42.) It is a wonder if Adam was spiritual in mind, etc., as far as which thought the false to be true.

« As one of us. » (AUG., de Gen. contra Manich., lib. II, c. 21.) It can be understood in two ways, « as one of us, » that is, God, etc., as far as until by God's mercy he should live again who had been dead.

(RUPERT.) Lest he eat of the tree of life, and, this being done, Adam, made such and so wretched, live for ever. For what 96Dif, having been made such, he should live for ever? assuredly nothing but eternal evil. For being already made wretched, if he be also eternal, what will he have but eternal wretchedness? Let us spare him, lest in this way it be ill with him « as one of us; » lest, as each of us is eternal (for the Father is eternal, the Son eternal, the Holy Spirit eternal), so he too be eternal, and for this the wicked eye of the devil laugh that he is truthful, who promised him the likeness of God. For it is indeed an evil for him to be temporal, but it would be worst of all to be eternal. Exceedingly evil and false is this likeness of God. Far better is it for him to be wholly unlike God. How? Namely thus, that, because he is wretched, he be also temporal, this is to be wholly unlike God. 97AFor God is both eternal and happy, and his is an eternal happiness, a happy eternity. Of these the devil lost the one, that is, happiness, but eternity he did not lose; and his is an eternal unhappiness, an unhappy eternity. Let us spare the man, I say; and because he has lost happiness, let us cut off eternity also from the unhappy one, that in neither may he be « as one of us; » ours is an eternal happiness, a happy eternity; let his be a temporal misery, or a miserable temporality; that eternity may then be restored to him to his profit, when happiness has been recovered. In great wrath, therefore, the Lord remembered great mercy, in this very thing, that he did not grant the tree of life to wretched man: him indeed whom he willed to be so unlike himself that he should not be in both respects like the devil, that is, that man should not live, equally with the devil, 97Buntil the last judgment, and, equally with him, pass over without temporal death to eternal burning.

VERS. 23.-- « And he sent him forth, » etc. (AUG., de Gen. contra Manich., l. II, c. 22.) Into a place suited to him; as very often a bad man, when he has begun to live among the good, if he has been unwilling to be changed for the better, is driven from the congregation of the good by the weight of his own depraved habit.

VERS. 24.-- « And he cast out Adam. » (HIERON. Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.) LXX: « And he cast him out, and made him dwell over against the paradise of pleasure, and he set the cherubim, and the flaming sword, which is turned about to guard the way of the tree of life. » There is another sense in the Hebrew; for it says, « and the Lord cast out, » namely Adam, « and 97Cmade the cherubim and the flaming sword to dwell before the paradise of pleasure. » Not that he made Adam dwell over against the paradise of pleasure, but that, when Adam had been cast out, he placed the cherubim and the flaming sword before the doors of paradise, that no one might be able to enter.

« And he placed, » etc. (AUG., ut supra. ) This is to be believed to have been done through the heavenly powers even in the visible paradise, so that through angelic ministry there was there a certain fiery guard, yet not in vain, but because it signifies something of the spiritual paradise.

« He placed, » etc. (STRAB.) That is, through the ministry of angels he established a fiery guard. The flaming sword is the tribulations, griefs and labors of this life, by which we are exercised; or the sentence of just condemnation which was given to man, who was so condemned 97Dthat he could be redeemed by Christ's passion. Whence the sword is called turning, because it can be removed. For it was removed for Enoch and Elias, and daily it is removed for the faithful who pass from this life to the blessedness above. Nor does he say a flame and a sword, but « a flaming sword: » for they who return to paradise must burn away with the fire of the Holy Spirit all the concupiscences of this life, and cut off with the sword of the word of God all things harmful.

« And he placed, » etc. (ISID., in Gen., tom. V.) The cherubim, fullness of knowledge, this is charity, which is the fullness of the divine law. The flaming sword, temporal punishments, which are turning, since times are revolving. To the tree of life, therefore, there is no return 98Aexcept through the cherubim, that is, the fullness of knowledge, that is, charity: and through the turning sword, that is, temporal endurance.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- « Adam vero, » etc. (AUG., lib. IX de Gen. ad litt., c. 4). In paradiso virginitas, extra nuptiae, etc., usque ad sed post in exsilio generata.

« Concepit et peperit, » etc. Cain et Abel de una matre geniti, figura sunt omnium hominum qui de radice peccati in hanc vitam propagantur; et alii terrenam civitatem et mortiferas delicias sunt amaturi, et quantum in se est ambitione possessuri; quos significat Cain, qui interpretatur possessio. Alii futuram civitatem quaesituri, et de hujus habitationis 98Bmiseriis lugentes, ad futuram gloriam toto desiderio transituri, quos significat Abel, qui interpretatur luctus, et est posterior: quia non prius est quod spirituale est, sed quod animale. Hinc Cain dicitur « agricola, » id est, terrenis operibus incumbens; Abel vero « pastor ovium, » simplicitatem scilicet et innocentiam diligens.

Allegorice. (ISID., in Gen. ) Duo filii Adae duos populos exprimunt, Judaicum scilicet natu majorem, etc., usque ad quasi non permittat ratio motum pravum ad effectum venire, sed studeat subjugare.

VERS. 2.-- « Fuit autem Abel, » etc. In quo significatur qui de terrena inhabitatione lugent. Abel namque luctus interpretatur, vel vapor, quia luctus fuit parentibus vel tanquam vapor cito disparuit.

98CVERS. 3, 4.-- « Factum est autem, » etc. « Abel quoque obtulit, » etc. Quia justus in omnibus quae agit per fidem et charitatem (de quibus caeterae virtutes oriuntur, et sine quibus nihil prosunt) Deo placere contendit, quod significatur in adipibus oblatis; unde psal. LXII: « Sicut adipe et pinguedine repleatur anima mea. » Sine charitate macilenta est anima et languida, et in nullo Deo placitura.

« Et respexit Dominus ad Abel, » etc. (HIER., Quaest. Hebr. in Gen., tom. III.) Unde hoc potuit scire Cain, nisi vera esset interpretatio Theodotionis: « Et inflammavit Dominus super Abel, et super sacrificium ejus, super Cain, et sacrificium suum non inflammavit? » Ignem autem de coelo venire solitum ad sacrificium devorandum, ut in dedicatione templi 98Dsub Salomone, legimus, et quando Elias in monte Carmeli construxit altare.

(ISID., ut supra. ) Si recte offeras, et non recte dividas, peccasti: quia etsi antea Judaei recte illa offerebant, in eo rei sunt quia Novum Testamentum a veteri non distinxerunt.

VERS. 5, 6, 7.-- . . . . « In foribus. » (ALC.) Id est, intrantem te et exeuntem peccatum comitabitur, nec Dominus custodiet introitum tuum et exitum tuum.

VERS. 8.-- « Dixitque Cain, » etc. (ISID., Quaest. in Gen., tom. V.) Non acquiescit Domino dicenti, Quiesce, etc., usque ad occiditur ergo Christus junioris populi caput, a majore natu.

VERS. 9.-- « Et ait Dominus ad Cain. » (ISID., ubi 99Asupra.) Interrogat Dominus Cain, non ignarus ut discat, sed judex ut puniat. Usque hodie Judaei, cum per Scripturas eos interrogamus de Christo, respondent se nescire quae dicimus. Mendax Cain ignoratio, Judaeorum est falsa negatio: essent vero Christi custodes, si corde crederent et ore faterentur.

VERS. 10.-- « Vox sanguinis, » etc. Magna virtus justitiae, quae vindictam expetit pro sanguine innocentis.

VERS. 11.-- « Nunc igitur maledictus eris, » etc. (RAB.) Notandum quod in peccato Adae terra maledicitur. Cain vero maledicitur, quia sciens damnationem primae praevaricationis, fratricidium addidit.

VERS. 11.-- « Nunc igitur maledictus eris, » etc. Per locum quae in loco sunt significantur; quasi maledictus 99Beris ab his qui sunt super terram, qui aperuerunt os suum in confessione peccatorum, accipere sanguinem, qui effusus in remissionem eorum de manu persecutoris, qui noluit esse sub gratia, sed sub lege, ut esset maledictus ab Ecclesia: quae intelligit et ostendit esse maledictum: qui enim ex operibus legis sunt, sub maledicto sunt.

VERS. 12.-- « Cum operatus, » etc. Ad vocem, non ad intellectum fit relatio, quia aliter hic terra, aliter supra accipitur: ibi pro Ecclesia, hic pro operatione terrena quam adhuc Judaeus exercet; id est carnalis circumcisio, pascha, sabbatum, et similia quae habent occultam virtutem intelligendae gratiae Dei, sed non dant Judaeis fructum nolentibus transire ad Christum. Potest in terra caro Christi intelligi, in 99Cqua salutem nostram Judaei crucifigendo sunt operati. « Mortuus est enim propter dilicta nostra, et resurrexit propter justificationem nostram: » quem quia negaverunt, justificati non sunt.

« Vagus et profugus, » etc. (ISID.) Dispersus in gentibus, profugus a Hierusalem. LXX: « gemens et tremens. » Gemens, dolore amissi regni; tremens, multitudine Christiani populi, ne occidaris. « Major est iniquitas mea, » etc. Culpam exaggerat, sed superbe confitetur, nec veniam flagitat.

VERS. 13.-- « Major est iniquitas mea. » (ISID.) Peccata peccatis adjiciens desperat, nec credit se veniam posse adipisci, quod est blasphemia in Spiritum sanctum, quae non remittitur in hoc saeculo nec in futuro: quia putat Deum aut nolle dimittere, 99Daut non posse, tanquam aut omnia non possit, aut invideat saluti. Non Deum sibi iratum grave putat, sed timet ne inveniatur et occidatur.

VERS. 14.-- « Omnis igitur qui invenerit me, » etc. (STRAB.) Quia ex tremore corporis et agitatione furiosae mentis, quicunque invenerit me, cognoscet me reum mortis.

VERS. 15.-- « Nequaquam ita fiet. » Id est, non morieris cito sicut tu vis, pro remedio tui cruciatus, sed punietur impersonaliter: id est, punitio fiet de te septuplum, quia vives in cruciatu usque ad septimam generationem, in qua a Lamech occideris; ergo omnis qui occiderit, subaudi, liberabit te ab illo cruciatu malae conscientiae. Hic sensus evidentius 100Apatet. Secundum LXX. « Omnis qui occiderit Cain septem vindictas exsolvet, » id est, quicunque te in septima generatione occiderit, exsolvet te a cruciatu per mortem, qui usque ad tempus illud reservandus es in vita, tantum ad poenam, quae poena cito finiretur, si cito interfectus esses. Ita qui Judaeos visibili morte puniret, septem vindictas ab eis auferret, quibus obligati sunt propter reatum occisi Christi, quia miserias et dispersiones eorum leviaret quas patiuntur.

« Omnis qui occiderit Cain, » etc. LXX: « Septem vindictas exsolvet. » Aquila, « septempliciter; » Symmachus, « septuplum; » Theodotion, « per hebdomadem » interpretantur, in quo significatur quia diu et perfecte punietur, ne quis homicidium audeat 100Biterare.

« Qui occiderit Cain, » etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Damas. ) Si quis te interfecerit « septuplum punietur, » id est, gravissima ultione punietur: non debuit homo occidere eum cui Deus reservavit vitam, vel ad poenam ipsius, vel ad correctionem aliorum.

« Sed omnis qui occiderit Cain. » (ISID., ubi supra. ) Per eclipsim legendum, etc., usque ad et impletur illud: « Disperge illos in virtute tua (Psal. LIII). »

(HIER., ubi supra. ) Quod Aquila posuit « septempliciter, » etc., usque ad et absolvi mereretur.

« Posuitque Dominus Cain signum, » etc. (ISID., Quaest. in Gen., tom. V.) Cum omnes gentes a Romanis subjugatae transierunt in ritus eorum, gens Judaea, sive sub gentibus sive sub Christianis regibus, 100Cnon reliquit signum circumcisionis, et carnalis observantiae, etc., usque ad ubi plantatus est paradisus.

« Habitavit in terra ad orientalem plagam Eden. » (HIER., ubi supra. ) LXX: « Habitavit in terra Ναΐδ Naid, in Hebraeo ( nod ) dicitur, id est, instabilis et fluctuans, et incertae sedis. Non est terra Naid, ut vulgus nostrum putat, sed expletur sententia Domini, dum huc atque illuc profugus aberrat.

VERS. 17.-- « Cognovit autem Cain uxorem suam. » (ISID., ibid.) Figurate progenies impiorum, in ipsa mundi origine fundamentum quaerens, in hac vita civitatem exstruxit quam vocavit Henoch, quod interpretatur dedicatio, quia in primordiis dedicant, dum in hac vita quae ante est, radicem cordis plantant, 100Dut hic ad votum floreant, et a futura gloria arescant. Abraham vero in casulis habitabat, quia sancti hospites et peregrinos se esse cognoscunt, exspectantes civitatem habentem fundamentum cujus artifex Deus est.

« Et aedificavit civitatem, » etc. (AUG., lib. XV de Civitate, cap. 8, tom. VII.) Quaeritur quibus adjutoribus eam aedificaverit, si plures homines non fuerint quam Scriptura dicit. Sed sciendum est multo plures fuisse tunc, quia diu vivendo plures poterant generare. Nec mirum, cum filii Israel per quadringentos annos sic multiplicati leguntur, ut non possit numerari exercitus.

VERS. 18.-- « Porro Enoch, » etc. (HIERON.) Non 101Ade omnibus his certa mysteria exsculpuntur, nec invenirentur, nisi prius quaedam radices historiae jacerentur; nec de ramis arborum fructus legeres, nisi truncos antea plantasses.

VERS. 19.-- « Qui accepit duas uxores, » etc. (ISID., ut supra. ) Notandum quod generationes ab Adam per Cain undenario terminantur, in quo transgressio signatur. Lamech namque septimus est, cui tres filii et una filia adduntur, ut undenarius impleatur, per quem peccatum significatur: nam et a femina clauditur generatio, quae initium peccati fuit, per quod voluptas carnis, quae resistit spiritui. Unde Noema vocatur, id est, voluptas. In hac progenie Lamech primus contra morem, contra naturam, per bigamiam adulterium commisit.

101BVERS. 20.-- « Genuitque Ada Jabel, » etc. (HIER. in quaest. Hebr. ) De bigamia nascitur Jabel habitans in tentoriis, quae mutabilia instabilitatem eorum figurant, qui circumferuntur omni vento doctrinae. Nascitur et Tubal, qui inventor musicae, etc., usque ad sicut Jabel, Tubal et Tubalcain.

VERS. 21.-- « Et nomen fratris ejus Jubal. » (RAB.) Hunc scribit Josephus musicam scripsisse in duabus columnis, una lapidea, altera latericia, quarum altera non dissolveretur diluvio, altera quae non solveretur incendio: quae duo judicia Adam ventura praedixerat.

VERS. 22.-- . . . « Tubalcain. (RAB.) Qui secundum Josephum res bellicas exercuit, et decenter artem ferrariam docuit; quaedam etiam quae ad oculorum 101Cpertinent concupiscentiam, invenit.

VERS. 23.-- « Dixitque Lamech, » etc. (RAB.) Aiunt Hebraei Lamech diu vivendo caliginem oculorum incurrisse, et adolescentem ducem et rectorem itineris habuisse. Exercens ergo venationem, sagittam direxit quo adolescens indicavit, casuque Cain inter fruteta latentem interfecit; et hoc est quod dicit: « Occidi virum in vulnus meum, » id est vulnere quod infixi non bestiam sed hominem occidi; unde et furore accensus occidit adolescentem.

VERS. 23.-- . . . « Occidi virum, » etc. (STRAB.) Id est Cain: « in vulnus meum, » quia pro illo vulnerabor, id est, occidar; « et adolescentulum in livorem meum, » quia pro illo quoque damnabor.

VERS. 24.-- « Septuplum ultio dabitur, » etc. 101D(ALC.) Allegorice. Peccatum Lamech (qui interpretatur humiliatus ) significat dejectionem humani generis quod praevaricante Adam corruit, nec per legem, vel per circumcisionem, nec per suam justitiam surgere potuit, donec post septuaginta septem generationes secundum Lucam Christus in mundum veniens, effusione sanguinis peccatum delevit; unde Matth. XVIII:

« Non dico tibi septies, sed usque septuagies septies, » hoc est, Judaeum recipiendum esse post septuaginta septem vindictas, si resipuerit, et Christi indulgentiam quaesierit. Septenarius universitatem significat: per quem ducto undenario fit septuagenarius septimus, in quo omnimoda transgressio signatur; 102Aquae tamen in Evangelio poenitenti dimitti praecipitur.

(HIERON., epist. ad Damas. ) Aiunt ab Adam usque ad Christum generationes esse septuaginta septem secundum Lucam. Sicut ergo septima generatione peccatum Cain solutum est: « Non enim judicabit Deus bis in idipsum, » et qui semel recepit in vita sua, non eosdem cruciatus patietur in morte quos passus est in vita sua: ita et Lamech, id est, totius mundi peccatum, Christi solvetur adventu. Referebat mihi quidam Hebraeus septuaginta septem animas exisse de Lamech, quae omnes interiere diluvio.

VERS. 25.-- « Cognovit quoque adhuc, » etc. Per Seth, qui interpretatur resurrectio, signatur vita Christi ex mortuis: quod in verbis matris significatur: 102B« Posuit mihi Dominus semen aliud, » quasi dicat Ecclesia: Quae luctum pertuli de mortuo, gaudeo de resuscitato.

VERS. 26.-- « Quem vocavit Enos, » etc. Enos interpretatur homo: qui « coepit invocare nomen Domini, » quia per mortem Christi, quam significavit Abel, et per resurrectionem, quam significat Seth, fit homo confitens Dominum conditorem, et vitae perditae reparatorem.

(HIERON.) « Quem vocavit Enos. » Enos interpretatur homo, qui « coepit invocare nomen Domini: » sicut Adam homo interpretatur, ita etiam Enos juxta Hebraicam veritatem homo, vel vir; et pulchre, quia hoc vocabulum habuit, dictum est quia initium fuit tunc invocandi nomen Domini: licet plerique Hebraeorum 102Carbitrantur quod tunc primum in nomine Domini et in similitudine ejus fabricata sunt idola.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- « But Adam, » etc. (AUG., lib. IX de Gen. ad litt., c. 4). In paradise virginity, outside it marriage, etc., as far as but afterwards begotten in exile.

« She conceived and bore, » etc. Cain and Abel, begotten of one mother, are a figure of all men who are propagated into this life from the root of sin; and some are to love the earthly city and its death-bearing delights, and, so far as in them lies, to possess them by ambition; these Cain signifies, who is interpreted possession. Others are to seek the city to come, and, mourning over the miseries of this habitation, 98Bto pass over with all their desire to the glory to come — these Abel signifies, who is interpreted mourning, and is the later: because that which is spiritual is not first, but that which is animal. Hence Cain is called « a husbandman, » that is, one bent upon earthly works; but Abel « a shepherd of sheep, » that is, one loving simplicity and innocence.

Allegorically. (ISID., in Gen. ) The two sons of Adam express two peoples, namely the Jewish, the elder by birth, etc., as far as as though reason did not permit the depraved motion to come to effect, but strove to subdue it.

VERS. 2.-- « And Abel was, » etc. In whom are signified those who mourn over their earthly dwelling. For Abel is interpreted mourning, or vapor, because he was a mourning to his parents, or, like a vapor, quickly vanished away.

98CVERS. 3, 4.-- « And it came to pass, » etc. « Abel also offered, » etc. Because the just man in all that he does strives to please God through faith and charity (from which the other virtues arise, and without which they profit nothing), which is signified in the fat that was offered; whence psal. LXII: « As with marrow and fatness let my soul be filled. » Without charity the soul is lean and languid, and in nothing will it please God.

« And the Lord had respect to Abel, » etc. (HIER., Quaest. Hebr. in Gen., tom. III.) Whence could Cain have known this, unless Theodotion's rendering were true: « And the Lord set fire upon Abel and upon his sacrifice, upon Cain and his sacrifice he did not set fire? » Now we read that fire was wont to come from heaven to devour the sacrifice, as at the dedication of the temple 98Dunder Solomon, and when Elias built an altar on Mount Carmel.

(ISID., ut supra. ) If thou offer rightly, and do not rightly divide, thou hast sinned: because, even though the Jews formerly offered those things rightly, in this they are guilty, that they did not distinguish the New Testament from the Old.

VERS. 5, 6, 7.-- . . . . « In the doors. » (ALC.) That is, sin will accompany thee going in and going out, nor will the Lord keep thy coming in and thy going out.

VERS. 8.-- « And Cain said, » etc. (ISID., Quaest. in Gen., tom. V.) He does not acquiesce in the Lord when he says, Be still, etc., as far as Christ therefore, the head of the younger people, is slain by the elder-born.

VERS. 9.-- « And the Lord said to Cain. » (ISID., ubi 99Asupra.) The Lord questions Cain, not as one ignorant that he may learn, but as a judge that he may punish. To this day the Jews, when we question them out of the Scriptures concerning Christ, answer that they do not know what we say. Cain's lying ignorance is the Jews' false denial: but they would be Christ's keepers, if they believed with the heart and confessed with the mouth.

VERS. 10.-- « The voice of the blood, » etc. Great is the power of justice, which demands vengeance for the blood of the innocent.

VERS. 11.-- « Now therefore thou shalt be cursed, » etc. (RAB.) It is to be noted that in Adam's sin the earth is cursed. But Cain is cursed, because, knowing the condemnation of the first transgression, he added fratricide.

VERS. 11.-- « Now therefore thou shalt be cursed, » etc. By the place are signified the things which are in the place; as though thou shalt be cursed 99Bby those who are upon the earth, who opened their mouth in the confession of sins, to receive the blood which was shed for the remission of them from the hand of the persecutor, who would not be under grace, but under the law, so that he should be cursed by the Church: which understands and shows him to be cursed: for they who are of the works of the law are under a curse.

VERS. 12.-- « When thou hast tilled, » etc. The reference is made to the word, not to the sense, because "earth" is taken here in one way, above in another: there for the Church, here for the earthly working which the Jew still practises; that is, carnal circumcision, the passover, the sabbath, and the like, which have a hidden power of understanding the grace of God, but do not give fruit to the Jews, who are unwilling to pass over to Christ. By "earth" the flesh of Christ can be understood, in 99Cwhich the Jews by crucifying wrought our salvation. « For he died for our sins, and rose again for our justification: » [Vulg. Rom 4:25 Qui traditus est, "who was delivered up"] and because they denied him, they were not justified.

« A vagabond and a fugitive, » etc. (ISID.) Scattered among the nations, a fugitive from Jerusalem. LXX: « groaning and trembling. » Groaning, from the grief of the kingdom he has lost; trembling, at the multitude of the Christian people, lest thou be slain. « My iniquity is greater, » etc. He exaggerates his fault, but confesses it proudly, and does not beg pardon.

VERS. 13.-- « My iniquity is greater. » (ISID.) Adding sins to sins he despairs, and does not believe that he can obtain pardon, which is blasphemy against the Holy Spirit, which is not forgiven in this world nor in the world to come: because he thinks that God either will not forgive, 99Dor cannot, as though either he could not do all things, or begrudged salvation. He does not count it a grievous thing that God is angry with him, but fears that he may be found and killed.

VERS. 14.-- « Every one therefore that shall find me, » etc. (STRAB.) Because from the trembling of the body and the agitation of a frenzied mind, whosoever shall find me will know me to be guilty of death.

VERS. 15.-- « It shall not be so. » That is, thou shalt not die quickly, as thou wishest, for a remedy of thy torment, but he shall be punished impersonally: that is, punishment shall be made of thee sevenfold, because thou shalt live in torment until the seventh generation, in which thou shalt be slain by Lamech; therefore every one who shall kill — supply the word — shall free thee from that torment of an evil conscience. This sense 100Aappears more evidently. According to the LXX. « Every one that killeth Cain shall pay seven vengeances, » that is, whosoever shall kill thee in the seventh generation shall release thee from torment by death, thou who art to be reserved in life until that time, only for punishment, which punishment would be quickly ended if thou wert quickly slain. So he who should punish the Jews with visible death would take away from them the seven vengeances by which they are bound because of the guilt of Christ slain, because he would lighten the miseries and dispersions which they suffer.

« Every one that killeth Cain, » etc. LXX: « Shall pay seven vengeances. » Aquila, « sevenfold; » Symmachus, « sevenfold; » Theodotion, « by a week » — so they render it, wherein it is signified that he shall be punished long and completely, lest any one dare 100Bto repeat a homicide.

« He that killeth Cain, » etc. (HIERON., epist. ad Damas. ) If any one kill thee « he shall be punished sevenfold, » that is, he shall be punished with the heaviest vengeance: a man ought not to have killed him for whom God reserved life, either for his own punishment, or for the correction of others.

« But every one that killeth Cain. » (ISID., ubi supra. ) It is to be read by ellipsis, etc., as far as and that is fulfilled: « Scatter them in thy strength (Psal. LIII). »

(HIER., ubi supra. ) That Aquila put « sevenfold, » etc., as far as and might deserve to be absolved.

« And the Lord set a mark upon Cain, » etc. (ISID., Quaest. in Gen., tom. V.) Though all the nations subdued by the Romans passed over into their rites, the Jewish nation, whether under Gentiles or under Christian kings, 100Cdid not leave the sign of circumcision and of carnal observance, etc., as far as where paradise was planted.

« He dwelt in the land at the eastern quarter of Eden. » (HIER., ubi supra. ) LXX: « He dwelt in the land Ναΐδ Naid, in the Hebrew it is called ( nod ), that is, unstable and wavering, and of uncertain seat. It is not the land Naid, as our common folk think, but the Lord's sentence is fulfilled, while he strays to and fro a fugitive.

VERS. 17.-- « And Cain knew his wife. » (ISID., ibid.) Figuratively the offspring of the impious, seeking a foundation in the very origin of the world, built in this life a city which he called Henoch, which is interpreted dedication, because in their first beginnings they dedicate, while in this life which is before them they plant the root of the heart, 100Dthat here they may flourish to their wish, and wither away from the glory to come. But Abraham dwelt in little cottages, because the saints know themselves to be guests and pilgrims, awaiting the city that has a foundation, whose builder is God.

« And he built a city, » etc. (AUG., lib. XV de Civitate, cap. 8, tom. VII.) It is asked with what helpers he built it, if there were not more men than Scripture says. But it is to be known that there were then far more, because by living long they were able to beget more. Nor is it a wonder, since the children of Israel are read to have been so multiplied in four hundred years that the army could not be numbered.

VERS. 18.-- « Moreover Enoch, » etc. (HIERON.) Not 101Afrom all these are sure mysteries carved out, nor would they be found, unless first certain roots of the history were laid down; nor wouldst thou gather fruit from the branches of the trees, unless thou hadst first planted the trunks.

VERS. 19.-- « Who took two wives, » etc. (ISID., ut supra. ) It is to be noted that the generations from Adam through Cain are terminated in the eleventh, in which transgression is signified. For Lamech is the seventh, to whom three sons and one daughter are added, that the eleventh may be filled out, by which sin is signified: for the generation is closed also by a woman, who was the beginning of sin, through which is the pleasure of the flesh, which resists the spirit. Whence she is called Noema, that is, pleasure. In this offspring Lamech first, against custom, against nature, committed adultery through bigamy.

101BVERS. 20.-- « And Ada bore Jabel, » etc. (HIER. in quaest. Hebr. ) Of bigamy is born Jabel dwelling in tents, which, being changeable, figure the instability of those who are carried about with every wind of doctrine. There is born also Tubal, who was the inventor of music, etc., as far as like Jabel, Tubal and Tubalcain.

VERS. 21.-- « And his brother's name was Jubal. » (RAB.) Josephus writes that he wrote music on two columns, the one of stone, the other of brick, of which the one would not be dissolved by the flood, the other which would not be dissolved by fire: which two judgments Adam had foretold were to come.

VERS. 22.-- . . . « Tubalcain. (RAB.) Who according to Josephus practised the arts of war, and fittingly taught the smith's craft; he also invented certain things which 101Cbelong to the concupiscence of the eyes.

VERS. 23.-- « And Lamech said, » etc. (RAB.) The Hebrews say that Lamech by long living fell into a darkening of the eyes, and had a young man as his leader and the director of his way. Practising the hunt, therefore, he aimed an arrow where the young man pointed, and by chance killed Cain lurking among the bushes; and this is what he says: « I have slain a man to my own wounding, » that is, by the wound which I inflicted I killed not a beast but a man; whence also, kindled with fury, he killed the young man.

VERS. 23.-- . . . « I have slain a man, » etc. (STRAB.) That is, Cain: « to my own wounding, » because for him I shall be wounded, that is, slain; « and a young man to my own bruising, » because for him too I shall be condemned.

VERS. 24.-- « Sevenfold vengeance shall be given, » etc. 101D(ALC.) Allegorically. The sin of Lamech (who is interpreted humbled ) signifies the casting down of the human race, which fell when Adam transgressed, and could not rise, neither by the law, or by circumcision, nor by its own justice, until, after seventy-seven generations according to Luke, Christ, coming into the world, blotted out sin by the shedding of his blood; whence Matth. XVIII:

« I say not to thee seven times, but until seventy times seven times, » that is, that the Jew is to be received after seventy-seven vengeances, if he shall come to himself again and seek Christ's indulgence. The number seven signifies universality: by which, the eleventh being multiplied, there comes the seventy-seventh, in which transgression of every kind is signified; 102Awhich nevertheless in the Gospel is commanded to be forgiven to one who repents.

(HIERON., epist. ad Damas. ) They say that from Adam to Christ there are seventy-seven generations according to Luke. As therefore in the seventh generation Cain's sin was loosed: « For God will not judge twice for the same thing, » and he who has once received in his life will not suffer in death the same torments which he suffered in his life: so also Lamech, that is, the sin of the whole world, shall be loosed by Christ's coming. A certain Hebrew told me that seventy-seven souls came forth from Lamech, who all perished in the flood.

VERS. 25.-- « He knew also again, » etc. Through Seth, who is interpreted resurrection, is signified the life of Christ from the dead: which is signified in the mother's words: 102B« The Lord hath given me another seed, » [Vulg. Gn 4:25 reads Deus, "God"] as though the Church said: I who bore mourning for the dead rejoice over him raised again.

VERS. 26.-- « Whom he called Enos, » etc. Enos is interpreted man: who « began to call upon the name of the Lord, » because through the death of Christ, which Abel signified, and through the resurrection, which Seth signifies, man becomes one confessing the Lord as founder, and as restorer of the life that was lost.

(HIERON.) « Whom he called Enos. » Enos is interpreted man, who « began to call upon the name of the Lord: » as Adam is interpreted man, so also Enos according to the Hebrew truth is man, or husband; and beautifully, because he had this name, it is said that then was the beginning of calling upon the name of the Lord: although very many of the Hebrews 102Cthink that then for the first time idols were fashioned in the name of the Lord and in his likeness.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Hic est liber generationis Adam. » Ad similitudinem Dei factus est. Alii, ad similitudinem Adae. Est quasi conclusio a superioribus cum recapitulatione. « In die qua creavit Deus hominem. » In die etenim creatus est, quia in claritate divinae contemplationis constitutus. « Ad similitudinem Dei. » Quia rationem ei dedit, qua intelligendo bona et mala discerneret, intellecta diligeret, et dilecta oblivioni non traderet, et in hoc Dei similitudinem retineret. Sed postquam per inobedientiam 102Dhanc similitudinem delevit, factus terrenus terrenos generavit. Unde qualis terrenus, tales et terreni.

(CHRYSOST., hom. 21 in Gen., tom. IV.) Considera quomodo iisdem verbis usus fuerit quibus et initio, ut doceret nos quod generationes illas, quasi reprobas factas, neque memoria post hac dignetur; sed ex Seth, qui nunc illis natus est, genealogiam orditur, ut ex hoc discas quantam Deus humani generis rationem habeat, et quomodo illos aversetur quibus sanguinaria mens est.

VERS. 3-23.-- « Vixit autem Adam, » etc. (HIERON., in Quaest. Hebr. ). Sciendum quod usque ad diluvium, ubi in nostris codicibus « ducentorum » et quod excurrit annorum genuisse quis dicitur, in Hebraeo 103Ahabeat « centum annos » et reliquos qui sequuntur.

« Et genuit, » etc. Quasi et iste portavit imaginem terreni qui pro Abel justo repositus est, et interpretatione nominis Christum significat. Sed omnes prius portant imaginem terreni hominis quam coelestis, licet multi ad adventum Christi per fidem eamdem gratiam habuerunt qua salvantur Christiani.

(STRAB.) Ferunt post mortem Abel vovisse Adam se uxorem non ultra cogniturum nec filium generaturum. Sed Deo jubente fregit votum, ut Dei Filius de eo nasceretur, et homo redimeretur; stirpe enim Cain nasci non debuit. Unde ante Seth nullum legitur genuisse, post eum vero dicitur; quia « genuit filios et filias. » Haec computatio genealogiarum, 103Bquasi ramos spargit historiarum, de quibus suis locis colliguntur fructus mysteriorum. Et notandum quia in generatione Seth nulla femina ponitur nominatim, sicut in generatione Cain: justorum enim est omnia viriliter agere, nihil femineum, nihil fragile usurpare. Procedit etiam haec generatio per denarium usque ad Noe, in quo praeceptorum signatur impletio, sicut per undenarium in generatione Cain transgressio. In generatione injustorum primus Enoch ponitur, qui dedicatio interpretatur, quia reprobi in praesenti vita spei suae radicem figunt, et ab amore immarcescibilis haereditatis arescunt. De quibus dicitur Joan. v : « Vidit stultum firma radice, et maledixit pulchritudini ejus statim. » In generatione justorum Enoch ab Adam septimus ponitur, quia hic 103Cmanentem civitatem non quaerunt, sed exspectationem usque in finem temporis extendunt, quod septenaria revolutione terminatur, cum aeternitas octava successerit. Unde iste Enoch qui ponitur septimus, et translatus est quia Deo placuit, septimam requiem significat, ad quam post hanc vitam, qui Deo perseveranter adhaeret, sine dilatione transfertur. Interpretatio quoque nominum hujus generationis idem signat: Adam interpretatur homo vel rubra terra; Seth, positio vel resurrectio; Enos, homo; Cainan, lamentatio; Malalehel, laudans Deum; Jared, roboratus; Enoch, dedicatio; Mathusalam, mortis emissio; Lamech, humiliatus; Noe, requies. Procedens ergo homo de terra, resurgens a peccatis, erit homo invocans Deum et deflens peccata pristina: sicque 103Dlaudans Deum, roboratus Spiritu Dei, dedicatur in vitam aeternam. Atque victor mortis Deo subjectus, requiem possidet sempiternam, quam significat Noe qui requies interpretatur, et decimo loco numeratur, quia per impletionem mandatorum possidetur regnum coelorum.

VERS. 24.-- « Ambulavitque cum Deo, » etc. (ISID., in Gen.) Enoch septimus ab Adam placens Deo, etc., usque ad nam ter quaterni ipsum faciunt.

(ALCUIN.) Enoch tanto tempore servatur a morte, in quo ostenditur quia homo semper viveret si non peccasset.

VERS. 25-30.-- « Vixit quoque Mathusala, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeritur quomodo Mathusala secundum annorum 104Acomputationem post diluvium vivere potuerit, cum omnes, praeter eos qui in arcam ingressi sunt, perierunt. Sed hanc quaestionem codicum mendositas peperit: non solum enim in Hebraeis aliter invenitur, verum etiam in LXX interpretatione, paucis codicibus septem annis ante diluvium Mathusala defunctus reperitur. Quod Mathusala juxta LXX ultra diluvium numerantur anni, inde est quia significat Christum, cujus vita nullam sentit aetatem, ut in majoribus quoque non sensisse diluvium videretur.

VERS. 31.-- « Et facti sunt omnes dies, » etc. (HIERON., in Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.) « Et fuerunt omnes dies Mathusalae quos vixit anni nongenti sexaginta novem, et mortuus est. » Juxta diligentem supputationem quatuordecim annis post diluvium vixis 104Bse refertur, etc., usque ad eo anno scilicet quo coepit esse diluvium.

« Noe vero cum quingentorum esset annorum, » etc. (Ibid.)

Noe interpretatur requies, quia sub illo omnia retro opera per diluvium quieverunt.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « This is the book of the generation of Adam. » He was made to the likeness of God. Others, to the likeness of Adam. It is as it were a conclusion from what has gone before, with a recapitulation. « In the day that God created man. » For in the day he was created, because he was set in the brightness of divine contemplation. « To the likeness of God. » Because he gave him reason, by which, discerning good and evil, he might love the things understood, and not hand over the things loved to forgetfulness, and in this retain the likeness of God. But after by disobedience 102Dhe had blotted out this likeness, being made earthly he begot earthly children. Whence, such as the earthly man is, such also are the earthly.

(CHRYSOST., hom. 21 in Gen., tom. IV.) Consider how he used the same words which he used also at the beginning, that he might teach us that he does not deign to remember those generations after this, as having been made reprobate; but from Seth, who has now been born to them, he begins the genealogy, that from this thou mayest learn how great account God takes of the human race, and how he turns away from those whose mind is bloody.

VERS. 3-23.-- « And Adam lived, » etc. (HIERON., in Quaest. Hebr. ). It is to be known that up to the flood, where in our codices someone is said to have begotten at « two hundred » and the years that run beyond, in the Hebrew 103Ait has « a hundred years » and the rest which follow.

« And he begot, » etc. As though he too bore the image of the earthly man, he who was put in the place of the just Abel, and by the interpretation of his name signifies Christ. But all bear the image of the earthly man before that of the heavenly, although many before the coming of Christ had through faith the same grace by which Christians are saved.

(STRAB.) They report that after Abel's death Adam vowed that he would not know his wife further nor beget a son. But at God's bidding he broke the vow, that the Son of God might be born from him, and man be redeemed; for he ought not to be born of the stock of Cain. Whence before Seth he is not read to have begotten any one, but after him it is said; because « he begot sons and daughters. » This reckoning of the genealogies 103Bspreads as it were the branches of the histories, from which in their own places the fruits of the mysteries are gathered. And it is to be noted that in the generation of Seth no woman is set down by name, as in the generation of Cain: for it belongs to the just to do all things manfully, to usurp nothing womanish, nothing frail. This generation also proceeds by the tenth up to Noe, in whom the fulfilling of the commandments is signified, as by the eleventh in the generation of Cain transgression is signified. In the generation of the unjust Enoch is set first, who is interpreted dedication, because the reprobate fix the root of their hope in the present life, and wither away from the love of the unfading inheritance. Of whom it is said, John V: « He saw the fool with a firm root, and cursed his beauty forthwith. » [Vulg. Job 5:3, where the words stand in the first person — Ego vidi … et maledixi, "I have seen … and I cursed"] In the generation of the just Enoch is set seventh from Adam, because these 103Cdo not seek a city that abides here, but stretch out their expectation to the end of time, which is terminated by a sevenfold revolution, when the eighth eternity shall have succeeded. Whence this Enoch, who is set seventh, and was translated because he pleased God, signifies the seventh rest, to which after this life he who perseveringly cleaves to God is transferred without delay. The interpretation also of the names of this generation signifies the same: Adam is interpreted man or red earth; Seth, placing or resurrection; Enos, man; Cainan, lamentation; Malalehel, praising God; Jared, strengthened; Enoch, dedication; Mathusalam, sending forth of death; Lamech, humbled; Noe, rest. Man therefore, proceeding from the earth, rising again from sins, will be a man calling upon God and weeping for his former sins: and so 103Dpraising God, strengthened by the Spirit of God, he is dedicated unto life eternal. And, a conqueror of death, made subject to God, he possesses everlasting rest, which Noe signifies, who is interpreted rest, and is numbered in the tenth place, because through the fulfilling of the commandments the kingdom of heaven is possessed.

VERS. 24.-- « And he walked with God, » etc. (ISID., in Gen.) Enoch, the seventh from Adam, pleasing God, etc., as far as for three times four make it.

(ALCUIN.) Enoch is preserved from death for so long a time, in which it is shown that man would always have lived if he had not sinned.

VERS. 25-30.-- « Mathusala also lived, » etc. (AUG.) It is asked how Mathusala, according to the 104Areckoning of the years, could have lived after the flood, since all perished except those who entered into the ark. But this question the faultiness of the codices has bred: for not only in the Hebrew is it found otherwise, but also in the LXX rendering, in a few codices Mathusala is found to have died seven years before the flood. That according to the LXX the years of Mathusala are reckoned beyond the flood, comes of this, that he signifies Christ, whose life feels no age, so that in the greater ones too he might seem not to have felt the flood.

VERS. 31.-- « And all the days were, » etc. (HIERON., in Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.) « And all the days of Mathusala which he lived were nine hundred sixty-nine years, and he died. » According to a careful computation he is reported to have lived 104Bfourteen years after the flood, etc., as far as in that year, namely, in which the flood began to be.

« But Noe, when he was five hundred years old, » etc. (Ibid.)

Noe is interpreted rest, because under him all the works behind were at rest through the flood.

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 1.-- « Cumque coepissent homines multiplicari super terram. » (HIER., in Quaest. Hebr. ) Verbum Hebraicum אלהים ( Elohim ) utriusque numeri est: Deum enim et deos significat: ideo Aquila « filios deorum » dicere ausus est, deos sanctos vel angelos intelligens, quem sequens Symmachus ait: « Videntes filii potentum filias hominum. »

104CVERS. 2.-- « Videntes filii Dei, » etc. (STRAB.) Filii Seth religiosi intelliguntur per filios Dei, qui victi concupiscentia, ex filiabus hominum, id est ex stirpe Cain, uxores acceperunt, et viros potentes genuerunt, immensos scilicet corporibus, superbos viribus, inconditos moribus, qui gigantes appellantur. Non est incredibile ab hominibus, non ab angelis, vel quibusdam daemonibus qui mulieribus sunt improbi, ejusmodi homines esse procreatos, quia et post diluvium corpora non solum virorum, sed et mulierum incredibili magnitudine exstiterunt.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « Dixitque Deus: Non permanebit Spiritus meus, » etc. (HIER., ibid.) Hebr. לאידון ( loiadon ). « Non judicabit spiritus meus homines in sempiternum, quoniam caro sunt. » Hoc est, quia 104Dfragilis est hominum conditio, etc., usque ad sed eorum quos erat diluvio deleturus.

« Eruntque dies illius, » etc. (STRAB.) Ante diluvium, scilicet ad agendam poenitentiam. Sed quia in malitia perseveraverunt ante praefixum terminum, centesimo anno deleti sunt.

VERS. 5.-- « Videns autem Dominus, » etc. Non in Deum poenitentia cadit, aut dolor cordis, cui est de omnibus tam fixa sententia quam certa praescientia: sed utitur Scriptura usitatis verbis, coaptans se nostrae parvitati, ut ex cognitis incognita cognoscamus. Nam ira Dei non est perturbatio animi, sed judicium quod irrogatur peccatori: cogitatio vero cordis ejus, mutandarum rerum immutabilis ratio.

105AVERS. 6-8.-- « Poenituit eum quia, » etc. (GREG., Moral. lib. V). Plerumque in sacro eloquio sapientes Dei consilium trahunt a sapientibus saeculi. Sic nunc, pro utilitate hominis, vocem in se passionis humanae ipse conditor hominum sumit, cum constet quia qui cuncta, priusquam venerint, conspicit, nihil fecerit quod poenitendo resipiscat. Ut ergo mirum non est, si spirituales plerumque utantur verbis carnalium, sic neque mirandum si ipse creator omnium, ut carnem pertrahat ad intellectum suum, in seipso carnis sermonem servet vel sumat.

VERS. 9-12.-- « Noe vir justus. » Hic per actus suos significat Christum, qui ait Matth. XI: « Discite a me quia mitis sum et humilis corde. » Solus justus invenitur, cui propter justitiam suam septem homines 105Bdonantur. Justus quoque Christus et perfectus, cui septem Ecclesiae septiformi Spiritu illuminatae in unam Ecclesia condonantur. Noe per aquam et lignum liberatur, et familia ejus; sic familia Christi per baptismum et crucem.

« In generationibus suis. » Quasi non consummatae justitiae, sed juxta generationem suam.

VERS. 13.-- « Disperdam eos cum, » etc. Tradunt doctores terrae virorem et fecunditatem longe esse inferiorem post diluvium; et ideo hominibus esum carnium concessum, cum antea tantum terrae fructibus victitarent.

VERS. 14.-- « Fac tibi arcam de lignis. » Scilicet fortibus et insolubilibus, et bene coarctatis; quae alia translatio dicit « quadrata, » ut nec vi ventorum 105Cnec inundatione solvetur.

« Mansiunculas, » etc. (STRAB.) LXX: « Bicamerata et tricamerata in arca facies: bicamerata in inferioribus, tricamerata in superioribus. » Quinque ergo mansionibus distincta sunt: prima, id est inferior, fuit stercoraria, quo stercora defluebant, ne qui erant in arca fetore laederentur; secunda, apothecaria; tertia, immitium animalium et serpentium; quarta, mansuetorum; quinta, id est suprema, hominum et avium. Ostium, ubi bicamerata et tricamerata jungebantur, id est, inter apothecariam et bestiarum habitationem. (AUG.) In prima habitatione in inferioribus semel camerata erat arca, in secunda super inferiorem habitationem bicamerata. In tertia vero super secundam tricamerata. Dicunt alii in inferioribus 105Dduas fuisse mansiunculas, et sic arcam fuisse bicameratam. Harum inferior stercora suscipiebat, secunda escas, quae apothecaria dicebatur; in superioribus tricamerata erat, ubi homines et animalia congrua distinctione continebantur.

(ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Arcam construxit Noe de lignis imputribilibus, etc., usque ad in quinque prophetizatus, in sexta evangelizatus.

VERS. 15. « Trecentorum cubitorum, » etc. (ISID., ibid.) Potest in trecentis cubitis lignum crucis ostendi, etc., usque ad quia unus Deus ex quo omnia, et una fides, et unum baptisma.

(GREG. hom. XVI in Ezech.) Arca quae trecentis cubitis fieri in longitudine jussa est, quinquaginta 106Ain latitudine, triginta in altitudine, etc., usque ad et per quem omnes proficiunt qui se peccatores noverunt.

VERS. 16-18.-- « In cubito consummabis summitatem ejus. » Sic Ecclesia in unitate collecta sublimatur atque perficitur; unde Matth. XI: « Qui non colligit mecum, dispergit. »

Ostium autem arcae, » etc. Nemo enim intrat in Ecclesiam, nisi per sacramentum remissionis peccatorum, quod de latere aperto emanavit.

« Coenacula et tristega, » etc. LXX: « Bicamerata et tricamerata. » Ex omnibus enim gentibus vel bipertitam multitudinem congregat Ecclesia, ex circumcisione et praeputio; vel tripartitam, ex tribus filiis Noe, quorum progenie repletur orbis. Inferiora 106Bautem arcae distincta sunt, quia in hac vita terrena est diversitas gentium: in summo autem omnes consummantur in unum, quia omnia et in omnibus Christus.

(ALCUIN.) Congrue arca in summo, velut tecto quodam angusto, cacuminata est, ut imbrium minas diffunderet, et ima in aquis stabilitate quadrata consistens, nec impulsu ventorum, nec impetu fluctuum, nec inquietudine animalium quae intus erant inclinari posset aut mergi.

(GREG. in Moral.) Arca in inferioribus ampla, in superioribus angusta, etc., usque ad grandes acervi palearum, quae ignibus comburuntur.

(GREG.) Moraliter autem, qui se ab amore mundi convertit, in corde aedificat arcam salutis, habens in 106Cse longitudinem, id est, Trinitatis fidem, ac longitudinem vitae et immortalitatis; latitudinem in charitate, qua potest bene facere etiam inimicis; altitudinem in spe, qua se erigat ad coelestia et summam actuum suorum ad unum referat.

VERS. 19-20.-- « Et ex cunctis, » etc. (AUG. l. XV de Civit. Dei, c. ult., t. VII.) Quaeritur de minutissimis, sic in Ecclesiae sacramentis versantur boni et mali.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « Tolles igitur, » etc. Solet quaeri de animalibus quae tantum putantur vesci carnibus, utrum praeter numerum ibi fuerint sine transgressione mandati, quae aliorum alendorum necessitas coegisset includi; vel, quod magis credendum est, praeter carnes alimenta esse potuerunt quae omnibus 106Dconvenirent. Multa enim animalia, quibus caro cibus, fructibus pomisque vescuntur, maxime fico et castaneis. Potuit ergo vir justus et sapiens, et divinitus admonitus, quae cuique congrueret, alimoniam, praeter carnes reperire et condere. Et omnibus vesci cogit fames, et nihil est quod Deus suave et salubre facere non possit, qui etiam ut sine cibo viverent divina facultate donaret.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 1.-- « And when men had begun to be multiplied upon the earth. » (HIER., in Quaest. Hebr. ) The Hebrew word ( Elohim ) is of both numbers: for it signifies God and gods: therefore Aquila dared to say « sons of gods, » understanding holy gods or angels, whom Symmachus follows, saying: « The sons of the mighty seeing the daughters of men. »

104CVERS. 2.-- « The sons of God seeing, » etc. (STRAB.) By the sons of God are understood the religious sons of Seth, who, overcome by concupiscence, took wives from the daughters of men, that is, from the stock of Cain, and begot mighty men, immense, namely, in body, proud in strength, disordered in manners, who are called giants. It is not incredible that men of this kind were procreated by men, not by angels or by certain demons who are shameless toward women, since after the flood too the bodies not only of men but also of women existed of incredible size.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « And God said: My spirit shall not remain, » etc. (HIER., ibid.) Hebr. ( loiadon ). « My spirit shall not judge men for ever, since they are flesh. » That is, because 104Dthe condition of men is frail, etc., as far as but of those whom he was about to destroy by the flood.

« And his days shall be, » etc. (STRAB.) Before the flood, namely, for the doing of penance. But because they persevered in malice before the appointed term, they were destroyed in the hundredth year.

VERS. 5.-- « And the Lord seeing, » etc. [Vulg. Gn 6:5 reads Deus, "God"] Repentance does not fall upon God, nor grief of heart, whose sentence concerning all things is as fixed as his foreknowledge is certain: but Scripture uses accustomed words, fitting itself to our littleness, that from things known we may come to know things unknown. For God's anger is not a perturbation of mind, but the judgment which is inflicted upon the sinner: and the thought of his heart is the unchangeable reason of things that are to be changed.

105AVERS. 6-8.-- « It repented him that, » etc. (GREG., Moral. lib. V). Very often in sacred discourse the wise draw the counsel of God from the wise men of the world. So now, for man's profit, the very founder of men takes upon himself the voice of human passion, though it is certain that he who beholds all things before they have come to pass has done nothing of which he should repent and change his mind. As therefore it is no wonder if spiritual men very often use the words of carnal men, so neither is it to be wondered at if the creator himself of all things, that he may draw the flesh to his own understanding, keeps or takes up in himself the speech of the flesh.

VERS. 9-12.-- « Noe a just man. » Here by his acts he signifies Christ, who says Matth. XI: « Learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart. » He alone is found just, to whom because of his justice seven men 105Bare given. Christ too is just and perfect, to whom seven Churches, enlightened by the sevenfold Spirit, are granted together into one Church. Noe is delivered through water and wood, and his household with him; so the household of Christ through baptism and the cross.

« In his generations. » As though not of consummate justice, but according to his own generation.

VERS. 13.-- « I will destroy them with, » etc. The doctors hand down that the greenness and fruitfulness of the earth was far inferior after the flood; and that therefore the eating of flesh was granted to men, whereas before they lived only on the fruits of the earth.

VERS. 14.-- « Make thee an ark of timber. » Namely of timber strong and indissoluble and well compacted; which another translation calls « square, » so that it should be dissolved neither by the force of the winds 105Cnor by the inundation.

« Little rooms, » etc. (STRAB.) LXX: « Thou shalt make in the ark things of two chambers and of three chambers: of two chambers in the lower parts, of three chambers in the upper. » They were therefore divided into five dwellings: the first, that is the lowest, was the dung-place, into which the dung flowed down, lest those who were in the ark should be harmed by the stench; the second, the storeroom; the third, of the fierce animals and of serpents; the fourth, of the tame; the fifth, that is the highest, of men and of birds. The door, where the two-chambered and the three-chambered parts were joined, that is, between the storeroom and the dwelling of the beasts. (AUG.) In the first dwelling, in the lower parts, the ark was of one chamber, in the second, above the lower dwelling, of two chambers. But in the third, above the second, of three chambers. Others say that in the lower parts 105Dthere were two little rooms, and that so the ark was of two chambers. Of these the lower received the dung, the second the food, which was called the storeroom; in the upper parts it was of three chambers, where men and animals were contained with a fitting distinction.

(ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Noe built the ark of imperishable timbers, etc., as far as prophesied in the fifth, evangelized in the sixth.

VERS. 15. « Of three hundred cubits, » etc. (ISID., ibid.) In the three hundred cubits the wood of the cross can be shown, etc., as far as because there is one God from whom are all things, and one faith, and one baptism.

(GREG. hom. XVI in Ezech.) The ark which was commanded to be made three hundred cubits in length, fifty 106Ain breadth, thirty in height, etc., as far as and through whom all advance who have known themselves to be sinners.

VERS. 16-18.-- « In a cubit thou shalt finish the top of it. » So the Church, gathered into unity, is lifted up and made perfect; whence Matth. XI: « He that gathereth not with me, scattereth. »

The door of the ark, » etc. For no one enters into the Church except through the sacrament of the remission of sins, which flowed forth from the opened side.

« Upper chambers and third stories, » etc. LXX: « Things of two chambers and of three chambers. » For out of all nations the Church gathers a multitude either two-parted, from the circumcision and the uncircumcision; or three-parted, from the three sons of Noe, by whose offspring the world is filled. But the lower parts 106Bof the ark are divided, because in this earthly life there is a diversity of nations: but at the top all are consummated into one, because Christ is all things and in all.

(ALCUIN.) Fittingly the ark was brought to a point at the top, as with a certain narrow roof, that it might scatter the threats of the rains, and, standing below in the waters four-square with stability, could be neither tilted nor sunk by the driving of the winds, nor by the onset of the waves, nor by the restlessness of the animals that were within.

(GREG. in Moral.) The ark broad in the lower parts, narrow in the upper, etc., as far as great heaps of chaff, which are burned up with fire.

(GREG.) But morally, he who turns himself away from the love of the world builds in his heart the ark of salvation, having in 106Chimself length, that is, the faith of the Trinity, and length of life and of immortality; breadth in charity, by which he can do good even to enemies; height in hope, by which he may raise himself to heavenly things and refer the sum of his acts to the One.

VERS. 19-20.-- « And of all, » etc. (AUG. l. XV de Civit. Dei, c. ult., t. VII.) It is asked concerning the tiniest creatures, so in the Church's sacraments the good and the bad are engaged.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « Take thou therefore, » etc. It is wont to be asked concerning the animals which are thought to feed only on flesh, whether they were there beyond the number without transgression of the commandment, which the necessity of feeding the others had compelled to be shut in; or, what is rather to be believed, whether besides flesh there could have been foods which would suit 106Dall. For many animals, whose food is flesh, feed on fruits and apples, especially on the fig and on chestnuts. A just and wise man, therefore, divinely admonished, was able to find out and lay up the nourishment which suited each, besides flesh. And hunger compels all to feed, and there is nothing which God cannot make sweet and wholesome, who would even grant by divine power that they should live without food.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1-3.-- « Dixitque Dominus, » etc. « Ex omnibus animantibus mundis tolles septena et septena, » etc. (RAB.) Non geminatum septenarium, id est quatuordecim, sed tantum septem debemus 107Aaccipere. Similiter duo et duo, non quatuor, sed tantum duo, masculum et feminam. Et notandum quod munda animalia impari numero, id est septenario introducuntur, immunda vero pari; quia impar numerus virtutis speciem portendit, par vero infirmitatem. Plura sunt munda, ut egressus Noe de arca haberet quid immolaret et manducaret.

(ISID. in Gen.) « Ex omnibus animantibus, » etc. Non quod plures sint boni quam mali, etc., usque ad qui dies a passione tertius in numero dierum qui per omne tempus volvuntur, et primus, et octavus.

(AUG. in Gen.) Quaeritur utrum tam magna arca centum annis potuit fabricari a quatuor hominibus, id est, Noe et filiis ejus tribus? Sed si non potuit, non erat magnum alios fabros adhibere, quamvis, 107Boperis sui mercede adepta, non curaverunt utrum Noe sapienter an inaniter faceret, et in eam non intraverunt, quia non crediderunt quod ille credidit.

VERS. 4.-- « Ego pluam, » etc. (ISID. Quaest. in Gen., c. 7.) Quia omnis reatus contra decalogum committitur per universum orbem, qui partibus quatuor dividitur: quater vero decem quadraginta fiunt; sive reatus contrahatur ex prosperitate, quod pertinet ad dies; sive ex adversitate, quod ad noctes, in baptismo abluitur.

VERS. 5-6.-- « Fecit ergo Noe omnia, » etc. « Eratque sexcentorum annorum, » etc. (ISID., Ibid). Quingentorum annorum erat Noe cum praecepit ei Dominus ut arcam faceret, etc., usque ad qui est ternarius quadratus.

107CVERS. 7-9.-- « Ingressus est Noe et filii ejus, uxor, » etc. (RAB.) Notandum quod seorsum ingreditur in arcam Noe cum filiis suis, et uxores eorum seorsum. Ideo distincte dicitur: « Ingressus est Noe et filii ejus, » et distincte additur, « uxor ejus et uxores filiorum ejus. » Mixtim autem viri cum mulieribus egressi sunt, sic enim scriptum est: « Egressus est Noe et uxor ejus.
