Tempus enim amplectendi et tempus abstinendi (Eccli. III). »

VERS. 10.-- « Cumque transissent, » etc. (ISID.) Quia in spe futurae quietis, quam significat septimus dies, baptizamur, omnis caro extra arcam diluvio consumpta est; quia extra Ecclesiam aqua baptismi, quamvis eadem sit, non valet ad salutem, sed ad perniciem.

107DVERS. 11-14.-- « Anno sexcentesimo, » etc. Allegorice. Notandum quod sexcentesimo anno inundaverunt aquae diluvii, quia sexta aetate mundi primo commendatum est sacramentum baptismi, et hoc septima decima die secundi mensis, quia denarius decalogum significat. Septenarius propter Sabbatum septimam requiem significat, et quia in decalogo renuntiatio vitiorum videtur, quicunque in baptismo initiatur, propter aeternam requiem, omnibus vitiis se abrenuntiare profitetur.

« Cataractae. » Fenestrae, nubes scilicet, quae sunt apertae, ut inde insolitae et majores pluviae funderentur. Cataractae vero proprie sunt ostia Nili, sed abusive pro omnibus fenestris ponuntur.

108A(RAB.) De arca solet quaeri utrum tanta capacitate, quanta describitur, animalia omnia quae ingressa dicuntur, et escas eorum ferre potuerit? Quod cubito geometrico solvit Origenes, asserens non frustra dictum esse Mosen in omni sapientia Aegyptiorum fuisse eruditum, qui geometriam dilexerunt, et cubitum geometricum quantum sex nostros continere.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « Bina et bina, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra.) Praecepit superius septena et septena de mundis, duo et duo tolli de immundis. Quomodo ergo nunc dicit duo et duo intrasse, sive de mundis, sive de immundis? Sed hoc refertur non ad numerum mundorum et immundorum, sed ad masculum et feminam. In omnibus enim duo sunt, masculus et femina.

108BNotandum vero quod dicitur: « In qua erat spiritus vitae, » non solum de hominibus, sed et de pecoribus dictum: quia quidam de Spiritu sancto volunt intelligi quod scriptum est, « insufflavit in faciem ejus spiritum vitae; » sed quidam codices habent melius, « flatum vitae. »

VERS. 17-19.-- « Quadraginta diebus, » etc. (ALC.) Quadragenario numero pluvia inundavit, qui tribulationem poenitentiae ostendit. Post quadraginta dies Noe fenestram aperuit, significans jejunantibus coelum aperiri. Unde Moyses, Elias, et ipse Salvator, quadragenario numero jejunia consecravit, tanquam tribus temporibus necessaria, ante legem, sub lege, sub gratia.

VERS. 20-22-- « Quindecim cubitis altior, » etc. 108C(AUG. in Gen.) Si terra Olympi montis (ut tradit historia) potuit invadere spatium tranquilli aeris, etc., usque ad ad tenendam quietem et fidem resurrectionis.

« Quindecim. » Sunt qui putant nec terrae qualitatem, nec altitudinem montium tantam ante diluvium fuisse qualis et quanta est hodie.

VERS. 23, 24.-- « Deleta sunt de terra. » (ALC.) Ideo magis terrena animalia quam aquatilia perierunt, quia de maledicta terra vivunt.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1-3.-- « And the Lord said, » etc. « Of all clean living creatures thou shalt take seven and seven, » etc. (RAB.) Not a doubled seven, that is fourteen, but only seven ought we to 107Aunderstand. Likewise two and two, not four, but only two, the male and the female. And it is to be noted that the clean animals are brought in by an odd number, that is by the number seven, but the unclean by an even one; because an odd number portends the appearance of virtue, but an even one weakness. The clean are the more numerous, that Noah, on going out of the ark, might have what he could offer in sacrifice and eat.

(ISID. in Gen.) « Of all living creatures, » etc. Not that the good are more numerous than the bad, etc., as far as which day is, reckoned from the passion, the third among the days that roll round through all time, and the first, and the eighth.

(AUG. in Gen.) It is asked: could so great an ark be built in a hundred years by four men, that is, by Noah and his three sons? But if it could not, it was no great matter to employ other workmen, although, 107Bonce the wage of their work had been got, they cared not whether Noah was acting wisely or to no purpose, and did not enter into it, because they did not believe what he believed.

VERS. 4.-- « I will rain, » etc. (ISID. Quaest. in Gen., c. 7.) Because every guilt against the decalogue is committed throughout the whole world, which is divided into four parts: and four times ten make forty; whether the guilt be contracted out of prosperity, which pertains to the days; or out of adversity, which pertains to the nights, it is washed away in baptism.

VERS. 5-6.-- « Noah therefore did all things, » etc. « And he was six hundred years old, » etc. (ISID., Ibid). Noah was five hundred years old when the Lord commanded him to make the ark, etc., as far as which is the square of three.

107CVERS. 7-9.-- « Noah went in, and his sons, his wife, » etc. (RAB.) It is to be noted that Noah goes into the ark apart with his sons, and their wives apart. Therefore it is said distinctly: « Noah went in, and his sons, » and it is distinctly added, « his wife and the wives of his sons. » But they went out mingled, the men with the women, for so it is written: « Noah went out, and his wife.[Vulg. Gn 8:18 reads Egressus est ergo Noe, et filii ejus : uxor illius, et uxores filiorum ejus cum eo, "Noah therefore went out, and his sons: his wife, and the wives of his sons with him."] For there is a time of embracing and a time of abstaining[Vulg. Ecl 3:5 reads tempus amplexandi, et tempus longe fieri ab amplexibus, "a time to embrace, and a time to be far from embraces."] (Eccli. III). »

VERS. 10.-- « And when they had passed, » etc. (ISID.) Because we are baptized in the hope of the rest to come, which the seventh day signifies, all flesh outside the ark was consumed by the flood; because outside the Church the water of baptism, though it be the same water, avails not unto salvation, but unto ruin.

107DVERS. 11-14.-- « In the six hundredth year, » etc. Allegorically. It is to be noted that in the six hundredth year the waters of the flood overflowed, because in the sixth age of the world the sacrament of baptism was first entrusted to us, and this on the seventeenth day of the second month, because the number ten signifies the decalogue. The number seven, on account of the Sabbath, signifies the seventh rest, and because in the decalogue the renunciation of vices is seen, whosoever is initiated in baptism professes, for the sake of eternal rest, to renounce all vices.

« The cataracts. » Windows, that is to say clouds, which were opened, that from them unwonted and greater rains might be poured out. But cataracts are properly the mouths of the Nile, though by misuse they are set for all windows.

108A(RAB.) Concerning the ark it is commonly asked: with so great a capacity as is described, could it carry all the animals that are said to have entered, and their food? Origen resolves this by the geometrical cubit, asserting that it was not said in vain that Moses was learned in all the wisdom of the Egyptians, who loved geometry, and that the geometrical cubit contains as much as six of ours.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « Two and two, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra.) He commanded above that seven and seven of the clean, and two and two of the unclean, be taken. How then does he now say that two and two entered, whether of the clean or of the unclean? But this is referred not to the number of the clean and the unclean, but to the male and the female. For in all there are two, male and female.

108BBut it is to be noted that what is said, « in which was the spirit of life, » is said not of men only, but of cattle also: because some would have that which is written, « he breathed into his face the spirit of life, »[Vulg. Gn 2:7 reads inspiravit in faciem ejus spiraculum vitae, "breathed into his face the breath of life."] understood of the Holy Spirit; but certain codices have better, « the breath of life. »

VERS. 17-19.-- « Forty days, » etc. (ALC.) The rain overflowed by the number forty, which shows the tribulation of penance. After forty days Noah opened the window, signifying that heaven is opened to them that fast. Whence Moses, Elias, and the Saviour himself consecrated fasts by the number forty, as being necessary in three times: before the law, under the law, under grace.

VERS. 20-22-- « Higher by fifteen cubits, » etc. 108C(AUG. in Gen.) If the earth of Mount Olympus (as history relates) could reach into the space of the tranquil air, etc., as far as to the holding fast of rest and of faith in the resurrection.

« Fifteen. » There are those who think that neither the quality of the earth nor the height of the mountains was, before the flood, such and so great as it is today.

VERS. 23, 24.-- « They were destroyed from the earth. » (ALC.) Therefore the animals of the land perished rather than those of the water, because they live off the accursed earth.

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 1-3.-- « Recordatus est autem Deus « Noe, » etc. « Adduxit spiritum super terram, » etc. (ALCUIN., quaest. in Gen.) De illo spiritu intelligi potest de quo dictum est, « Spiritus Dei ferebatur super 108Daquas: » tunc ferebatur, ut, congregatis in suum [Al., unum] locum, terra appareat: nunc adducitur, ut, ablatis aquis diluvii, faciem terrae revelaret. Potest per spiritum ventus intelligi, juxta illud psalmi CVI: « Et stetit spiritus procellae, » cujus scilicet flatibus aqua recederet.

VERS. 4.-- « Requievitque arca mense septimo, » etc. (ISID.) Septenariam requiem significat, et quia perfecti requiescunt ibi, supradictae quoque quadraturae numerus iteratur. Nam vigesima septima die secundi mensis commendatur sacramentum, et rursus vigesima septima die septimi mensis confirmatur, cum arca requievit: quod enim promittitur in spe, exhibetur in re.

109A(AUG.) Quaeritur utrum post centum quinquaginta dies haec facta sunt, an per recapitulationem omnia rememorata quae post quadraginta dies pluviae coeperunt fieri, ut hoc solum « centum quinquaginta dies » pertineat, quod usque ad ipsos montes aqua est exaltata, aut de fontibus abyssi jam cessante pluvia, aut quia mansit in altitudine nullo spiritu siccante. Caetera vero quae dicta sunt non post centum quinquaginta dies omnia facta sunt, sed commemorata: quae ex fine quadraginta dierum fieri coeperunt.

VERS. 5, 6.-- « Decimo enim mense. » Notandum quod decimo mense apparuerunt cacumina montium, quae quindecim cubitis excesserant aquae diluvii, quia sacramenta aeternae quietis et gloriam 109Bresurrectionis (quam nescivit sapientia superborum) intellexit eminentia sanctorum per impletionem praeceptorum; unde Psalmi CXVIII: « A mandatis tuis intellexi. » Et alibi, Luc X: « Abscondisti haec a sapientibus et prudentibus, et revelasti ea parvulis. »

VERS. 7.-- « Qui egrediebatur, » etc. (ISID.) Quod post quadraginta dies corvus non rediit, aut aquis interceptus, aut cadavere natante illectus, figurat immunditia cupiditatis teterrimos: et ideo his quae foris sunt in mundo nimis intentos, aut rebaptizari, aut ab his quos praeter arcam, id est Ecclesiam, baptismus occidit, seduci et teneri.

(AUG.) Quaeritur utrum corvus mortuus sit, an aliquo modo vivere potuerit? Si enim fuit terra ubi requiesceret, similiter columba requiem invenisset. 109CUnde conjicitur quod cadaveri potuit corvus insidere, quod naturaliter refugit columba.

VERS. 8.-- « Emisit quoque. » (ISID.) Emissa columba, nec inventa requie reversa, significat per novum Testamentum requiem sanctis in hoc mundo non esse promissam: post quadraginta enim dies emissa est, quo numero vita quae in mundo agitur designatur.

(STRAB.) Arca continet corvum et columbam, et Ecclesia bonos et malos. Corvus emissus non revertitur, quia reprobi illecebris saeculi inhaerentes pereunt. Columba rediit, portans ramum olivae virentibus foliis in ore suo, quia sancti, simplicitate gaudentes, munus pacis in ore portant, dicentes: (Psal. XIX) « Cum his qui oderunt pacem eram pacificus: 109Dcum loquebar illis, » etc.

VERS. 9.-- « Reversa est. » (AUG.) Quaestio est quomodo columba non invenerit ubi resideret, cum jam nudata essent cacumina montium, secundum narrationis ordinem? Quod recapitulatione potest solvi, ut posterius narrata intelligantur quae facta sunt prius, aut potius nondum siccata fuerunt.

VERS. 10.-- « Exspectatis, » etc. Post septem dies alios, dimissa columba propter septenariam operationem spiritualem, olivae fructuosum surculum retulit, signans multos extra Ecclesiam baptizatos, si pinguedo charitatis non defuerit, posteriore tempore in ore columbae, tanquam in osculo pacis, ad unitatis societatem reduci.

110AVERS. 11.-- « Portans ramum. » (ALC.) Columba ramum olivae, expulso teterrimo alite, ad Noe post diluvium portat, quia columba Spiritus sancti, ad Christum post baptismum veniens, ramo refectionis et luminis pacem annuntiat.

VERS. 12.-- « Exspectavitque nihilominus septem alios dies, » etc. (ISID. in Gen.) Post alios septem dies dimissa columba, nec reversa, significat finem saeculi, quando requies sanctorum erit, non in sacramento spei, quo nunc Ecclesia consociatur, quandiu bibitur quod de latere Christi manavit, sed in perfectione salutis, cum tradetur regnum Deo et Patri, ut in illa conspicua contemplatione veritatis nullis egeamus mysteriis.

VERS. 13.-- « Sexcentesimo primo anno, » etc. 110B(Ibid.) Vitae Noe, id est, peractis sexcentis annis aperitur arcae tectum, etc., usque ad uno addito, propter unitatis vinculum.

VERS. 14.-- « Mense secundo. » (STRAB.) Historice. Id est, Maio, Noe annum fecit in arca, et ipsa die qua ingressus fuerat, egressus est. Ingressus es enim vicesima septima die mensis secundi; num dicit quia egressus est septima et vicesima die ejusdem mensis. Sed sciendum est quia si praesenti diei addantur undecim, qualis luna hodie est, talis erit post annum ipsa die. Quando ergo Noe ingressus est, decima septima luna, vel septima decima dies mensis secundi fuit. Ideo post annum undecim additis, fuit vicesima septima dies et decima septima luna.

« Mense secundo, » etc. Mystice. Sicut supra dictum 110Cest, decima septima die sexcentesimi anni aquis diluvii terra madefacta est, sed vigesima septima die secundi mensis arefacta est, quando praeceptum est ut de arca egrederentur, et pactum acciperent, ne ultra aquis diluvii perirent. In quo intelligi potest quia per decem et septem (sicut supra) status inchoantium significari potest, quando vitiis abrenuntiatur; per viginti septem promotio perfectorum in consummatione virtutum. Si enim decem et septem decem addas, fiunt viginti septem. Si enim decem praeceptis quibus peccatis renuntiatur, evangelicam perfectionem adjicias qua praecipitur non solum non irasci, sed etiam inimicos diligere, et similia, ad illam aetatem perfectionis pervenisti, in qua nec sacramentis praedictis egeas; et de arca 110Dexiens, id est, de spe ad speciem perveniens, requiem in sacramentis promissam invenies.

VERS. 15-19.-- « Egredere de arca, » etc. (ISID.) Juncti exeunt, qui disjuncti intraverunt, quia, « nunc caro concupiscit adversus spiritum, et spiritus adversus carnem. » Sed in resurrectione justorum perfecta pace spiritui corpus adhaerebit, nulla mutabilitatis indigentia vel concupiscentia resistente.

VERS. 20.-- « Et tollens, » etc. (AUG.) Ingressa sunt in arcam munda et immunda animalia, sed post egressum non offeruntur Deo nisi munda. Quod vero ait: « Non percutiam omnem animam viventem sicut feci, » et addidit quae largitate bonitas donat indignis, forte Novi Testamenti indulgentia 111Afigurata est, ut praeterita ultio ad legis pertineat severitatem, hoc ad gratiae bonitatem.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « Sensus enim, » etc. (GREG., Moral. libr. XXXVIII.) Nemo sibi attribuat, si cogitationes vicerit: quia corruptionis malum, quod quisque ab ortu carnalium desideriorum sumpsit, exercet in provectu aetatis: et nisi hoc citius divinae fortitudinis reprimat manus, omne bonum naturae culpa vorat in profundum; « Neque qui plantat est aliquid, neque qui rigat, sed qui incrementum dat Deus (I Cor. III). »

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 1-3.-- « And God remembered « Noah, » etc. « He brought a spirit over the earth, » etc. (ALCUIN., quaest. in Gen.) It can be understood of that spirit of which it was said, « The Spirit of God was borne over 108Dthe waters: » then it was borne, that, the waters being gathered into their own [Al., unum] place, the earth might appear: now it is brought, that, the waters of the flood being taken away, it might lay bare the face of the earth. By the spirit a wind can be understood, according to that of Psalm CVI: « And the spirit of the storm stood, » by whose blasts, namely, the water would draw back.

VERS. 4.-- « And the ark rested in the seventh month, » etc. (ISID.) It signifies the sevenfold rest, and, because the perfect rest there, the number of the aforesaid squaring is repeated also. For on the twenty-seventh day of the second month the sacrament is committed, and again on the twenty-seventh day of the seventh month it is confirmed, when the ark rested: for what is promised in hope is exhibited in fact.

109A(AUG.) It is asked whether these things were done after the hundred and fifty days, or whether by recapitulation all those things are recalled which began to be done after the forty days of rain, so that this alone pertains to the « hundred and fifty days », that the water was raised as far as the mountains themselves, either from the fountains of the deep, the rain having now ceased, or because it remained at that height with no wind drying it. But the rest of the things that have been said were not all done after the hundred and fifty days, but recalled: things which began to be done from the end of the forty days.

VERS. 5, 6.-- « For in the tenth month. » It is to be noted that in the tenth month the tops of the mountains appeared, which the waters of the flood had overtopped by fifteen cubits, because the eminence of the saints understood, through the fulfilling of the precepts, the sacraments of eternal rest and the glory 109Bof the resurrection (which the wisdom of the proud knew not); whence Psalm CXVIII: « By thy commandments I have understood. » And elsewhere, Luke X: « Thou hast hid these things from the wise and prudent, and hast revealed them to little ones. »

VERS. 7.-- « Who went forth, » etc. (ISID.) That the raven did not return after forty days, either cut off by the waters or enticed by a floating carcase, figures those made most foul by the uncleanness of covetousness: and therefore that those too intent upon the things which are outside in the world are either rebaptized, or are seduced and held fast by those whom baptism outside the ark, that is, outside the Church, has slain.

(AUG.) It is asked: did the raven die, or could it in some way live? For if there was earth where it might rest, the dove would likewise have found rest. 109CWhence it is conjectured that the raven could settle on a carcase, which the dove by nature shuns.

VERS. 8.-- « He sent forth also. » (ISID.) The dove sent forth, and returned without having found rest, signifies that through the New Testament rest is not promised to the saints in this world: for it was sent forth after forty days, by which number the life that is passed in the world is designated.

(STRAB.) The ark contains a raven and a dove, and the Church good men and bad. The raven sent out does not return, because the reprobate, clinging to the enticements of the world, perish. The dove returned, bearing a branch of olive with green leaves in her mouth, because the saints, rejoicing in simplicity, bear the gift of peace in their mouth, saying: (Psal. XIX) « With them that hated peace I was peaceable: 109Dwhen I spoke to them, » etc.

VERS. 9.-- « She returned. » (AUG.) It is a question: how did the dove not find where to settle, when the tops of the mountains had already been laid bare, according to the order of the narrative? This can be resolved by recapitulation, so that things narrated later are understood to have been done earlier, or rather, they had not yet been dried.

VERS. 10.-- « Having waited, » etc. After other seven days, the dove being let go on account of the sevenfold spiritual working, brought back a fruitful shoot of olive, signifying that many baptized outside the Church, if the richness of charity be not wanting, are at a later time brought back, in the mouth of the dove, as in the kiss of peace, to the fellowship of unity.

110AVERS. 11.-- « Bearing a branch. » (ALC.) The dove bears a branch of olive to Noah after the flood, the most foul bird having been driven out, because the dove of the Holy Spirit, coming to Christ after his baptism, announces peace with the branch of refreshment and of light.

VERS. 12.-- « And he waited nevertheless other seven days, » etc. (ISID. in Gen.) The dove let go after another seven days, and not returning, signifies the end of the world, when the rest of the saints shall be, not in the sacrament of hope, in which the Church is now joined together, so long as that is drunk which flowed from the side of Christ, but in the perfection of salvation, when the kingdom shall be delivered up to God and the Father, so that in that clear contemplation of truth we may have need of no mysteries.

VERS. 13.-- « In the six hundred and first year, » etc. 110B(Ibid.) Of Noah's life, that is, six hundred years being completed, the roof of the ark is opened, etc., as far as one being added, on account of the bond of unity.

VERS. 14.-- « In the second month. » (STRAB.) Historically. That is, in May, Noah completed a year in the ark, and on the very day on which he had entered, he went out. For he entered on the twenty-seventh day of the second month; does it say that he went out on the seventh and twentieth day of the same month. But it must be known that, if eleven be added to the present day, such as the moon is today, such will it be on that same day a year hence. When therefore Noah entered, it was the seventeenth moon, or the seventeenth day of the second month. Therefore after a year, eleven being added, it was the twenty-seventh day and the seventeenth moon.

« In the second month, » etc. Mystically. As was said above, 110Con the seventeenth day of the six hundredth year the earth was soaked with the waters of the flood, but on the twenty-seventh day of the second month it was dried, when it was commanded that they should go out of the ark and receive a compact, that they should no more perish by the waters of a flood. In which it can be understood that by ten and seven (as above) the state of those beginning can be signified, when vices are renounced; by twenty-seven, the advancement of the perfect in the consummation of the virtues. For if thou add ten to ten and seven, they become twenty-seven. For if to the ten precepts by which sins are renounced thou add the evangelical perfection, by which it is commanded not only not to be angry, but even to love one's enemies, and the like, thou hast come to that age of perfection in which thou needest not even the aforesaid sacraments; and going out 110Dof the ark, that is, passing over from hope to sight, thou shalt find the rest promised in the sacraments.

VERS. 15-19.-- « Go out of the ark, » etc. (ISID.) They go out joined who entered separated, because « now the flesh lusteth against the spirit, and the spirit against the flesh. »[Vulg. Gal 5:17 reads Caro enim concupiscit adversus spiritum, spiritus autem adversus carnem, with enim and autem for Migne's nunc and et.] But in the resurrection of the just the body will cleave to the spirit in perfect peace, with no want of mutability or concupiscence resisting.

VERS. 20.-- « And taking, » etc. (AUG.) Clean and unclean animals entered into the ark, but after the going out none are offered to God but the clean. But in that he says: « I will not strike every living soul as I have done, »[Vulg. Gn 8:21 reads non igitur ultra percutiam, "I will therefore no more strike"; Migne's lemma lacks ultra.] and added those things which his goodness grants of its bounty to the unworthy, perhaps the indulgence of the New Testament 111Ais figured, so that the vengeance that is past belongs to the severity of the law, and this to the goodness of grace.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « For the sense, » etc. (GREG., Moral. libr. XXXVIII.) Let no one ascribe it to himself if he has overcome his thoughts: because the evil of corruption, which each one has taken up from the rising of carnal desires, he plies as his age advances; and unless the hand of the divine strength repress it the sooner, guilt devours every good of nature into the depth: « Neither he that planteth is anything, nor he that watereth, but God that giveth the increase (I Cor. III). »

CAPUT IX.

VERS. 1.-- « Benedixitque Deus Noe et filiis; » etc. « Crescite et multiplicamini. » Homo in terrorem 111Bcaeteris animalibus praeponitur, in solatium transactae vindictae, et ne pauci homines a pluribus bestiis opprimantur, et sciant se irrationabilibus, sed non rationabilibus dominari.

VERS. 2, 3.-- « Et terror vester, » etc. (GREG. libr. II Moral. c. 11.) Potentibus viris magna est virtus humilitatis, etc., usque ad quo non suam gloriam, sed subditorum quaerunt utilitatem.

VERS. 4-8.-- « Carnem cum sanguine. » (ISID.) Dantur eis cuncta animalia in escam, quia in vocatione gentium ad fidem nulla distantia est, quod in disco Petri figuratur. Sanguinem manducare prohibentur, ne vita pristina quasi suffocata in conscientia teneatur, sed effundatur per confessionem. Esus carnium concessus videtur post diluvium, propter 111Cinfecunditatem terrae et hominis fragilitatem.

VERS. 9-12.-- « Ecce ego statuam pactum, » etc. Testamentum posuit Deus inter se et homines et omnem animam vivam, ne perdat eam diluvio, arcum scilicet, qui apparet in nubibus, qui nunquam nisi de sole resplendet. Illi enim non pereunt diluvio separati ab Ecclesia, qui in prophetis et in omnibus sanctis Scripturis, tanquam in Dei nubibus, agnoscunt gloriam Christi, non suam quaerunt. Sed ne adoratores hujus solis intumescant, sciant ita Christum significari per solem sicut per leonem, per agnum, per lapidem, similitudinis causa, non proprietatis substantia.

VERS. 13-17.-- « Arcum meum ponam, » etc. (ALC. in Gen.) Arcum dedit Deus in signum securitatis, 111Dne homines formidolosi timerent altero diluvio deleri, pluviarum inundationes saepe cernentes. Hic in coelo positus est, ut ab omnibus videri possit, et pro quacunque tribulatione ad eum attollamus oculos cordis, qui habitat in coelis. Quia vero arcus non est nisi ex radiis solis et humida nube, videtur quod ante diluvium non fuissent pluviae. Poterat autem ex rore et fontium inundatione terra fecundari.

(STRAB.) Arcus duos habet colores, caeruleum et igneum, qui duo judicia exprimunt: unum aquae quod praeteriit; aliud ignis, quod venturum creditur in fine saeculi: unde caeruleus color extrinsecus, igneus vero intrinsecus.

112AVERS. 18-20.-- « Erant igitur filii Noe, » etc. (HIER. in Q. Hebr.) Saepe LXX non valentes heth litteram, quia duplicem aspirationem sonat, in Graecum vertere, χ chi Graecam addiderunt, docentes in hujusmodi vocabulis esse aspirandum. Unde Cham transtulerunt pro eo quod est Ham, a quo Aegyptus usque hodie lingua Aegyptiorum dicitur Ham.

VERS. 21.-- « Bibensque vinum, » etc. Christus quoque inebriatus, dum passus; nudatus, dum crucifixus. « In tabernaculo suo, » id est, inter domesticos sanguinis sui, tunc nudata est mortalitas carnis. « Judaeis scandalum, gentibus stultitiam, ipsis autem vocatis Judaeis et gentibus, » quasi Sem et Japheth, « Dei virtus, et Dei sapientia: quia quod stultum est Dei, sapientius est hominibus. »

112B(ALCUIN.) Post diluvium Noe inebriatus est, quia forte nesciebat vino posse inebriari. Nec enim ante diluvium leguntur homines vino usi. Ideo de Noe specialiter dicitur quod plantaverit vineam. Nudatio femoris sequitur ebrietatem sicut libido satietatem.

VERS. 22.-- « Quod cum vidisset, » etc. (PROCOP. in Gen.) Ex his circumstantiis Chami peccatum amplificatur. Despectui habet parentem, effutit quam turpiter jacuerit pater, nec uni ex fratribus nuntiat, sed utrisque: si plures praesto fuissent, facinus detexisset omnibus: ridiculum ei visum est facinus cui minime debebat; dignum erat id factum pudore et cautela, ne ad caeteros ejus infamia dimanaret: nec his qui domestici erant id annuntiat, sed externis.

112C(ISID.) Judaei, Christi mortem videntes, subsannaverunt. Sem vero et Japheth, duo populi ex praeputio et circumcisione credentes, aversi operuerunt, etc., usque ad « tanquam in pacem anguli concurrentes. »

VERS. 23.-- « At vero Sem, » etc. (GREG. in Moral.) Aversari dicimur quod reprobamus. Quid est ergo quod filii verenda patris superjectis dorso palliis aversi veniendo operuerunt, nisi quia bonis subditis sic praepositorum suorum mala displicent, ut ab aliis nota occultent: operimentum aversi deferunt, quia dijudicantes factum, et venerantes magistrum, nolunt videre quod tegunt.

VERS. 24, 25.-- « Maledictus Chanaan puer, » etc. (Ibid.) Non praetermittit Deus inulta peccata, sed ita 112Djudicium in nostra correctione incipit, ut in reproborum damnatione quiescat. Incipit enim judicium a domo Dei. Quis ergo finis eorum qui non crediderunt, qui temporalia flagella non sentiunt? Quia in aeternum peribunt; quorum nequitia Cham peccante figurata est. Cui dicitur: « Maledictus Chanaan puer, » etc. Patre peccante, filius sententiam accepit, quia reproborum nequitia hic diu proficit, sed perit in posterum.

VERS. 26.-- « Benedictus Dominus Deus Sem. » Cum sit Deus omnium gentium, quasi proprio vocabulo etiam in ipsis gentibus dicitur Deus Israel, quod est ex benedictione Japheth, quia in populo gentium orbem terrae occupavit Ecclesia, quod praenuntiabatur 113Acum diceretur: « Dilatet Deus Japheth. »

« Chanaan. » (STRAB.) A quo Chananaei, qui hac maledictione prophetantur, expellendi de loco habitationis.

VERS. 27-29.-- « Dilatet Deus Japheth, » etc. (HIERON.) De Sem Hebraei, de Japheth gentiles. Quia igitur lata est multitudo credentium, a latitudine, quae Japheth dicitur, latitudo nominatur. Quod autem ait: « Et habitet in tabernaculis Sem, » de nobis prophetatur qui in eruditione et scientia Scripturarum, ejecto Israel, versamur.

« Dilatet Deus Japheth, » etc. (ISID.) Sem major natu, ipse est Judaicus populus, ex quo patriarchae, et prophetae, et apostoli, etc., usque ad id est opus eorum.

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 1.-- « And God blessed Noah and his sons; » etc. « Increase and multiply. » Man is set over 111Bthe rest of the animals for their terror, as a solace for the vengeance that is past, and lest a few men be overwhelmed by the more numerous beasts, and that they may know that they have dominion over the irrational, but not over the rational.

VERS. 2, 3.-- « And your terror, » etc. (GREG. libr. II Moral. c. 11.) In powerful men the virtue of humility is great, etc., as far as by which they seek not their own glory, but the profit of their subjects.

VERS. 4-8.-- « Flesh with blood. » (ISID.) All animals are given them for food, because in the calling of the Gentiles to the faith there is no difference, which is figured in the dish of Peter. They are forbidden to eat blood, lest the former life be held as it were stifled within the conscience, but let it be poured out through confession. The eating of flesh seems to have been granted after the flood, on account of 111Cthe barrenness of the earth and the frailty of man.

VERS. 9-12.-- « Behold, I will establish my compact, » etc. God set a testament between himself and men and every living soul, that he might not destroy it by a flood, the bow, namely, which appears in the clouds, which never shines save from the sun. For those perish not in the flood, separated from the Church, who in the prophets and in all the holy Scriptures, as in the clouds of God, acknowledge the glory of Christ and seek not their own. But lest the worshippers of this sun swell up, let them know that Christ is signified by the sun just as by the lion, by the lamb, by the stone, for the sake of likeness, not by any substance of property.

VERS. 13-17.-- « I will set my bow, » etc. (ALC. in Gen.) God gave the bow for a sign of security, 111Dlest fearful men should dread to be destroyed by another flood, often beholding the overflowing of the rains. This is set in the heaven, that it may be seen by all, and that in whatever tribulation we may lift up to him the eyes of our heart who dwells in the heavens. But because the bow is not save from the rays of the sun and a moist cloud, it seems that before the flood there had been no rains. The earth, however, could be made fruitful from dew and from the overflowing of the springs.

(STRAB.) The bow has two colours, blue and fiery, which express two judgments: one of water, which is past; the other of fire, which is believed to be to come at the end of the world: whence the blue colour is on the outside, but the fiery within.

112AVERS. 18-20.-- « These therefore were the sons of Noah, » etc. (HIER. in Q. Hebr.) Often the Seventy, not being able to turn the letter heth into Greek, because it sounds a double aspiration, added the Greek χ chi, teaching that in words of this kind an aspiration is to be made. Whence they rendered Cham for that which is Ham, from whom Egypt is to this day called Ham in the language of the Egyptians.

VERS. 21.-- « And drinking wine, » etc. Christ also was made drunk, when he suffered; laid bare, when he was crucified. « In his own tabernacle, » that is, among those of his own household by blood, then was the mortality of his flesh laid bare. « To the Jews a scandal, to the Gentiles foolishness, but to them that are called, both Jews and Gentiles, » as it were Sem and Japheth, « the power of God and the wisdom of God: because that which is foolish of God is wiser than men. »[Vulg. 1Cor 1:23–24 reads Judaeis quidem scandalum, gentibus autem stultitiam, ipsis autem vocatis Judaeis, atque Graecis Christum Dei virtutem: Graecis, "Greeks," for Migne's second gentibus, and Christum Dei virtutem in the accusative for his Dei virtus.]

112B(ALCUIN.) After the flood Noah was made drunk, because perhaps he did not know that one could be made drunk with wine. For neither are men read to have used wine before the flood. Therefore it is said of Noah especially that he planted a vineyard. The uncovering of the thigh follows drunkenness, as lust follows satiety.

VERS. 22.-- « And when he had seen, » etc. (PROCOP. in Gen.) From these circumstances the sin of Cham is enlarged. He holds his parent in contempt, he blabs out how basely his father had lain, nor does he tell it to one of his brothers, but to both: if more had been at hand, he would have uncovered the deed to all: the deed seemed a thing of laughter to him to whom it least ought; that deed was worthy of shame and of caution, lest the infamy of it should spread abroad to the rest: nor does he announce it to those who were of the household, but to outsiders.

112C(ISID.) The Jews, seeing the death of Christ, mocked. But Sem and Japheth, two peoples believing out of the uncircumcision and out of the circumcision, turned away and covered him, etc., as far as « as it were running together into the peace of the corner. »

VERS. 23.-- « But Sem indeed, » etc. (GREG. in Moral.) We are said to turn away from that which we reprove. What then is it that the sons, with cloaks thrown over their backs, covered their father's shameful parts, coming turned away, unless that to good subjects the evils of their superiors so displease that they hide them, once known, from others: they bear the covering turned away, because, judging the deed and revering their master, they will not see what they cover.

VERS. 24, 25.-- « Cursed be Chanaan the boy, »[Vulg. Gn 9:25 reads Maledictus Chanaan, servus servorum erit fratribus suis, with no word answering to puer.] etc. (Ibid.) God does not pass over sins unavenged, but so 112Dbegins judgment in our correction that it rests in the damnation of the reprobate. For judgment begins at the house of God. What then is the end of those who have not believed, who feel no temporal scourges? Because they shall perish for ever; whose wickedness was figured in the sin of Cham. To whom it is said: « Cursed be Chanaan the boy, » etc. The father sinning, the son received the sentence, because the wickedness of the reprobate here prospers long, but perishes hereafter.

VERS. 26.-- « Blessed be the Lord God of Sem. » Though he be the God of all nations, yet even among those nations he is called, as by a proper name, the God of Israel, which is from the blessing of Japheth, because in the people of the Gentiles the Church has taken possession of the world, which was foretold 113Awhen it was said: « God enlarge Japheth. »

« Chanaan. » (STRAB.) From whom the Chananites, who by this curse are prophesied to be driven out of the place of their habitation.

VERS. 27-29.-- « God enlarge Japheth, » etc. (HIERON.) From Sem the Hebrews, from Japheth the Gentiles. Because therefore the multitude of believers is broad, from breadth, which is called Japheth, breadth is named. But that which he says: « And let him dwell in the tabernacles of Sem, » is prophesied of us, who are occupied in the learning and knowledge of the Scriptures, Israel being cast out.

« God enlarge Japheth, » etc. (ISID.) Sem, the elder by birth, is himself the Jewish people, from whom the patriarchs and the prophets and the apostles, etc., as far as that is, their work.

CAPUT X.

113BVERS. 1.-- « Hae sunt generationes, » etc. Benedictus duobus filiis Noe, et medio maledicto, generationes eorum texuntur, ex quibus septuaginta duae generationes sunt ortae: quindecim de Japheth, triginta de Cham, viginti septem de Sem. Ad quas misit Dominus septuaginta duos discipulos.

« Sem » (ALC.) Sem, ut aestimant, Asiam; Cham Africam, Japheth Europam sortitus est.

« Japheth. » Minoris filii, de quo nati sunt septem filii, qui tenuerunt septentrionalem regionem a Tauro et Amano, montibus Ciliciae et Syriae, usque ad fluvium Tanaim, in Europa vero usque ad Gadira. Nomina vero locis et gentibus relinquentes, ex quibus plurima mutata, caetera permanent.

VERS. 2-6.-- « Gomer. » Hi sunt Galatae, secundum 113CJosephum, qui Latine Gallograeci dicuntur.

« Thubal. » Iberes qui et Hispani, a quibus Celtiberia, licet quidam Italicos suspicentur.

« Chanaan. » Hic obtinuit terram quam Judaei postea possederunt, ejectis Chananaeis.

VERS. 8.-- « Regma et Saba, » etc. (HIERON.) Paulatim antiqua nomina perdiderunt, et quae nunc pro veteribus habeant, ignoratur.

« Saba. » Alia quam superior, et differunt in scriptura: illa enim per samech, haec per schin scribitur; ubi enim dicimus: Reges Arabum et Saba dona adducent, » in Hebraeo habetur שבא et סבא scheba et seba.

VERS. 8.-- « Nemrod. » Qui ultra naturam coelum penetrare voluit. Significat diabolum, qui ait: 113D« Ascendam super astra coeli. » Nemrod, secundum Josephum, nova regni cupiditate tyrannidem arripuit, et fuit auctor aedificandae turris quae tangeret coelum. « Ipse coepit esse potens, » vel « gigas, » secundum alios. Sed antea gigantes natos Scriptura commemorat. Sed forte post diluvium novitas generis humani reparandi memoratur, « quia ipse coepit esse gigas super terram. »

VERS. 9.-- « Robustus venator. » (ALC.) Id est, hominum oppressor et exstinctor, quos allexit ut turrem contra Deum construerent.

VERS. 10, 12.-- « Babylon. » Est confusio, quia ibi facta est linguarum divisio.

VERS. 11.-- « Assur. » Imperium Assyriorum, 114Aquod primum fuit sub Nino, qui aedificavit Ninivem, quae propter poenitentiam pulchra appellatur.

VERS. 13-20.-- « At vero Mesraim, » etc. (HIERON.) Exceptis Laabin, a quibus Libyes postea nominati sunt, qui prius Phutaei vocabantur; et Chasluim, qui postea Philistiim dicti sunt, quos nos corrupte Palaestinos dicimus, caeterae sex gentes nobis ignotae sunt, quia bello Aethiopico subversae. Possederunt autem terram a Gaza usque ad extremos fines Aegypti.

« Ludim et Anamim. Laabim, Nephetuim, » etc. Haec omnia ignorantur, vel quia deletae sunt gentes bello Aethiopico, vel quia ignoratur quae nomina nunc habeant.

VERS. 21.-- « De Sem. » De hoc novissime loquitur, 114Bquia ejus generationem, et generationis lineam prosequitur.

« Heber. » Qui tamen quintus a filio Noe Sem. Utrum quia Hebraei dicuntur ex illo appellati, vel quia per illum generatio ad Abraham transit? Quid ergo probabilius sit Hebraeos dici tanquam Heberaeos, vel tanquam Abraaeos, merito quaeritur.

VERS. 22-24.-- « Assur. » Qui aedificavit Ninivem.

VERS. 25-30.-- « Natique sunt Heber filii, » etc. Heber, transitor, a quo Hebraei, id est, transitores. Veri Hebraei sunt, qui de praesentibus ad futura, de caducis ad mansura transeunt. Hae gentes a fluvio Cono possederunt omnem regionem quae vocatur Geria.

CHAPTER X.

113BVERS. 1.-- « These are the generations, » etc. Two sons of Noah being blessed and the middle one cursed, their generations are woven out, from whom seventy-two generations arose: fifteen from Japheth, thirty from Cham, twenty-seven from Sem. To which the Lord sent seventy-two disciples.

« Sem » (ALC.) Sem, as they reckon, obtained Asia by lot; Cham Africa, Japheth Europe.

« Japheth. » Of the younger son, of whom were born seven sons, who held the northern region from Taurus and Amanus, mountains of Cilicia and Syria, as far as the river Tanais, but in Europe as far as Gadira. Leaving their names to the places and to the nations, of which very many have been changed, while the rest remain.

VERS. 2-6.-- « Gomer. » These are the Galatians, according to 113CJosephus, who in Latin are called Gallograeci.

« Thubal. » The Iberians, who are also the Spaniards, from whom Celtiberia, though some suspect them to be Italians.

« Chanaan. » He obtained the land which the Jews afterwards possessed, the Chananites being cast out.

VERS. 8.-- « Regma and Saba, » etc. (HIERON.) Little by little they lost their ancient names, and what names they now have in place of the old ones is unknown.

« Saba. » Another than the one above, and they differ in the writing: for that one is written with samech, this with schin; for where we say: The kings of the Arabians and of Saba shall bring gifts, » in the Hebrew there stands [two Hebrew words stand here on Migne's plate; our digitized source has lost both, leaving only the conjunction that stood between them.] scheba and seba.

VERS. 8.-- « Nemrod. » Who wished to pierce heaven beyond nature. He signifies the devil, who says: 113D« I will ascend above the stars of heaven. »[Vulg. Is 14:13 reads In caelum conscendam, super astra Dei exaltabo solium meum, "I will climb up into heaven, above the stars of God I will exalt my throne."] Nemrod, according to Josephus, seized upon tyranny in a new lust of rule, and was the author of the building of the tower which should touch heaven. « He himself began to be mighty, » or « a giant, » according to others. But Scripture records that giants were born before this. But perhaps the newness of the human race to be repaired after the flood is being recorded, « because he himself began to be a giant upon the earth. »

VERS. 9.-- « A stout hunter. » (ALC.) That is, an oppressor and destroyer of men, whom he enticed to build a tower against God.

VERS. 10, 12.-- « Babylon. » It is confusion, because there the division of tongues was made.

VERS. 11.-- « Assur. » The empire of the Assyrians, 114Awhich was first under Ninus, who built Nineveh, which on account of its penitence is called beautiful.

VERS. 13-20.-- « But Mesraim indeed, » etc. (HIERON.) Except the Laabin, from whom the Libyans were afterwards named, who before were called Phutaeans; and the Chasluim, who were afterwards called Philistiim, whom we corruptly call Palestinians, the other six nations are unknown to us, because they were overthrown in the Ethiopian war. But they possessed the land from Gaza as far as the farthest borders of Egypt.

« Ludim and Anamim. Laabim, Nephetuim, » etc. All these are unknown, either because the nations were destroyed in the Ethiopian war, or because it is not known what names they now have.

VERS. 21.-- « Of Sem. » Of him he speaks last, 114Bbecause he pursues his generation, and the line of that generation.

« Heber. » Who nevertheless is the fifth from Sem the son of Noah. Whether because the Hebrews are said to be named from him, or because through him the generation passes over to Abraham? What therefore is the more probable, that the Hebrews are called as it were Heberaeans, or as it were Abrahaeans, is rightly asked.

VERS. 22-24.-- « Assur. » Who built Nineveh.

VERS. 25-30.-- « And there were born sons to Heber, » etc. Heber, a passer-over, from whom the Hebrews, that is, passers-over. They are true Hebrews who pass over from things present to things to come, from things that fall away to things that abide. These nations, from the river Conus, possessed all the region which is called Geria.

CAPUT XI.

114CVERS. 1.-- « Erat autem terra labii unius, » etc. (AUG., in Gen.) Dictum est superius quod filii Noe, vel filiorum ejus, distributi sunt per terras secundum linguas suas. Sed hoc per recapitulationem postea commemorat quod prius erat. Sed obscuritatem facit, quod eo genere locutionis ita contexit, quasi de his narratio quae postea facta sunt consequatur.

VERS. 2.-- « Cumque proficiscerentur. » (CHRYSOST., in Gen.) Vide quomodo humanum genus non potest subsistere intra suos limites, sed amplius concupiscens, majora semper appetit. Atque hoc est quod humanum genus perdit, quia non vult naturae suae mensuram agnoscere, sed semper majora desiderat. Quoniam « viderunt, inquit, campum in terra 114DSennaar, » migrantes, et relicto priori loco illic habitaverunt.

VERS. 3.-- « Venite. » Timebant iterum diluvium; ideo auctore Nemrod voluerunt sibi constituere turrem, in qua diluvium non timerent. Stulta et impia audacia, unde secuta vindicta est et linguarum divisio.

(ISID. in Gen.) Turris, mundi superbia, vel haereticorum dogmata, etc., usque ad unitas confessionis et fidei.

VERS. 7.-- « Venite igitur, » etc. Ad angelos dixisse intelligitur; vel secundum illud: « Faciamus hominem ad imaginem et similitudinem nostram. » Nam singulari numero subjunctum est: « et divisit 115Aeos, » etc., sicut ibi: « Fecit Deus hominem ad imaginem et similitudinem suam. »

(GREG. Moral. lib. II, c. 5.) « Venite, » etc. Dicitur his qui Deo adhaerent, « Venite, » quia a divina contemplatione nunquam decrescere, sed semper accrescere, et nunquam corde recedere, stabili motu est semper venire. Ascendunt angeli Creatorem conspiciendo, descendunt creaturam illicite se erigentem examine districtionis premendo. Dicere ergo, « Descendamus, et confundamus linguam eorum, » est in semetipso quod recte agatur ostendere, et per vim internae visionis eorum mentibus exhibenda judicia occultis motibus inspirare.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « Divisit eos. » (ALC.) In hac divisione linguarum nihil novum fecit Deus, sed dicendi 115Bmodos et formas loquelarum diversis gentibus divisit. Unde easdem syllabas et ejusdem potestatis litteras aliter conjunctas in diversis linguis invenimus, et saepe eadem nomina vel verba aliud significantia; unde ubi dicitur psalmo II: « In virga ferrea, » in Graeco habetur, ἐν ραβδῳ σιδηρᾶ, in raudo sidera. Igitur in Latino sidera non ferrea significat, sicut in Graeco, sed astra.

(AUG. lib. XVI de Civit. c. 4.) Merito malus punitur affectus, etiam cui non succedit effectus. Genus vero poenae quale fuit? Quoniam enim dominatio imperantis in lingua est, ibi damnata est superbia, ut non intelligeretur jubens homini, qui noluit intelligere ut obediret jubenti Deo.

VERS. 10-25.-- « Sem centum erat annorum. » 115C(BEDA, Hexaem., lib. III, t. II, col. 131.) Magna quaestio nobis nascitur secundum litteram, quomodo Sem biennio post diluvium centum esse annorum dicatur, cum supra quingentesimo anno Noe natus asseratur, et sexcentesimo anno ejusdem Noe diluvium venisse legatur, etc., usque ad « quia tulit eum Deus. »

VERS. 26-30.-- « Thare genuit Abram et Nachor. » (AUG. quaest. 25 in Gen.) Non simul, sed suis temporibus, ex quo gignere coepit. Nec attendendus est in his ordo nativitatis, sed significatio futurae dignitatis, in qua excellit Abram.

VERS. 31-32.-- « In Ur Chaldaeorum. » (HIERON.) In Hebraeo in Ur Chesdim (אורכשדים), id est, in igne Chaldaeorum. Hic fabulantur Hebraei quod Abram in 115Dignem sit missus, quia ignem noluit adorare, quem Chaldaei colunt, et Dei auxilio liberatus, de idololatriae igne effugerit. Unde ad eum dicitur: « Ego sum qui eduxi te de Ur Chaldaeorum. »

« Aram patris Melchae. » (Ibid.) Idem pater utriusque. Aram filius Thare, frater Abrae et Nachor, duas filias genuit, Melcham et Sarai, cognomento Jescham. Melcham duxit Nachor, et Sarai Abram. Nondum enim inter patruos et neptes nuptiae fuerant prohibitae, quae in principio etiam inter fratres et sorores sunt celebratae.

« Tulit itaque Thare Abram, » etc., usque, facti sunt dies Thare ducentorum quinque annorum, et mortuus est in Haran. » (AUG.) Prius dicitur, « quod 116Acum esset Thare septuaginta annorum genuit Abram, et postea cum omnibus suis mansit in Charan, et mortuus est. » Et dictum est Abrae ut exiret de Charan, et exivit cum esset septuaginta quinque annorum. Sed per recapitulationem ostenditur vivo patre Dominum praecepisse, etc., usque ad a quo Judaei dicti propter regiam tribum.

CHAPTER XI.

114CVERS. 1.-- « And the earth was of one lip, » etc. (AUG., in Gen.) It was said above that the sons of Noah, or of his sons, were distributed through the lands according to their tongues. But this, which was earlier, he afterwards records by recapitulation. Yet he makes obscurity in this, that he so weaves it together by that manner of speech as though the narration of these things followed upon the things which were done afterwards.

VERS. 2.-- « And when they set out. » (CHRYSOST., in Gen.) See how the human race cannot subsist within its own bounds, but, coveting more, always seeks greater things. And this is what destroys the human race, that it will not acknowledge the measure of its own nature, but always desires greater things. Since, « they saw, he says, a plain in the land of 114DSennaar, »[Vulg. Gn 11:2 reads invenerunt campum in terra Senaar, "they found a plain in the land of Senaar."] migrating, and, the former place being left, they dwelt there.

VERS. 3.-- « Come. » They feared a flood again; therefore, Nemrod being the author of it, they wished to set up for themselves a tower in which they should not fear a flood. A foolish and impious boldness, whence followed vengeance and the division of tongues.

(ISID. in Gen.) The tower, the pride of the world, or the dogmas of the heretics, etc., as far as the unity of confession and of faith.

VERS. 7.-- « Come therefore, » etc. He is understood to have said this to the angels; or according to that: « Let us make man to our image and likeness. » For it is subjoined in the singular number: « and he divided 115Athem, » etc., as there: « God made man to his own image and likeness. »[Vulg. Gn 1:27 reads Et creavit Deus hominem ad imaginem suam, "And God created man to his own image," without et similitudinem.]

(GREG. Moral. lib. II, c. 5.) « Come, » etc. It is said to those who cleave to God, « Come, » because never to decrease from the divine contemplation but always to increase, and never to withdraw in heart, is, by a steadfast motion, always to be coming. The angels ascend by beholding the Creator, they descend by pressing down, with the sifting of strictness, the creature that unlawfully lifts itself up. To say therefore, « Let us go down and confound their tongue, » is to show within himself what is rightly done, and, by the force of the inward vision, to breathe with hidden motions into their minds the judgments that are to be displayed.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « He divided them. » (ALC.) In this division of tongues God made nothing new, but divided the 115Bmodes of speaking and the forms of speech among the various nations. Whence we find the same syllables and letters of the same force joined otherwise in different tongues, and often the same nouns or verbs signifying something else; whence, where it is said in Psalm II: « With a rod of iron, » in the Greek there stands ἐν ραβδῳ σιδηρᾶ, in raudo sidera. Therefore in Latin sidera does not signify ferrea, as it does in the Greek, but astra.

(AUG. lib. XVI de Civit. c. 4.) Rightly is the evil affection punished, even that upon which no effect follows. But of what kind was the manner of the punishment? For since the dominion of him who commands lies in the tongue, pride was condemned there, so that he who commanded should not be understood by man, the man who would not understand so as to obey God who commanded.

VERS. 10-25.-- « Sem was a hundred years old. » 115C(BEDA, Hexaem., lib. III, t. II, col. 131.) A great question arises for us according to the letter, how Sem is said to be a hundred years old two years after the flood, when above he is asserted to have been born in the five hundredth year of Noah, and the flood is read to have come in the six hundredth year of the same Noah, etc., as far as « because God took him. »

VERS. 26-30.-- « Thare begot Abram and Nachor. » (AUG. quaest. 25 in Gen.) Not at the same time, but in their own times, from the point at which he began to beget. Nor is the order of birth to be regarded in these, but the signification of the dignity to come, in which Abram excels.

VERS. 31-32.-- « In Ur of the Chaldees. » (HIERON.) In the Hebrew in Ur Chesdim ( )[Migne prints the Hebrew word inside these parentheses; our digitized source has lost it and leaves them empty.], that is, in the fire of the Chaldees. Here the Hebrews tell the tale that Abram was cast into 115Dthe fire because he would not adore the fire which the Chaldees worship, and, delivered by God's help, escaped out of the fire of idolatry. Whence it is said to him: « I am he that brought thee out of Ur of the Chaldees. »[Vulg. Gn 15:7 reads Ego Dominus qui eduxi te de Ur Chaldaeorum, "I am the Lord who brought thee out of Ur of the Chaldees."]

« Aram the father of Melcha. »[Vulg. Gn 11:29 reads Aran patris Melchae; Migne prints the name Aram here and again in the gloss below.] (Ibid.) The same is the father of both. Aram, son of Thare, brother of Abraham and of Nachor, begot two daughters, Melcha and Sarai, surnamed Jescha. Nachor married Melcha, and Abram Sarai. For marriages between uncles and nieces had not yet been forbidden, which in the beginning were celebrated even between brothers and sisters.

« Thare therefore took Abram, » etc., as far as, the days of Thare were two hundred and five years, and he died in Haran. » (AUG.) It is said first, « that 116Awhen Thare was seventy years old he begot Abram, and afterwards remained with all his own in Charan, and died. » And it was said to Abraham that he should go out of Charan, and he went out when he was seventy-five years old. But by recapitulation it is shown that the Lord had given the command while his father was living, etc., as far as from whom the Jews were named, on account of the royal tribe.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- « Egredere de terra, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra.) Consideranda est narratio Stephani de hac re, etc., usque ad sic igitur locatus est in terra illa Abram, quando ibi vixit usque ad nativitatem Jacob.

(STRAB.) Notandum quod jam egressus fuerat de terra sua, sed tunc egressus fuerat corpore, non 116Bmente et voluntate; habebat enim animum forsitan revertendi, quando dictum est ei: « Egredere, » etc.

(ISID.) Reliquit terram Christus et cognationem Judaeorum, et magnificatus est in populis gentium. Nobis quoque exeundum est de terra nostra, id est, de facultatibus et opibus terrenis, et cognatione, id est conversatione vitiorum, quae, nobis a nativitate cohaerentia, quasi affinitates consanguinitatis sunt conjuncta; et « de domo patris, » id est de memoria mundi, ut ei renuntiantes possimus cum populo Dei dilatari, et in terram promissionis coelestis, cum tempus venerit, introduci.

VERS. 2-3.-- Faciamque te in gentem magnam, » etc. (ISID.) Duo promittuntur Abrae, quod scilicet terram Chanaan semen ejus possidebit, cum dicitur: 116C« Et veni in terram quam monstrabo tibi. » Aliud autem longe praestantius, non de carnali, sed de spirituali semine, quo pater futurus est, non tantum gentis Judaicae, sed omnium gentium fidem suam sequentium; quod ita promittitur: « Et benedicentur in te omnes gentes terrae. »

VERS. 4.-- « Septuaginta quinque annorum. » (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.) Si Thare pater Abrae in regione Chaldaea, etc., usque ad et ibi diutius moratus sit.

VERS. 8-12.-- « Bethel. » (RAB.) Interpretatur domus Dei, quae post Bethaven vocata est, id est domus idoli.

« Hai. » Mystice. Hai oculus vel fons interpretatur. Patriarcha ergo tendens ad terram promissionis, tetendit tabernaculum inter Bethel 116Det Hai, quia sancti fonte baptismi regenerati, in cursu hujus vitae circumspecti, ad Dei tabernaculum, id est, coelestem properant civitatem.

VERS. 13-14.-- « Dic ergo, obsecro, » etc. (AUG. lib. XVI de Civ., c. 19.) Quaeritur cur patriarcha voluit mentiri, etc., usque ad et vitam suam et uxoris pudicitiam.

VERS. 15-20.-- « Sublata est mulier. » (RAB.) Excusatur Sarai, quae in domum Pharaonis transfertur invita, non solum in hoc quod non inquinatur corpus nec anima nisi mentis consensu, sed etiam in hoc quod conveniente et permittente viro suo factum est.

(AUG. Quaest. in Gen.) Apud Pharaonem tandiu 117Apotuit esse intacta, quia in Esther legitur quod puellae non statim ad reges introducebantur, sed uno anno custodiebantur, et sex mensibus oleo myrrhino ungebantur, et aliis sex mensibus in diversis odoramentis erant. Potuit ergo per has inducias temporis intacta manere. Et interim bene usi sunt Abram Aegyptii, donec Pharao a Deo flagellatus, et cognoscens quod esset uxor ejus, redderet intactam.

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- « Go out of the land, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra.) The narrative of Stephen on this matter is to be considered, etc., as far as thus therefore Abram was settled in that land, when he lived there until the birth of Jacob.

(STRAB.) It is to be noted that he had already gone out of his own land, but had then gone out in body, not 116Bin mind and will; for he had perhaps a mind of returning, when it was said to him: « Go out, » etc.

(ISID.) Christ left the land and the kindred of the Jews, and was magnified among the peoples of the Gentiles. We too must go out of our land, that is, out of earthly means and riches, and out of our kindred, that is, the converse of the vices, which, cleaving to us from our birth, are joined to us as it were by the affinities of blood; and « out of the house of our father, » that is, out of the memory of the world, that renouncing it we may be enlarged with the people of God, and, when the time shall come, be brought into the land of the heavenly promise.

VERS. 2-3.--And I will make thee into a great nation, » etc. (ISID.) Two things are promised to Abraham, namely, that his seed shall possess the land of Chanaan, when it is said: 116C« And come into the land which I shall show thee. » But the other far more excellent, not of carnal but of spiritual seed, whereof he is to be the father, not only of the Jewish nation, but of all the Gentiles that follow his faith; which is thus promised: « And in thee shall all the nations of the earth be blessed. »[Vulg. Gn 12:3 reads atque in te benedicentur universae cognationes terrae, "and in thee shall all the kindreds of the earth be blessed."]

VERS. 4.-- « Seventy-five years old. » (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.) If Thare, the father of Abraham, in the Chaldean region, etc., as far as and tarried there a longer while.

VERS. 8-12.-- « Bethel. » (RAB.) It is interpreted the house of God, which afterwards was called Bethaven, that is, the house of an idol.

« Hai. » Mystically. Hai is interpreted eye or fountain. The patriarch therefore, making for the land of promise, pitched his tent between Bethel 116Dand Hai, because the saints, regenerated by the font of baptism, circumspect in the course of this life, hasten to the tabernacle of God, that is, to the heavenly city.

VERS. 13-14.-- « Say therefore, I beseech thee, » etc. (AUG. lib. XVI de Civ., c. 19.) It is asked why the patriarch was willing to lie, etc., as far as both his own life and his wife's chastity.

VERS. 15-20.-- « The woman was taken up. » (RAB.) Sarai is excused, who is carried into the house of Pharao unwilling, not only in this, that neither body nor soul is defiled save by the consent of the mind, but also in this, that it was done with her husband agreeing and permitting.

(AUG. Quaest. in Gen.) With Pharao she could 117Abe so long untouched, because in Esther it is read that the maidens were not brought in to the kings at once, but were kept for one year, and for six months were anointed with oil of myrrh, and for another six months were among divers perfumes. She could therefore through these delays of time remain untouched. And meanwhile the Egyptians used Abram well, until Pharao, scourged by God and knowing that she was his wife, gave her back untouched.

CAPUT XIII

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Ascendit ergo Abram, » etc. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.) De Aegypto liberatus bene ascendisse legitur, sed quomodo potuit dives esse valde? Sed in Hebraica veritate habetur כבד gravis vehementer: Aegypti enim pondere gravabatur. Et 117Blicet videantur esse divitiae auri et argenti, si autem Aegyptiae sunt, sancto viro graves sunt; nec, ut in LXX legitur, « Abiit unde venerat in desertum usque Bethel, » sed, secundum Hebraeum, « Abiit in itinere suo per austrum usque Bethel. » De Aegypto enim profectus est, non ut desertum peteret, quod in Aegypto reliquerat, sed ut per austrum, qui aquiloni contrarius est, veniret ad domum Dei, ubi erat tabernaculum ejus inter Bethel et Hai.

VERS. 3-5.-- « In Bethel, » etc. Est autem Bethel civitatula in duodecimo lapide ab Helia ad dexteram euntibus Neapolim: quae prius Luza, id est Amygdalus, vocabatur, in tribu Benjamin: Hai vero in tribu Juda separata sacerdotibus; Bethel, domus Dei; Hai, oculus vel fons: bene ergo tabernaculum 117Cinter Bethel et Hai figens, altare Domino aedificavit, quia sancti a mundi principe et saeculi voluptatibus recedentes, abluti sancto fonte baptismi, cursum praesentis vitae considerate ducentes, et hostiam laudis et boni operis super altare fidei Deo exhibentes, ad domum Dei, id est ad coelestem Hierusalem, feliciter tendunt.

VERS. 6-9.-- « Erat quippe substantia eorum multa, » etc. « Ne quaeso sit, » etc. Nos quoque nihil concordiae et charitati praeponamus, sed per dexteram prosperitatis, vel sinistram adversitatis, in pace et dilectione perseveremus. Rixa inter pastores Lot et pastores Abrae controversiam significat inter pastores catholicos et subversores haereticos. Unde et Lot haeresiarchas significat, qui declinans interpretatur; 117Det circa Jordanem, id est descensum, regionem sibi elegit. Pastores ejus, haereticos et schismaticos, qui pulchra et declivia petunt, nec in montem cum Mose ascendunt ad Dominum, pacem et unitatem continentes; et optionem quo volunt eundi accipiunt; et gratiam fugientes, libero arbitrio se committunt. Pastores vero Abrae catholicos doctores significant, qui haereticos etiam localiter fugiunt, secundum illud ad Titum III: « Haereticum hominem post primam et secundam correctionem devita: » et hoc inter gentes Chananaei et Pheresaei, in terra scilicet promissionis, quia inter catholicos et haereticos, quorum sit regnum coelorum, solet esse contentio.

118A« Fratres enim sumus. » Tribus modis dicuntur fratres in sancta Scriptura: natura, ut Jacob et Esau; cognatione, ut Abram et Lot; gente, sicut omnes Judaei. Unde si attenuatus fuerit frater tuus et vendiderit se tibi, etc.

VERS. 10-12.-- « Sicut paradisus, » etc. Hinc discimus esse paradisum terrenum, cui comparatur terrena irrigatio. Haec enim regio plana erat et campestris, et irrigata Jordane fluente, sicut Aegyptus Nilo.

VERS. 13.-- « Coram Domino. » Cui crimen puniendum committitur: nam et hominibus manifestum crimen, sed non puniendum.

VERS. 14.-- « Leva oculos, » etc. Quasi aspice per quatuor climata orbis; possessio Palestinae 118Bpromittitur ei et semini ejus. Quod in quibusdam codicibus, mare pro occidente ponitur, ideo fit quia Palaestina mare habet ab occidente.

VERS. 15-17.-- « Omnem terram, » etc. Non hoc solum promissum est. Non enim dictum est, Tantum terrae tibi dabo quantum vides; sed, Tibi dabo terram quam vides. Cum enim et ulterior undique dabatur, haec praecipue dabatur quae videbatur. Unde et addidit: « Surge et perambula terram, » etc., ut perambulando scilicet perveniat ad eam quam in uno loco stans videre non poterat. Significatur autem ea terra quam acceperat Israel semen Abrae secundum carnem, non secundum fidem: quod futurum erat sicut arena maris, quia numerari non possit.

118C« Tibi dabo. » Christo promittitur haereditas omnium gentium, qui Ecclesiam suam per totum mundum diffudit, cui Pater dicit: « Postula a me, et dabo tibi gentes haereditatem tuam (Psal. II). » Et psalmo LXXI: « Et dominabitur a mari usque ad mare. »

VERS. 18.-- « In Hebron. » Civitas est in tribu Juda, quae et Cariatharbe dicitur, id est, civitas quatuor; קרית CHARIAT enim Hebraice civitas, ארבע arbe quatuor. Ibi enim situs est Adam maximus et Abram, Isaac et Jacob.

CHAPTER XIII

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Abram therefore went up, » etc. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.) Delivered out of Egypt, he is rightly read to have gone up, but how could he be exceeding rich? But in the Hebrew verity there stands exceeding heavy: for he was weighed down by the burden of Egypt. And 117Balthough riches of gold and of silver may seem to be riches, yet if they are Egyptian they are heavy to a holy man; nor, as it is read in the Seventy, « He went away whence he had come into the desert as far as Bethel, » but, according to the Hebrew, « He went away on his journey by the south as far as Bethel. » For he set out from Egypt, not that he might seek the desert, which he had left behind in Egypt, but that by the south, which is contrary to the north, he might come to the house of God, where his tabernacle was between Bethel and Hai.

VERS. 3-5.-- « In Bethel, » etc. Now Bethel is a little city at the twelfth milestone from Helia, on the right hand as men go to Neapolis: which formerly was called Luza, that is, Almond, in the tribe of Benjamin: but Hai is in the tribe of Juda, set apart for the priests; Bethel, the house of God; Hai, eye or fountain: rightly therefore, pitching his tabernacle 117Cbetween Bethel and Hai, he built an altar to the Lord, because the saints, withdrawing from the prince of the world and from the pleasures of the age, washed in the holy font of baptism, leading the course of the present life considerately, and offering to God upon the altar of faith the sacrifice of praise and of good work, tend happily to the house of God, that is, to the heavenly Jerusalem.

VERS. 6-9.-- « For their substance was much, » etc. « Let there not be, I pray thee, » etc. Let us also set nothing before concord and charity, but through the right hand of prosperity, or the left hand of adversity, let us persevere in peace and in love. The quarrel between the shepherds of Lot and the shepherds of Abraham signifies the controversy between catholic pastors and heretical subverters. Whence Lot also signifies the heresiarchs, who is interpreted declining; 117Dand about the Jordan, that is, a going down, he chose a region for himself. His shepherds, the heretics and schismatics, who seek out fair and sloping places, and go not up into the mountain with Moses to the Lord, holding peace and unity; and they receive the choice of going whither they will; and fleeing grace, they commit themselves to free will. But the shepherds of Abraham signify catholic teachers, who flee from heretics even in place, according to that to Titus III: « A man that is a heretic, after the first and the second correction, avoid: »[Vulg. Tit 3:10 reads post unam et secundam correptionem, "after one and a second rebuke."] and this among the nations of the Chananite and the Pherezite, that is, in the land of promise, because between catholics and heretics there is wont to be contention whose is the kingdom of heaven.

118A« For we are brethren. » In three ways are men called brethren in holy Scripture: by nature, as Jacob and Esau; by kindred, as Abram and Lot; by nation, as all the Jews. Whence, if thy brother be impoverished and sell himself to thee, etc.

VERS. 10-12.-- « As a paradise, » etc. Hence we learn that there is an earthly paradise, to which the earthly watering is compared. For this region was flat and of open country, and watered by the flowing Jordan, as Egypt by the Nile.

VERS. 13.-- « Before the Lord. » To whom the punishing of the crime is committed: for the crime was manifest to men also, but not to be punished.

VERS. 14.-- « Lift up thine eyes, » etc. As if to say, look through the four regions of the world; the possession of Palestine 118Bis promised to him and to his seed. That in certain codices mare is put for occidente, it comes about because Palestine has the sea on the west.

VERS. 15-17.-- « All the land, » etc. Not this only was promised. For it was not said, I will give thee as much of the land as thou seest; but, I will give thee the land which thou seest. For since land further off was being given on every side, this especially was given which was seen. Whence he added also: « Arise and walk through the land, » etc., namely, that by walking through it he might come to that which, standing in one place, he could not see. But that land is signified which Israel, the seed of Abraham according to the flesh, not according to faith, had received: which was to be as the sand of the sea, because it cannot be numbered.

118C« I will give to thee. » To Christ is promised the inheritance of all nations, who spread his Church through the whole world, to whom the Father says: « Ask of me, and I will give thee the nations for thine inheritance (Psal. II). » And in Psalm LXXI: « And he shall rule from sea to sea. »

VERS. 18.-- « In Hebron. » It is a city in the tribe of Juda, which is also called Cariatharbe, that is, the city of four; for CHARIAT in Hebrew is [two Hebrew words stand here on Migne's plate, one before CHARIAT and one before arbe; our digitized source has lost both, which has run his two italic phrases together into one.] a city, arbe four. For there is laid Adam the greatest, and Abram, Isaac and Jacob.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Amraphel. » Interpretatur dixit ut caderet, et est rex Sennaar, quae excussio dentium 118Dvel fetor eorum interpretatur. Hic significat haereticos, qui ad casum tendunt, et verbis dolosis fetidum sensum evomunt.

« Arioch. » Ebrius vel ebrietas interpretatur: stultitiam gentilium significat, qui iniquitate ebrii creaturam pro Creatore colunt, et saeculi illecebris volvuntur. « Chodorlahomor. » Decorus manipulus interpretatur, qui est rex Elam, id est, hujus saeculi: hypocrisim significat, quae licet vana sit et perniciosa, tamen decora in superficie videtur, sed in futuro quid fuerit apparebit.

« Thadal. » Sciens jugum vel explorator, qui est rex gentium; avaritiam significat, quae sagax et versuta multos sibi subjugat.

119A« Amraphel, » etc. Hi quatuor reges figurant quatuor principalia vitia, quatuor virtutibus principalibus contraria; quae quinque sensus per quinque reges significatos subjugare nituntur, per concupiscentiam carnis, per concupiscentiam oculorum, per ambitionem saeculi.

VERS. 2-13.-- « Balae. » (HIERON.) Hebraice devoratio dicitur. Tradunt Hebraei hanc alibi Salissam dici, et vitulam consternantem, quod tertio terrae motu absorpta sit. Et ex eo tempore quo Sodoma et Gomorrha, Adame et Seboim divino igne sunt subversae, illa parvula nuncupetur. Segor enim transfertur in parvam, quae Syra lingua Zoara dicitur. Vallis autem Salinarum, in qua fuerunt putei bituminis, post Dei iram et sulphuris pluviam, in mare 119BMortuum versa est. quod a Graecis stagnum Asphalti, id est bituminis, dicitur.

Mystice. Quatuor reges quinque superant, dum contrariae potestates quinque sensibus corporis abutentes vitiis illaqueant et captivant.

Allegorice. Tales reges superant primum Bara, qui interpretatur malitia vel creatura, et est rex Sodomorum, id est caecitatis, vel pecudis silentis, cum eis qui in vetusta malitia perseverant, more pecudum caecitatem libidinis sequi persuadent. Vincunt et Bersa, id est in angustia constitutum, regem Gomorrhae, id est populi tumentis et seditiosi, cum eos qui curis saeculi angustiantur ac nefas rapinae agere non formidant, obruunt et prosternunt. Vincunt quoque Sennaab regem Adamae, id 119Cest terrenae, cum eos qui terrena tantum sapiunt in luxuria permanere hortantur, ut dicant: « Comedamus et bibamus, cras enim moriemur (I Cor. XV). » Vincunt Semeber, qui interpretatur ibi perditio, et est rex Seboim, id est cinguli maeroris, cum per iracundiam et invidiam incautos decipiunt; unde Job V: « Stultum interficit iracundia, parvulum occidit invidia. » Regem quoque Bale vincunt, cum pusillanimes in desperationem mittunt; Bale namque interpretatur praecipitatio vel absorbitio: ad hoc maxime diabolus certat ut post lapsum peccati in desperationem mittat.

VERS. 4-6.-- « Duodecim, » etc. Duodenarius superfluus est numerus. In qua superfluitate serviunt quinque sensus nostri vanae superstitioni, quam 119Dsignificat Chodorlahomor, et quatuor vitiis sibi adjacentibus; et recedere volentes gravem persecutionem patiuntur, et saepe superantur.

VERS. 7-12.-- « Percusseruntque. » (HIERON.) Alia editio habet, etc., usque ad quia ibi populum Deus judicavit.

« Raphaim in Astaroch, » etc. Nomina locorum et gentium significant carnaliter viventes, quos facile vitia vincunt.

VERS. 13.-- « Hebraeo. » LXX, transfluviali; quia scilicet de Mesopotamia veniens transito fluvio Euphrate, sedem posuit in terra Chanaan. Unde Jesus Nave: « Quid vultis servire diis patrum vestrorum qui sunt trans flumen? »

120AVERS. 14-17.-- « Quod cum audisset Abram, » etc. (ISID.) Mystice. Similiter fides nostra cum firmata est Spiritu principali, quinque sensus corporis quasi quinque reges subjicit, verbo Dei triumphans de homine exteriori. Ille in trecentis decem et octo adversarios debellavit, quia nos in sacramento crucis, quam tau littera significat, quae trecenta significat, quinque sensus carnales, nos antea variis vitiis captivantes, superamus. Decem et octo, ex ternario et senario multiplicatus, fidem Trinitatis et perfectionem significat operis quibus virtutum contra vitia plena victoria perficitur.

(STRAB.) Allegorice. Bellum Abrae cum quatuor regibus significat bellum virtutum et vitiorum. Pater ergo fidei, et prima via credendi, pro fratre suo 120BLot dimicavit et vicit, non in multitudine exercitus, sed in trecentis decem et octo expeditis vernaculis. Sic quisque pro anima sua contra spirituales nequitias bellum gerat cum trecentis decem et octo vernaculis, id est, cum auxilio sanctae crucis: tau enim littera, quae habet figuram crucis, trecenta exprimit.

Dan autem unus est de fontibus Jordanis; alter enim Jor vocatur, quod interpretatur rivus; duobus ergo fontibus, qui non procul a se distant, in unum rivulum foederatis, Jordanis appellatur.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « At vero Melchisedech, » etc. (HIER.) Nunc aiunt Hebraei esse Sem filium Noe, et computatione annorum ostendunt eum usque ad Isaac vixisse: et omnes primogenitos a Noe usque ad 120CAaron pontifices fuisse. Salem vero postea dicta est Hierusalem; Apostolus memorans Melchisedech, « sine patre et matre, » refert ad Christum, et per eum ad Ecclesiam gentium, quia gloria capitis ad membra refertur. Quoniam benedixit Abrae circumciso, et in eo Levi, et sic Aaron, de quo postea sacerdotium. Ex quo vult colligere sacerdotium Ecclesiae habentis praeputium benedixisse circumciso sacerdotio Synagogae. Quia autem ait: « Tu es sacerdos in aeternum secundum ordinem Melchisedech, » ministerium nostrum veri ordinis signatur, non per Aaron irrationalibus victimis immolandis, sed oblato pane et vino, id est, corpore Domini et sanguine consecrari.

(STRAB.) Melchisedech, qui interpretatur rex justitiae, 120Det dicitur rex pacis, Christum significat, per quem reconciliati sumus, et qui in fine saeculi juste judicaturus est.

VERS. 20-23.-- « Dedit ei decimas. » (ISID.) Sciens ergo patriarcha, etc., usque ad partim stipendia legitime pugnantibus debita non abnegans.

VERS. 24.-- « Qui venerunt, » etc. Utitur enim aliquando fides testimoniis infidelium; unde Apostolus ad Titum I: « Malae bestiae, pigri ventres; » sed ad utilitatem fidelium.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Amraphel. » He is interpreted he said that it should fall, and he is king of Sennaar, which is interpreted the shaking out of teeth 118Dor their stench. He signifies the heretics, who tend toward a fall, and with deceitful words vomit out a stinking meaning.

« Arioch. » He is interpreted Drunken or drunkenness: he signifies the folly of the Gentiles, who, drunk with iniquity, worship the creature for the Creator, and are rolled about in the enticements of the world. « Chodorlahomor. » He is interpreted A comely sheaf, who is king of Elam, that is, of this world: he signifies hypocrisy, which, although it be vain and pernicious, yet seems comely on the surface, but in the time to come what it was shall appear.

« Thadal. » Knowing the yoke or a scout, who is king of the nations; he signifies avarice, which, shrewd and crafty, subjugates many to itself.

119A« Amraphel, » etc. These four kings figure the four principal vices, contrary to the four principal virtues; which strive to subjugate the five senses signified by the five kings, through the concupiscence of the flesh, through the concupiscence of the eyes, through the ambition of the world.

VERS. 2-13.-- « Balae. » (HIERON.) In Hebrew it is called devouring. The Hebrews hand down that this is elsewhere called Salissam, and a heifer that dismays, because it was swallowed up by a third earthquake. And that from that time in which Sodom and Gomorrha, Adama and Seboim were overthrown by divine fire, it is named the little one. For Segor is translated as little, which in the Syriac tongue is called Zoara. But the valley of the Salt-pits, in which were wells of bitumen, after the wrath of God and the rain of brimstone, was turned into the Dead 119BSea. which by the Greeks is called the lake of Asphalt, that is, of bitumen.

Mystically. Four kings overcome five, while the contrary powers, abusing the five senses of the body, ensnare them with vices and lead them captive.

Allegorically. Such kings overcome first Bara, who is interpreted malice or a creature, and is king of the Sodomites, that is, of blindness, or of a silent beast, when they persuade those who persevere in the old malice to follow the blindness of lust after the manner of beasts. They conquer also Bersa, that is, set in straitness, king of Gomorrha, that is, of a swelling and seditious people, when they overwhelm and cast down those who are straitened by the cares of the world and fear not to work the wickedness of rapine. They conquer likewise Sennaab king of Adama, that 119Cis, of the earthly, when they exhort those who savour earthly things only to remain in luxury, so that they say: « Let us eat and drink, for tomorrow we shall die (I Cor. XV). » They conquer Semeber, who is interpreted there destruction, and is king of Seboim, that is, of the girdle of grief, when through anger and envy they deceive the incautious; whence Job V: « Anger killeth the foolish, and envy slayeth the little one. » They conquer also king Bale, when they cast the faint-hearted into despair; for Bale is interpreted a headlong fall or a swallowing up: to this above all the devil strives, that after the fall of sin he may cast into despair.
