VERS. 4-6.-- « Twelve, » etc. The number twelve is a superfluous number. In which superfluity our five senses serve the vain superstition which 119DChodorlahomor signifies, and the four vices lying next to it; and when they would withdraw, they suffer a grievous persecution, and are often overcome.

VERS. 7-12.-- « And they smote. » (HIERON.) Another edition has, etc., as far as because there God judged the people.

« Raphaim in Astaroch, »[Vulg. Gn 14:5 reads Raphaim in Astarothcarnaim.] etc. The names of the places and of the nations signify those who live carnally, whom vices easily overcome.

VERS. 13.-- « Hebrew. » The Seventy, one from beyond the river; because, namely, coming from Mesopotamia, the river Euphrates being crossed, he set his seat in the land of Chanaan. Whence Josue the son of Nave: « Why will you serve the gods of your fathers who are beyond the river? »[Vulg. Jos 24:14–15 has no such sentence; it reads auferte deos quibus servierunt patres vestri in Mesopotamia and utrum diis, quibus servierunt patres vestri in Mesopotamia.]

120AVERS. 14-17.-- « And when Abram had heard this, » etc. (ISID.) Mystically. In like manner our faith, when it has been strengthened by the principal Spirit, subjects the five senses of the body as five kings, triumphing by the word of God over the outward man. He overcame his adversaries in three hundred and eighteen, because we, in the sacrament of the cross, which the letter tau signifies, which signifies three hundred, overcome the five carnal senses that before held us captive in divers vices. Eighteen, multiplied out of three and six, signifies the faith of the Trinity and the perfection of work, by which the full victory of the virtues against the vices is accomplished.

(STRAB.) Allegorically. The war of Abraham with the four kings signifies the war of the virtues and of the vices. The father therefore of faith, and the first way of believing, fought for his brother 120BLot and conquered, not in a multitude of an army, but in three hundred and eighteen trained servants of his household. So let each man wage war for his own soul against spiritual wickednesses with three hundred and eighteen servants of his household, that is, with the help of the holy cross: for the letter tau, which has the figure of a cross, expresses three hundred.

Now Dan is one of the springs of the Jordan; for the other is called Jor, which is interpreted a stream; the two springs therefore, which are not far distant from one another, being joined into one streamlet, it is called Jordan.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « But Melchisedech indeed, » etc. (HIER.) Now the Hebrews say that he is Sem the son of Noah, and by the reckoning of the years they show that he lived until Isaac: and that all the firstborn from Noah to 120CAaron were high priests. But Salem was afterwards called Jerusalem; the Apostle, recording Melchisedech « without father and mother, » refers it to Christ, and through him to the Church of the Gentiles, because the glory of the head is referred to the members. Since he blessed Abraham circumcised, and in him Levi, and so Aaron, from whom afterwards the priesthood. From which he would gather that the priesthood of the Church, which has the uncircumcision, blessed the circumcised priesthood of the Synagogue. But because he says: « Thou art a priest for ever according to the order of Melchisedech, » our ministry of the true order is signified, to be consecrated not through Aaron by the immolating of irrational victims, but by the offering of bread and wine, that is, of the body and blood of the Lord.

(STRAB.) Melchisedech, who is interpreted king of justice, 120Dand is called king of peace, signifies Christ, through whom we have been reconciled, and who at the end of the world is to judge justly.

VERS. 20-23.-- « He gave him tithes. » (ISID.) The patriarch therefore, knowing, etc., as far as partly not denying the stipends due to those who fight lawfully.

VERS. 24.-- « Who came, » etc. For faith sometimes uses the testimonies of unbelievers; whence the Apostle to Titus I: « Evil beasts, slothful bellies; » but to the profit of the faithful.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 1.-- « His itaque transactis, » etc., « Noli timere. » Triumphata multitudine vitiorum, confortatur 121Aspes cujusque fidelis a Domino ne titubet, sed mercedem exspectet.

VERS. 2.-- « Dixitque Abram. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.) « Ego vado sine liberis, » etc. usque ad ab hoc aiunt Damascum et nominatam, et conditam.

VERS. 5-8.-- « Suspice coelum, » etc. Superius dixit: « Erit semen tuum sicut arena maris, » quia in illa plebe quidam erant carnales et terreni, qui arenae comparantur, quia aridi, et a bonis operibus infecundi; alii quasi stellae fulgentes.

(RAB.) Duplex significatio promissi seminis datur. Ostensis stellis dicitur ei: « Sic faciam semen tuum, » id est, gentem Christianam, cujus tu pater futurus es, in fide, scilicet resurrectionis lumine 121Bcoruscare. Ostensa vero arena dicitur: « Sic erit semen tuum, » etc., id est, copiosa erit gens Judaeorum, sed arida et infecunda.

VERS. 9-10.-- « Sume, inquit, mihi, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Vacca triennis, plebs Judaica posita sub jugo legis, etc., usque ad quando per ignem separabuntur boni a malis.

« Vaccam triennem, et capram trimam, » etc. Quia ab Adam usque ad Noe, et inde usque ad Abram, deinde usque ad David, populus adolevit.

VERS. 11.-- « Descenderuntque volucres, » etc. (GREG., lib. XVI Moral., c. 20.) Tropologice. In ipso orationis sacrificio ingerunt se importunae cogitationes, rapere vel violare cupientes: sed manu sanctae discretionis debemus custodire quod foris offerimus, 121Cne maligni spiritus et perversae cogitationes rapiant.

VERS. 12.-- « Pavor irruit, » etc. (AUG. in Quaest. Hebr.) Tractanda est ista quaestio propter eos qui contendunt istas perturbationes non cadere in animum sapientis, etc., usque ad non dicendam perturbationem.

VERS. 13-15.-- « Scito praenoscens, » etc. (ISID.) Populus Israel in Aegypto serviturus prophetatur: non quod Aegyptiis annis quadringentis servierit, sed quia hic numerus in illa afflictione completus est, quia ab illo tempore computatur quod ista Abrahae promittuntur.

« Scito praenoscens, » etc. (AUG. de Civ. lib. XVI, c. 24.) Non sic accipiendum est, tanquam in illa durissima servitute quadringentos annos populus Dei 121Dfuerit, etc., usque ad populus Dei in Aegypto dura servitute afflictus est.

VERS. 16-21.-- « Generatione autem, » etc. (HIERON. epist. ad Damas.) Moses vero alibi dicit, etc., usque ad et ad Salomon perveniunt.

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- « These things therefore being done, » etc., « Fear not. » When the multitude of vices has been triumphed over, the hope 121Aof every faithful man is strengthened by the Lord, that it may not waver, but may look for its reward.

VERS. 2.-- « And Abram said. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr.) « I go without children, » [Vulg. Gn 15:2 ego vadam absque liberis, "I shall go without children"] etc. as far as from him, they say, Damascus was both named and founded.

VERS. 5-8.-- « Look up to heaven, » etc. Above he said: « Thy seed shall be as the sand of the sea, » because in that people some were carnal and earthly, who are compared to sand, since they are dry and unfruitful of good works; others were as shining stars.

(RAB.) A twofold signification of the promised seed is given. When the stars are shown, it is said to him: « So will I make thy seed, » that is, the Christian nation, whose father thou art to be in faith, namely to glitter 121Bwith the light of the resurrection. But when the sand is shown, it is said: « So shall thy seed be, » etc., that is, the nation of the Jews shall be numerous, but dry and unfruitful.

VERS. 9-10.-- « Take me, said he, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) The heifer of three years, the Jewish people set under the yoke of the law, etc., as far as when by fire the good shall be separated from the evil.

« A heifer of three years, and a she-goat of three years, » etc. Because from Adam to Noah, and thence to Abram, and then to David, the people grew up.

VERS. 11.-- « And the birds came down, » etc. (GREG., lib. XVI Moral., c. 20.) Tropologically. In the very sacrifice of prayer importunate thoughts thrust themselves in, desiring to snatch it away or to violate it: but with the hand of holy discretion we ought to guard what we offer without, 121Clest malignant spirits and perverse thoughts snatch it away.

VERS. 12.-- « Dread rushed in, » [Vulg. Gn 15:12 sopor, "a deep sleep"] etc. (AUG. in Quaest. Hebr.) This question is to be handled on account of those who contend that these disturbances do not fall upon the mind of the wise man, etc., as far as a disturbance not to be named.

VERS. 13-15.-- « Know thou beforehand, » etc. (ISID.) The people of Israel is prophesied as about to serve in Egypt: not that it served the Egyptians four hundred years, but because this number was completed in that affliction, since it is reckoned from the time at which these things are promised to Abraham.

« Know thou beforehand, » etc. (AUG. de Civ. lib. XVI, c. 24.) It is not to be so understood, as though the people of God 121Dhad been four hundred years in that most harsh servitude, etc., as far as the people of God was afflicted in Egypt with hard servitude.

VERS. 16-21.-- « But in the generation, » etc. (HIERON. epist. ad Damas.) But Moses says elsewhere, etc., as far as and they arrive at Solomon.

CAPUT XVI.

VERS. 1.-- « Igitur Sarai uxor Abram, » etc. (RAB.) Non est adulter Abram si, vivente uxore, ancillae jungitur, quia nondum promulgata erat lex Evangelii unius uxoris: et genus suum multiplicandum audierat, sed per quam mulierem nesciebat, quia Saram sterilem noverat. Non fuit in coitu libidinosa voluptas, sed prolis habendae pia charitas: 122Auxor quoque hoc voluit et coegit, quae filios habere non poterat; ideo ex alia suscipere cupiebat quem in locum proprii adoptaret.

VERS. 2-11.-- « Ingredere ad ancillam, » etc. (HIERON.) Nota diligenter quod procreatio filiorum in Hebraeo aedificatio dicta est, etc., usque ad tanquam per angelum lata in gratiam redeat et se subjiciat.

(RAB.) Hae duae mulieres sunt duo Testamenta: Agar, Vetus, quod in Synagoga Judaicum populum servituti nutriebat obnoxium; Sara, Novum, quod populum Christianum in libertatem fidei generavit. Multiplicati sunt ancillae filii, ut non numerarentur prae multitudine, qui supra arenae comparantur. Ipse ergo populus ferocitate sua aemulus, et omnibus bonis contrarius, et maxime cum Christianum populum 122Btoto orbe invidia stimulante persequitur, et longe lateque dispersus, vagando et negotiando omnibus est onerosus.

VERS. 12.-- « Hic erit ferus homo. » Vel rusticus, secundum alios. (HIERON.) In Hebraeo habetur phara quod interpretatur onager. Significat semen ejus habitaturum in deserto, id est, Saracenos vagos, incertisque sedibus, qui omnes gentes quibus desertum ex latere jungitur incursantes, impugnantur ab omnibus.

VERS. 13.-- « Vocavit autem Agar. » Recte nomen ejus ita vocavit, quia gratuito consulit miseris et humiles consolatur. Unde: « Tu laborem et dolorem consideras, et tibi derelictus est pauper, » etc. « Profecto hic vidi posteriora videntis me. » Tale est 122Cillud: « Videbis posteriora mea, nam facies non apparebit tibi. » « Non enim homo videbit faciem meam et vivet (Exod. XXXIII). » Illam enim manifestationem sapientiae Dei nemo potest videre et vivere. Ipsa est species ad quam suspirat qui Deum diligit toto corde, tota anima, tota mente: ad quam contemplandam, etiam proximum diligendo sicut seipsum, quantum potest, aedificat. Posteriora vero ejus, id est, incarnationem ejus, quam assumpsit postremis temporibus, nunc per fidem et dilectionem desiderando contemplamur, et in futuro aeterna contemplatione fruemur.

VERS. 14-16.-- « Propterea appellavit puteum illum, » etc. (HIERON.) Usque hodie puteus Agar demonstratur in deserto Arabiae, inter Cades et Barad, 122Dquod significat veteris legis et novae Scripturam, quam conditor noster, vita viventium, spes morientium, intuitu piae miserationis mortalibus dedit, ut edocti et a vitiis correcti, Dei visione perpetuo digni esse mereantur.

CHAPTER XVI.

VERS. 1.-- « Now Sarai the wife of Abram, » etc. (RAB.) Abram is no adulterer if, while his wife lives, he is joined to the handmaid, because the Gospel's law of one wife had not yet been promulgated: and he had heard that his stock was to be multiplied, but by what woman he knew not, since he knew Sara to be barren. There was in the intercourse no lustful pleasure, but a devout love of having offspring: 122Ahis wife too willed this and compelled it, she who could not have sons; and therefore she desired to receive from another one whom she might adopt in the place of her own.

VERS. 2-11.-- « Go in to the handmaid, » etc. (HIERON.) Note diligently that the procreation of sons is in the Hebrew called a building up, etc., as far as that, brought back as it were by an angel, she may return into favour and subject herself.

(RAB.) These two women are the two Testaments: Agar, the Old, which in the Synagogue nourished the Jewish people, liable to servitude; Sara, the New, which begot the Christian people into the liberty of faith. The sons of the handmaid were multiplied, so that they could not be numbered for their multitude, who above are compared to sand. That people therefore, a rival by its own fierceness, and contrary to all good things, and most of all when, with envy goading it, it persecutes the Christian people 122Bthroughout the whole world, and scattered far and wide, is burdensome to all by wandering and by trafficking.

VERS. 12.-- « He shall be a wild man. » Or a rustic, according to others. (HIERON.) In the Hebrew there stands phara which is interpreted a wild ass. It signifies that his seed shall dwell in the desert, that is, the Saracens, wandering and of uncertain abodes, who, raiding all the nations to whom the desert is joined on the flank, are assailed by all.

VERS. 13.-- « And Agar called. » Rightly did she so call his name, because he freely takes counsel for the wretched and comforts the lowly. Whence: « Thou considerest labour and sorrow, and to thee is the poor man left, » etc. « Verily here have I seen the hinder parts of him that seeth me. » Of like sort is 122Cthat saying: « Thou shalt see my hinder parts, for my face shall not appear to thee. » [Vulg. Ex 33:23 faciem autem meam videre non poteris, "but my face thou canst not see"] « For man shall not see my face and live (Exod. XXXIII). » [Vulg. Ex 33:20 non enim videbit me homo et vivet, "for man shall not see me and live"] For that manifestation of the wisdom of God no man can see and live. It is that very form for which he sighs who loves God with his whole heart, his whole soul, his whole mind: for the contemplating of which, by loving his neighbour also as himself, so far as he can, he builds up. But his hinder parts, that is, his incarnation, which he took upon him in the last times, we now contemplate by longing, through faith and love, and in the time to come we shall enjoy them in eternal contemplation.

VERS. 14-16.-- « Therefore she called that well, » etc. (HIERON.) To this day the well of Agar is shown in the desert of Arabia, between Cades and Barad, 122Dwhich signifies the Scripture of the old law and of the new, which our Creator, the life of the living, the hope of the dying, gave to mortals out of regard of his loving compassion, that being taught and corrected from their vices they may deserve to be for ever worthy of the vision of God.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1-4.-- . . . . . . « Ponamque foedus, » etc. In Hebraeo berith ( ) quod non testamentum, sed foedus, sive pactum significat.

VERS. 5-7.-- « Nec ultra vocabitur nomen tuum Abram. » (HIERON.) Dicunt Hebraei quod Deus ex nomine suo, quod apud illos tetragrammaton est, h litteram Abrahae, et Sarae addiderit. Dicebatur 123Aautem Abram, quod est pater excelsus; postea dictus est Abraham, quod est pater multarum, nam gentium in nomine non habetur, sed subauditur. Nec mirandum quod cum apud Graecos et nos a littera videatur addita, h littera addita dicatur: idioma enim Hebraeae linguae h consuevit scribere et a legere: sicut econtrario, h pronuntiare et a scribere.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « Daboque tibi et semini tuo. . . .et omnem terram Chanaan. » Chanaan interpretatur motus eorum: cujus terra a fidelibus in possessionem accipitur, cum mens prius diversis circumacta motibus, in melius mutata, postmodum irreflexis oculis in anteriora extenditur

« In possessionem aeternam. » (AUG.) Scriptura vocat aeternum cujus finis non constituitur, aut quod 123Bnon ita fit ut deinceps non sit faciendum, quantum pertinet ad curam vel potestatem facientis; unde: Serviet aeternum qui parvo nesciet uti.

VERS. 10.-- « Circumcidetur ex vobis masculinum, » etc. (STRAB.) Tribus de causis hoc praecipitur Abrahae. Primo ut per obedientiam mandati placeat, per cujus praevaricationem Adam displicuerat; secundo, ut hoc signo a caeteris nationibus discerneretur; tertio, ut in eo membro circumcisus quo libido solet dominari, castitatem sibi servandam et impudicitiam rescindendam cognosceret.

VERS. 11-13.-- « Carnem praeputii. » (RAB. STRAR.) Circumcisio facta in uno membro significat circumcisionem spiritualem, quae in corde et in omnibus 123Csensibus exhibenda est. Circumcidendum est cor, ne noxia cogitemus; oculi, ne vanitatem videamus; aures circumcidendae sunt, id est obturandae, ne audiant sanguinem: et sic in omnibus sensibus celebranda est circumcisio.

(ALCUIN. in Gen., tom. I.) Ideo autem Abrahae circumcisio commendata est, etc., usque ad unde olim praevaricatores generaverunt ad mortem.

Allegorice. Jam tunc propter futuram peregrinationem, ne misceretur semen ejus gentibus, circumcisionis pactum datur in signum: quia significat naturam renovatam per baptismum, post spoliationem veteris hominis per Christum, qui resurrexit post baptismum. Servos vero vernaculos, emptitios etiam, circumcidi praecepit, ut doceret ad omnes 123Dpertinere redemptionis gratiam. Ibi et parentum mutantur nomina, ut omnia resonent novitatem. Abram, pater excelsus; Abraham, pater multarum gentium.

VERS. 14.-- « Masculus cujus praeputii caro, » etc. (ISID.) Quaeritur quomodo pueri circumcisi (qui pactum Dei irritum non fecerunt, sed qui eos circumcidere neglexerunt) perire debeant? Sed pactum Dei non secundum opus, sed secundum originem in primo homine irritum fecerunt, in quo omnes peccaverunt. Nascuntur enim omnes, non proprie sed originaliter, peccatores. Ideo pereunt omnes qui per regenerationem non liberantur.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « Sarai uxorem tuam, » etc. 124A(ISID.) Declaratur promissio de vocatione gentium in Isaac filio promissionis, id est gratiae, non naturae, quia de sene patre et sterili matre: et quia hoc non per generationem, sicut Ismael, sed per regenerationem futurum erat, imperata est circumcisio, quando Sarae filius promittitur in typo Ecclesiae, non quando Ismael, qui typus Judaeorum.

(HIERON.-ISID.) Errant qui putant primum Saram per unum r scriptum fuisse, et postea additum esse alterum r. Et quia r apud Graecos centum significat, multas super hoc nomine suspicantur ineptias, cum et utcunque velint vocabulum commutatum, non Graecam, sed Hebraicam debet habere rationem. Hebraeum est enim. Nemo enim altera lingua quemlibet vocans, etymologiam vocabuli sumit ex 124Baltera. Sarai ergo primum vocata est ( ) per scin, res, iod. Sublata ergo iod addita est he littera, quae pro a legitur, et vocata est Sara ( ). Antea enim dicebatur princeps mea, quia quasi unius familiae mater: postea absolute princeps, omnium scilicet gentium princeps futura. Quidam pessime suspicantur eam prius lepram fuisse vocatam, et postea principem, cum lepra sarai dicatur ( ), quod in nostra lingua videtur habere aliquam similitudinem, sed Hebraeo penitus est diversa. Scribitur enim per zadich et ain et res et tau; quod a tribus superioribus, id est, schin, res et he, quibus scribitur Sara, manifeste distat.

VERS. 17, 18.-- « Cecidit Abraham in faciem suam, et risit in corde suo. » (HIERON.) Post paulum 124Csequitur: « Et vocabis nomen ejus Isaac: » qui risus interpretatur de risu Abrahae, non de risu Sarae. Postquam enim ex risu Abrahae vocatus est filius ejus Isaac, legitur Saram risisse.

« Nomen ejus Isaac. » Sciendum quod quatuor in Veteri Testamento nominibus suis aperte, antequam nascerentur, vocati sunt: Ismael, Isaac, Samsom et Josias. Lege Scripturas.

VERS. 19.-- « Constituam pactum meum, » etc. Pactum vero Domini cum Isaac, populo scilicet fideli, quem mater Ecclesia per fidem et baptismum generavit. « Et foedus sempiternum, » quia aeternae vitae possessio ei soli promittitur, qui cum capite suo Christo regnum coeli semper possidebit.

VERS. 20.-- « Super Ismael, » etc. Historice. 124DIsmael populum Judaeorum significat, qui multiplicatus est valde; duodecim duces generavit, cum multitudo carnalium Judaeorum ex duodecim patriarchis genita, in duodecim tribus est divisa.

VERS. 21.-- « In anno altero. » Annus alter, quo Sara genuit Isaac, tempora sunt regenerationis, quibus fusus est sanguis Christi et genus humanum ad vitam restauratum.

VERS. 22.-- « Ascendit Deus ad Abraham, » etc. ( Glossa Graeca. ) Ergo, ut ita dicam, descenderat, et humilem se, circumcisionis legem statuens, praebuerat.

VERS. 23-27.-- « Tulit autem Abraham, » etc. Non intendebat Deus ut tantum praeputium carnis 125AAbrahae et sobolis ejus circumcideretur, sed ei, quem a cunctis elegerat, aeterni Testamenti munera conferebat; unde Paulus Phil. III: « Nos sumus enim circumcisio, qui spiritu Deo servimus. » Et alibi, Rom. II: « Non enim qui in manifesto Judaeus est, neque quae in manifesto, in carne, est circumcisio. » Circumcidamus ergo Dei verbo aures, labia, cor, praeputium et omnia membra carnis. De circumcisione aurium dicitur Luc. VIII: « Qui habet aures audiendi, audiat; » incircumcisis auribus verba sapientiae et veritatis non possumus audire. Circumcidamus labia, ne aliquid turpe, vel malum, vel otiosum loquamur. Circumcidit carnem praeputii, qui sola causa posteritatis, certis et legitimis temporibus, conjugio utitur: qui aliter agit, 125Bnon circumcidit. Ecclesia vero, gratia Christi pro se crucifixi roborata, non solum se ab illicitis temperat, sed etiam a concessis, et tanquam virgo sponsa Christi pudicis virginibus floret, in quibus vera circumcisio praeputii servatur in carne. Qui autem non obscoenis desideriis, vel sensibus haereticis, et blasphemis assertionibus, moechatur, sed fidei puritatem in conscientia custodit, corde circumcisus est: et de eo dici potest: « Beati mundo corde (Matth. V), » etc. Circumcidantur manus, ne mala faciant, pedes, ne in malum currant: visus, ne illicita concupiscat; gustus, ut ad gloriam Dei manducet et bibat; odoratus, ut Christi odorem capiat, et operibus misericordiae odorem suavitatis spargat. Singula quoque membra, si in officiis Deo serviant, 125Ccircumcisa sunt. Si ultra praescriptam legem luxurient, incircumcisa.

(HIERON.-STRAB.) Quaeri solet quomodo feminae salvabantur, quae non circumcidebantur? Fide scilicet et hostiarum oblatione, sicut ante circumcisionem viri sancti mundabantur unde: « Fide purificans corda eorum. »

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1-4.-- . . . . . . « And I will set my covenant, » etc. In the Hebrew berith ( ), which signifies not a testament, but a covenant, or a compact.

VERS. 5-7.-- « Neither shall thy name any more be called Abram. » (HIERON.) The Hebrews say that God added out of his own name, which among them is the tetragrammaton, the letter h to Abraham and to Sara. Now he was called 123AAbram, which is an exalted father; afterward he was called Abraham, which is a father of many, for of nations is not had in the name, but is understood. Nor is it to be wondered at that, whereas among the Greeks and among us the letter a seems to have been added, the letter h is said to have been added: for it is the idiom of the Hebrew tongue to write h and to read a: as, on the contrary, to pronounce h and to write a.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « And I will give to thee and to thy seed. . . .and all the land of Chanaan. » Chanaan is interpreted their motion: whose land is received into possession by the faithful, when the mind, first driven about by divers motions, being changed for the better, is afterward stretched out toward the things before with eyes that turn not back

« Into an everlasting possession. » (AUG.) Scripture calls that eternal whose end is not appointed, or that which 123Bis not so done that it is not thenceforth to be done, so far as pertains to the care or the power of the doer; whence: He shall serve for ever who shall not know how to use a little.

VERS. 10.-- « The male among you shall be circumcised, » etc. (STRAB.) For three causes is this enjoined upon Abraham. First, that he might please by the obedience of the commandment, by the transgression whereof Adam had displeased; secondly, that by this sign he might be distinguished from the other nations; thirdly, that being circumcised in that member wherein lust is wont to have dominion, he might know that chastity was to be kept by him and unchastity to be cut away.

VERS. 11-13.-- « The flesh of the foreskin. » (RAB. STRAR.) Circumcision made in one member signifies the spiritual circumcision, which is to be shown forth in the heart and in all 123Cthe senses. The heart is to be circumcised, that we think not hurtful things; the eyes, that we see not vanity; the ears are to be circumcised, that is, to be stopped, that they hear not blood: and so in all the senses circumcision is to be celebrated.

(ALCUIN. in Gen., tom. I.) And circumcision was for this cause commended to Abraham, etc., as far as whence of old transgressors begot unto death.

Allegorically. Even then, because of the pilgrimage to come, lest his seed should be mingled with the nations, the compact of circumcision is given for a sign: because it signifies nature renewed through baptism, after the stripping off of the old man through Christ, who rose again after baptism. But he commanded the home-born servants, and the bought ones also, to be circumcised, that he might teach that the grace of redemption pertains to all 123D. There too the names of the parents are changed, that all things may sound of newness. Abram, an exalted father; Abraham, a father of many nations.

VERS. 14.-- « The male whose flesh of his foreskin, » etc. (ISID.) It is asked: how ought circumcised [circumcisi; read incircumcisi, "uncircumcised"] boys (who did not make void the compact of God, but they who neglected to circumcise them) to perish? But they made void the compact of God not according to their deed, but according to their origin in the first man, in whom all have sinned. For all are born sinners, not properly but originally. Therefore all perish who are not set free through regeneration.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « Sarai thy wife, » etc. 124A(ISID.) The promise concerning the calling of the Gentiles is declared in Isaac, the son of promise, that is, of grace, not of nature, since he is of an aged father and a barren mother: and because this was to be not by generation, as Ismael was, but by regeneration, circumcision was commanded, when the son of Sara is promised in the type of the Church, not when Ismael is, who is the type of the Jews.

(HIERON.-ISID.) They err who think that Sara was at first written with one r, and that afterward another r. was added. And because r among the Greeks signifies a hundred, they surmise many follies about this name, whereas, however they may wish the word to have been changed, it ought to have not a Greek but a Hebrew reckoning. For it is Hebrew. For no one, calling anyone whatsoever in one language, takes the etymology of the word from 124Banother. Sarai therefore was at first called ( ) by scin, res, iod. When therefore the iod was taken away, the letter he was added, which is read for a, and she was called Sara ( ). For before she was called my princess, because she was as it were the mother of one family: afterward, absolutely, a princess, being namely about to be princess of all nations. Some most wretchedly surmise that she was first called leprosy, and afterward a princess, since leprosy is called sarai ( ), which in our tongue seems to have some likeness, but in the Hebrew is utterly different. For it is written by zadich and ain and res and tau; which manifestly differs from the three above, that is, schin, res and he, by which Sara is written.

VERS. 17, 18.-- « Abraham fell upon his face, and laughed in his heart. » (HIERON.) A little after 124Cthere follows: « And thou shalt call his name Isaac: » which laughter is interpreted of the laughter of Abraham, not of the laughter of Sara. For after his son was called Isaac from the laughter of Abraham, Sara is read to have laughed.

« His name Isaac. » It is to be known that four in the Old Testament were called openly by their own names before they were born: Ismael, Isaac, Samson and Josias. Read the Scriptures.

VERS. 19.-- « I will establish my compact, » etc. But the compact of the Lord is with Isaac, that is, with the faithful people, whom mother Church has begotten through faith and baptism. « And an everlasting covenant, » because the possession of eternal life is promised to him alone who with his head, Christ, shall possess the kingdom of heaven for ever.

VERS. 20.-- « Upon Ismael, » etc. Historically. 124DIsmael signifies the people of the Jews, which was multiplied exceedingly; he begot twelve princes, since the multitude of carnal Jews, begotten of the twelve patriarchs, was divided into twelve tribes.

VERS. 21.-- « In the other year. » The other year, wherein Sara bore Isaac, is the times of regeneration, in which the blood of Christ was poured out and the human race restored to life.

VERS. 22.-- « God went up to Abraham, » [Vulg. Gn 17:22 ascendit Deus ab Abraham, "God went up from Abraham"] etc. ( Glossa Graeca. ) Therefore, so to speak, he had come down, and had shown himself humble, in appointing the law of circumcision.

VERS. 23-27.-- « And Abraham took, » etc. God did not intend that only the foreskin of the flesh of Abraham 125Aand of his offspring should be circumcised, but upon him whom he had chosen out of all he was conferring the gifts of the eternal Testament; whence Paul, Phil. III: « For we are the circumcision, who serve God in the spirit. » And elsewhere, Rom. II: « For it is not he that is in the open who is a Jew, neither is that circumcision which is in the open, in the flesh. » Let us therefore circumcise by the word of God our ears, our lips, our heart, our foreskin and all the members of the flesh. Of the circumcision of the ears it is said, Luc. VIII: « He that hath ears to hear, let him hear; » with uncircumcised ears we cannot hear the words of wisdom and of truth. Let us circumcise our lips, that we speak nothing base, or evil, or idle. He circumcises the flesh of the foreskin who uses marriage for the sole cause of posterity, at fixed and lawful seasons: he who does otherwise 125Bdoes not circumcise. But the Church, strengthened by the grace of Christ crucified for her, refrains herself not only from things unlawful, but even from things allowed, and as a virgin spouse of Christ flourishes with chaste virgins, in whom the true circumcision of the foreskin is kept in the flesh. But he who does not commit adultery by obscene desires, or by heretical opinions and blasphemous assertions, but keeps the purity of faith in his conscience, is circumcised in heart: and of him it can be said: « Blessed are the clean of heart (Matth. V), » etc. Let the hands be circumcised, that they do no evil, the feet, that they run not into evil: the sight, that it covet not unlawful things; the taste, that it eat and drink to the glory of God; the smell, that it may take the odour of Christ, and by works of mercy scatter abroad the odour of sweetness. Each several member also, if it serve God in its offices, 125Cis circumcised. If they run riot beyond the law prescribed, uncircumcised.

(HIERON.-STRAB.) It is wont to be asked: how were women saved, who were not circumcised? By faith, namely, and by the offering of victims, as before circumcision holy men were cleansed whence: « Purifying their hearts by faith. »

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Apparuit autem ei Dominus in convalle Mambre. » (ISID. in Gen. ) Abraham triplicem figuram gerit: primam, Salvatoris, qui relicta cognatione venit in mundum; alteram, Patris, quia Filium unicum immolavit; tertiam (quae in hoc loco apparet), sanctorum qui adventum Christi cum gaudio 125Dsusceperunt. Tabernaculum Abrahae Hierusalem significat, ubi pro tempore prophetae et apostoli habitaverunt, et Christus adveniens a credentibus exceptus, ab incredulis in ligno suspensus est.

VERS. 2, 3.-- « Tres viri. » Tres viri Christi pronuntiant adventum, quem duo angeli comitantur, Moses secundum quosdam, et Elias; unus legislator, qui in lege adventum Domini indicavit: alter, qui in fine venturus est, secundum adventum Christi nuntiaturus, et Evangelium ejus praedicaturus. Unde transfigurato Domino in monte cum eo videntur. « Tres vidit, et unum adoravit, » Salvatorem scilicet ostendens, cujus adventum praestolabatur. Unde: « Abraham vidit diem meum et gavisus 126Aest (Joan. VIII). » Praevidit enim mysterium futuri sacramenti. Unde pedes eorum lavit, ut in fine mundi purificationem lavacri futuram monstraret; pedes enim novissima significant.

« Quos cum vidisset. » (GREG., lib. IX Moral., c. 51.) Moraliter. Abraham tribus angelis occurrit, quia vir et dominus domus, scilicet spiritualis intellectus, debet in cognitione Trinitatis claustra carnis excedere, et quasi habitationis infimae januam exire. Cura carnis, quam Sara significat, foris ad jactantiam non appareat, sed post tergum viri, id est, sub discretione spiritus, solis necessariis intenta, nesciat procaciter detegi, sed verecunde moderari.

(Ibid., lib. XXVIII, c. 4). Abraham non potuisset angelos videre, hospitio suscipere et cibos adhibere, 126Bnisi corpus ex aere assumpsissent: non enim apparerent, non cibos sumerent, nisi solidum corpus ex coelesti elemento haberent. Nec mirum quod modo angeli, modo Dominus vocantur, quia angeli vocantur, qui exterius ministrant, et Dominus ostenditur, qui eis interius imperat.

VERS. 4, 5.-- « Sed afferam. » Ad hospitalitatis officia revertitur, quae etiam angelis exhiberi possunt, cum ex elementis corpoream formam assumunt: quod Abraham intelligens, deitatem in unitate adorabat, et dispensationi angelicae in forma visibili ministrabat.

VERS. 6.-- « Tria sata. » Haec in typum Ecclesiae dicuntur, quae de tribus mundi partibus in unitatem fidei congregata est, et quasi panis sine fermento 126Cigne Spiritus sancti decocta est.

(STRAB.) Satum genus est mensurae. Tria sata tres filios Noe significant, ex quibus omne genus hominum disseminatum est. Significatur ergo Ecclesia, vel evangelica doctrina per totum mundum disseminanda, et in tribus mundi partibus, Asia, Africa et Europa, multiplicanda.

« Tria sata, » de quibus Sara subcinericios panes facit, tres filios significant Noe, ex quibus omne genus hominum natum est: qui divinae Trinitati credentes aqua baptismatis per Saram, id est Ecclesiam, conspersi sunt, et in unum panem corporis Christi redacti. Haec sunt tria sata farinae, quae mulier in Evangelio fermentavit: azymi sunt panes, quia sine fermento malitiae, et angore [ Al., rancore] 126Dnequitiae, et fervore perversae doctrinae, oportet esse credentium unitatem.

« Subcinericios. » Subcinericii sunt, ut per poenitentiam delictorum Spiritus sancti vapore decocti, esca Deo acceptabilis efficiantur.

VERS. 7.-- « Vitulum tenerrimum. » Vitulus tener et saginatus Christi corpus est, quod pro salute mundi ad arborem crucis immolatum est. Hic est vitulus qui prodigo filio occiditur.

VERS. 8.-- « Butyrum et lac » cum carne vituli apposuit, quia lac veterem legem signat; unde I Cor. III: « Lac potum dedi vobis, non escam, » etc. Tradiderat enim illis legis mandatum, quasi lac de duarum tabularum expressum uberibus, hoc est 127Atestamentum fidei, quia nondum poterant evangelicae doctrinae solidam escam sumere. Butyrum uberrimum et pinguissimum evangelicum est testimonium, quod velut oleum fidelibus in signum datur. Vitulum cum lacte et butyro apposuit, quia nec corpus Domini sine lacte legis, nec hoc sine Evangelio esse potest. Sub arbore autem sederunt in Dominicae passionis signum, cujus praedicatores sunt.

VERS. 9.-- « Ecce in tabernaculo est, » etc. (STRAB.) Abraham foris cum angelis, Sara in tabernaculo est: quia fortium et doctorum est aperte praedicare, exemplis instruere: infirmorum et insipientium, discere et subditos esse.

« VERS. 10.--Et habebit filium, » etc. Promittit Deus Sarae sterili filium, dicens: Circa hoc tempus 127Bveniam; non tempus, sed qualitatem sui adventus significat, quoniam per filium repromissum fidelis populus erat nasciturus. Unde Isa. LIV et Gal. IV: « Laetare sterilis, quae non parturis, » etc.

« Quo audito, etc. (AUG. Quaest in Gen. ) Arguitur Sara quia risit, et non Abraham, cum similiter riserit; quia illius risus admirationis fuit, hujus dubitationis, quod dijudicare potuit qui corda novit. Negat Sara se risisse cum Deum non possit latere, sed forte homines putabat. Abraham vero Deum intelligebat, sed humanitatis officia praebendo, quae tantum infirmae carni necessaria sunt; mirum est: nisi homines prius arbitratus est, sed in quibus Deum loqui intelligeret, quibusdam signis divinae majestatis apparentibus: sicut in hominibus Deum 127Capparuisse saepe Scriptura testatur, sed postea angelos fuisse cognoverunt.

VERS. 11.-- « Erant autem, » etc. Seniorum aetas minor est quam senum, etsi senes appellentur seniores. Unde, secundum medicos, senior vir de muliere seniore filios facere non potest, etiamsi adhuc muliebria fluant. Secundum hoc Abraham miratur Dei potentiam de promissione filii, et hoc miraculum posuisse Apostolum accipere possumus. Emortuum enim corpus non omnino ad generationem erat, si mulier juvenilis aetatis fuisset: sed de provecta aetate generare non poterat: de Cethura enim postea generavit, quia juvencula fuit. Tradunt enim medici quia senex de femina provectioris aetatis (etsi muliebria contingant ei) generare non possit, 127Dde juvencula autem possit. Rursus mulier provectae aetatis, etsi adhuc muliebria contingant ei, de seniore parere non potest, de juvene potest. Miraculum ergo fuit, quia uterque senex et provectae aetatis, et desierant muliebria Sarae.

VERS. 12-14.-- « Voluptati operam dabo? » etc. (GREG. lib. IX Moral., c. 51.) Cura carnis, etc., usque ad quid mens nostra aliud quam gaudium parit?

VERS. 15.-- « Non risi, etc. (AMBR.) Vel ideo negavit se risisse, quia ignoravit; ideo risit, quia prophetavit.

VERS. 17-19.-- « Num celare potero Abrahae quae « gessurus sum. » Bene Dominus Abrahae secreta sua 128Acommittit, quem voluntatis suae et secretorum fidelem exsecutorem cognovit; de quo ipse carne nasciturus erat « in quo sunt omnes thesauri sapientiae et scientiae absconditi (II Cor. II). » Promittit quoque Abrahae non solum praemia, sed obedientiae filiorum ejus justitiam, et circa eos promissa compleri. Rursumque promittitur Isaac, « in gentem magnam futurus, » et quod « in eo benedicentur omnes gentes terrae. » Quibus verbis duo illi promittuntur: gens Judaeorum secundum carnem, et omnes gentes per fidem.

VERS. 20.-- « Clamor Sodomorum et Gomorrhae, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen. ) Si haec verba non dubitantis, sed irascentis et minantis accipiamus, nulla est quaestio. Humano enim more ad homines loquitur 128BDeus, et ira ejus sine perturbatione est, quia immutabilis est. Saepe autem sic minando dicimus: Videamus si non tibi facio, et si non potero tibi facere; vel sciam, id est experiar utrum non possim. Sed perturbatio non cadit in Deum. Mos autem humanae locutionis est, cui Deus se coaptat, ut humanae infirmitati conveniat.

(GREG. lib. V Moral., cap. 7.) Peccatum vero cum voce, culpa est in actione. Peccatum vero cum clamore, culpa cum libertate.

VERS. 21-23.-- « Descendam et videbo, » etc. (GREG., Moral., c. 33.) Mala hominum non ante credamus quam probemus. Et ecce per angelos ad cognoscenda mala descendit, et tanto crimine involutos 128Cinveniens, quasi patientiam praetermisit, et eos igne judicii ante judicii diem praevenit: qui cum tranquillitate judicat, ut majora crimina tarde credantur, et veraciter agnita citius puniantur.

(GREG., lib. I Reg., c. 178, tom. I.) Quo descenderet Deus, ut quae essent facta cognosceret? Aut quod non is, qui ubique est, sciret? Sed ut nostrae ignorantiae exemplum discretionis daret, quatenus debeamus mala gravia audita non credere, ipse se dicit ad cognoscendum descendere, de quo omnibus liquet quia et non descendens omnia sciret.

(HILAR., lib. IX, de Trin.) Habemus nescientem Deum, quod tamen non nesciat. Nam cum peccata magna valde sciat esse, rursum descendit ut videat an consummati sunt; et si nondum consummati 128Dsunt, ut sciat: intelligimus eum non ea tunc scire, quia prius nesciat; sed tunc scire, quia tempus ad agendum sit. Nescire ergo Deum, non est ignorantiae diminutio, sed temporis plenitudo.

VERS. 24-26.-- « Si fuerint quinquaginta justi, » etc. (ISID.) Quinquagenarius numerus, a quo usque ad decem descendit, poenitentiam significat. Unde et quinquagesimus psalmus poenitentialis est. Si ergo converterentur, salvarentur. Sed cum Deus aspicit delinquentes nolle ad poenitentiam converti, illico ardorem immoderatae luxuriae igne gehennae compescit. Usque ad decem justos non perire Sodomam dixit, quia si in quolibet per decem praeceptorum custodiam Christi nomen inveniatur, non per 129Aibit. Denarii enim numeri figura et crucem Christi significat.

VERS. 27.-- « Loquar ad Dominum, » etc. (GREG. lib. XVIII Moral., c. 33.) Mystice. Sancti quanto magis divinitatis interna prospiciunt, tanto magis se nihil esse conspiciunt. Nusquam legitur Abraham se cinerem et pulverem professum esse, nisi cum meruit Dei locutionem habere. Et ait: « Loquar ad Dominum meum, cum sim pulvis, » etc. Fortasse enim aliquid se esse crederet, si veram essentiam, quae super ipsum est, non sensisset. Unde et David exclamat: « Memento, Domine, quoniam pulvis sum (Psal. X), » etc.

Moraliter. Quosdam per tumorem sensus non elevat, sed per misericordiae opera; quidam dum se 129Bterrenis abundare conspiciunt, veras divitias non requirunt, et aeternum regnum non amant, quia temporalia sufficere putant. Non est ergo sensus in crimine, sed affectus. Cuncta enim quae Deus condidit bona sunt. Sed qui bonis male utitur, hic agit ut per edacitatis ingluviem eo, quo vivere debuit, pane moriatur. Pauper ad requiem Lazarus venerat, superbum divitem cruciabant tormenta (Luc. XVI): dives tamen Abraham fuerat, qui Lazarum in sinu tenebat. Ait tamen: « Loquar ad Dominum meum cum sim pulvis et cinis, » etc. Quid divitias suas aestimabat, qui seipsum pulverem cineremque putabat? Aperte cernimus in quo loco se posuerat, qui se pulverem et cinerem, etiam cum Deo loqueretur, aestimabat. Qua ergo poena feriendi sunt, qui 129Cad summa non proficiunt et de minimis se extollunt?

VERS. 28-33.-- « Quinque fuerint. » Quinque civitates igneis imbribus combustae. Omnes enim qui quinque corporis sensus libidinose tractaverunt, futuro incendio sunt cremandi.

« Quid si inventi, » etc. (ALC.) Non necesse est hoc de omni loco accipere. De Sodomis autem potuit dici, quia sciebat Deus nec decem esse ibi, et ideo sic respondebatur Abrahae ad exaggerationem iniquitatis Sodomorum

« Decem. » Denarius crucem Christi designat quia X littera, qua denarius exprimitur, in figura crucis formatur.

« Dixit. » Sciebat enim nec unum ibi esse praeter 129DLot; ad exaggerandam iniquitatem hoc dicitur, sicut apud Jeremiam: « Videte si inveniatis hominem facientem justitiam (Jer. V); quasi: Invenite vel unum, et parco eis.

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « And the Lord appeared to him in the vale of Mambre. » (ISID. in Gen. ) Abraham bears a threefold figure: the first, of the Saviour, who leaving his kindred came into the world; the second, of the Father, because he offered up his only Son; the third (which appears in this place), of the saints who received the coming of Christ with joy 125D. The tabernacle of Abraham signifies Jerusalem, where in their season the prophets and the apostles dwelt, and Christ at his coming was received by them that believed, and by them that believed not was hanged upon the tree.

VERS. 2, 3.-- « Three men. » Three men announce the coming of Christ, whom two angels accompany, Moses according to some, and Elias; the one a lawgiver, who in the law showed the coming of the Lord: the other, who is to come at the end, to announce the second coming of Christ and to preach his Gospel. Whence, when the Lord was transfigured on the mountain, they are seen with him. « He saw three, and adored one, » showing namely the Saviour, whose coming he was awaiting. Whence: « Abraham saw my day and was 126Aglad (Joan. VIII). » [Vulg. Jo 8:56 Abraham pater vester exsultavit ut videret diem meum : vidit, et gavisus est, "Abraham your father rejoiced that he might see my day: he saw it, and was glad"] For he foresaw the mystery of the sacrament to come. Whence he washed their feet, that he might show the purification of the laver that was to be at the end of the world; for the feet signify the last things.

« Whom when he had seen. » (GREG., lib. IX Moral., c. 51.) Morally. Abraham ran to meet three angels, because the man and master of the house, that is, the spiritual understanding, ought in the knowledge of the Trinity to pass beyond the barriers of the flesh, and as it were to go out at the door of the lowest dwelling. Let the care of the flesh, which Sara signifies, not appear abroad for ostentation, but behind the man's back, that is, under the discretion of the spirit, intent upon necessary things alone, let it not know how to uncover itself shamelessly, but to govern itself modestly.

(Ibid., lib. XXVIII, c. 4). Abraham could not have seen the angels, received them into his lodging and set food before them, 126Bunless they had taken a body out of air: for they would not appear, they would not take food, unless they had a solid body of a heavenly element. Nor is it strange that now they are called angels, now the Lord, because they are called angels who minister without, and the Lord is shown, who commands them within.

VERS. 4, 5.-- « But I will fetch. » He returns to the offices of hospitality, which can be shown even to angels, when they take a bodily form out of the elements: which Abraham understanding, adored the Godhead in unity, and ministered to the angelic dispensation in visible form.

VERS. 6.-- « Three sata. » These things are said in the type of the Church, which out of the three parts of the world is gathered into the unity of faith, and as bread without leaven 126Cis baked by the fire of the Holy Ghost.

(STRAB.) A satum is a kind of measure. Three sata signify the three sons of Noah, out of whom the whole race of men was sown abroad. Therefore the Church is signified, or the evangelical doctrine that is to be sown throughout the whole world, and to be multiplied in the three parts of the world, Asia, Africa and Europe.

« Three sata, » of which Sara makes the loaves baked under the ashes, signify the three sons of Noah, out of whom the whole race of men was born: who, believing in the divine Trinity, were sprinkled with the water of baptism through Sara, that is, the Church, and were brought together into the one bread of the body of Christ. These are the three sata of meal which the woman in the Gospel leavened: they are unleavened loaves, because without the leaven of malice, and the anguish [ Al., rancore] 126Dof wickedness, and the fervour of perverse doctrine, the unity of believers ought to be.

« Baked under the ashes. » They are baked under the ashes, that being baked by the vapour of the Holy Ghost through repentance for their sins, they may be made food acceptable to God.

VERS. 7.-- « A calf most tender. » The tender and fatted calf is the body of Christ, which for the salvation of the world was offered up upon the tree of the cross. This is the calf that is killed for the prodigal son.

VERS. 8.-- « Butter and milk » he set before them with the flesh of the calf, because milk signifies the old law; whence I Cor. III: « Milk I gave you to drink, not meat, » etc. For he had delivered to them the commandment of the law, as milk pressed out of the breasts of the two tables, that is 127Athe testament of faith, because they could not yet take the solid meat of the evangelical doctrine. The most rich and fat butter is the evangelical testimony, which like oil is given to the faithful for a sign. He set the calf before them with milk and butter, because neither can the body of the Lord be without the milk of the law, nor this without the Gospel. And they sat under the tree in sign of the Lord's passion, whereof they are the preachers.

VERS. 9.-- « Behold she is in the tabernacle, » etc. (STRAB.) Abraham is without with the angels, Sara is in the tabernacle: because it belongs to the strong and the learned to preach openly, to instruct by examples: to the weak and the unwise, to learn and to be subject.

« VERS. 10.--And she shall have a son, » etc. God promises a son to the barren Sara, saying: About this time 127BI will come; he signifies not the time, but the quality of his coming, since through the son promised again the faithful people was to be born. Whence Isa. LIV and Gal. IV: « Rejoice, thou barren, that travailest not, » etc.

« Which when she had heard, etc. (AUG. Quaest in Gen. ) Sara is reproved because she laughed, and not Abraham, although he had laughed in like manner; because Abraham's laughter was of wonder, Sara's of doubt, which he could discern who knows the heart. Sara denies that she laughed, whereas she cannot be hidden from God, but perhaps she thought that they were men. But Abraham understood God, yet in rendering the offices of humanity, which are necessary only to weak flesh; it is strange: unless he first judged them men, but men in whom he understood God to speak, certain signs of the divine majesty appearing: as Scripture often testifies that God 127Cappeared in men, but afterward they knew that they had been angels.

VERS. 11.-- « And they were, » etc. The age of the elderly is less than that of the old, even though the old be called elderly. Whence, according to the physicians, an elderly man cannot get children of an elderly woman, even though the courses of women still flow. According to this Abraham wonders at the power of God concerning the promise of a son, and we can take it that the Apostle set down this miracle. For the body was not altogether dead for generation, if the woman had been of youthful age: but of one of advanced age he could not beget: for of Cethura he afterward begot, because she was young. For the physicians hand down that an old man cannot beget of a woman of more advanced age (even though the courses of women befall her), 127Dbut of a young one he can. Again a woman of advanced age, even though the courses of women still befall her, cannot bear by an elderly man, but by a young one she can. It was therefore a miracle, because both were old and of advanced age, and the courses of women had ceased for Sara.

VERS. 12-14.-- « Shall I give myself to pleasure? » etc. (GREG. lib. IX Moral., c. 51.) The care of the flesh, etc., as far as what else does our mind bring forth than joy?

VERS. 15.-- « I did not laugh, etc. (AMBR.) Or she therefore denied that she had laughed, because she was ignorant; she therefore laughed, because she prophesied.

VERS. 17-19.-- « Can I hide from Abraham what « I am about to do. » Well does the Lord commit his secrets to Abraham, 128Awhom he knew to be a faithful executor of his will and of his secrets; of whom he himself was to be born in the flesh, « in whom are all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge hidden (II Cor. II). » He promises also to Abraham not only rewards, but the justice of the obedience of his sons, and that the promises should be fulfilled concerning them. And again Isaac is promised, « about to be a great nation, » and that « in him shall all the nations of the earth be blessed. » [Vulg. Gn 18:18 benedicendae sint in illo omnes nationes terrae, "all the nations of the earth shall be blessed in him"] By which words two things are promised him: the Jewish nation according to the flesh, and all the Gentiles through faith.

VERS. 20.-- « The cry of Sodom and Gomorrha, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen. ) If we take these words as those not of one doubting, but of one angry and threatening, there is no question. For after the manner of men God speaks 128Bto men, and his anger is without disturbance, because he is unchangeable. But often in threatening we say thus: Let us see whether I do not do it to thee, and whether I shall not be able to do it to thee; or, let me know, that is, let me try whether I cannot. But disturbance does not fall upon God. It is the custom of human speech, to which God fits himself, that he may suit himself to human weakness.

(GREG. lib. V Moral., cap. 7.) But sin with a voice is a fault in action. But sin with a cry is a fault with liberty.

VERS. 21-23.-- « I will go down and see, » etc. (GREG., Moral., c. 33.) Let us not believe men's evil deeds before we prove them. And behold, through angels he came down to know their evils, and finding them wrapped in so great a crime 128C, he as it were passed over his patience, and forestalled them with the fire of judgment before the day of judgment: who judges with tranquillity, that the greater crimes may be slowly believed, and, when truly known, more swiftly punished.

(GREG., lib. I Reg., c. 178, tom. I.) Whither should God go down, that he might know what things had been done? Or what should he not know, who is everywhere? But that he might give to our ignorance an example of discretion, that we ought not to believe grievous evils on hearing them, he says of himself that he goes down to know, of whom it is plain to all that even without going down he would know all things.

(HILAR., lib. IX, de Trin.) We have God not knowing that which nevertheless he is not ignorant of. For since he knows the sins to be exceeding great, he goes down again that he may see whether they are consummated; and if they are not yet consummated 128D, that he may know: we understand that he does not then know them because he was ignorant before; but that he then knows, because it is the time for acting. Therefore for God not to know is not a diminution of ignorance, but the fulness of time.

VERS. 24-26.-- « If there be fifty just men, » etc. (ISID.) The number fifty, from which he descends down to ten, signifies penance. Whence also the fiftieth psalm is penitential. If therefore they had been converted, they would have been saved. But when God sees the offenders unwilling to be converted to penance, straightway he restrains the heat of immoderate luxury with the fire of hell. He said that up to ten just men Sodom should not perish, because if in any man the name of Christ be found through the keeping of the ten commandments, he shall not 129Aperish. For the figure of the number ten signifies also the cross of Christ.

VERS. 27.-- « I will speak to the Lord, » etc. (GREG. lib. XVIII Moral., c. 33.) Mystically. The more the saints look into the inward things of the Godhead, the more do they see themselves to be nothing. Nowhere is Abraham read to have professed himself ashes and dust, save when he deserved to have speech with God. And he said: « I will speak to my Lord, whereas I am dust, » etc. For perhaps he would have believed himself to be something, if he had not felt the true essence which is above him. Whence David also cries out: « Remember, Lord, that I am dust (Psal. X), » etc.

Morally. Some it lifts up not through the swelling of understanding, but through works of mercy; certain men, while they see themselves 129Bto abound in earthly things, do not seek the true riches, and love not the eternal kingdom, because they think temporal things sufficient. The fault therefore is not in the understanding, but in the affection. For all things which God founded are good. But he who uses good things ill, acts so that through the gluttony of voracity he dies by that bread whereby he ought to have lived. Lazarus the poor man had come to rest, torments were racking the proud rich man (Luc. XVI): yet Abraham had been rich, who held Lazarus in his bosom. Yet he says: « I will speak to my Lord, whereas I am dust and ashes, » etc. How did he value his riches, who accounted himself dust and ashes? Openly we discern in what place he had set himself, who accounted himself dust and ashes even while he spoke with God. With what punishment therefore are they to be struck, who 129Cmake no progress toward the highest things and exalt themselves over the least?

VERS. 28-33.-- « There be five. » Five cities were burned with showers of fire. For all who have handled the five senses of the body lustfully are to be burned in the fire to come.

« What if there be found, » etc. (ALC.) It is not necessary to take this of every place. But of Sodom it could be said, because God knew that there were not even ten there, and therefore it was thus answered to Abraham, to the aggravating of the iniquity of the Sodomites

« Ten. » The number ten designates the cross of Christ, because the letter X, by which ten is expressed, is formed in the figure of a cross.

« He said. » For he knew that there was not even one there besides 129DLot; this is said to the aggravating of their iniquity, as in Jeremias: « See if you find a man doing justice (Jer. V); [Vulg. Jr 5:1 an inveniatis virum facientem judicium, "whether you can find a man doing judgment"] as if to say: Find but one, and I spare them.

CAPUT XIX.

VERS. 1.-- « Veneruntque duo angeli, » etc. Notandum quod tres angeli numero impari Abrahae apparent, quia numero impari Deus gaudet. Sodomam venerunt pari numero, id est duo, qui numerus primus ab unitate recedit, et princeps est alteritatis, quasi femineam figurans mollitiem. Venerunt in « vespere, » quia jam Sodomitis occubuerat sol justitiae. « Qui cum vidisset, » etc. Dum occurrit Lot 130Aet adorat, angelos videtur intelligere; dum ad refectionem corporis invitat, homines videtur putare. Sic de his et de illis qui Abrahae apparuerunt idem intelligendum est. Ex hoc autem loco Scripturae et multis aliis commendatur virtus hospitalitatis. Unde Paulus Heb. XIII: « Hospitalitatem nolite oblivisci: per hanc enim placuerunt quidam Deo, angelis hospitio receptis. »

( Glossa Graeca. ) Notandum ubi beneficia Dei explicantur, Deum adesse; ubi vero supplicia exercentur, angelos mitti; ut sciamus Deo benefacere, jucundum et placitum Deo proprie esse ulcisci vero Deo ingratum esse.

VERS. 2.-- « Lavate. » Quod hospitibus solet fieri, ut affectus terreno pulvere sordidati, et acedia, et 130Bpusillanimitate, tanquam pulverulentis squaloribus, emundentur. Unde in monasteriis pro venientibus de via oratio celebratur.

VERS. 3-7.-- « Compulit illos. » (ALC.) Angeli quasi coacti domum Lot ingrediuntur, ut charitas ipsius tentata et probata remuneraretur, et ut ostenderetur quantum esset hospitalitatis bonum. Hospitalem domum ingressi sunt, et hospitem liberaverunt; clausas hospitibus domos ignis ingressus peccatores perdidit.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « Habeo duas filias. » (AUG., Q. in Gen.) Utrum admittenda sit compensatio flagitiorum, vel quorumlibet peccatorum, ut faciamus mala, ne fiant graviora: an perturbationi Lot, non sit consilio tribuendum, merito quaeritur? et nimirum 130Cpericulosissime haec compensatio admittitur. Si autem perturbatio mentis fuerit, nullo modo est imitanda.

VERS. 10.-- « Et introduxerunt ad se Lot. » (GREG., l. VI Moral., cap. 16.) Malis adversantibus intra domum Lot reducitur et munitur: quia quisque justus pravorum insidias sustinens, ad mentem revertitur, et manet interritus. Sodomitae in domo Lot ostium invenire nequeunt: quia corruptores mentium contra vitam justi nullum reprehensionis aditum inveniunt. Percussi caecitate quasi domum circumeunt, quia invidentes facta et dicta perscrutantur. Sed quia de vita justi, fortis et laudabilis actio ubique obviat, errantes parietem palpant.

VERS. 11-13.-- « Percusseruntque caecitate. » (AUG. 130Dubi supra.) Graece ἀορασία, id est, avidentia, si possit dici, quae faciat non videri, non omnia, sed quod non est opus. Quomodo enim deficerent quaerendo ostium, si caecitate percussi essent, ut nihil viderent? sua enim calamitate turbati, nihil ulterius requirerent. Hac ἀορασία percussi erant, qui Eliseum quaerebant, et qui Christum post resurrectionem cum ipso ambulantes non cognoscebant.

VERS. 14.-- « Egressus, » etc. (HIER., in Q. Hebr.) In sequentibus filiae Lot virgines fuisse leguntur; et ipse ad Sodomitas dixit: « Ecce duae filiae meae quae non cognoverunt virum, » etc. Nunc autem dicitur habuisse generos. Sed quidam arbitrantur eas quae viros habuerunt in Sodomis perisse, et quae virginet 131Aerant cum patre exisse. Sed Hebraica veritas habet. « Egressus est Lot, et locutus est ad sponsos qui accepturi erant filias ejus: » virgines ergo filiae necdum fuerant matrimonio copulatae.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « Cumque esset mane, » etc. (ISID.) Mystice. Lot frater Abraham, justus et hospitalis in Sodomis, significat corpus Christi, quod in omnibus sanctis inter impios gemit, quorum factis non consentit, et a quorum permixtione liberabitur in fine saeculi, illis damnatis supplicio-ignis aeterni

VERS. 17-19.-- « In monte. » (STRAB.) Moraliter. Mons altitudinem virtutum significat, ad quam angelus hortatur, secundum illud Marci XIX. « Si vis perfectus esse, vade et vende omnia quae habes, » etc. Qui vero viderit se non posse conscendere, melius 131Best ut in Segor, id est in parvula remaneat, contentus laicali et communi vita, quam montem virtutis arripiat et ad saecularia redeat. Virginitas altus mons est ad quam angelus hortatur: si quis videt se non posse conscendere, maneat in Segor, id est in legitimo conjugio, quia melius est mediocri bono uti, quam per abrupta libidinis praecipitari. Unde I Cor. VII: « Propter fornicationem unusquisque uxorem habeat. » « Nec possum in monte: » scilicet perturbatus non credebat Domino, quem in angelis agnoscebat, sicut perturbatus filias prostituebat.

VERS. 20.-- « Est civitas, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorice. Sodoma interpretatur caecitas, et exprimit mundana desideria: altitudo montium, speculatio perfectorum. Sed quia multi mundi illecebras fugiunt, tamen 131Cin actione positi contemplationis apicem subire nequeunt, exivit Lot a Sodomis, sed ad montana non pervenit. Unde Lot ait: « Est civitas haec juxta, » etc. « Juxta » dicitur, et tamen ad salutem tuta, quia activa vita nec a mundi curis ex toto discreta, nec a gaudio aeternae salutis est aliena.

« Ad quam possum, » etc. Qui ad contemplativam ascendere non sufficiunt, ad activam vitam confugiunt, in qua salvantur.

(GREG., Past. III, 28.) Moral. Ardentem Sodomam fugere, etc., uque ad unde ad Lot dicitur:

VERS. 21-23.-- « Ecce etiam in hoc suscepi preces tuas, » quia cum Deo oratio funditur, talis vita conjugii non damnatur; unde I Cor. VII: « Nolite 131Dfraudare invicem, » etc.

VERS. 24.-- « Igitur Dominus pluit super Sodomam, » etc. (GREG.) Sulphur, fetor carnis, ignis, ardor carnalis desiderii; cum Dominus carnis scelera punire decrevisset, qualitate ultionis innotuit macula criminis. Sulphur enim fetet, ignis ardet; qui ergo ad perversa desideria carnis fetore arserant, jure sulphure et igne perierunt, ut ex justa poena discerent quod injusto desiderio fecissent.

(STRAB.) Notandum quia primos peccatores aqua diluvii exterminavit, qui similiter offenderant Deum concupiscentia carnis. Sodomitae vero graviori supplicio, ignis scilicet et sulphuris diluvio, perierunt. Licet enim illi mensuram excederent, quasi naturaliter 132Apeccabant; isti vero contra naturam: ideo gravissimi supplicii exemplo damnati sunt.

VERS. 25.-- « Universos habitatores. » (ALC.) Infantes cum parentibus in Sodomis cremati sunt, ne posset adverti impiissimum facinus Sodomitarum, si de origine eorum aliquod remaneret vestigium. Et provisum est illis, ne diu viventes sequerentur exempla patrum et gravius punirentur. Parentes quoque tam pro se quam pro eis rei sunt. Mors enim filiorum crimen est parentum. Ideo futuri sunt accusatores eorum. Est etiam qualecunque beneficium, non reum esse, qui gloriosus non est; prodest enim pauperem non esse, qui rex esse non potest.

VERS. 26-28.-- « Respiciensque uxor ejus. » (ISID.) Mystice. Uxor, eorum genus figurat qui gratia vocati 132Bretro aspiciunt, et ad ea quae reliquerant redire contendunt; de quibus dicitur Luc. IX: « Nemo ponens manum in aratro, et respiciens retro, aptus est regno Dei. » Ideo prohibetur illis retro aspicere. In quo ostenditur non esse redeundum ad veterem vitam, his qui per gratiam sunt regenerati, si ultimum cupiunt evadere incendium; uxor retrospiciens in salem versa est, exemplum facta scilicet, et condimentum unde alii saliantur. Unde Christus ait Luc. XVII: « Mementote uxoris Lot, » ut nos scilicet tanquam sale condiret, ne negligentes essemus, sed prudenter caveremus.

VERS. 29.-- « Cum enim subverteret, » etc. Innuit Scriptura magis meritis Abrahae Deum liberasse Lot, ut intelligamus justum esse dictum, quodammodo, 132Cmaxime quia colebat unum verumque Deum, et comparatione Sodomorum, inter quos vivens ad simile scelus flecti non potuit. « Recordatus est Abrahae. » Patet meritis Abrahae Lot fuisse liberatum, quem Scriptura appellat justum. Et sciendum quia justus erat, non sicut Abraham, sed comparatione Sodomitarum.

VERS. 30.-- « Ascenditque, » etc. Sic conjugati de humilitate activae vitae aliquando ascendunt ad altitudinem contemplativae.

« Ascenditque Lot de Segor, » etc. (HIER. in Q. Hebr., tom. III.) Quaeritur cur cum primum montis fugae Segor praetulerit, etc., usque ad ambigere non debuit.

VERS. 31.-- « Dixitque major ad minorem, » etc. 132DQuod pro excusatione dicitur filiarum, quia genus humanum defecisse putaverunt, ideoque cum patre accubuerunt. Non autem excusat patrem quod sequitur, « et nescivit quod dormissent cum eo; » appungunt enim desuper Hebraei obelo, quasi superfluum, quasi incredibile sit, quia rerum natura non capiat coire quempiam nescientem.

(STRAB.) Lot inexcusabilis est, primo quia angelo non credidit; deinde quia inebriatus est, et fuit peccatum causa peccati.

(HIERON.) Sic prava suggestio et delectatio, ut incestae mulieres, insidiantur continentibus, ut accepta occasione, torpentes vino fallaciae imbuant, et a statu rectitudinis quasi ignorantes dejiciant.

133AVERS. 32.-- « Veni, inebriemus, » etc. (ALC.) Didicerant duae filiae Lot consummationem mundi futuram per ignem: sed, tanquam puellae non perfectae, neque sciebant quod, Sodomitis igne vastatis, multum spatium integrum remaneret; et suspicatae sunt tale aliquid factum, quale in temporibus Noe audierant, et ad reparandum genus humanum superstites se esse cum patre. Et quamvis grande crimen concubitus patris, gravior videbatur impietas, si servata castitate spem posteritatis delerent. Ideo consilium ineunt, et patris moestitiam vel rigorem vino molliunt, et singulis singulae noctibus suscipiunt ab ignorante conceptum, nec repetunt.

VERS. 33-35.-- « Dederunt, » etc. (ISID.) Lot in hoc loco legem significat, quia quidam ex illa procreati, 133Bet sub illa positi, male intelligendo quodam modo se inebriant; eamque non legitime utendo, infidelitatis opera pariunt. Bona est enim lex, si quis ea legitime utatur.

VERS. 36.-- « Conceperunt ergo, » etc. (HIER.) Moab ( ) interpretatur ex patre, et totum nomen etymologiam habet. Ammon vero, cujus quasi causa nominis redditur, filius generis mei, sive, ut melius est in Hebraeo, benammi ( ) id est filius populi mei, sic derivatur, ut ex parte sensus nominis, ex parte ipse sit sermo. Ammon enim, a quo Ammonitae, vocatur populus meus.

VERS. 37, 38. « Vocavit nomen ejus Moab, » etc. (THEOD.) Videtur haec impudentior, quae suum incestum ad posteros quoque transmittere voluit. Moab 133Cenim de patre significat: altera verecundior, quae filio nomen indidit, Benammi, id est filius populi mei.

CHAPTER XIX.

VERS. 1.-- « And there came two angels, » etc. It is to be noted that three angels appear to Abraham in an odd number, because God rejoices in an odd number. To Sodom they came in an even number, that is, two, which number is the first that departs from unity, and is the chief of otherness, figuring as it were a womanish softness. They came in the « evening, » because the sun of justice had already set for the Sodomites. « Who when he had seen, » etc. While Lot runs to meet them 130Aand adores, he seems to understand that they are angels; while he invites them to the refreshing of the body, he seems to think that they are men. So of these and of those who appeared to Abraham the same is to be understood. And from this place of Scripture and from many others the virtue of hospitality is commended. Whence Paul, Heb. XIII: « Forget not hospitality: for by this some have pleased God, having received angels to their lodging. » [Vulg. Hbr 13:2 per hanc enim latuerunt quidam, angelis hospitio receptis, "for by this some have entertained angels unawares"]

( Glossa Graeca. ) It is to be noted that where the benefits of God are set forth, God is present; but where punishments are executed, angels are sent; that we may know that to do good is proper to God, delightful and pleasing to God, but that to take vengeance is unwelcome to God.

VERS. 2.-- « Wash. » Which is wont to be done for guests, that the affections, soiled with earthly dust, and with sloth, and 130Bwith faint-heartedness, as with dusty filth, may be cleansed. Whence in monasteries a prayer is celebrated for those who come from the road.

VERS. 3-7.-- « He pressed them. » (ALC.) The angels as though compelled enter the house of Lot, that his charity, being tried and approved, might be rewarded, and that it might be shown how great a good hospitality is. They entered a hospitable house, and set the host free; the fire, entering the houses that were shut to guests, destroyed the sinners.

VERS. 8, 9.-- « I have two daughters. » (AUG., Q. in Gen.) It is deservedly asked: is a balancing of outrages, or of any sins whatever, to be admitted, that we may do evils lest worse be done: or is it to be attributed to Lot's disturbance, and not to his counsel? and doubtless 130Cthis balancing is admitted most perilously. But if it was a disturbance of mind, it is in no wise to be imitated.

VERS. 10.-- « And they brought in Lot to them. » (GREG., l. VI Moral., cap. 16.) While the wicked oppose him, Lot is brought back within the house and fortified: because every just man, bearing the snares of the depraved, returns to his mind, and remains undismayed. The Sodomites cannot find the door in the house of Lot: because the corrupters of minds find no entrance of reproof against the life of the just man. Struck with blindness they go round the house as it were, because in their envy they scrutinize his deeds and words. But because on every side, out of the just man's life, a strong and praiseworthy action meets them, they wander and grope at the wall.

VERS. 11-13.-- « And they struck them with blindness. » (AUG. 130Dubi supra.) In Greek ἀορασία, that is, avidentia, if it may be so called, which causes that a thing be not seen, not all things, but that which is not needful. For how would they fail in seeking the door, if they had been struck with blindness so that they saw nothing? for troubled by their own calamity they would have sought nothing further. With this ἀορασία were they struck who sought Eliseus, and who, walking with Christ after the resurrection, did not know him.

VERS. 14.-- « He went out, » etc. (HIER., in Q. Hebr.) In what follows the daughters of Lot are read to have been virgins; and he himself said to the Sodomites: « Behold I have two daughters who have not known man, » [Vulg. Gn 19:8 Habeo duas filias, quae necdum cognoverunt virum, "I have two daughters who have not yet known man"] etc. But now he is said to have had sons-in-law. But some think that those who had husbands perished in Sodom, and that those who were virgins 131Awent out with their father. But the Hebrew verity has. « Lot went out, and spoke to the spouses who were to receive his daughters: » therefore his daughters, being virgins, had not yet been joined in marriage.

VERS. 15, 16.-- « And when it was morning, » etc. (ISID.) Mystically. Lot, the brother of Abraham, just and hospitable in Sodom, signifies the body of Christ, which in all the saints groans among the wicked, to whose deeds it does not consent, and from whose commingling it shall be set free at the end of the world, when they are condemned to the punishment of eternal fire

VERS. 17-19.-- « In the mountain. » (STRAB.) Morally. The mountain signifies the height of the virtues, to which the angel exhorts, according to that of Marci XIX. « If thou wilt be perfect, go and sell all that thou hast, » [Vulg. Mt 19:21 vade, vende quae habes, et da pauperibus, "go, sell what thou hast, and give to the poor"] etc. But he who shall see that he cannot climb up, it is better 131Bthat he remain in Segor, that is, in the little one, content with a lay and common life, than that he seize upon the mountain of virtue and return to secular things. Virginity is a high mountain, to which the angel exhorts: if any man sees that he cannot climb up, let him remain in Segor, that is, in lawful wedlock, because it is better to use a middling good than to be hurled down the precipices of lust. Whence I Cor. VII: « Let every man have a wife because of fornication. » « Neither can I in the mountain: » that is, being disturbed he did not believe the Lord, whom he recognized in the angels, as being disturbed he prostituted his daughters.

VERS. 20.-- « There is a city, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorically. Sodom is interpreted blindness, and expresses worldly desires: the height of the mountains, the contemplation of the perfect. But because many flee the enticements of the world, and yet 131Cbeing set in action cannot reach the summit of contemplation, Lot went out from Sodom, but did not come to the mountains. Whence Lot says: « There is this city near, » etc. It is called « Near, » and yet it is safe for salvation, because the active life is neither wholly severed from the cares of the world, nor a stranger to the joy of eternal salvation.

« To which I can, » etc. Those who are not able to ascend to the contemplative life flee to the active life, in which they are saved.

(GREG., Past. III, 28.) Morally. To flee burning Sodom, etc., as far as whence it is said to Lot:

VERS. 21-23.-- « Behold also in this I have heard thy prayers, » because when prayer is poured out to God, such a life of wedlock is not condemned; whence I Cor. VII: « Do not 131Ddefraud one another, » etc.

VERS. 24.-- « And the Lord rained upon Sodom, » etc. (GREG.) Brimstone, the stench of the flesh, fire, the burning of carnal desire; when the Lord had determined to punish the crimes of the flesh, by the quality of the vengeance the stain of the crime was made known. For brimstone stinks, fire burns; they therefore who had burned unto perverse desires with the stench of the flesh, perished justly by brimstone and fire, that from a just punishment they might learn what they had done by unjust desire.

(STRAB.) It is to be noted that the water of the flood destroyed the first sinners, who in like manner had offended God by the concupiscence of the flesh. But the Sodomites perished by a heavier punishment, namely by a flood of fire and brimstone. For although those exceeded the measure, they sinned as it were naturally 132A; but these against nature: therefore they were condemned by the example of the heaviest punishment.

VERS. 25.-- « All the inhabitants. » (ALC.) The infants were burned with their parents in Sodom, lest the most impious deed of the Sodomites could be turned aside, if any trace of their stock remained. And it was provided for them, lest living long they should follow the examples of their fathers and be punished more grievously. The parents also are guilty as much for themselves as for them. For the death of the sons is the crime of the parents. Therefore they shall be their accusers. It is also some kind of benefit, for him who is not glorious, not to be guilty; for it profits him who cannot be a king not to be poor.

VERS. 26-28.-- « And his wife looking back. » (ISID.) Mystically. The wife figures the kind of those who, being called by grace 132B, look back, and strive to return to the things they had left; of whom it is said, Luc. IX: « No man putting his hand on the plough, and looking back, is fit for the kingdom of God. » Therefore it is forbidden them to look back. Wherein it is shown that there is no returning to the old life for those who have been regenerated through grace, if they desire to escape the last burning; the wife looking back was turned into salt, being made namely an example, and a seasoning whereby others may be salted. Whence Christ says, Luc. XVII: « Remember the wife of Lot, » that he might season us as it were with salt, that we should not be negligent, but should beware prudently.

VERS. 29.-- « For when he overthrew, » etc. Scripture intimates that God delivered Lot rather by the merits of Abraham, that we may understand that he was called just in a certain manner, 132Cchiefly because he worshipped the one and true God, and by comparison with the Sodomites, among whom living he could not be bent to the like crime. « He remembered Abraham. » It is plain that Lot was delivered by the merits of Abraham, whom Scripture calls just. And it is to be known that he was just, not as Abraham was, but by comparison with the Sodomites.

VERS. 30.-- « And he went up, » etc. So the married sometimes ascend from the lowliness of the active life to the height of the contemplative.

« And Lot went up from Segor, » etc. (HIER. in Q. Hebr., tom. III.) It is asked why, when at first he preferred Segor to flight into the mountain, etc., as far as he ought not to have doubted.

VERS. 31.-- « And the elder said to the younger, » etc. 132DWhich is said in excuse of the daughters, because they thought that the human race had failed, and therefore lay with their father. But that which follows does not excuse the father, « and he knew not that they had slept with him; » for the Hebrews mark it above with an obelus, as superfluous, as though it were incredible, because the nature of things does not admit that anyone should have intercourse unknowing.

(STRAB.) Lot is inexcusable, first because he did not believe the angel; then because he was made drunk, and sin was the cause of sin.

(HIERON.) So depraved suggestion and delight, like incestuous women, lie in wait for the continent, that, having taken occasion, they may steep with the wine of deceit those who are torpid, and cast them down from the state of uprightness as though unwitting.

133AVERS. 32.-- « Come, let us make him drunk, » etc. (ALC.) The two daughters of Lot had learned that the consummation of the world was to be by fire: but, as girls not perfect, neither did they know that, when the Sodomites had been laid waste by fire, much space remained entire; and they supposed that some such thing had been done as they had heard of in the times of Noah, and that they were left surviving with their father for the repairing of the human race. And although the lying with their father was a great crime, a heavier impiety seemed if, keeping their chastity, they should destroy the hope of posterity.
