Therefore they take counsel, and soften their father's sadness or sternness with wine, and each on her several night receives conception from him without his knowledge, and they do not repeat it.

VERS. 33-35.-- « They gave, » etc. (ISID.) Lot in this place signifies the law, because certain men begotten of her, 133Band set under her, by understanding ill do in a manner make themselves drunk; and by using her not lawfully, they bring forth the works of unbelief. For the law is good, if a man use it lawfully.

VERS. 36.-- « They conceived therefore, » etc. (HIER.) Moab ( ) is interpreted from the father, and the whole name has its etymology. But Ammon, of whom as it were the reason of the name is rendered, son of my kindred, or, as it is better in the Hebrew, benammi ( ) that is son of my people, is so derived that in part it is the sense of the name, in part the word itself. For Ammon, from whom the Ammonites, is called my people.

VERS. 37, 38. « She called his name Moab, » etc. (THEOD.) This one seems the more shameless, who wished to transmit her incest even to posterity. For Moab 133Csignifies from the father: the other the more modest, who gave her son the name Benammi, that is son of my people.

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 1.-- « Profectus inde, » etc. Cades, fons judicii; Sur, angustia; quibus figuratur patientia.

VERS. 2-6.-- « Dixitque de Sara, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeri solet quomodo in illa aetate Abraham pro Sarae pulchritudine periclitari timuerit: sed formae illius vis miranda est, quae adhuc amari poterat.

« Dixitque de Sara, » etc. (ISID., in Gen., tom. VI.) Tacuit uxorem, dixit sororem, ne ipse occideretur, et illa captiva ab alienigenis possideretur, certus quod a Deo inviolata servaretur, sicut apud Pharaonem, 133Detc., usque ad « Nolite vocare patrem super terram, unus est enim Pater vester qui in coelis est. »

« Dixitque, » etc. (AUG.) Quod dicitur Abimelech pro Sara, « Et peperci tibi ne peccares in me, » advertendum est Deum fecisse, quando admonuit uxorem esse Abrahae, quam putabat sororem. Et notandum quod in Deum peccatur, cum talia committuntur quae putant homines vel non esse peccata, vel levia haberi tanquam in carne peccata.

VERS. 7-11.-- « Orabit pro te, » etc. (CHRYSOST., hom. 45 in Gen.) Scias autem quod illius preces tibi vitam praebebunt.

VERS. 12.-- « Alias autem, » etc. (HIERON.) Quoniam 134Ain Hebraeo habetur vegam ( ) id est, etiam vere « soror mea est, filia patris mei, sed non filia matris meae, » et sonat magis quod soror Abrahae fuerit. In excusationem dicimus nondum tales nuptias lege prohibitas.

« Filia patris. » Alia translatio, apertius, « a patre, et non ex matre; » erat enim filia Aran fratris Abrahae, soror Lot.

VERS. 13.-- « Postquam autem eduxit me Deus de domo patris mei, dixi ad eam, » etc. Postquam sermo Dei repulsus est a Judaea, omnes fideles sunt Christi fratres et sorores; unde Matth. XII: « Quicunque fecerit voluntatem Patris mei, qui in coelis est, ipse frater meus, et soror, et mater. »

134BVERS. 14.-- « Tulit igitur. » Allegorice Abraham significat Christum, Sara Ecclesiam. Abraham honoratur propter Sarae pulchritudinem, et Christus propter Ecclesiam; unde psal. LXVII: « Mirabilis Deus in sanctis suis. » Habebat Sara virum, sed latebat; habet Ecclesia Christum qui in saeculo non videtur, in coelo receptus.

VERS. 15.-- « Ubicunque tibi, » etc. Qui aliquando terrore principum credere distulerant, eisdem jubentibus ad Ecclesiam, quae Christi soror et uxor est, sese contulerunt, et per omnes mundi partes gloriam Christi et Ecclesiae dilataverunt.

VERS. 16.-- « Mille argenteos, » etc. Quasi jocando loquitur: quia illa dixit fratrem suum esse qui 134Cmaritus erat, et hortatur ut in posterum hoc meminerit, ne deinceps simile improperium deprehensa incurrat. Mille argentei dantur Christo, cum eloquentes doctores mundi de gentili conversatione ad fidem Christi tenendam et praedicandam assumuntur. Millenarius enim ex denario quadratus, stabilem et solidam designat evangelicae praedicationis doctrinam.

VERS. 17.-- « Orante autem. » Orante Filio Patrem, sanatur infecunditas mundanorum, et principum, et subditorum populorum.

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 1.-- « Setting forward thence, » etc. Cades, the fountain of judgment; Sur, narrowness; by which patience is figured.

VERS. 2-6.-- « And he said of Sara, » etc. (AUG.) It is commonly asked how at that age Abraham could fear peril on account of Sara's beauty: but the force of her form is to be wondered at, which could still be loved.

« And he said of Sara, » etc. (ISID., in Gen., tom. VI.) He was silent that she was his wife, and said she was his sister, lest he himself should be killed and she be possessed as a captive by strangers, being sure that she would be kept inviolate by God, as at Pharaoh's court, 133Detc., as far as « Call not [any man] father upon earth, for one is your Father who is in heaven. »

« And he said, » etc. (AUG.) As to what is said to Abimelech on Sara's behalf, « And I spared thee, that thou shouldst not sin against me, » [Vulg. custodivi te, "I kept thee"] it is to be observed that God did this when he warned him that she whom he thought a sister was Abraham's wife. And it is to be noted that sin is committed against God when such things are done as men think either not to be sins, or to be reckoned light, as being sins in the flesh.

VERS. 7-11.-- « He shall pray for thee, » etc. (CHRYSOST., hom. 45 in Gen.) But know thou that his prayers shall grant thee life.

VERS. 12.-- « Otherwise also, » etc. (HIERON.) Since 134Ain the Hebrew there stands vegam ( ) that is, also truly « she is my sister, the daughter of my father, but not the daughter of my mother, » and it sounds rather that she was Abraham's sister. In excuse we say that such marriages were not yet forbidden by law.

« The daughter of my father. » Another translation, more plainly, « from a father, and not from a mother; » for she was the daughter of Aran, Abraham's brother, and Lot's sister.

VERS. 13.-- « But after God brought me out of my father's house, I said to her, » etc. After the word of God was rejected from Judaea, all the faithful are Christ's brothers and sisters; whence Matth. XII: « Whosoever shall do the will of my Father, who is in heaven, he is my brother, and sister, and mother. »

134BVERS. 14.-- « He took therefore. » Allegorically Abraham signifies Christ, Sara the Church. Abraham is honored on account of Sara's beauty, and Christ on account of the Church; whence psal. LXVII: « God is wonderful in his saints. » Sara had a husband, but he lay hidden; the Church has Christ, who is not seen in the world, being received into heaven.

VERS. 15.-- « Wheresoever it shall please thee, » etc. They who had once put off believing through fear of princes, at the bidding of those same princes betook themselves to the Church, which is Christ's sister and wife, and spread abroad the glory of Christ and of the Church through all the parts of the world.

VERS. 16.-- « A thousand pieces of silver, » etc. He speaks as though in jest: because she called him her brother who 134Cwas her husband, and he exhorts her to remember this hereafter, lest she afterwards, being caught, incur a like reproach. A thousand pieces of silver are given to Christ when eloquent teachers of the world are taken up out of Gentile converse to hold and to preach the faith of Christ. For the number of a thousand, squared from the number ten, designates the stable and solid doctrine of evangelical preaching.

VERS. 17.-- « And when he prayed. » When the Son prays the Father, the barrenness of worldly men, and of princes, and of subject peoples is healed.

CAPUT XXI.

VERS. 1-3.-- « Visitavit autem Deus Saram, » etc. Circumcisionis figura multiformis est. Signaculum 134Denim est secundum Apostolum justitiae fidei, Abrahae et semini ejus, et judicium castigandi eos qui ad hoc semen et fidem pertinerent; et prophetia nascitur de hoc semine Salvatoris, qui nos in praesenti per baptismum ab omni peccato mundaret, et in futuro post resurrectionem ab omni mortis corruptione liberaret. Octonarius autem resurrectionis gloriae convenit: Dominus enim octavo die, id est post septimam sabbati resurrexit; et nos post sex aetates hujus saeculi, et septimam sabbati, id est, quietem animarum, quae interim in alia vita geritur, quasi octavo tempore resurgemus, ab omnibus carnalibus concupiscentiae corruptionibus et vitiis (in quibus luxuria maxime regnat) exspoliati.

135AVERS. 4.-- « Circumcidit » etc. (STRAB.) Circumcisio cum ob multas causas praecepta sit, specialiter figuram Christi praetendit, qui nos per baptismum veteri homine exuit, et in futuro post resurrectionem circumcidet omnem mortalitatem et corruptionem. Quod autem octavo die circumcisus est Isaac, designatur octava saeculi aetas, quando generaliter resurrectio sanctorum celebrabitur.

VERS. 8.-- « Crevit igitur puer, » etc. Crescit, qui ascensiones in corde disponens, ad solidum doctrinae cibum pervenit. Unde cresce, et manducabis me.

« Crevit, » etc. (AUG.) Nec in die nativitatis, nec in die circumcisionis filii sui, sed in die ablactationis convivium fecit: quia debet magnum esse gaudium 135Bspiritualis aetatis, quando talis factus est homo qui pascatur solido cibo, eis simile quibus dicitur I Cor. III: « Lac vobis potum dedi, non escam. »

VERS. 9.-- « Cumque vidisset Sara. » etc. (HIER.) Dupliciter hoc exponitur: vel quod idolis ludos fecerit, juxta illud Exod. XXXII: « Sedit populus manducare et bibere, et surrexerunt ludere. » Vel quod adversum Isaac, quasi majoris aetatis loco, sibi primogenita vindicaverit; quod Sara audiens non tulit.

VERS. 10.-- « Ejice ancillam hanc, » etc. (BED., c. 21; ISID., c. 17, in Gen.) Quaeritur cur antea Sara voluit de ancilla filium suscipere, aut cur cum matre 135Ceum nunc jubet expelli? Quod non facit zelo accensa, sed prophetiae mysterio compulsa. Agar enim, secundum Apostolum, in servitutem genuit carnalem populum; Sara vero spiritualem et liberum, qua libertate Christus nos liberavit. Significabatur ergo hoc mysterio, priorem populum in servitute generatum non manere in domo Sarae, id est Ecclesiae, in aeternum, nec esse haeredem vel consortem Christianorum, nec cum filio nobili, id est populo fideli, regnum gloriae possidere.

VERS. 11, 12.-- « Dure accepit, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeritur quare contristatus sit Abraham, cum haec prophetia fuerit, quam ipse magis quam Sara noscere debuit: sed ex revelatione hoc dixit Sara, quod sibi prius fuerat revelatum. Ille vero paterno affectu 135Dcommotus est, cui Dominus postea revelavit. Vel forte ambo prius nescierunt quid esset, et per Saram nescientem prophetizatum est cum muliebri animo mota est propter ancillae superbiam.

VERS. 13.-- « Quia semen tuum. » (AUG.) Notandum quod et Ismael a Deo semen Abrahae dictus est, quod Apostolus ait Rom. IX: « Non filii carnis, sed filii promissionis aestimabuntur in semine. » Sed hoc proprie ad Isaac pertinet, qui non filius carnis, sed promissionis, et semen spirituale ex promissione est, non ex carne.

VERS. 14.-- « Tollens panem. » etc. (ALC. EX BEDA, c. 21.) Cum ergo Abraham ejiceret Agar, tollens panem, ideo humeris imposuit, etc. usque ad 136Aquia quidam de populo sub umbra ligni crucis refugium petituri sunt.

« Sumpsit panem et utrem aquae. » (AUG. Q. in Gen. tom. III.) Quaeritur quomodo imposuit in humeris puerum tam grandem? Nam qui fuerat antequam esset natus Isaac, tredecim annorum circumcisus, cum esset Abraham nonaginta novem, et natus sit Isaac centenario patre; ludebat autem Ismael cum Isaac quando Sara commota est, utique cum puero grandiusculo, qui jam fuerat ablactatus: profecto plus quam sedecim annorum fuit quando cum matre expulsus est. Sed ut hoc quod cum parvulo lusit, per recapitulationem dictum accipiamus, antequam ablactaretur Isaac; etiam sic amplius quam tredecim annorum puer humeris matris non potuit imponi; 136Bsed subaudiendum est, dedit puerum suum, etc.

« Imposuit scapulae ejus. » (HIER. in Q. Hebr.) Quando Isaac natus est, tredecim annorum erat Ismael, et post ablactationem ejus cum matre expellitur. Apud Hebraeos varia opinio est, aliis asserentibus quinto anno ablactationis tempus ejus statutum, aliis duodecimo. Nos igitur, ut breviorem eligamus aetatem, post decem et octo annos supputamus Ismaelem ejectum cum matre, nec convenire jam adolescentem matris sedisse cervicibus. Verum est autem illud Hebraeae linguae idioma, quod omnis filius ad comparationem parentum infans vocetur et parvulus. Romae quoque usque hodie omnes filii infantes vocantur.

VERS. 15-21.-- « Abjecit puerum. » etc. (HIER.) 136CQuod sequitur: « Et projecit puerum subter unam abietem, et abiens sedit contra longe quasi jactus sagittae. » Dixit enim: Non videbo mortem parvuli mei, et sedit contra eum; et statim jungitur. Exclamavitque puer et flevit; et audivit Dominus vocem pueri de coelo, ubi erat, et dixit angelus ad Agar: Nullum moveat. In Hebraeo enim prius hoc scriptum est: « Non videbo mortem pueri mei. » Ita consequenter legitur, quod Agar sederit contra puerum, et levaverit vocem, et fleverit, et exaudierit Deus vocem parvuli. Flente enim matre et mortem filii praestolante, Deus exaudivit puerum, quem pollicitus erat futurum in gentem magnam. Mater enim non suam mortem, sed filii deplorat.

(AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Quaeritur quomodo 136Dmater, etc., usque ad sicut saepe faciunt cujuslibet aetatis ambulantes.

VERS. 17.-- « Exaudivit autem. » (BED. et ISID. ubi supra.) Puer exclamat plorans, et Deus exaudivit; et monstrante angelo, illa fontem aspicit, et pro illis dicitur qui ad Christum convertuntur, et deflentes peccata exaudiuntur, apertis oculis cordis vident fontem aquae, id est, Christum, qui ait Joan. VII « Qui sitit, veniat ad me et bibat. » Angelus Eliam significat, per quem populus crediturus est. Unde Malach. IV: « Ecce ego mittam vobis Eliam, qui convertat corda patrum in filios. »

« Angelus Domini. » (ISID.) Quod eum qui loquitur ad Agar, prius angelum, postea Dominum Scriptura 137Apronuntiat, Filium Dei fuisse credendum est, qui per legem et prophetas locutus est, qui per obedientiam paternae voluntatis angelus vocatur, secundum naturam Deus.

VERS. 22.-- « Eodem tempore. » (AUG. ubi supra.) Quaeri potest quando cum Abimelech pactum fecit Abraham, etc., usque ad quem illa nesciret.

VERS. 23-30.-- « Jura ergo, » etc. Saepe qui foris sunt, fidelium conversatione inducti eisdem desiderant sociari, et eorum exemplis ad melius informari, ut et sibi consulant et proximorum saluti.

VERS. 31, 32.-- « Idcirco vocatus est, » etc. Duplex causa est cur appellatus sit in Hebraeo Bersabee: vel quia septem agnas accepit Abimelech de manu 137BAbrahae. Septem enim dicuntur sabee, vel quia ibi juraverunt, sabaa enim significat juramentum. Si autem hanc causam supra hoc nomen legimus, per prolepsim dictum est; sicut Bethel, et Galgala, quae, antequam haberent haec vocabula, aliter vocabantur. Notandum ex prioribus et praesenti loco quod Isaac non sit natus ad quercum Mambre. ( Alon Hebraice Latine quercus ) ut in Hebraeo habetur, sed in Geraris, ubi Bersabee usque hodie oppidum est, quae provincia ante non grande tempus ex divisione praesidum Palaestinae Salutaris dicta est. Unde habitavit Abraham in terra Palaestinorum diebus multis.

VERS. 33, 34.-- « Abraham vero, » etc. (AUG.) Abraham (sicut Stephanus dicit) non acceperat haereditatem 137Cibi, nec spatium pedis. Sed intelligenda est haereditas, quam Deus gratis daturus erat, non pretio empta. Intelligitur autem spatium circa puteum ad illud emptionis spatium quo fuerant septem agnae datae, quando Abimelech et Abraham sibi juraverunt.

CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1-3.-- « And God visited Sara, » [Vulg. Dominus, "the Lord"] etc. The figure of circumcision is manifold. For it is 134Daccording to the Apostle a seal of the justice of faith, to Abraham and to his seed, and a judgment for chastising those who should belong to this seed and faith; and there arises from this seed a prophecy of the Saviour, who should cleanse us in the present life from all sin through baptism, and in the time to come, after the resurrection, free us from all corruption of death. And the number eight befits the glory of the resurrection: for the Lord rose on the eighth day, that is, after the seventh, the sabbath; and we, after the six ages of this world and the seventh, the sabbath, that is, the rest of souls which meanwhile is passed in the other life, shall rise again as at an eighth season, stripped of all the carnal corruptions and vices of concupiscence (in which lust chiefly reigns).

135AVERS. 4.-- « He circumcised » etc. (STRAB.) Although circumcision was enjoined for many causes, it holds forth in a special way the figure of Christ, who through baptism strips us of the old man, and in the time to come, after the resurrection, will circumcise away all mortality and corruption. And in that Isaac was circumcised on the eighth day, the eighth age of the world is designated, when the resurrection of the saints shall be generally celebrated.

VERS. 8.-- « The child grew therefore, » etc. He grows who, disposing ascents in his heart, comes to the solid food of doctrine. Whence, grow, and thou shalt eat me.

« He grew, » etc. (AUG.) Not on the day of his son's birth, nor on the day of his circumcision, but on the day of his weaning did he make a feast: because great ought to be the joy 135Bof the spiritual age, when a man has been made such as may be fed with solid food, like to those to whom it is said I Cor. III: « I gave you milk to drink, not meat. »

VERS. 9.-- « And when Sara had seen. » etc. (HIER.) This is expounded in two ways: either that he made sport for idols, according to that of Exod. XXXII: « The people sat down to eat and to drink, and they rose up to play. » Or that against Isaac, as standing in the place of the elder in age, he claimed the birthright for himself; which Sara hearing did not bear.

VERS. 10.-- « Cast out this bondwoman, » etc. (BED., c. 21; ISID., c. 17, in Gen.) It is asked: why did Sara before wish to receive a son by the bondwoman, or why does she now bid him be cast out 135Cwith his mother? Which she does not do inflamed with jealousy, but compelled by the mystery of prophecy. For Agar, according to the Apostle, brought forth the carnal people unto bondage; but Sara the spiritual and free people, with which freedom Christ has set us free. It was signified therefore by this mystery that the former people, begotten in bondage, does not remain in the house of Sara, that is, of the Church, for ever, nor is heir or partner of Christians, nor possesses the kingdom of glory together with the noble son, that is, the faithful people.

VERS. 11, 12.-- « He took it hard, » etc. (AUG.) It is asked why Abraham was made sorrowful, since this was a prophecy which he rather than Sara ought to have known: but Sara said from revelation what had first been revealed to her. He however was moved 135Dby a father's affection, to whom the Lord afterwards revealed it. Or perhaps both of them at first knew not what it was, and it was prophesied through Sara who knew it not, when she was moved with a woman's mind on account of the bondwoman's pride.

VERS. 13.-- « For thy seed. » (AUG.) It is to be noted that Ismael also was called by God the seed of Abraham, whereas the Apostle says Rom. IX: « Not the children of the flesh, but the children of the promise shall be counted in the seed. » But this belongs properly to Isaac, who is not the son of the flesh, but of the promise, and is spiritual seed from the promise, not from the flesh.

VERS. 14.-- « Taking bread. » etc. (ALC. EX BEDA, c. 21.) When therefore Abraham cast out Agar, taking bread, he laid it upon her shoulders for this cause, etc. as far as 136Abecause certain of the people are to seek refuge under the shadow of the wood of the cross.

« He took bread and a bottle of water. » [Vulg. tollens, "taking" — the participle Migne's own lemma prints above] (AUG. Q. in Gen. tom. III.) It is asked: how did he lay upon her shoulders a boy so great? For he who, before Isaac was born, had been circumcised at thirteen years old, when Abraham was ninety-nine, and Isaac was born when his father was a hundred; and Ismael was playing with Isaac when Sara was moved, doubtless with a boy somewhat grown, who had already been weaned: assuredly he was more than sixteen years old when he was driven out with his mother. But granting that we take what is said of his playing with the little one as said by recapitulation, before Isaac was weaned; even so a boy of more than thirteen years could not be laid upon his mother's shoulders; 136Bbut it must be supplied, he gave over her boy, etc.

« He laid it on her shoulder. » (HIER. in Q. Hebr.) When Isaac was born, Ismael was thirteen years old, and after his weaning he is driven out with his mother. Among the Hebrews the opinion varies, some asserting the time of his weaning to have been appointed at the fifth year, others at the twelfth. We therefore, that we may choose the shorter age, reckon Ismael to have been cast out with his mother after eighteen years, and that it did not befit one already a youth to have sat upon his mother's neck. But that idiom of the Hebrew tongue is true, that every son is called an infant and a little one in comparison with his parents. At Rome too, even to this day, all sons are called infants.

VERS. 15-21.-- « She cast the boy. » etc. (HIER.) 136CThat which follows: « And she cast the boy under one fir tree, and going away sat over against him afar off, as it were a bowshot. » [Vulg. abjecit puerum subter unam arborum … seditque e regione procul quantum potest arcus jacere, "under one of the trees" … "as far as a bow can shoot"] For she said: I shall not see the death of my little one, and she sat over against him; and at once it is joined. And the boy cried out and wept; and the Lord heard the voice of the boy from heaven, where he was, and the angel said to Agar: Let nothing move. For in the Hebrew this is written first: « I shall not see the death of my boy. » So it is read consequently, that Agar sat over against the boy, and lifted up her voice, and wept, and God heard the voice of the little one. For while the mother wept and awaited the death of her son, God heard the boy, whom he had promised should be for a great nation. For the mother bewails not her own death, but her son's.

(AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) It is asked how 136Dthe mother, etc., as far as as those walking, of whatever age, often do.

VERS. 17.-- « And he heard. » (BED. et ISID. ubi supra.) The boy cries out weeping, and God heard him; and, an angel showing it, she beholds the fountain, and it is said for those who are converted to Christ, and who, weeping for their sins, are heard, and with the eyes of the heart opened see the fountain of water, that is, Christ, who says Joan. VII « He that thirsteth, let him come to me and drink. » [Vulg. Si quis sitit, "If any man thirst"] The angel signifies Elias, through whom the people is to believe. Whence Malach. IV: « Behold I will send you Elias, who shall turn the hearts of the fathers unto the children. » [Vulg. Mal. 4:5-6, two verses joined: Eliam prophetam, antequam veniat dies Domini … Et convertet cor patrum ad filios]

« The angel of the Lord. » [Vulg. angelus Dei, "the angel of God"] (ISID.) In that Scripture pronounces him who speaks to Agar first an angel, afterwards the Lord, 137Ahe is to be believed to have been the Son of God, who spoke through the law and the prophets, who through obedience to the Father's will is called an angel, but according to nature is God.

VERS. 22.-- « At the same time. » (AUG. ubi supra.) It can be asked when Abraham made a compact with Abimelech, etc., as far as which she knew not.

VERS. 23-30.-- « Swear therefore, » etc. Often they who are without, drawn on by the converse of the faithful, desire to be joined to them and to be formed to better things by their examples, that they may take counsel both for themselves and for their neighbours' salvation.

VERS. 31, 32.-- « Therefore it was called, » etc. There is a twofold cause why it was called in Hebrew Bersabee: either because Abimelech received seven ewe-lambs from the hand 137Bof Abraham. For seven are called sabee, or because they swore there, for sabaa signifies an oath. But if we read this cause above this name, it is said by prolepsis; as Bethel and Galgala, which, before they had these names, were called otherwise. It is to be noted from what goes before and from the present passage that Isaac was not born at the oak of Mambre ( Alon in Hebrew, in Latin an oak ) as is had in the Hebrew, but in Gerara, where Bersabee is a town even to this day, which province not long ago was called Salutaris from the division of the governors of Palestine. Whence Abraham dwelt in the land of the Palestinians many days.

VERS. 33, 34.-- « But Abraham, » etc. (AUG.) Abraham (as Stephen says) had not received an inheritance 137Cthere, nor the space of a foot. But the inheritance is to be understood which God was about to give freely, not bought with a price. And the space about the well is understood in reference to that space of purchase for which the seven ewe-lambs had been given, when Abimelech and Abraham swore to one another.

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 1.-- « Quae postquam, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Jacobus dicit quod neminem tentat Deus; sed usualiter, tentare, pro eo quod est probare, dicimus. Jacobus autem dicit de ea tentatione, qua quisque peccato [ Al., peccator] implicatur. Unde I Thess. I: « Ne forte tentaverit vos is qui tentat. » Alibi enim scriptum est Deut. XIII: « Tentat 137Dvos Deus vester, ut sciat si diligatis eum, id est, vos scire faciat. » Vires enim dilectionis suae homo ignorat, nisi experimento cognoscat.

VERS. 2.-- « Tolle filium, » etc. (ALC. in Gen., tom. I.) Non statim jussus est Abraham filium occidere, sed triduo ad immolandum ducere, ut longitudine temporis tentatio augeretur. Per triduum enim, crescentibus curis, paterna viscera cruciantur, et prolixo spatio pater filium intuetur, cibum cum eo sumit, tot noctibus pendet puer in amplexu patris, cubat in gremio, et per singula momenta in paterno affectu dolor occidendi filii cumulatur.

« Quem diligis, » etc. (BEDA in Gen., c. 22.) De charitatis admonitione et nominis recordatione tentatio 138Acumulatur, et paternus affectus movetur in memoria promissionis, quia dictum est: « In Isaac vocabitur tibi semen, » tanquam si ille occideretur, tota spes promissionis frustraretur.

« Vade in terram. » (HIER. Q. Hebr., tom. III.) Aiunt Hebraei hunc montem esse in quo postea templum aedificatum est in area ornae Jebusaei. Unde II, Par. III: « Coeperunt aedificare templum mense secundo, secunda die mensis, in monte Moria, » qui idcirco illuminans interpretatur et lucens, quia ibi est debir ( ) id est oraculum Dei, et spiritus qui homines docet veritatem et prophetias inspirat.

VERS. 3.-- « Asinum suum ducens, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., c. 18.) Asinus insensatam stultitiam Judaeorum significat, quae portabat omnia sacramenta et 138Bnesciebat.

VERS. 4.-- « Die autem tertio. » (HIER. ubi supra.) Notandum quia de Geraris usque ad montem Moria, id est sedem templi, iter trium dierum sit: et Abraham illuc die tertio pervenisse dicitur. Male ergo putant Abraham illo tempore habitasse apud quercum Mambre, cum ad montem Moria vix unius diei plenum iter sit. Triduum quo venerunt ad locum sacrificii, tres aetates significat, ante legem, ab Abraham usque ad Mosen; sub lege, a Mose usque ad Joannem; inde usque ad Dominum; et quidquid restat, tertius dies est gratiae, in qua tertia aetate sacrificium completum est Christi.

VERS. 5-8.-- « Exspectate hic, » etc. (ISID. ubi supra.) Audi Apostolum: « Caecitas ex parte contingit 138Cin Israel, » hoc est, « cum asino, » ut « plenitudo gentium intraret, » hoc est, « postquam adoraverimus. » Ubi sacrificium crucis per gentes fuerit praedicatum, hoc est, « ubi plenitudo gentium intraverit, revertemur ad vos, » hoc est, « et sic omnis Israel salvus fiet. » Duo servi dimissi, nec perducti ad locum sacrificii, Judaei sunt qui, serviliter viventes et carnaliter sapientes, non intellexerunt humilitatem et passionem Christi. Duo quidem, quia in duas partes dividendi, quod factum est peccante Salomone, quando divisus est populus loco regni, non errore impietatis, quibus dicitur Jer. III: « adversatrix Israel, et praevaricatrix Juda. »

« Ego et puer, » etc. (ALC.) Indubitanti animo mactare filium volebat. Laudandus in constantia 138Dofferendi et in fide suscitandi. Sciebat enim certissime Deum fallere non posse, et licet puer occideretur, promissionem tamen Dei salvam permanere. Unde Apostolus Hebr. XI: « Fide Abraham non haesitavit, cum unicum offerret, in quo acceperat repromissionem, credens quia et a mortuis potens est suscitare Deus. »

« Dixit Abraham. » (ISID. ubi supra.) Abraham, unicum filium ducens ad immolandum, Deum Patrem significat. Abraham senex filium suscepit, Deus autem non senescit, sed promissio de Christo quodammodo senuerat, quando natus est. Inchoavit ab Adam, quando dictum est: « Erunt duo in carne una » (Gen. II); et completa est sexta aetate saeculi. 139ASenectus Sarae in plebe Dei, hoc est, in multitudine prophetarum, hoc idem significat quia in fine temporum ex plebe sanctarum animarum natus est Christus. Sterilitas ejus significat quod in hoc saeculo spe salvi facti sumus, et in Christo, tanquam in Isaac, omnes nati sumus, quem partum Ecclesiae [ Al., Ecclesia] in fine temporum non natura, sed gratia procreavit. Abraham ergo Deum Patrem significat, Isaac Christum. Sicut enim Abraham unicum et dilectum filium victimam Deo obtulit, sic Deus Pater unigenitum Filium pro nobis tradidit. Et sicut Isaac ligna portabat, quibus imponendus erat, sic Christus crucem, in qua figendus erat.

VERS. 9-11.-- « Cumque alligasset, » etc. (ISID. ubi supra.) Isaac ligatis pedibus altari superponitur, 139Bet Christus cruci affigitur. Sed quod figuratum est per Isaac, translatum est ad arietem, quia Christus ovis. Ipse enim filius, quia natus; aries, quia immolatus. In vepribus haeret aries, crux cornua habet. Si enim duo ligna compingantur, crucis species redditur. Unde Habac. III: « Cornua in manibus ejus. » Cornibus ergo haerens aries, Christus crucifixus est. Vepres autem spinae: spinae iniquae, quae Dominum suspenderunt. Inter spinas enim peccatorum suspensus est. Unde Jer.: « Spinis peccatorum suorum circumdedit me populus hic (Jer. XXXVIII.) » Alii hunc arietem in vepribus ligatum, Christum ante immolationem spinis coronatum intelligunt.

VERS. 12.-- « Non peperceris, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra.) Nunquid non pepercit Abraham filio suo 139Cpropter angelum? Sed in angelo figuratus est Dominus, qui Deus est et magni consilii angelus. Erat enim in angelo Deus, et ex ejus persona loquebatur. Hoc magis in sequentibus apparebit, cum dicitur: « Et vocavit angelus Domini Abraham secundo de coelo, dicens: Per memetipsum juravi, dicit Dominus. » Non enim facile invenitur Christum Patrem dicere tanquam Dominum, praesertim ante incarnationem. Nam secundum carnem hoc congrue dicitur, secundum quam in psalmo II dicitur: « Dominus dixit ad me, » etc. Quod autem dictum est ibidem, c. 9: « Dixit Dominus Domino meo, » etc., ad prophetam hoc refertur qui loquitur; sicut illud: « Pluit Dominus a Domino, » id est, Dominus noster, scilicet Filius, a Domino nostro, Patre scilicet.

139DVERS. 13.-- « Arietem. » (ALC.) Historice. Aries qui pro Isaac immolatus est, non putavimus, sed verus est. Ideo magis putatur angelum eum aliunde attulisse, quam ibi de terra post sex dierum opera Dominum procreasse.

« Arietem inter vepres, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.) Aquila veprem vel spinetum, posuit, etc., usque ad quod ita Hebraice dicitur.

VERS. 14-19.-- « Appellavitque nomen, » etc. (HIER. ubi supra.) Pro eo quod hic habetur, etc., usque ad per incarnationem scilicet.

VERS. 20-24.-- « Melcha. » (HIER. ubi supra.) De Melcha uxore Nachor, etc., usque ad et in ipso nomine legitur in Isaia.

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 1.-- « Which things after they were done, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) James says that God tempts no man; but in ordinary usage we say "to tempt" for that which is "to prove". James however speaks of that temptation by which every man is entangled in sin [ Al., peccator]. Whence I Thess. I: « Lest perhaps he that tempteth should have tempted you. » For elsewhere it is written Deut. XIII: « 137DYour God tempteth you, that he may know whether you love him, that is, that he may make you to know. » [Vulg. quia tentat vos Dominus Deus vester, ut palam fiat utrum diligatis eum an non, "the Lord your God, … that it may appear openly whether you love him or not"] For a man knows not the powers of his own love, unless he learns it by experience.

VERS. 2.-- « Take thy son, » etc. (ALC. in Gen., tom. I.) Abraham was not bidden to kill his son at once, but to lead him for three days to the sacrifice, that by the length of the time the temptation might be increased. For through three days, with cares increasing, a father's bowels are tormented, and for a long space the father gazes upon his son, takes food with him, so many nights the boy hangs in his father's embrace, lies in his lap, and at every moment the grief of killing his son is heaped up in a father's love.

« Whom thou lovest, » etc. (BEDA in Gen., c. 22.) By the reminder of love and the recalling of the name the temptation 138Ais heaped up, and a father's affection is stirred at the memory of the promise, because it was said: « In Isaac shall thy seed be called, » as though, if he were killed, the whole hope of the promise would be frustrated.

« Go into the land. » (HIER. Q. Hebr., tom. III.) The Hebrews say that this mountain is that in which the temple was afterwards built, on the threshing-floor of Orna the Jebusite. Whence II, Par. III: « They began to build the temple in the second month, on the second day of the month, in mount Moria, » [Vulg. Et coepit Salomon aedificare domum Domini … Coepit autem aedificare mense secundo, anno quarto regni sui, "Solomon began to build the house of the Lord … in the second month, in the fourth year of his reign"] which is for that cause interpreted illuminating and shining, because there is debir ( ) that is, the oracle of God, and the Spirit who teaches men the truth and inspires prophecies.

VERS. 3.-- « Leading his ass, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., c. 18.) The ass signifies the senseless folly of the Jews, which bore all the sacraments and 138Bknew them not.

VERS. 4.-- « And on the third day. » (HIER. ubi supra.) It is to be noted that from Gerara as far as mount Moria, that is, the seat of the temple, is a journey of three days: and Abraham is said to have arrived there on the third day. Wrongly therefore do they think that Abraham dwelt at that time by the oak of Mambre, since to mount Moria there is scarce a full day's journey. The three days in which they came to the place of the sacrifice signify three ages, before the law, from Abraham to Moses; under the law, from Moses to John; thence to the Lord; and whatever remains, the third day is that of grace, in which third age the sacrifice of Christ was completed.

VERS. 5-8.-- « Stay here, » etc. (ISID. ubi supra.) Hear the Apostle: « Blindness in part happeneth 138Cin Israel, » that is, « with the ass, » that « the fulness of the Gentiles might come in, » that is, « after we have adored. » When the sacrifice of the cross shall have been preached among the Gentiles, that is, « when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, we will return to you, » that is, « and so all Israel shall be saved. » The two servants left behind, and not brought to the place of the sacrifice, are the Jews who, living slavishly and savouring carnally, understood not the humility and passion of Christ. Two indeed, because they were to be divided into two parts, which came to pass when Solomon sinned, when the people was divided in respect of the kingdom, not by the error of impiety, to whom it is said Jer. III: « Israel the adversary, and Juda the prevaricator. » [Vulg. aversatrix Israel, comparatione praevaricatricis Judae, "the backslider Israel, in comparison of the treacherous Juda"]

« I and the boy, » etc. (ALC.) With undoubting mind he willed to slay his son. He is to be praised in the constancy 138Dof offering and in the faith of raising up. For he knew most certainly that God cannot deceive, and that, though the boy were killed, yet the promise of God would remain safe. Whence the Apostle Hebr. XI: « By faith Abraham hesitated not, when he offered up his only-begotten, in whom he had received the promise, believing that God is able to raise up even from the dead. » [Vulg. Hbr 11:17,19 Fide obtulit Abraham Isaac, cum tentaretur, "By faith Abraham offered Isaac, when he was tried"; the opening words are not in Hebrews]

« Abraham said. » (ISID. ubi supra.) Abraham, leading his only son to be sacrificed, signifies God the Father. Abraham received a son in old age, but God grows not old, yet the promise concerning Christ had in a manner grown old when he was born. It began from Adam, when it was said: « They shall be two in one flesh » (Gen. II); and it was completed in the sixth age of the world. 139ASara's old age in the people of God, that is, in the multitude of the prophets, signifies this same thing, because in the end of the times Christ was born of the people of holy souls. Her barrenness signifies that in this world we are saved by hope, and that in Christ, as in Isaac, we are all born, which birth of the Church [ Al., Ecclesia] in the end of the times not nature but grace brought forth. Abraham therefore signifies God the Father, Isaac Christ. For as Abraham offered his only and beloved son as a victim to God, so God the Father delivered up his only-begotten Son for us. And as Isaac carried the wood upon which he was to be laid, so Christ the cross on which he was to be fastened.

VERS. 9-11.-- « And when he had bound, » etc. (ISID. ubi supra.) Isaac with his feet bound is laid upon the altar, 139Band Christ is fastened to the cross. But that which was figured by Isaac was transferred to the ram, because Christ is a sheep. For he is a son, because born; a ram, because sacrificed. The ram sticks fast in the briars, the cross has horns. For if two pieces of wood be joined together, the shape of a cross is produced. Whence Habac. III: « Horns in his hands. » The ram therefore sticking fast by the horns is Christ crucified. But briars are thorns: wicked thorns, which hanged the Lord. For among the thorns of sinners he was hanged. Whence Jer.: « With the thorns of their sins this people hath encompassed me (Jer. XXXVIII.) » Others understand this ram bound in the briars to be Christ crowned with thorns before his immolation.

VERS. 12.-- « Thou hast not spared, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra.) Did Abraham then not spare his son 139Con account of the angel? But in the angel the Lord was figured, who is God and the angel of great counsel. For God was in the angel, and spoke in his person. This will appear the more in what follows, when it is said: « And the angel of the Lord called Abraham a second time from heaven, saying: By my own self have I sworn, saith the Lord. » For it is not easily found that Christ calls the Father Lord, especially before the incarnation. For according to the flesh this is congruously said, according to which it is said in the second psalm: « The Lord said to me, » etc. But that which is said in the same place, c. 9: « The Lord said to my Lord, » etc., is referred to the prophet who speaks; as that: « The Lord rained from the Lord, » that is, our Lord, namely the Son, from our Lord, namely the Father.

139DVERS. 13.-- « The ram. » (ALC.) Historically. The ram which was sacrificed for Isaac, we have not imagined [it], but it is true. Therefore it is rather thought that an angel brought it from elsewhere, than that the Lord created it there out of the earth after the work of the six days.

« The ram among the briars, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.) Aquila put a briar or a thorn-brake, etc., as far as which is so said in Hebrew.

VERS. 14-19.-- « And he called the name, » etc. (HIER. ubi supra.) For that which is had here, etc., as far as namely through the incarnation.

VERS. 20-24.-- « Melcha. » (HIER. ubi supra.) Of Melcha the wife of Nachor, etc., as far as and in that very name it is read in Isaias.

CAPUT XXIII.

  • 140A

VERS. 1-6.-- « Vixit autem Sara, » etc. « Et mortua est in civitate Arbee, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.) Quae est in valle: hoc in authenticis libris non habetur, etc., usque ad sicut in libro Josue aperte demonstratur.

VERS. 7-15.-- « Surrexit Abraham, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen.) Quaeritur quomodo Abraham adoravit populum terrae, etc., usque ad et ideo adorator corrigendus erat.

VERS. 16.-- « Quod cum audisset, » etc. (HIER., de locis Hebr.) In Hebraeo, sicut posuimus, etc., usque ad cum etiam reprehendatur, qui invitus acceperit.

VERS. 17-20.-- « Confirmatusque est ager, » etc. 140B(GREG., lib. VI Moral., c. 25.) Innuit sepulcrum duplex, etc., usque ad vel propter operationis instantiam contemplanda postponit.

« Spelunca. » Et circa eam ager arborosus ad sepeliendam Saram quadringentis siclis argenti per patriarcham a dominis terrae emitur: quia ad vacandum sapientiae et studendum actioni, sub foliis mysticorum verborum, tanquam sub fructuosis arboribus, non aliter praedicatoribus quietus locus acquiritur, nisi per evangelicam doctrinam, argenti nitore fulgentem, terrenis potestatibus ab ipsis aeterna beatitudo suadeatur. Quae bene centenario quater ducto figuratur, quia a laeva in dextram transfertur.

CHAPTER XXIII.

  • 140A

VERS. 1-6.-- « And Sara lived, » etc. « And she died in the city of Arbee, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.) Which is in the valley: this is not had in the authentic books, etc., as far as as is openly shown in the book of Josue.

VERS. 7-15.-- « Abraham rose up, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen.) It is asked how Abraham adored the people of the land, etc., as far as and therefore the adorer was to be corrected.

VERS. 16.-- « And when he had heard, » etc. (HIER., de locis Hebr.) In the Hebrew, as we have set it down, etc., as far as since he also is reproved who has received unwillingly.

VERS. 17-20.-- « And the field was confirmed, » etc. 140B(GREG., lib. VI Moral., c. 25.) He hints at a double sepulchre, etc., as far as or on account of the urgency of action puts off the things to be contemplated.

« The cave. » And the wooded field round about it is bought for the burial of Sara by the patriarch from the lords of the land for four hundred sicles of silver: because, for being free for wisdom and giving study to action, under the leaves of mystical words, as under fruitful trees, a quiet place is not otherwise acquired for preachers, unless by the evangelical doctrine, shining with the brightness of silver, eternal blessedness be urged upon earthly powers by them. Which is well figured by a hundred taken four times, because it is carried over from the left hand to the right.

CAPUT XXIV.

  • 140C

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Erat autem Abraham senex . . . . . Dixitque ad servum seniorem: Pone manum, » etc. (GREG.) Manum sub femore jubet ponere, et per Deum coeli jurare, quia illius caro per illud membrum descensura erat, qui filius Abrahae esset ex humanitate, et divinus ex divinitate; quasi dicat: Tange Filium meum, et jura per Deum meum. Verumtamen non super femur, sed sub femore manum ponere jubet, quia inde descensurus erat, qui homo, sed super homines esset; unde dignum non fuit ut manum super femur poneret, quia nulla caro super illam est.

VERS. 3-8.-- « Ut non accipias. » (GREG.) Allegorice. Isaac de filiabus Chananaeorum uxor prohibetur, 140Dquia Christo nullae reproborum animae conjunguntur. De cognatione uxor duci praecipitur, quia sola electorum Ecclesia Christo copulanda erat, quam ipse Unigenitus ex praedestinatione et praescientia jam extraneam non habebat. Puer qui ad uxorem deducendam mittitur, prophetarum et apostolorum, omniumque doctorum ordo est; qui dum verbum praedicationis faciunt, cujusque animae quasi conjungendae unigenito Filio provisores fiunt.

VERS. 9.-- « Posuit ergo, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.) Tradunt Hebraei quia in sanctificatione ejus, etc., usque ad et pondus tribulationis fortiter ferant, quod cameli significant.

VERS. 10.-- « Ex omnibus bonis ejus, » etc. (GREG.) 141AQuia his quae de Domino loquuntur, in semetipsis virtutum divitias ostendunt, ut tanto citius ad sequendum Deum invitent, quanto suis auditoribus in seipsis monstrant quae narrant.

VERS. 11-13.-- « Juxta puteum. » (GREG.) Juxta fontem stetit, et ex praefixa sententia, quae esset eligenda proposuit, quia sancti praedicatores verba fluentia considerant. Unde colligunt quem vel quibus auditoribus fiduciam certitudinis assumant.

VERS. 14-16.-- « Igitur puella, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen. ) Quaerendum est quid differant illicitae augurationes a petitione hac, etc., usque ad quo tentare Deum prohibetur.

« Inclina hydriam tuam. » Humilia saecularem facundiam ad hauriendum propheticum sermonem, ut 141Bvoluntatem tuam persuasioni meae inclinem.

VERS. 17-21.-- « Occurritque ei servus, et ait: Pauxillum, » etc. (GREG.) Allegorice. Omnis praedicator animam auditoris sitit, ideo servus petit, Rebecca potum tribuit: quia electorum Ecclesia desiderio praedicatorum ex virtute suae fidei satisfacit. Quae enim Deum, quem audit, confitetur, praedicatori aquam refectionis et refrigerii offert. Et notandum quod hydriam ab humero inclinans posuit, quia illa confessio placida est quae a bono opere procedit. Vel aquam praebuit, quia in eo quod credidit, vacua non remansit. Mox enim praedicavit quod audivit, et dicendo multos ex se praedicatores protulit. Aqua enim in hydria, scientia praedicationis in mensura. Studet enim non plus sapere quam oportet sapere. 141CHydria in ulna, mensura praedicationis in opere. Nec solum omnibus comitibus ejus, sed et camelis potum praebet, quia verbum vitae non solum prudentibus, sed etiam stultis praedicatur. Rom. I: « Sapientibus et insipientibus debitor sum. » Vel aqua etiam jumentis datur, cum cura carnis quomodo sit habenda disponitur, ut ex voluptate non impendatur, et ex necessitate non negetur; unde Rom. XIII: « Carnis curam ne feceritis in desideriis; » et Ephes. V: « Nemo enim unquam carnem suam odio habuit. »

VERS. 22-24.-- « Protulit vir, » etc. (HIER.) Beka ( ), quod hic pro didrachmate ponitur, semiuncia est: Schekel vero ( ), qui Latine, sed corrupte, siclus dicitur, uncia. (GREG.) Quia praedicator 141Dauditum Ecclesiae per obedientiam, et manus per operationem exornat. Inaures duorum siclorum sunt, armillae decem, quia prima virtus obedientiae in charitate est, quae dilectio Dei et proximi est; et bonum opus decalogi completione perficitur, ut cum bona agi coeperint, mala nulla agantur.

VERS. 25-28.-- « Palearum quoque. » Quia Ecclesia recipiens spiritualia, reddit praedicatoribus terrena stipendia; unde I Cor. IX: « Si vobis spiritualia seminavimus, magnum est si nos carnalia vestra metamus.

« Locus spatiosus. » A priore populo naturae legem se Ecclesia novisse monstravit, et praedicationem amplo charitatis gremio suscepit: doctori enim spatiosa 142Amansio est, in corde auditoris latitudo charitatis; unde secundae Corinth. sexto: « Non angustiamini in nobis, sed in visceribus vestris; » quasi suscipiendae doctrinae locum mentis dilatate, ad cogitanda carnalia coarctate.

VERS. 29-32.-- « Habebat, » etc. Quia carnales fidelibus conjuncti, dum spiritualia dona conspiciunt, admiratione suspensi, si non usque ad opera, tamen usque ad fidem suscipiendam praedicationem admittunt: dum enim bonos miraculis fulgere considerant, quae de aeternitate audiunt non recusant, quamvis Ecclesiam moribus non sequentes, in carnali operatione remaneant.

« Habebat autem. » Quia sunt carnales fidelibus conjuncti, quorum familiaritate aliquando ad audiendum 142Bverbum prodeunt, et videntes dona Dei collata fidelibus mirantur, et verbum corde percipiunt, quod bonis moribus implere non satagunt.

VERS. 33-36.-- « Non comedam. » Quia sunt qui doctores ex temporalibus stipendiis retinere parati sunt: sed illi nisi prius obtineant aeterna, recipere nolunt. Si enim in animabus fructum non inveniunt, sumere stipendia corporibus contemnunt; pedes non lavant, quia laboris desideria nulla consolatione relevant.

VERS. 37-41.-- « Et adjuravit, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra. ) Sententia est eadem, verba non omnia, vel ipsa, vel ita dicta sunt; quod propter eos dicendum, qui evangelistas calumniantur quod in aliquibus verbis non omnino conveniunt, cum res et sententia eadem; 142Chunc enim librum unus scripsit, qui ea quae supra dixit, cum mandaret Abraham, vel reliqua ponere potuit, si ad rem pertinere judicaret: cum veritas narrationis non exigit, nisi ut rerum sententiarumque veritas sit, quibus voluntas (propter quam intimandam verba fiunt) satis appareat.

VERS. 42-52.-- « Veni ergo hodie, » etc. (HIERON.) In Hebraeo scriptum est, etc., usque ad ostendant igitur Judaei alicubi positum alma, ubi adolescentulam et non virginem sonet.

VERS. 53-58.-- « Prolatisque, » etc. (GREG. ubi supra. ) Postquam conjugii causam puer obtinuit, etc., usque ad « Dedit ea Rebeccae. » Rebecca non ornatur auro Bathuelis, quia non sunt digna ornamenta barbari et imperiti. De domo Abrahae accipit ornamenta, 142Dquia patientia de domo sapientiae ornatur; a puero Abrahae suscipit inaures, id est, aurea in auribus verba; et brachilia, id est, aurea in manibus opera, et alia ornamenta; quibus figurantur diversa virtutum genera.

VERS. 59-60.-- « Et nutricem illius. » Honestum erat ut ad nuptias absque parentibus virgo proficiscens, nutricis solatio foveretur.

VERS. 61.-- « Igitur Rebecca, » etc. (GREG.) Quia Ecclesia habet secum minoris meriti animas, quae ipsam moribus sequuntur: sed ad contemplationis thorum non provehuntur. Pueros quoque quosdam habuit in comitatu, quia cum prophetis fuerunt qui bene viverent, etsi prophetiae spiritum non haberent: 143Aet cum apostolis et doctoribus, qui vitam eorum tenerent, et tamen non praedicarent.

« Qui festinus, » etc. Festinus puer ad dominum redit, quia praedicatores cum auditorum vitam obtinent, Deo, de cujus munere hoc habent, non sibi praebent.

VERS. 62.-- « Eo tempore Isaac, » etc. (GREG. ubi supra. ) Vivens et videns Dominus est, etc., usque ad modo spineam coronam, modo crucem sustinuit.

« Habitabat, » etc. (PATERIUS in Gen. ex dictis GREG.) Isaac, Rebecca veniente, habitabat in terra australi, quia Christus, veniente ad se Ecclesia, in illorum mentibus mansit quos non corporis frigus, sed fervor charitatis tenuit: qualis erat Anna, Simeon, et aliqui de populo Judaeorum.

143BVERS. 63.-- « Et egressus fuerat, » etc. (HIER.) Per terram australem, Geraram significat, unde ad immolandum adductus fuerat. Egressus est, ut exerceretur in campo. In Hebraeo legitur: « Egressus est Isaac, ut loqueretur in agrum, inclinata jam vespera; » ubi significatur quod Dominus solus orabat in monte, et Isaac, qui typus Domini fuit, ad orationem quasi virum justum a domo egressum nona hora, vel ante solis occasum, spirituales victimas Deo obtulisse.

(GREG. ubi supra. ) Ager mundus est, quo Christus egressus est, quia visibiliter apparere dignatus est.

Unde Habac. III: « Egressus es in salutem populi tui. » Isaac ad meditandum in agro exiit, quia Christus formam humilitatis praebens, exercitium longanimitatis, 143Cpassionis, et patientiae exempla monstravit, sicut solent juvenes exercitati armorum usu meditari. Armorum meditatio est passionis frequentatio. Qui enim verbera, sputa, lanceam crucemque pertulit, passionem usque ad mortem in se frequentari permisit. Passiones arma dicimus, quia per ipsas ab adversariis liberamur; unde Luc. XXI: « In patientia vestra possidebitis animas vestras. »

« Inclinata, etc. Quia passionum exercitia juxta finem mundi suscepit; unde psal. CXL: Elevatio manuum mearum sacrificium vespertinum. »

VERS. 64.-- « Descendit de camelo, » etc. (GREG., lib. I, Moral., c. 14.) Rebecca ad Isaac in camelo deducitur, etc., usque ad super tortuosa camelorum dorsa divitias portant.

143DVERS. 65, 66.-- « Quis est ille homo, » etc. (GREG. apud Paterium. ) Rebecca viso Isaac, quis sit, requisito puero, cognoscit: quia Ecclesia per prophetarum et apostolorum dicta, quid de Christo credere debeat, intelligit. Quae se mox pallio cooperuit, quia quanto Christi mysteria penetrat, tanto de anteacta vita confunditur. Unde infirmitatem suam pallio operire nititur; et quae prius in camelo libere gestatur, descendens verecundia tegitur, cui quasi verecundius dicitur: Quem enim fructum habuisti tunc in illis in quibus nunc erubescis?

« At illa, » etc. (HIERON.) Alia editio, « teristrum, » quod etiam nunc genus est Arabici vestimenti quo mulieres provinciae illius velantur.

144AVERS. 67.-- « Qui introduxit eam, » etc. (GREG. ut supra. ) Hanc Isaac in tabernaculo matris suae introduxit, et uxorem accepit, quia in loco Synagogae, de qua natus est Christus, Ecclesiam diligit: ut quae cognatione, id est praedestinatione, proxima fuerat, amore conjuncta uxor fiat.

« Et intantum dilexit eam, » etc. (GREG. ut supra. ) Quia ex lucro Ecclesiae Christus tristitiam ex perditione Synagogae detersit, quia dum Ecclesia ex gentilitate veniens usque ad thorum contemplationis perducitur, Judaea pro nihilo habetur. Isaac quoque risus, Rebecca vero patientia dicitur: risus vero ex laetitia, patientia ex tribulatione. Et quamvis Ecclesia jam sit coelestis gaudii contemplatione suspensa, habet tamen in se quod de carnis pondere 144Bdoleat. Isaac vero et Rebecca junguntur, id est, risus et patientia, secundum illud Rom. XII: « Spe gaudentes, in tribulatione patientes, » ut prospera laetificent de contemplatione, adversa de tribulatione perturbent.

CHAPTER XXIV.

  • 140C

VERS. 1, 2.-- « And Abraham was old . . . . . And he said to his elder servant: Put thy hand, » etc. (GREG.) He bids him put his hand under his thigh, and swear by the God of heaven, because through that member his flesh was to come down who should be Abraham's son by his humanity, and divine by his divinity; as though he said: Touch my Son, and swear by my God. Yet he bids him put his hand not upon the thigh but under the thigh, because thence was to come down one who should be man, yet above men; whence it was not fitting that he should put his hand upon the thigh, because no flesh is above that flesh.

VERS. 3-8.-- « That thou take not. » (GREG.) Allegorically. A wife of the daughters of the Chanaanites is forbidden to Isaac, 140Dbecause no souls of the reprobate are joined to Christ. A wife is bidden to be taken from his kindred, because the Church of the elect alone was to be wedded to Christ, whom the Only-begotten himself, by predestination and foreknowledge, did not already hold as a stranger. The servant who is sent to bring back the wife is the order of the prophets and apostles, and of all teachers; who, while they perform the word of preaching, become the providers, as it were, of every soul that is to be joined to the only-begotten Son.

VERS. 9.-- « He put therefore, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr.) The Hebrews hand down that in his sanctification, etc., as far as and that they should bear bravely the weight of tribulation, which the camels signify.

VERS. 10.-- « Of all his goods, » etc. (GREG.) 141ABecause they who speak of the Lord show in themselves the riches of the virtues, that they may invite men so much the more quickly to follow God, as they show forth in themselves to their hearers the things they tell of.

VERS. 11-13.-- « By the well. » (GREG.) He stood by the fountain, and from a sentence fixed beforehand set forth which one was to be chosen, because holy preachers consider the flowing words. Whence they gather from what hearer, or from what hearers, they may take the confidence of certainty.

VERS. 14-16.-- « The maid therefore, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen. ) It is to be asked what unlawful auguries differ from this petition, etc., as far as whereby it is forbidden to tempt God.

« Let down thy pitcher. » Humble thy worldly eloquence to draw up the prophetic word, that 141BI may bend thy will to my persuasion.

VERS. 17-21.-- « And the servant ran to meet her, and said: Give me a little, » etc. (GREG.) Allegorically. Every preacher thirsts for the soul of his hearer, therefore the servant asks, Rebecca gives the drink: because the Church of the elect satisfies the desire of preachers out of the strength of her faith. For she who confesses the God whom she hears, offers to the preacher the water of refreshment and cooling. And it is to be noted that she set down the pitcher, bending it from her shoulder, because that confession is pleasing which proceeds from a good work. Or she supplied water, because in that which she believed she did not remain empty. For at once she preached what she had heard, and by speaking brought forth many preachers out of herself. For the water in the pitcher is the knowledge of preaching in measure. For she studies not to be more wise than it behoveth to be wise. 141CThe pitcher on the arm is the measure of preaching in work. And she gives drink not only to all his companions, but to the camels also, because the word of life is preached not only to the wise, but also to the foolish. Rom. I: « To the wise and to the unwise I am a debtor. » Or water is given even to the beasts, when it is ordered how care of the flesh is to be had, that it be not spent upon pleasure, and be not denied to necessity; whence Rom. XIII: « Make not provision for the flesh in its desires; » and Ephes. V: « For no man ever hated his own flesh. »

VERS. 22-24.-- « The man brought forth, » etc. (HIER.) Beka ( ), which is here put for a didrachma, is half an ounce: but Schekel ( ), which in Latin, though corruptly, is called siclus, is an ounce. (GREG.) Because the preacher 141Dadorns the hearing of the Church by obedience, and its hands by work. The earrings are of two sicles, the bracelets of ten, because the first virtue of obedience lies in charity, which is the love of God and of the neighbour; and good work is perfected by the completion of the decalogue, so that when good things have begun to be done, no evil things may be done.

VERS. 25-28.-- « Straw also. » Because the Church, receiving spiritual things, renders earthly stipends to preachers; whence I Cor. IX: « If we have sown unto you spiritual things, it is a great thing if we reap your carnal things.

« A large room. » From the former people the Church showed that she had known the law of nature, and she received the preaching in the ample bosom of charity: for the teacher's spacious 142Adwelling is the breadth of charity in the hearer's heart; whence in the sixth of the second to the Corinthians: « Be not straitened in us, but in your own bowels; » [Vulg. angustiamini autem in visceribus vestris, "but you are straitened in your own bowels"] as if to say, enlarge the room of your mind for receiving doctrine, straiten it for thinking upon carnal things.

VERS. 29-32.-- « He had, » etc. Because carnal men joined to the faithful, while they behold spiritual gifts, hang in wonder, and if not so far as works, yet so far as the receiving of the faith they admit the preaching: for while they consider that good men shine with miracles, they refuse not the things they hear of eternity, although, not following the Church in their morals, they remain in carnal working.

« And he had. » Because there are carnal men joined to the faithful, by whose familiarity they sometimes come forth to hear 142Bthe word, and seeing the gifts of God bestowed on the faithful they marvel, and receive the word in the heart, which they are not at pains to fulfil in good morals.

VERS. 33-36.-- « I will not eat. » Because there are those who are ready to retain teachers out of temporal stipends: but they [the teachers] are unwilling to receive them [the stipends], unless they first obtain eternal things. For if they do not find fruit in souls, they scorn to take stipends for bodies; they wash not the feet, because they relieve the desires of labour with no consolation.

VERS. 37-41.-- « And he made me swear, » etc. (AUG. ubi supra. ) The meaning is the same, the words not all, or the same, or so spoken; which is to be said on account of those who calumniate the evangelists because in some words they do not altogether agree, although the matter and the meaning be the same; 142Cfor one man wrote this book, who could have set down the things he said above, when Abraham gave the charge, or the rest, had he judged them to belong to the matter: since the truth of a narration does not require anything except that there be truth of things and of meanings, whereby the will (for the making known of which words are made) may sufficiently appear.

VERS. 42-52.-- « I came therefore to-day, » etc. (HIERON.) In the Hebrew it is written, etc., as far as let the Jews therefore show alma set down anywhere where it sounds a young girl and not a virgin.

VERS. 53-58.-- « And bringing forth, » etc. (GREG. ubi supra. ) After the servant obtained the cause of the marriage, etc., as far as « He gave them to Rebecca. » Rebecca is not adorned with the gold of Bathuel, because the ornaments of a barbarian and unskilled man are not worthy. From the house of Abraham she receives ornaments, 142Dbecause patience is adorned from the house of wisdom; from Abraham's servant she receives earrings, that is, golden words in the ears; and bracelets, that is, golden works in the hands, and other ornaments; by which are figured the diverse kinds of the virtues.

VERS. 59-60.-- « And her nurse. » It was seemly that a virgin setting out to her wedding without her parents should be cherished with the solace of a nurse.

VERS. 61.-- « Rebecca therefore, » etc. (GREG.) Because the Church has with her souls of lesser merit, who follow her in their morals: but they are not advanced to the couch of contemplation. She had also certain servants in her company, because with the prophets there were men who lived well, even though they had not the spirit of prophecy: 143Aand with the apostles and teachers, men who held to their life, and yet did not preach.

« Who in haste, » etc. The servant returns in haste to his master, because preachers, when they obtain the life of their hearers, present it to God, of whose gift they have it, not to themselves.

VERS. 62.-- « At that time Isaac, » etc. (GREG. ubi supra. ) The Living and Seeing One is the Lord, etc., as far as he bore now a crown of thorns, now a cross.

« He dwelt, » etc. (PATERIUS in Gen. ex dictis GREG.) Isaac, when Rebecca came, was dwelling in the south country, because Christ, when the Church came to him, abode in the minds of those whom not the cold of the body but the fervour of charity held: such as were Anna, Simeon, and some of the people of the Jews.

143BVERS. 63.-- « And he was gone forth, » etc. (HIER.) By the south country he signifies Gerara, whence he had been brought to be sacrificed. He went forth, that he might exercise himself in the field. In the Hebrew it is read: « Isaac went forth to speak into the field, the evening being now come on; » where it is signified that the Lord prayed alone upon the mountain, and that Isaac, who was a type of the Lord, went forth from the house at the ninth hour, or before sunset, to prayer as a just man, and offered spiritual victims to God.

(GREG. ubi supra. ) The field is the world, into which Christ went forth, because he vouchsafed to appear visibly.

Whence Habac. III: « Thou wentest forth for the salvation of thy people. » Isaac went out into the field to meditate, because Christ, holding forth the form of humility, showed the exercise of long-suffering, 143Cof passion, and examples of patience, as trained young men are wont to meditate in the use of arms. The meditation of arms is the frequenting of the passion. For he who bore stripes, spittings, the lance and the cross, suffered his passion to be frequented in himself even unto death. We call the passions arms, because through them we are freed from our adversaries; whence Luc. XXI: « In your patience you shall possess your souls. »

« The day being now far spent, etc. Because he took upon him the exercises of the passions near the end of the world; whence psal. CXL: The lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice. »

VERS. 64.-- « She came down from the camel, » etc. (GREG., lib. I, Moral., c. 14.) Rebecca is brought to Isaac upon a camel, etc., as far as upon the crooked backs of camels they carry riches.

143DVERS. 65, 66.-- « Who is that man, » etc. (GREG. apud Paterium. ) Rebecca, having seen Isaac, learns who he is by asking the servant: because the Church, by the sayings of the prophets and apostles, understands what she ought to believe concerning Christ. And she straightway covered herself with a cloak, because the more she penetrates the mysteries of Christ, the more she is confounded at her past life. Whence she strives to cover her infirmity with a cloak; and she who before was carried freely upon the camel, coming down is covered with shame, to whom it is said as it were more shamefacedly: What fruit therefore hadst thou then in those things of which thou art now ashamed?

« But she, » etc. (HIERON.) Another edition, « teristrum, » which even now is a kind of Arabian garment wherewith the women of that province are veiled.

144AVERS. 67.-- « Who brought her in, » etc. (GREG. ut supra. ) Isaac brought her into his mother's tent, and took her to wife, because in the place of the Synagogue, of which Christ was born, he loves the Church: that she who by kindred, that is, by predestination, had been nearest, may by love be joined as his wife.

« And he loved her so much, » etc. (GREG. ut supra. ) Because from the gain of the Church Christ wiped away the sorrow that came of the loss of the Synagogue, because while the Church, coming out of the Gentile world, is brought to the couch of contemplation, Judaea is held for nothing. Isaac too is called laughter, and Rebecca patience: and laughter comes of gladness, patience of tribulation. And although the Church be now suspended in the contemplation of heavenly joy, yet she has in herself somewhat whereat she may grieve 144Bfor the weight of the flesh. But Isaac and Rebecca are joined together, that is, laughter and patience, according to that of Rom. XII: « Rejoicing in hope, patient in tribulation, » that prosperous things may gladden out of contemplation, adverse things disturb out of tribulation.

CAPUT XXV.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Abraham vero aliam duxit uxorem, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorice. Mortua Sara duxit Abraham Ceturam. Non propter incontinentiam, quia jam grandaevus: nec propter filiorum procurationem, quia illi semen quasi stellae coeli ex Isaac promissum erat. Sed sicut Agar et Ismael figuraverunt carnales Veteris Testamenti, sic Cetura et filii ejus haereticos, qui se ad Testamentum Novum 144Cexistimant pertinere. Sed utraque concubina, sola Sara uxor; unde Cant. VI: « Una est columba mea, » etc.

Allegorice. Post assumptionem Ecclesiae adhaeserunt Christo multae animae haeretica pravitate deceptae, quae non catholicos, sed schismaticos genuerunt, qui unitatem Ecclesiae diviserunt: quos significant filii Ceturae, qui inferius numerantur.

(HIER., in Q. Hebr. ) Cetura, Hebraice, copulata, etc., usque ad quanto enim quis est carne fessus, tanto spiritu robustus et sapientiae complexibus aptus.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « Filii Dadan fuerunt Assurim, et Lathusim, et Laomim. At vero ex Madian ortus est Epha et Opher et Henoc et Abida. » (HIER.) Assurim in negotiatores transferri putant, etc., usque ad 144Det a plerisque filiis Abrahae ex Cetura occupatas Indiae regiones.

VERS. 5-7.-- « Deditque Abraham, » etc. (ISID.) Quia dantur munera filiis concubinarum, id est carnalium; sed non perveniunt ad regnum promissum haeretici, vel Judaei, quia carnalia lucra sequuntur; solus Isaac est haeres, quia non filii carnis existimantur in semine, sed promissionis.

VERS. 8-11.-- « Et deficiens mortuus est in senectute bona, provectaeque aetatis, et plenus dierum, congregatusque est ad populum suum. Et sepelierunt eum, » etc. (HIERON.) male in LXX additum est: « Deficiens mortuus est Abraham, » quia non convenit Abrahae deficere vel minui. Illud quoque 145Aquod posuimus « in senectute bona senex » et plenius in Graecis codicibus ponitur, « plenus dierum; » quod cum sensum videatur exponere, eo quod luce et diei operibus plenus occubuerit, magis tamen ad anagogen facit, si simpliciter ponatur « plenus. »

( Glossa Graeca. ) Ecce Moses vitam venturi saeculi novit, nemo enim his qui non sunt, apponi potest.

VERS. 12.-- « Hae sunt generationes, » etc. Hi populi ante filios Isaac computantur, qui cum sint de semine Abrahae, non tamen computantur in semine, quia prius est quod animale est, deinde quod spirituale. Unde post computationem carnalium, ponitur genealogia Isaac, de quo facta est promissio, et electorum propaganda successio.

145BVERS. 13-15.-- « Et haec nomina filiorum ejus in vocabulis et generationibus suis. Primogenitus Ismaelis Nabajoth, deinde Cedar, et Abel, » etc. (HIERON.) Duodecim filii nascuntur Ismaeli, primogenitus Nabajoth, a quo omnis regio ab Euphrate usque ad mare Rubrum Nabathea dicitur, quae pars Arabiae est. Nam familiae ipsorum oppida, pagi et tribus, horum appellatione celebrantur. Ab uno ex his, Cedar in deserto, et Duma alia regio, et Thema ad austrum, et Chedma, alias Chethma, ad orientalem plagam dicitur. Quod in extremo capituli juxta LXX legimus, « contra faciem omnium fratrum suorum habitabat, » verius est quod posuimus, « coram omnibus fratribus suis occubuit, » id est, in manibus filiorum suorum mortuus est, superstitibus 145Cadhuc liberis. Fratres enim filii appellantur; unde Jacob: « Ponatur coram fratribus meis, et fratribus tuis, et dijudicent inter nos. » Non enim credendum est quod Jacob, exceptis liberis suis, aliquos fratres secum habuerit.

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Duodecim principes tribuum suarum. Et facti sunt anni vitae Ismael, » etc. Duodenarius, sicut sacratus est in apostolis et in apostolicis viris, sic exsecrabilis est in reprobis contra fidem Trinitatis et Evangelium per quatuor mundi partes dilatatis.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « Habitavit autem ab Hevila usque Sur, quae respicit Aegyptum, » etc. Regio quam circumivit Phison ab Hevila, nepote Noe, sic vocata est, et est solitudo contra faciem Aegypti. Sur quoque 145Dest solitudo inter Cades et Barad, extendens desertum usque ad mare Rubrum et Aegypti confinia.

VERS. 20.-- « De Mesopotamia sororem Laban. » Regio sterilis, quae sic Graece vocatur quia media inter duo flumina Tigrim et Euphratem. Nam μέσος medius, ποταμὸς fluvius dicitur; hinc hippopotamus id est, fluvialis equus.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « Deprecatusque . . . sed collidebantur in utero parvuli, » etc. (HIER.) Promotione vel collisione LXX posuerunt, « ludebant » vel « recalcitrabant: » Aquila, « confligebant filii in utero ejus. » Symmachus, « in similitudine navis in superficie ferebantur. » (GREG., Dial. I, 8.) Quae sancti 146Aorando efficiunt, etc., usque ad is enim, in quo Deus multiplicare semen Abrahae praedestinaverat, oratione obtinuit ut filios haberet.

« Qui exaudivit eam. » (CHRYSOST.) Ne putes e vestigio et protinus assecutum esse quod optabat: nam viginti annis mansit orans et deprecans Deum. Nam sicut docuit nos quot annos habuit quando duxit Rebeccam (nempe quadraginta), ita assignavit nobis quot annorum fuerit Isaac, quando nati sunt ei filii. « Sexagenarius, inquit, erat quando nati sunt parvuli. »

« Perrexitque, » etc., (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Quaeritur quo ierit, cum nondum essent prophetae vel sacerdotes tabernaculi aut templi Domini? Forsitan ad locum ubi aram constituit Abraham. 146BSed quomodo ibi responsa darentur, tacet Scriptura; an per aliquem sacerdotem? quem mirum est, si erat, non fuisse nominatum, et nullam sacerdotum factam mentionem esse. Forsitan ibi, cum orando allegassent desideria sua, dormiebant, ut per somnum monerentur: vel adhuc vivebat Melchisedech, cujus tanta fuit excellentia, ut dubitaretur utrum homo an angelus fuerit. Vel forte etiam illo tempore erant homines Dei, in quibus Deus posset interrogari. Quidquid horum, vel forte aliud fuerit. Rebecca tamen secundum Scripturam Dominum interrogavit, et responsum ejus meruit.

VERS. 23.-- « Qui respondens ait: Duae gentes, » etc. (AUG.) Historica proprietate responsum invenitur completum, cum propter Israel, id est, Jacob, 146Cminor filius superavit Idumaeos, id est gentem quam propagavit Esau, eosque per David tributarios fecit; quod diu fuerunt usque ad regem Joram (IV Reg. VIII). Tunc enim Idumaei rebellaverunt, et jugum de cervice excusserunt, sicut prophetizavit Isaac, quando minorem pro majore benedixit, et hoc idem dixit majori, cum ipsum postea benedixit.

VERS. 24-26.-- « Jam tempus, » etc. (HIER.) Ubi nos « pilosum » posuimus, in Hebraeo habetur seyr ( ); unde Esau, sicut alibi legimus, seyr, id est pilosus dictus est.

« Protinus alter, » etc. (ISID.) Hoc figuraliter factum etiam Judaeis non credentibus notum est: qualiter populus Ecclesiae populum Synagogae superavit, et plebs Judaeorum, tempore major, servivit 146Dminori populo Christianorum.

(BED. in hoc cap., tom. II.) In singulis quoque hoc dici potest. « Duae gentes et duo populi » intra nos, sunt, scilicet vitiorum et virtutum; iste minor, ille major: plures enim sunt mali quam boni, et vitia plura virtutibus. Sed tamen populus populum superat, « et major serviet minori, » caro scilicet spiritui, et vitia virtutibus. Praecedit Esau, rufus, et totus ut pellis hispidus: deinde exit Jacob tenens plantam fratris; prior enim populus, prophetarum et Christi sanguine pollutus, peccati et nequitiae squalore circumdatus: cujus minor plantam tenuit, quia majorem populum minor superavit.

VERS. 27, 28.-- « Factus est Esau, » etc. (GREG.) 147AEorum vitam significat, qui exterioribus voluptatibus fusi carnem sequuntur. Agricola quoque dicitur, quia amatores saeculi tanto colunt exteriora quanto interiora sua inculta relinquunt.

« Simplex. » (GREG.) Quia curis exterioribus spargi refugiunt simplices, cogitatione et conscientiae habitatione consistunt. Habitat enim in tabernaculis, qui se intra mentis secreta constringit, ne dum ad multa foris inhiat, a seipso cogitatione alienatus recedat.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « Coxit autem Jacob, » etc. (HIERON.) Rubeum vel fulvum: Hebraice Edom dicitur: ab eo autem quod rubeo cibo Esau primogenita vendidit, fulvi, id est Edom nomen accepit.

VERS. 31, 32.-- « Cui dixit Jacob, » etc. (ISID.) 147BQuod Esau primogenita propter escam fratri minori vendidit, et postea paterna benedictione sibi promissa privatus est, Judaicum populum significat: qui in Exodo primogenitus Dei filius appellatur, et propter saeculi lucra non solum primatus sui honorem amisit, sed et coelestis regni praemium praeparatum adipisci non meruit. Unde per exprobrationem dicitur Matth. XII: « Auferetur a vobis regnum Dei et dabitur gentibus, » etc. Primogenitura autem vestis erat sacerdotalis, qua induti majores natu cum benedictione patris victimas Deo, velut pontifices, offerebant: hoc dono, terreni amoris desiderio, caruerunt Judaei, cum gloria futuri regni.

VERS. 33, 34.-- « Juravit Esau, » etc. (GREG., lib. XXX, Moral., c. 13.) Sciendum autem quod nos quinque 147Cmodis gulae vitium tentat, etc., usque ad et non quae edendi libido suggerit.

CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « But Abraham took another wife, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorically. Sara being dead, Abraham took Cetura. Not on account of incontinence, since he was already of great age: nor on account of the procuring of children, since seed as the stars of heaven had been promised him from Isaac. But as Agar and Ismael figured the carnal men of the Old Testament, so Cetura and her sons the heretics, who 144Cdeem themselves to belong to the New Testament. But both were concubines, Sara alone the wife; whence Cant. VI: « One is my dove, » etc.

Allegorically. After the assumption of the Church many souls deceived by heretical depravity clave to Christ, and these brought forth not catholics but schismatics, who divided the unity of the Church: whom the sons of Cetura signify, who are numbered below.

(HIER., in Q. Hebr. ) Cetura, in Hebrew, joined, etc., as far as for the more a man is wearied in the flesh, the more robust is he in spirit and the fitter for the embraces of wisdom.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « The sons of Dadan were Assurim, and Lathusim, and Laomim. But of Madian was born Epha and Opher and Henoc and Abida. » (HIER.) They think that Assurim is to be rendered merchants, etc., as far as 144Dand that the regions of India were occupied by very many of the sons of Abraham by Cetura.

VERS. 5-7.-- « And Abraham gave, » etc. (ISID.) Because gifts are given to the sons of the concubines, that is, of carnal men; but heretics, or Jews, do not attain to the promised kingdom, because they follow carnal gains; Isaac alone is the heir, because not the children of the flesh are counted in the seed, but of the promise.

VERS. 8-11.-- « And decaying he died in a good old age, and of advanced years, and full of days, and was gathered to his people. And they buried him, » etc. (HIERON.) it is wrongly added in the LXX: « Abraham decaying died, » because it does not befit Abraham to decay or to be diminished. That also 145Awhich we have set down « in a good old age an old man, » and which is put more fully in the Greek codices, « full of days; » which, though it seem to expound the sense, in that he died full of light and of the works of day, yet makes the more for the anagogy if it be simply set down « full. »

( Glossa Graeca. ) Behold, Moses knew the life of the world to come, for no man can be added to those who are not.

VERS. 12.-- « These are the generations, » etc. These peoples are reckoned before the sons of Isaac, who, although they be of the seed of Abraham, are yet not reckoned in the seed, because that which is animal is first, then that which is spiritual. Whence after the reckoning of the carnal, the genealogy of Isaac is set down, of whom the promise was made, and the succession of the elect to be propagated.

145BVERS. 13-15.-- « And these are the names of his sons in their titles and generations. The firstborn of Ismael Nabajoth, then Cedar, and Abel, » [Vulg. Adbeel] etc. (HIERON.) Twelve sons are born to Ismael, the firstborn Nabajoth, from whom all the region from the Euphrates to the Red Sea is called Nabathea, which is a part of Arabia. For their families, towns, villages and tribes are celebrated by the naming of these. From one of these, Cedar in the desert, and Duma another region, and Thema to the south, and Chedma, otherwise Chethma, to the eastern quarter, is named. As to what we read at the end of the chapter according to the LXX, « he dwelt over against the face of all his brethren, » it is truer which we have set down, « he fell asleep before all his brethren, » that is, he died in the hands of his sons, his children 145Cyet surviving. For sons are called brethren; whence Jacob: « Let it be set before my brethren and thy brethren, and let them judge between us. » [Vulg. Gn 31:37 pone hic … et judicent inter me et te, "put it here … and let them judge between me and thee"] For it is not to be believed that Jacob, apart from his own children, had any brethren with him.

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Twelve princes of their tribes. And the years of the life of Ismael were, » etc. The number twelve, as it is consecrated in the apostles and in apostolic men, so is it execrable in the reprobate against the faith of the Trinity and the Gospel spread abroad through the four quarters of the world.
