VERS. 18, 19.-- « And he dwelt from Hevila as far as Sur, which looketh towards Egypt, » etc. The region which Phison went about was so called from Hevila, the grandson of Noe, and it is a wilderness over against the face of Egypt. Sur also 145Dis a wilderness between Cades and Barad, stretching the desert as far as the Red Sea and the borders of Egypt.

VERS. 20.-- « From Mesopotamia the sister of Laban. » A barren region, which is so called in Greek because it lies in the middle between two rivers, the Tigris and the Euphrates. For μέσος is said middle, ποταμὸς a river; hence hippopotamus, that is, a river horse.

VERS. 21, 22.-- « And he besought . . . but the little ones struggled in her womb, » etc. (HIER.) For the stirring or the collision the LXX put « they played » or « they kicked back: » Aquila, « the sons fought in her womb. » Symmachus, « they were borne upon the surface after the likeness of a ship. » (GREG., Dial. I, 8.) The things which the saints 146Aeffect by praying, etc., as far as for he in whom God had predestined to multiply the seed of Abraham obtained by prayer that he might have sons.

« Who heard her. » [Vulg. eum, "him" — Isaac, who is the one that prayed] (CHRYSOST.) Think not that he obtained forthwith and at once what he desired: for he continued twenty years praying and beseeching God. For as he taught us how many years he had when he took Rebecca (namely forty), so he assigned to us of how many years Isaac was when sons were born to him. « He was sixty years old, he says, when the little ones were born. »

« And she went, » etc., (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) It is asked: whither did she go, since there were as yet no prophets or priests of the tabernacle or of the temple of the Lord? Perhaps to the place where Abraham set up an altar. 146BBut how answers were given there, Scripture is silent; was it through some priest? And it is a wonder, if there was one, that he was not named, and that no mention of priests was made. Perhaps there, when they had laid their desires before God in prayer, they slept, that they might be admonished through a dream: or Melchisedech was yet living, whose excellence was so great that it was doubted whether he were a man or an angel. Or perhaps even at that time there were men of God, in whom God could be consulted. Whichever of these, or perhaps somewhat else, it was. Rebecca however according to the Scripture consulted the Lord, and deserved his answer.

VERS. 23.-- « Who answering said: Two nations, » etc. (AUG.) By historical propriety the answer is found fulfilled, when for Israel's sake, that is, Jacob's, 146Cthe younger son overcame the Idumeans, that is, the nation which Esau propagated, and made them tributaries through David; which they were for a long time, until king Joram (IV Reg. VIII). For then the Idumeans rebelled, and shook the yoke from their neck, as Isaac had prophesied when he blessed the younger before the elder, and said this same thing to the elder, when he afterwards blessed him.

VERS. 24-26.-- « The time was now come, » etc. (HIER.) Where we have put « hairy », in the Hebrew there stands seyr ( ); whence Esau, as we read elsewhere, was called seyr, that is, hairy.

« Forthwith the other, » etc. (ISID.) That this was done figuratively is known even to the Jews who believe not: how the people of the Church overcame the people of the Synagogue, and the people of the Jews, the greater in time, served 146Dthe lesser people of the Christians.

(BED. in hoc cap., tom. II.) In individual men also this may be said. « Two nations and two peoples » are within us, namely of the vices and of the virtues; the one lesser, the other greater: for the evil are more than the good, and the vices more than the virtues. Yet the people overcomes the people, « and the elder shall serve the younger, » namely the flesh the spirit, and the vices the virtues. Esau goes before, red, and all hairy as a skin: then comes forth Jacob holding his brother's heel; for the former people, polluted with the blood of the prophets and of Christ, was encompassed with the squalor of sin and of wickedness: whose heel the younger held, because the lesser people overcame the greater.

VERS. 27, 28.-- « Esau became, » etc. (GREG.) 147AIt signifies the life of those who, poured out upon outward pleasures, follow the flesh. He is also called a husbandman, because lovers of the world cultivate outward things the more, the more they leave their own inward things untilled.

« Simple. » (GREG.) Because simple men shun to be scattered abroad by outward cares, and abide in thought and in the dwelling of conscience. For he dwells in tents who binds himself within the secret places of the mind, lest, while he gapes outwards after many things, he depart from himself, estranged in thought.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « And Jacob boiled, » etc. (HIERON.) Red or tawny: in Hebrew it is called Edom: and from this, that Esau sold his birthright for red food, he received the name of the tawny, that is, of Edom.

VERS. 31, 32.-- « To whom Jacob said, » etc. (ISID.) 147BThat Esau sold his birthright to his younger brother for food, and was afterwards deprived of the paternal blessing promised him, signifies the Jewish people: who in Exodus is called the firstborn son of God, and for the gains of the world not only lost the honour of his primacy, but also deserved not to obtain the prepared reward of the heavenly kingdom. Whence by way of reproach it is said Matth. XII: « The kingdom of God shall be taken away from you and shall be given to the Gentiles, » [Vulg. Mt 21:43 dabitur genti facienti fructus ejus, "shall be given to a nation yielding the fruits thereof"] etc. But the birthright was a priestly garment, clothed in which the elder-born offered victims to God with their father's blessing, as pontiffs: of which gift, through desire of earthly love, the Jews were bereft, together with the glory of the kingdom to come.

VERS. 33, 34.-- « Esau swore, » etc. (GREG., lib. XXX, Moral., c. 13.) But it is to be known that the vice of gluttony tempts us in five 147Cways, etc., as far as and not the things which the lust of eating suggests.

CAPUT XXVI.

VERS. 1-7.-- « Orta autem fame super terram, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Quaeritur quando hoc factum fuerit, quod Isaac abiit in Gerara, etc., usque ad ut intelligamus temporalia nec dari posse, nec prosperari debere, nisi ab uno Deo.

VERS. 8-11.-- « Cumque pertransissent, » etc. (ISID.) A tempore Christi usque ad Constantini tempora non in conventu populorum, sed tanquam per latebras praedicabatur fides, in secreto congregabantur Christiani: tandem videntes Christiani principes saeculi per miracula Christi majestatem contemperatam 147Dhumanae infirmitati, ut possint sociari, et esse « duo in carne una, » jam in manifesto Christi et Ecclesiae sacramentum praedicari permiserunt, et diu fuisse absconditum doluerunt.

« Vidit eum jocantem, » etc. (AUG.) Quid sibi velit, in sacramento Christi et Ecclesiae, etc., usque ad « ut habitaret in nobis. »

VERS. 12, 13.-- « Seminavit autem Isaac in terra illa, » etc. (HIERON.) Licet in aliena terra seminaverit Isaac, non puto quod tanta ei hordei fertilitas fuerit. Unde melius arbitror quod habetur in Hebraeo, et quod Aquila transtulit: « Invenit in illo anno centuplum aestimatum. » Tacens autem Scriptura genus frugum quod centuplicaverit, videtur 148Acunctarum in illo ostendisse virtutum multiplicationem.

VERS. 14-16.-- « Ob haec invidentes, » etc. (ISID.) Isaac, Christus, qui primum vult fodere puteos, etc., usque ad « ibimus. »

« Puteos et obstruxerunt, » etc. (GREG.) Moraliter. Saepe cum verbis divinis intendimus, daemonum insidias gravius toleramus; quae menti nostrae terrenarum cogitationum pulverem aspergunt, ut intentionis oculos a luce intimae visionis obscurent. Nos enim puteos fodimus, cum Scripturae alta penetramus, quos occulte Allophyli replent: quia terrenas cogitationes ingerunt et divinae scientiae aquam tollunt, unde psal. CXVIII: « Declinate a me, maligni, et scrutabor mandata Domini. »

148BVERS. 17.-- « Et ille discedens, » etc. (HIERON.) Et abiit inde Isaac, et venit in Geraram vallem, et habitavit ibi. Pro valle, torrentem habent in Hebraeo. Nunquam enim Isaac, postquam magnus factus est, habitare potuit in valle, sed in torrente. De quo scriptum est psal. CIX: « De torrente in via bibet. » De quo Elias in tempore famis bibit; sed quia non erat perfectus ut Christus, torrens ei aruit. Christus vero etiam in torrente traditus est, dedicans regenerationem nostram et baptismi sacramentum.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « Fodit alio puteos, » etc. (ISID. ex Greg.) Isaac apud alienigenam gentem puteos fodit: in quo monemur ut in hac peregrinatione cogitationum profunda penetremus, et quousque verae intelligentiae aqua redeat, inquisitionis manus ad 148Cexhaurienda cordis terrena non torpescat. Allophyli insidiantes puteos replent, quia immundi spiritus, cum nos studiose fodere conspiciunt, molestias tentationum cogitationibus ingerunt.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « Adversus pastores, » etc. (HIERON.) Aquila et Symmachus, « adversum et contrarium, » transtulerunt, sed in Hebraeo habetur « Satana; » unde patet Satanam contrarium interpretari.

VERS. 22-25.-- « Latitudo. » In Hebraeo Rehobot (רחבות), latitudines. Unde probatur illud quod supra cap. X dictum est: « Ipse aedificavit Niniven » et rabooth, id est, plateas ejus.

« Nunc dilatavit, » etc. Dilatatus est Isaac, et replevit omnem terram scientia Trinitatis: et in toto 148Dorbe latitudinem Ecclesiae collocavit, prius tamen in Judaea notus, et in Israel nominatus: postea « in omnem terram exivit sonus eorum (Psal. XVIII), » id est apostolorum, qui per universum orbem foderunt puteos, et aqua omnibus ostenderunt baptizantes omnes gentes in nomine Patris, et Filii et Spiritus sancti (Matth. XVIII). » Puteos Abraham, quos aperuit, sic vocavit sicut pater ejus: Moses enim et prophetae apud nos suis nominibus appellantur, nec horum mutantur vocabula puteorum.

VERS. 26-31.-- « Ad quem locum, » etc. (HIERON.) Abimelech non semper pacem habet cum Isaac, etc., usque ad et alia plura sunt in quibus nobiscum philosophi dissident, vel concordant.

149APro « Ochozath, » pronubo, in Hebraeo habetur « collegium amicorum ejus, » ut tam hominem significet quam amicorum turbam, quae cum rege venerat: in quibus fuit Phicol, princeps exercitus illius.

« Abimelech, et Ochozath, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Duo cum Abimelech venerunt: Ochozath, gener ejus, etc., usque ad sapientibus et insipientibus debitor est.

VERS. 32-35.-- « Ecce autem venerunt. » (HIER.) Nescio quomodo in LXX « et venerunt pueri Isaac nuntiantes ei de puteo quem foderunt, et dixerunt ei: Non invenimus aquam. Et vocavit nomen ejus Juramentum. » Quae enim etymologia est propterea vocari Juramentum, etc., usque ad non congruit 149But Isaac aquam non inveniret.

CHAPTER XXVI.

VERS. 1-7.-- « Now a famine having arisen upon the land, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) It is asked when this was done, that Isaac went into Gerara, etc., as far as that we may understand that temporal things can neither be given, nor ought to prosper, save from the one God.

VERS. 8-11.-- « And when they had passed through, » etc. (ISID.) From the time of Christ until the times of Constantine the faith was preached not in the assembly of the peoples, but as it were through hiding-places, and the Christians were gathered together in secret: at length the princes of the world, being now Christians, seeing through the miracles of Christ his majesty tempered 147Dto human infirmity, that they might be joined together and be « two in one flesh, » permitted the sacrament of Christ and the Church to be preached now openly, and grieved that it had long lain hidden.

« He saw him sporting, » etc. (AUG.) What it means, in the sacrament of Christ and the Church, etc., as far as « that he might dwell in us. »

VERS. 12, 13.-- « Now Isaac sowed in that land, » [Vulg. Gen. 26:12 Sevit, "planted"] etc. (HIERON.) Although Isaac sowed in a foreign land, I do not think that so great a fruitfulness of barley was his. Whence I judge it better what is had in the Hebrew, and what Aquila translated: « He found in that year a hundredfold reckoned. » But Scripture, keeping silence as to the kind of crops which yielded a hundredfold, seems 148Ato have shown in him the multiplication of all virtues.

VERS. 14-16.-- « For these things envying, » etc. (ISID.) Isaac, Christ, who first wills to dig wells, etc., as far as « we shall go. »

« The wells they also stopped up, » etc. (GREG.) Morally. Often, when we are intent upon the divine words, we endure the more grievously the snares of demons; which sprinkle upon our mind the dust of earthly thoughts, that they may darken the eyes of our purpose from the light of the inward vision. For we dig wells when we penetrate the deep things of Scripture, which the Allophyli secretly fill up: because they thrust in earthly thoughts and take away the water of divine knowledge, whence in psalm CXVIII: « Depart from me, O malignant ones, and I will search the commandments of the Lord. »

148BVERS. 17.-- « And he departing, » etc. (HIERON.) And Isaac went thence, and came into the valley of Gerara, and dwelt there. In place of valle, torrentem they have in the Hebrew. For never, after he was made great, could Isaac dwell in a valley, but in a torrent. Of which it is written in psalm CIX: « He shall drink of the torrent in the way. » Of which Elias drank in the time of famine; but because he was not perfect as Christ, the torrent dried up for him. But Christ also was delivered up in the torrent, dedicating our regeneration and the sacrament of baptism.

VERS. 18, 19.-- « He dug wells elsewhere, » etc. (ISID. ex Greg.) Isaac dug wells among a foreign nation: wherein we are admonished that in this pilgrimage we penetrate the deep things of our thoughts, and that, until the water of true understanding return, the hand of inquiry grow not sluggish in 148Cdraining out the earthly things of the heart. The Allophyli, lying in wait, fill up the wells, because unclean spirits, when they behold us digging earnestly, thrust the vexations of temptations into our thoughts.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « Against the shepherds, » etc. (HIERON.) Aquila and Symmachus translated « adverse and contrary, » but in the Hebrew there is « Satana; » whence it is plain that Satan is interpreted contrary.

VERS. 22-25.-- « Breadth. » In the Hebrew Rehobot ( ) [Migne prints a Hebrew word within these parentheses; our digitized source has lost it and left them empty. Recovery requires the plate.], breadths. Whence that is proved which was said above in chapter X: « He himself built Ninive » and rabooth, that is, the streets thereof.

« Now he hath enlarged, » etc. Isaac was enlarged, and filled the whole earth with the knowledge of the Trinity: and in the whole 148Dworld he set the breadth of the Church, being nevertheless first known in Judea, and named in Israel: afterwards « their sound went forth into all the earth (Psal. XVIII), » that is, the sound of the apostles, who throughout the universal world dug wells, and by water showed [it] to all, baptizing all nations in the name of the Father, and of the Son and of the Holy Ghost (Matth. XVIII). » The wells of Abraham, which he opened, he called by the same names as his father: for Moses and the prophets are called among us by their own names, nor are the names of these wells changed.

VERS. 26-31.-- « To which place, » etc. (HIERON.) Abimelech has not always peace with Isaac, etc., as far as and there are many other things in which the philosophers disagree with us, or agree.

149AIn place of « Ochozath, » pronubo, there is in the Hebrew « the company of his friends, » so that it may signify both the man and the crowd of friends which had come with the king: among whom was Phicol, the prince of his army.

« Abimelech, and Ochozath, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Two came with Abimelech: Ochozath, his son-in-law, etc., as far as he is a debtor to the wise and to the unwise.

VERS. 32-35.-- « But behold, they came. » (HIER.) I know not how in the LXX « and the servants of Isaac came, telling him of the well which they had dug, and they said to him: We have not found water. And he called the name of it, Oath. » For what etymology there is, that it should for this cause be called Oath, etc., as far as it does not agree 149Bthat Isaac should not find water.

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Senuit autem Isaac, » etc. (HIPP. mart., ex Hieron., epist. ad Damasum, tom. I.) Mystice. Isaac portat imaginem Dei Patris, Rebecca Spiritus sancti, Esau populi prioris et zabuli, Jacob Ecclesiae et Christi. Senectus Isaac, consummationem mundi; oculi caligantes periisse fidem de mundo, et religionis lumen neglectum esse, significant. Quia filius major vocatur, acceptio est legis Judaeorum. Quia escas ejus et capturam dilexit pater: homines sunt ab errore salvati, quos per doctrinam justus quisque venatur. Sermo Dei, repromissionis benedictio, et spes regni futuri, in quo cum Christo 149Csunt regnaturi, et verum sabbatum celebraturi. Rebecca plena Spiritu sancto, sciens quod audisset antequam pareret, quia « major serviet minori: » haec formam gerit Spiritus sancti, quae quod futurum esse noverat in Christo, ante meditabatur in Jacob, loquitur ad filium minorem. « Vade ad gregem et affer mihi duos haedos optimos; » praefigurans carneum Salvatoris adventum, in quo eos liberaret qui peccatis tenebantur obnoxii. Haedi enim ubique peccatores significant. Duo afferri jubentur, ut duorum populorum assumptio significetur. Teneri scilicet et humani, docibiles scilicet et innocentis animae. Stola Esau est fides, id est Scripturae Hebraeorum quae illis primo datae sunt quibus populus gentilium postea indutus; pelles circumdatae 149Dbrachiis ejus, peccata utriusque populi, quae Christus in extensione manuum cruci secum affixit: ipse enim in corpore suo, non sua, sed aliena peccata portabat.

VERS. 2.-- « Vocavitque Esau. » (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Esau venator, et agricola Jacob vero simplex habitabat in tabernaculis. Esau populum Hebraicum, Jacob gentilem populum significat. Isaac vero Deum, qui utrumque populum, illum per legem, istum per fidem, sibi filios fecit. Esau agricola erat, quia Judaeus pro terrenis Deo serviebat, cui non est dictum, ut esset pauper spiritu, mitis, pacificus, misericors, persecutionem patiens, ut copiosam mercedem haberet in coelis; sed: « Si 150Acustodieritis mandata mea, dabo vobis pluvias temporibus suis, » etc., quae pertinent ad carnis delicias. Fuit etiam venator, quia per effusionem sanguinis arietum et vitulorum, et hircorum, Deum placabat. « Jacob simplex habitabat in tabernaculis, » quia electi de gentibus fide et voluntate Deo placere studuerunt. Per tabernacula, diversas per orbem Ecclesias vel diversos ordines intelligimus

VERS. 3-5.-- « Sume arma, » etc. Isaac diligebat Esau, quia de venatione ejus vescebatur. Rebecca diligebat Jacob, Isaac Deum; Rebecca autem praescientiam Dei figurat, quia diligebat Deus populum Judaeorum, eo quod assidue eorum oblationibus placabatur. Sed praesciens populum gentium multo meliorem futurum, per praescientiae suae gratiam 150Bamplius diligebat. Esau in venatione morante, ut de agrestibus animalibus cibos patri exhiberet et benediceretur, mater Jacob ad benedictionem percipiendam patri applicuit: quia adhuc Judaei in legalibus morantur, et ad fidem Christi tarde occurrunt, mater gratia gentiles fidem avide suscipientes ad percipiendam benedictionem Judaeis debitam obtulit. Qualiter autem benedictionis donum promeruerint, testantur opera Jacob, quae pro benedictione suscipienda gessit.

VERS. 6-13. « Dixit filio suo Jacob: Audivi patrem tuum loquentem, » etc. Allegorice. Per duos haedos confessionem peccatorum gentilis populi et poenitentiam possumus accipere. Haedi optimi, quia confessio peccatorum et poenitentia valde est Deo acceptabilis. 150CUnde Luc. XV: « Gaudium erit in coelo super uno peccatore poenitentiam agente, » etc. Historice. Per unum peccatorem populum gentium, per nonaginta novem justos, Judaeos intelligit qui sibi videbantur justi in observantia legis. Unde Luc. XVI: « Ut quid justificatis vos coram hominibus, » etc.; qui imperfecto numero ponuntur, quia nullum ad perfectum adduxit lex.

VERS. 14.-- « Paravit illa cibos, » etc. (ISID.) Mystice. Per delectabiles escas quae de haedis praeparantur Isaac, intelligimus poenitentiae opera, etc., usque ad peccata praeterita ante oculos mentis saepe producit.

VERS. 15-19.-- « Et vestibus Esau, » etc. (HIER.) In hoc loco tradunt Hebraei primogenitos functos 150Dofficio sacerdotum, et habuisse vestimentum sacerdotale, quo induti victimas offerebant, antequam Aaron in sacerdotium eligeretur.

VERS. 20-24.-- « Quomodo, inquit, tam cito invenire potuisti, fili mi? » (HIPPOLYT.) Quaerit Isaac a Jacob cur tam cito venerit, admiratus scilicet velocem credentium fidem. Cibi delectabiles offeruntur, quia hostia Deo placens, salus peccatorum. Quia post esum sequitur benedictio, et ejus odore perfruitur, virtutem resurrectionis et regni aperte pronuntiat. « Ecce odor filii mei sicut odor agri pleni. » Haec est benedictio. Odore nominis Christi, sicut ager, mundus impletur. Benedictio est de rore coeli, id est pluvia divini verbi; et de pinguedine terrae, 151Aid est congregatione populorum; multitudine frumenti et vini, haec est multitudo, quam colligit de sacramento corporis et sanguinis sui. Illi serviunt populi, id est gentiles conversi. Ipsum adorant tribus populi, per circumcisionem conversi credentes. Ipse est Dominus fratrum suorum, id est Judaeorum. Ipsum adorant filii matris ejus, quia secundum carnem natus est ex ea: ipsum qui maledixerit, maledicetur; et qui benedixerit, benedicetur. Christus ex ore populi Patrem ignorantis benedicitur, id est, veraciter dicitur, sed a Judaeis alius benedici putatur, qui ab errantibus exspectatur.

VERS. 25, 26.-- « Quos cum oblatos comedisset, » etc. Mystice. Postquam Isaac cibum sumpsit, Jacob obtulit ei vinum: sic per cibum activae vitae 151Bopera designantur; per vinum, quod mentes alienat, contemplativa accipitur, quae ab amore saeculi mentes electorum alienas facit. Post cibum et potum. Jacob benedicitur, quia illi benedictionem coelestem a Deo percipiunt qui eum usque in finem bonis operibus placare student.

VERS. 27.-- « Benedicens, ait, » etc. (GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.) Alter ad venandum mittitur, etc., usque ad quae major foras exiens reliquit intus.

(GREG., ibid.) Isaac, caligantibus oculis, Jacob filium nesciens benedixit; quae ventura erant praevidit, et qui praesens assisteret, nescivit. Constat enim quia Judaei, qui plene legem didicerunt, adventum Christi cognoverunt. Unde Matth. II, Herodes a 151Cprincipibus sacerdotum inquirit ubi Christus nasceretur; cui protinus responderunt, « In Bethlehem. » Prius ergo noverant quem passionis tempore cum despicerent, ignorabant; quorum notitia prior, et ignorantia posterior, Isaac caligante signatur; qui dum Jacob benediceret, quid in futuro ei eveniret praevidebat, sed quod praesens assisteret nesciebat. Sic Judaeorum populus prophetiae mysteria accepit, sed caecos oculos in contemplatione tenuit, quia praesentem non vidit, de quo multa in futuro praevidit. Ante se enim positum cernere non valuit, cujus adventus potentiam longe ante nuntiavit.

(GREG., hom. 10 in Ezech., tom. II.) Isaac filium nescit, quem benedixit, etc., usque ad et quia per quosdam in contemplationem surgit, per quosdam in 151Dactivae vitae opera pinguescit. Sequitur:

VERS. 28, 29.-- « Det tibi Deus de rore coeli, » etc. Ros desuper et subtiliter cadit, et toties de rore coeli accipimus, quoties per infusionem contemplationis intimae de supernis aliquid tenuiter videmus. Cum vero bona opera per corpus agimus, de terrae pinguedine ditamur.

« Abundantiam frumenti, » etc. (ISID.) Haec est multitudo quam colligit de sacramento corporis et sanguinis sui.

VERS. 30-32.-- « Venit Esau, » etc. Sero Esau ad patrem revertitur, quia prior populus in fine convertetur. Unde psal. LVIII: « Convertentur ad vesperam, et famem patientur ut canes, et circumibunt 152Acivitatem. » Et alibi: « Aperuerunt ora sua, sicut comedens in abscondito. »

VERS. 33-35. « Expavit Isaac. » Benedictionem repromissam repetente majore filio, expavit Isaac, et alium pro alio se benedixisse cognovit; nec indignatur cognito sacramento, sed confirmabat benedictionem dicens: « Et benedixi eum, et benedictus est. » Haec est prima benedictio Isaac minori populo Christianorum data. Sed nec major filius omnino despectus, quia, cum intraverit plenitudo gentium, tunc omnis Israel salvus erit. Cujus secundae benedictionis prophetia est. « In pinguedine terrae, et in rore coeli desuper erit benedictio tua. « In pinguedidine terrae, » id est, in fecunditate rerum, et potentia regni, quae in illo populo fuit; « et in rore coeli, » 152Beloquiis Dei. Ipsis enim eloquia Dei credita sunt, et legis testamenta. « Vives in gladio: » quia sanguini populus ille deditus, necem in Christo et prophetis exercuit, « Et servies fratri tuo minori, » id est, populo Christiano. « Tempusque veniet, cum excutias et solvas jugum ejus de cervicibus tuis; » cum scilicet per cognitionem fidei et gratiam Christi conversus, deposueris onus legis; quando jam tunc non servies minori, sed per fidem frater vocaberis.

« Expavit Isaac, » etc. Ut quaedam mentis alienatio sequeretur, quae exstasis dicitur, quae in magnarum rerum revelationibus fieri solet. Unde intelligendum est spiritualiter admonitum esse Isaac, ut confirmaret benedictionem in Jacob, cui potius 152Cirascendum fuit, quia patrem fefellit. In Adam quoque exstasis processit, antequam diceret: « Erunt duo in carne una (Gen. II), » quod est « magnum sacramentum in Christo et in Ecclesia. »

« Benedixique ei, » etc. (ALC.) Si justi viri voluntas bona est, quid est quod Isaac non Esau quem voluit, sed Jacob quem noluit, benedixit? Justi hominis, quantum ad conscientiam, voluntas bona est: Deus vero solus de futuris judicat. Isaac humano more filium majorem benedicendum putavit: sed Deus, qui occultorum est cognitor, minorem dignum benedictione ostendit, ut non hominis ostenderet esse benedictionem, sed Dei: ideo in Numeris ad Mosen et Aaron sacerdotes dicitur: « Ponite nomen meum super filios Israel. Ego Dominus 152Dbenedicam eos (Num. VI). » Sacerdotis est benedicere, Dei effectum tribuere. Ut autem intellexit Isaac per spiritum prophetiae benedictionem minori destinatam, ait, « Benedixi, et erit benedictus. »

VERS. 36-39.-- « Juste vocatum est nomen ejus Jacob: Supplantavit enim me, » etc. (HIER.) Jacob supplantator interpretatur. Ab eo igitur quod fratrem ante deceperit, allusit ad nomen, qui ideo ante Jacob vocatus est, quod in ortu plantam fratris apprehendit.

VERS. 40.-- « Et fratri tuo servies, » etc. Quia Idumaei tributarii fuerunt Israel, qui Jacob vocatur.

« Tempusque veniet, cum excutias et solvas jugum ejus. » , etc. Hoc impletum est quando rebellaverunt 153AIdumaei ne essent sub Juda. « Tempusque veniet, » etc. Quasi: Judaicus populus a servitute peccati in fine liberabitur, ut cum « plenitudo gentium intraverit, omnis Israel salvus fiet (Rom. XI). »

VERS. 41.-- « Dixitque in corde suo. » Cum Scriptura dicat, Esau in corde suo de morte fratris tractasse, quomodo vel a quo sunt haec nuntiata Rebeccae? Sed datur intelligi quod ei divinitus omnia revelabantur. Propter insanabile odium Judaeorum, per spiritum consilii praeceptum est apostolis ut, relicta Judaea, transirent ad gentes.

« Venient dies luctus, » etc. Nolebat enim hoc facere vivente patre, ne incurreret odium ejus, et malediceretur ab eo.

(DIOD.) Quod autem scriptum est, « dixit Esau in 153Bcorde suo, » significat Esau mortem fratris non subita ira, sed perfecto mentis consensu exoptasse.

VERS. 42.-- « Nuntiata sunt haec Rebeccae, » etc. Hic consequenter ponitur matris obviatio, cum dicitur, « Nuntiata sunt haec. » Ex quo patet quod Esau praedictam machinationem non sic celavit in corde suo, quin appareret exterius in signis et verbis.

« Quae mittens et vocans Jacob, » etc. Ut induceret eum ad dandum locum irae et recedendum ad tempus. Sequitur:

« Cur utroque orbabor filio? » etc. Ac si diceret: Si invadat te, poterit esse quod ambo eritis interfecti; vel si unus remaneat, oportebit quod sit fugitivus a me.

153C« Dixit quoque Rebecca, » etc. Quia Jacob non poterat convenienter recedere sine scitu patris, ideo Rebecca convertit se ad inducendum Isaac ut mitteret Jacob filium suum ad Laban, ut ibi acciperet uxorem dicens.

« Taedet me, » etc. Id est propter uxores Esau: non autem dixit ipsi Isaac aliam causam, scilicet de machinatione Esau in mortem Jacob, ne in senectute sua nimis affligeretur.

« Si acceperit Jacob uxorem, » etc. Ac si diceret: Si diligis vitam meam, mitte eum ad domum fratris mei, ut ibi accipiat uxorem.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Now Isaac grew old, » etc. (HIPP. mart., ex Hieron., epist. ad Damasum, tom. I.) Mystically. Isaac bears the image of God the Father, Rebecca of the Holy Ghost, Esau of the former people and of the devil, Jacob of the Church and of Christ. The old age of Isaac signifies the consummation of the world; his darkening eyes, that faith has perished from the world, and that the light of religion has been neglected. Because he is called the elder son, it is the receiving of the law of the Jews. Because the father loved his meats and his hunting: they are men saved from error, whom every just man hunts by his teaching. The word of God, the blessing of the promise, and the hope of the kingdom to come, in which with Christ 149Cthey are to reign, and to celebrate the true sabbath. Rebecca, full of the Holy Ghost, knowing what she had heard before she bore, that « the elder shall serve the younger: » she bears the form of the Holy Ghost, who, what she knew was to be in Christ, beforehand meditated in Jacob, speaks to the younger son. « Go to the flock and bring me two of the best kids; » prefiguring the fleshly coming of the Saviour, in which he should set free those who were held liable to sins. For kids everywhere signify sinners. Two are commanded to be brought, that the taking up of the two peoples might be signified. That is, of a tender and human, that is, of a teachable and innocent soul. The robe of Esau is the faith, that is, the Scriptures of the Hebrews which were first given to them, with which the Gentile people was afterwards clothed; the skins put about 149Dhis arms, the sins of both peoples, which Christ, in the stretching out of his hands, fastened with himself to the cross: for he in his own body bore not his own, but others' sins.

VERS. 2.-- « And he called Esau. » (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Esau a hunter, and a husbandman, but Jacob, a simple man, dwelt in tents. Esau signifies the Hebrew people, Jacob the Gentile people. But Isaac [signifies] God, who made both peoples sons to himself, the one by the law, the other by faith. Esau was a husbandman, because the Jew served God for earthly things, to whom it was not said that he should be poor in spirit, meek, peaceable, merciful, suffering persecution, that he might have a plentiful reward in the heavens; but: « If 150Ayou keep my commandments, I will give you rains in their seasons, » etc., which pertain to the delights of the flesh. He was also a hunter, because by the pouring out of the blood of rams and of calves, and of goats, he appeased God. « Jacob, a simple man, dwelt in tents, » because the elect of the Gentiles studied to please God by faith and by will. By the tents we understand the divers Churches throughout the world, or the divers orders

VERS. 3-5.-- « Take thy arms, » etc. Isaac loved Esau, because he fed upon his hunting. Rebecca loved Jacob, Isaac [signifies] God; but Rebecca figures the foreknowledge of God, because God loved the people of the Jews, in that he was continually appeased by their oblations. But foreknowing that the people of the Gentiles would be far better, by the grace of his foreknowledge 150Bhe loved them more abundantly. While Esau tarried at the hunt, that he might set meats from the wild beasts before his father and be blessed, the mother brought Jacob to his father to receive the blessing: because the Jews still tarry in the things of the law, and come late to the faith of Christ, the mother, grace, offered the Gentiles, eagerly taking up the faith, to receive the blessing owed to the Jews. But in what manner they earned the gift of the blessing, the works of Jacob bear witness, which he did in order to receive the blessing.

VERS. 6-13. « She said to her son Jacob: I have heard thy father speaking, » etc. Allegorically. By the two kids we may understand the confession of sins of the Gentile people and its penance. The best kids, because the confession of sins and penance is very acceptable to God. 150CWhence Luke XV: « There shall be joy in heaven upon one sinner doing penance, » etc. Historically. By the one sinner he understands the people of the Gentiles, by the ninety-nine just, the Jews, who seemed to themselves just in the observance of the law. Whence Luke XVI: « For what cause do you justify yourselves before men, » [Vulg. Luc. 16:15 Vos estis qui justificatis vos coram hominibus, "You are they who justify yourselves before men" — a statement, not a question] etc.; who are set down in an imperfect number, because the law brought no one to perfection.

VERS. 14.-- « She prepared meats, » etc. (ISID.) Mystically. By the delightful meats which are prepared for Isaac from the kids, we understand the works of penance, etc., as far as it often brings past sins before the eyes of the mind.

VERS. 15-19.-- « And with the garments of Esau, » etc. (HIER.) In this place the Hebrews hand down that the firstborn discharged 150Dthe office of priests, and had a priestly vestment, clothed wherein they offered victims, before Aaron was chosen unto the priesthood.

VERS. 20-24.-- « How, said he, couldst thou find it so quickly, my son? » (HIPPOLYT.) Isaac asks of Jacob why he had come so quickly, marvelling, that is, at the swift faith of believers. Delightful meats are offered, because a victim pleasing to God is the salvation of sinners. Because after the eating the blessing follows, and he enjoys the odour thereof, he openly declares the power of the resurrection and of the kingdom. « Behold the smell of my son as the smell of a full field. » This is the blessing. With the odour of the name of Christ, as a field, the world is filled. The blessing is of the dew of heaven, that is, the rain of the divine word; and of the fatness of the earth, 151Athat is, the gathering together of peoples; of the multitude of corn and wine, this is the multitude which he gathers from the sacrament of his body and blood. Peoples serve him, that is, the converted Gentiles. The tribes of the peoples adore him, believers converted through circumcision. He is the Lord of his brethren, that is, of the Jews. The sons of his mother adore him, because according to the flesh he was born of her: he that shall curse him, shall be cursed; and he that shall bless him, shall be blessed. Christ is blessed from the mouth of a people ignorant of the Father, that is, is truly spoken of, but by the Jews another is thought to be blessed, who is looked for by them that err.

VERS. 25, 26.-- « Which when he had eaten, being offered him, » etc. Mystically. After Isaac had taken food, Jacob offered him wine: so by the food the works of the active life 151Bare signified; by the wine, which estranges the mind, the contemplative is understood, which makes the minds of the elect estranged from the love of the world. After the food and the drink. Jacob is blessed, because they receive the heavenly blessing from God who study to appease him unto the end by good works.

VERS. 27.-- « Blessing him, he said, » etc. (GREG., hom. 6 in Ezech.) The one is sent to hunt, etc., as far as which the elder, going forth abroad, left within.

(GREG., ibid.) Isaac, with darkened eyes, blessed his son Jacob not knowing him; the things that were to come he foresaw, and who stood present he knew not. For it is certain that the Jews, who had fully learned the law, knew the coming of Christ. Whence in Matthew II, Herod inquires of 151Cthe chief priests where Christ should be born; and they answered him forthwith, « In Bethlehem. » Therefore they had known him before, whom, at the time of the passion, while they despised him, they knew not; whose earlier knowledge, and later ignorance, is signified by the darkening of Isaac; who, while he blessed Jacob, foresaw what should befall him in time to come, but knew not that he stood present. So the people of the Jews received the mysteries of prophecy, but held their eyes blind in contemplation, because they saw not him present, of whom they foresaw many things in time to come. For they were not able to discern him set before them, the power of whose coming they had long before announced.

(GREG., hom. 10 in Ezech., tom. II.) Isaac knows not the son whom he blessed, etc., as far as and because through some it rises up into contemplation, through some it grows fat in 151Dthe works of the active life. There follows:

VERS. 28, 29.-- « God give thee of the dew of heaven, » etc. Dew falls from above, and finely, and we receive of the dew of heaven as often as, by the infusion of inward contemplation, we see something finely of the things above. But when we do good works through the body, we are enriched from the fatness of the earth.

« Abundance of corn, » etc. (ISID.) This is the multitude which he gathers from the sacrament of his body and blood.

VERS. 30-32.-- « Esau came, » etc. Late does Esau return to his father, because the former people shall be converted at the end. Whence in psalm LVIII: « They shall return at evening, and shall suffer hunger like dogs, and shall go round about 152Athe city. » And elsewhere: « They opened their mouths, as one that eateth in secret. »

VERS. 33-35. « Isaac was affrighted. » When the elder son sought again the promised blessing, Isaac was affrighted, and knew that he had blessed the one for the other; nor is he indignant, the sacrament being known, but he confirmed the blessing, saying: « And I have blessed him, and he is blessed. » [Vulg. Gen. 27:33 benedixique ei, et erit benedictus, "and I have blessed him, and he shall be blessed"] This is the first blessing of Isaac, given to the lesser people of the Christians. But neither is the elder son altogether despised, because, when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, then all Israel shall be saved. Of which second blessing there is a prophecy. « In the fatness of the earth, and in the dew of heaven from above shall be thy blessing. « In the fatness pinguedidine of the earth, » that is, in the fruitfulness of things, and in the power of the kingdom, which was in that people; « and in the dew of heaven, » 152Bthe eloquences of God. For to them were entrusted the eloquences of God, and the testaments of the law. « Thou shalt live by the sword: » because that people, given over to blood, wrought slaughter upon Christ and the prophets, « And thou shalt serve thy younger brother, » [Vulg. Gen. 27:40 et fratri tuo servies, without minori] that is, the Christian people. « And the time shall come, when thou shalt shake off and loose his yoke from thy neck; » that is, when, converted through the knowledge of faith and the grace of Christ, thou shalt have laid down the burden of the law; when then thou shalt no longer serve the younger, but through faith shalt be called brother.

« Isaac was affrighted, » etc. So that a certain alienation of mind should follow, which is called ecstasy, which is wont to come to pass in the revelations of great things. Whence it is to be understood that Isaac was spiritually admonished to confirm the blessing upon Jacob, with whom he ought rather 152Cto have been angry, because he deceived his father. In Adam also an ecstasy went forth, before he said: « They shall be two in one flesh (Gen. II), » which is « a great sacrament in Christ and in the Church. »

« And I have blessed him, » etc. (ALC.) If the will of a just man is good, how is it that Isaac blessed not Esau whom he wished, but Jacob whom he wished not? The will of a just man, as far as pertains to conscience, is good: but God alone judges of things to come. Isaac after the manner of men thought that the elder son was to be blessed: but God, who is the knower of hidden things, showed the younger worthy of the blessing, that he might show the blessing to be not of man, but of God: therefore in Numbers it is said to Moses and Aaron, the priests: « Put my name upon the children of Israel. I the Lord 152Dwill bless them (Num. VI). » [Vulg. Num. 6:27 Invocabuntque nomen meum super filios Israel, et ego benedicam eis, "And they shall invoke my name upon the children of Israel, and I will bless them"] It belongs to the priest to bless, to God to grant the effect. But when Isaac understood through the spirit of prophecy that the blessing was destined to the younger, he said, « I have blessed him, and he shall be blessed. »

VERS. 36-39.-- « Rightly is his name called Jacob: for he hath supplanted me, » etc. (HIER.) Jacob is interpreted a supplanter. Therefore from this, that he had before deceived his brother, he alluded to the name, who was for this cause called Jacob before, because at his birth he took hold of his brother's heel.

VERS. 40.-- « And thou shalt serve thy brother, » etc. Because the Idumeans were tributaries to Israel, who is called Jacob.

« And the time shall come, when thou shalt shake off and loose his yoke. » , etc. This was fulfilled when the 153AIdumeans rebelled that they might not be under Juda. « And the time shall come, » etc. As if to say: The Jewish people shall at the end be set free from the servitude of sin, so that when « the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, all Israel shall be saved (Rom. XI). »

VERS. 41.-- « And he said in his heart. » Since Scripture says that Esau treated in his heart of his brother's death, how or by whom were these things told to Rebecca? But it is given to be understood that all things were divinely revealed to her. Because of the incurable hatred of the Jews, by the spirit of counsel it was commanded to the apostles that, Judea being left, they should pass over to the Gentiles.

« The days of mourning shall come, » etc. For he would not do this while his father lived, lest he should incur his hatred, and be cursed by him.

(DIOD.) But that which is written, « Esau said in 153Bhis heart, » signifies that Esau longed for his brother's death not with a sudden anger, but with a perfected consent of the mind.

VERS. 42.-- « These things were told to Rebecca, » etc. Here consequently is set down the mother's meeting of it, when it is said, « These things were told. » From which it is plain that Esau did not so hide the aforesaid contrivance in his heart, but that it appeared outwardly in signs and in words.

« Who sending and calling Jacob, » etc. That she might induce him to give place to wrath and to withdraw for a time. There follows:

« Why shall I be deprived of both my sons? » etc. As if she should say: If he assail thee, it may be that you shall both be slain; or if one remain, it will behove that he be a fugitive from me.

153C« Rebecca also said, » etc. Because Jacob could not fittingly withdraw without his father's knowledge, therefore Rebecca turned herself to inducing Isaac to send Jacob his son to Laban, that he might there take a wife, saying.

« I am weary, » etc. That is, because of the wives of Esau: but she did not tell Isaac himself another cause, namely of Esau's contrivance against the life of Jacob, lest in his old age he should be too much afflicted.

« If Jacob take a wife, » etc. As if she should say: If thou lovest my life, send him to the house of my brother, that he may there take a wife.

CAPUT XXVIII.

VERS. 1-9.-- « Vocavit itaque Isaac, » etc. (AUG., 153DQ. in Gen.) Quod habent Latini codices: « Vade in Mesopotamiam in domum Bathuel, » etc., Graeci habent: « Fuge in Mesopotamiam, » etc., ut intelligatur etiam Isaac intellexisse quod Esau de morte fratris cogitavit.

« Profectus vero. » (ISID.) Jacob fugiens dolos fratris, relicta domo patria, et parentibus, vadit in regionem longinquam, ut accipiat uxorem. Similiter Christus, relictis parentibus secundum carnem, id est Judaeis, et patria, id est Hierosolyma, et omnibus Judaeae regionibus, abiit in gentes, ut acciperet sibi Ecclesiam, secundum illud Oseae II: « Vocabo non plebem meam, » etc.

VERS. 10.-- « Igitur egressus Jacob, » etc. (GREG., 154Alib. V Moral., c. 11, tom. I.) In itinere dormire, etc., usque ad « quoniam suavis est Dominus. »

VERS. 11.-- « Tulit de lapidibus, » etc. (ISID.) Dormitio Jacob in itinere, mors Christi in cruce. Lapis capiti suppositus, Christus est secundum humanitatem, quem Jacob unxit, ut Christus signaretur, χρίστος enim Grece, unctus Latine. Caput autem Christus Deus. Lapis ergo capiti Jacob suppositus, signat humanitatem Christi conjunctam Deo assumenti. « Caput enim viri Christus, caput Christi Deus (I Cor. XI.)

VERS. 12.-- « Viditque in somnis, » etc. (GREG., Regist. l. I, ep. 24.) Jacob praedicatores signat qui non solum caput Ecclesiae, id est Christum contemplando 154Bappetunt, sed ad membra illius miserando descendunt: quod angeli ascendentes descendentesque demonstrant. Hinc Moses crebro tabernaculum intrat, et exit; et qui intus ad contemplationem rapitur, foris infirmantium negotiis urgetur; intus arcana considerat, foris onera carnalium portat. Qui de dubiis semper ad tabernaculum recurrit, coram testamenti arca Dominum consulit, exemplum rectoribus praebens, ut cum foris ambigunt quid disponant, ad mentem quasi ad tabernaculum redeant, et velut coram testamenti arca Dominum consulant, de quibus dubitant, apud se intus sacri eloquii paginas requirentes.

Moraliter. In itinere dormire est in via hujus saeculi ab impedimento actionum saecularium quiescere. 154CJacob dormiens angelos vidit: quia illi divina conspiciunt, qui ab appetitu temporalium rerum oculos claudunt: quos bene clausos diabolus aperuit dicens: « In quacunque die comederitis, aperientur oculi vestri (Gen. III). »

« Vidit in somnis, » etc. (ISID.) Angeli per scalam ascendentes et descendentes, evangelistae sunt et praedicatores Christi, ascendentes ad intelligendam Divinitatem; omnem creaturam excedentes, ut inveniant in principio Verbum apud Deum, per quem omnia facta sunt. Descendentes ut inveniant eum, « factum ex muliere, factum sub lege, » etc. In illa etiam scala a terra usque ad coelum, a carne usque ad spiritum: quia in illa carnales proficiendo, quasi ascendendo spirituales fiunt. Ad quos lacte nutriendos 154Dspirituales descendunt, quia non possunt eis loqui quasi spiritualibus, sed quasi carnalibus. Ipse est sursum in capite, ipse deorsum in corpore suo, id est Ecclesia; ipse scala, qui ait Joan. XIV: « Ego sum via, » etc. Ad ipsum ascenditur, ut in excelsis intelligatur: et descenditur, ut in membris parvulis nutriatur; per illum se erigunt, ut sublimem exspectent: et humiliant, ut eum sublimiter et temperanter annuntient.

VERS. 13-15.-- « Ego sum Dominus Deus Abraham, » etc. Mystice designat mandatum divinitatis ad humanitatem Christi directum, cui terra, id est, Ecclesia, promittitur; et semini illius, id est Christiano populo. Unde psalm. II: « Postula a me, 155Aet dabo tibi gentes haereditatem tuam, et possessionem tuam terminos terrae. »

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Cumque evigilasset, » etc. Quisquis post torporem inertiae evigilaverit, et per exercitium boni operis vere exsurrexerit, in Ecclesia supereminentem gratiam Dei et introitum regni coelestis intelliget.

VERS. 18.-- « Surgens ergo, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Quod « erexit » Jacob, « lapidem quem capiti supposuerat » in titulum, et superfudit oleum, nihil fecit idololatriae simile: quia non vel tunc vel postea frequentavit lapidem adorando aut ei sacrificando; sed signum fuit et prophetia pertinens ad unctionem, unde Christus dicitur.

VERS. 19-21.-- « Appellavitque nomen urbis 155BBethel., » etc. (HIER., de locis Hebraicis.) Ab eo quod dictum est, « quam terribilis est locus iste, » etc., loco nomen imponit Bethel, id est domum Dei, qui ante Luza vocabatur, id est, nux vel amygdalum. Unde ridicule quidam verbum Hebraicum ulam, nomen urbis esse putant: cum ulam interpretetur prius. Ordo ergo iste est lectionis: Vocavit nomen illius loci Bethel, et prius Luza vocabulum civitatis. Antiquae vero scripturae verbo ulam vel olam ולאם plenae sunt, quod nihil significat nisi prius vel ante vestibulum, vel superliminare, vel postes, etc.

VERS. 22.-- « Et lapis iste, vocabitur domus Dei, » etc. Prophetia est domus Dei ibi futurae, ibi et ipse rediens Deo sacrificavit.

CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1-9.-- « Isaac therefore called, » etc. (AUG., 153DQ. in Gen.) What the Latin codices have: « Go into Mesopotamia, into the house of Bathuel, » etc., the Greeks have: « Flee into Mesopotamia, » etc., so that it may be understood that Isaac also understood that Esau had thought upon his brother's death.

« And setting out. » (ISID.) Jacob, fleeing the wiles of his brother, leaving his father's house and his parents, goes into a far country, that he may take a wife. In like manner Christ, leaving his parents according to the flesh, that is, the Jews, and his country, that is, Jerusalem, and all the regions of Judea, went away to the Gentiles, that he might take to himself the Church, according to that of Osee II: « I will call that which is not my people, » etc.

VERS. 10.-- « Jacob therefore going forth, » etc. (GREG., 154Alib. V Moral., c. 11, tom. I.) To sleep in the way, etc., as far as « for the Lord is sweet. »

VERS. 11.-- « He took of the stones, » etc. (ISID.) The sleeping of Jacob in the way, the death of Christ on the cross. The stone set under his head is Christ according to his humanity, whom Jacob anointed, that Christ might be signified, for χρίστος in Greek is anointed in Latin. But the head is Christ God. The stone therefore set under the head of Jacob signifies the humanity of Christ joined to the God who assumed it. « For the head of the man is Christ, the head of Christ is God (I Cor. XI.)

VERS. 12.-- « And he saw in his sleep, » etc. (GREG., Regist. l. I, ep. 24.) Jacob signifies preachers, who not only, by contemplating, 154Bseek after the head of the Church, that is, Christ, but descend by compassion to his members: which the angels ascending and descending show forth. Hence Moses frequently enters the tabernacle, and goes out; and he who within is caught up to contemplation, without is pressed by the business of the weak; within he considers hidden things, without he bears the burdens of the carnal. He who in doubtful matters ever runs back to the tabernacle, consults the Lord before the ark of the testament, giving an example to rulers, that when they are in doubt without as to what they should ordain, they may return to the mind as to a tabernacle, and, as it were before the ark of the testament, may consult the Lord concerning the things they doubt of, seeking within themselves the pages of the sacred eloquence.

Morally. To sleep in the way is, in the way of this world, to rest from the hindrance of worldly actions. 154CJacob sleeping saw angels: because they behold divine things who close their eyes from the desire of temporal things: which, being well closed, the devil opened, saying: « In whatsoever day you shall eat, your eyes shall be opened (Gen. III). »

« He saw in his sleep, » etc. (ISID.) The angels ascending and descending by the ladder are the evangelists and the preachers of Christ, ascending to the understanding of the Divinity; passing beyond every creature, that they may find in the beginning the Word with God, by whom all things were made. Descending, that they may find him « made of a woman, made under the law, » etc. In that ladder also from earth to heaven, from flesh to spirit: because in it the carnal, by advancing, as it were by ascending, become spiritual. To whom, to be nourished with milk, 154Dthe spiritual descend, because they cannot speak to them as to spiritual men, but as to carnal. He himself is above in the head, he himself below in his body, that is, the Church; he himself the ladder, who says in John XIV: « I am the way, » etc. To him men ascend, that he may be understood in the heights: and they descend, that he may be nourished in his little members; through him they raise themselves up, that they may look for him on high: and they humble themselves, that they may announce him loftily and with measure.

VERS. 13-15.-- « I am the Lord God of Abraham, » etc. Mystically it signifies the command of the Divinity directed to the humanity of Christ, to whom the land, that is, the Church, is promised; and to his seed, that is, to the Christian people. Whence psalm II: « Ask of me, 155Aand I will give thee the nations for thy inheritance, and the utmost parts of the earth for thy possession. »

VERS. 16, 17.-- « And when he had awaked, » etc. Whosoever shall have awaked after the torpor of sloth, and shall have truly risen up through the exercise of good work, shall understand in the Church the surpassing grace of God and the entrance of the heavenly kingdom.

VERS. 18.-- « Rising therefore, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen., tom. III.) That Jacob « set up » « the stone which he had put under his head » for a title, and poured oil upon it, he did nothing like to idolatry: because neither then nor afterwards did he frequent the stone by adoring it or by sacrificing to it; but it was a sign and a prophecy pertaining to the anointing, whence Christ is named.

VERS. 19-21.-- « And he called the name of the city 155BBethel., » etc. (HIER., de locis Hebraicis.) From that which was said, « how terrible is this place, » etc., he imposes on the place the name Bethel, that is, the house of God, which before was called Luza, that is, a nut or an almond. Whence certain men ridiculously think that the Hebrew word ulam, is the name of the city: whereas ulam is interpreted before. This therefore is the order of the reading: He called the name of that place Bethel, and before, Luza was the name of the city. But the ancient writings are full of the word ulam or olam, which signifies nothing but before or before the porch, or a lintel, or doorposts, etc.

VERS. 22.-- « And this stone shall be called the house of God, » etc. It is a prophecy of the house of God that was to be there, and there he himself, returning, sacrificed to God.

CAPUT XXIX.

  • 155C

VERS. 1-10.-- « Profectus ergo Jacob, » etc. « Vidit puteum in agro, » etc. Aqua putei scientia Veteris Testamenti: quam lapis claudebat, quia littera legis spiritualem sensum in eo celabat; sed veniente Christo lapis remotus est, cum per praedicationem Novi Testamenti umbra legis exclusa et veritas Evangelii patefacta est.

Allegorice. Per puteum baptismus, per agrum mundus exprimitur: per tres greges juxta puteum accubantes, signantur illi qui ad fidem Trinitatis capiendam pertinent, in humilitate expetentes baptismi gratiam.

« Lapidem. » Duritia infidelitatis, qua abjecta, percipitur baptismus vitae. Pastores autem sunt praelati 155DEcclesiae, qui, amoto lapide, per Jacob adaquare greges cupiunt: quia Christo auferente infidelitatem ab eorum cordibus quos praedestinavit ad sacramentum baptismi, abluuntur unda purificationis.

VERS. 11-16.-- « Osculatus est eam. » (AUG.) Consuetudinis fuit maxime in illa simplicitate antiquorum, ut propinqui propinquos oscularentur, et hodie fit in multis locis. Sed quaeri potest quomodo illa ab ignoto osculum acceperit, si postea indicavit Jacob propinquitatem? Ergo intelligendum est: aut illum, qui propinquitatem noverat, fidenter in osculum irruisse, aut postea Scripturam narrasse per recapitulationem, cum primum Jacob indicaverit quis esset.

VERS. 16-19.-- « Nomen majoris Lia, » etc. Victorinus 156Amartyr Rachel et Liam in similitudinem Ecclesiae et Synagogae interpretatus est. Liam majorem natu Synagogam significare existimat, quia prior populum Dei genuit: quae oculis lippa dicitur, quia lex per Mosen data cooperata est et signata. Rachel minor et pulchra, prius sterilis, post fecunda, Ecclesiam signat, quae tempore posterior, sed sancta corpore et spiritu. Oculi ejus decori, quia Evangelium videre meruerunt. Sed diu sterilis dum Synagoga populum generavit. Pro Rachel Jacob servivit, et supponitur ei Lia: quia Christus ut Ecclesiam sibi assumeret, prius sibi Synagogam conjunxit.

VERS. 20-35.-- « Servivit ergo Jacob pro Rachel septem annis. » Servitus Jacob septem annorum pro duabus uxoribus, praesentis vitae tempus signat, 156Bquod septem diebus volvitur; in quo Dominus formam servi accepit, obediens Patri usque ad mortem. Jacob servivit, quia « filius hominis non venit ministrari, sed ministrare (Matth. XX): » Ille oves pavit, et Christus dixit (Joan. X): « Ego sum pastor bonus. » Ille pro mercede varium pecus sibi abstulit: Christus diversarum gentium varietatem sibi congregavit. Ille tres virgas amputatis corticibus in alveis aquarum posuit, ut earum contemplatione multiplicarentur oves: et Dominus noster in aqua baptismatis trium personarum nomina Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus sancti, populo fideli proposuit, ut qui hoc pleno corde prospexerit, efficiatur ovis Dei.

« Et videbantur illi pauci dies, » etc. Quomodo hoc dictum sit quaerendum est, cum et breve tempus 156Clongum videatur amantibus? Dictum est ergo propter laborem servitutis, quem facilem faciebat amor.

(AUG., lib. XXII contra Faustum.) Pro ingenti crimine objiciuntur Jacob quatuor uxores. Sed quoniam tunc mos erat, crimen non erat; nunc autem crimen est, quia mos non est, etc., usque ad nisi concupiscentiae carnalis flagrantia.

(ISID., in Gen., tom. V.) Duae sunt ergo uxores Jacob liberae, ambae enim filiae remissionis peccatorum, etc., usque ad et vidit in principio Verbum apud Deum, et vult parere, et non potest, quia « generationem ejus quis enarrabit? » (Act. VIII.)

(ISID.) Duae liberae uxores Jacob, quia duae vitae nobis in corpore Jesu Christi praedicantur, etc., usque ad Sed hoc non potest in terra morientium, quod 156Dhis verbis significatur.

VERS. 26.-- « Non est in loco nostro consuetudinis, » etc. (ID., ibid.) Major appellatur quae tempore prior est, etc., usque ad ad unum ergo tendendum, sed propter hoc multa ferenda.

(ID., ibid.) Proinde vita quae studio contemplationis competit, etc., usque ad unde infra XXX: « Zelabat Rachel sororem suam. »

(ID., ibid.) Proinde quia purus intellectus de illa divina substantia, etc., usque ad non quia numen.

CHAPTER XXIX.

  • 155C

VERS. 1-10.-- « Jacob therefore setting out, » etc. « He saw a well in the field, » etc. The water of the well is the knowledge of the Old Testament: which the stone closed, because the letter of the law hid in it the spiritual sense; but when Christ came the stone was removed, when through the preaching of the New Testament the shadow of the law was shut out and the truth of the Gospel laid open.

Allegorically. By the well baptism, by the field the world is expressed: by the three flocks lying beside the well are signified those who pertain to the receiving of the faith of the Trinity, in humility seeking the grace of baptism.

« The stone. » The hardness of unbelief, which being cast away, the baptism of life is received. But the shepherds are the prelates 155Dof the Church, who, the stone being removed, desire through Jacob to water the flocks: because, when Christ takes away unbelief from the hearts of those whom he has predestined to the sacrament of baptism, they are washed with the wave of purification.

VERS. 11-16.-- « He kissed her. » (AUG.) It was the custom, chiefly in that simplicity of the ancients, that kinsfolk should kiss kinsfolk, and it is done today in many places. But it may be asked: how did she receive a kiss from one unknown, if Jacob afterwards declared the kinship? It is therefore to be understood: either that he, who knew the kinship, confidently rushed to a kiss, or that Scripture afterwards narrated it by recapitulation, since Jacob first declared who he was.

VERS. 16-19.-- « The name of the elder was Lia, » etc. Victorinus 156Athe martyr interpreted Rachel and Lia into the likeness of the Church and the Synagogue. He judges that Lia, the elder by birth, signifies the Synagogue, because she first bore the people of God: who is said to be blear-eyed, because the law given through Moses was wrought together and sealed. Rachel, the younger and the fair, first barren, afterwards fruitful, signifies the Church, which is later in time, but holy in body and in spirit. Her eyes are comely, because they deserved to see the Gospel. But she was long barren while the Synagogue brought forth a people. Jacob served for Rachel, and Lia is put under him in her stead: because Christ, that he might take the Church to himself, first joined the Synagogue to himself.

VERS. 20-35.-- « Jacob therefore served seven years for Rachel. » The servitude of Jacob of seven years for two wives signifies the time of the present life, 156Bwhich is rolled round in seven days; in which the Lord took the form of a servant, obedient to the Father unto death. Jacob served, because « the Son of man came not to be ministered unto, but to minister (Matth. XX): » He fed sheep, and Christ said (Joan. X): « I am the good shepherd. » He took to himself for a reward the party-coloured flock: Christ gathered to himself the variety of divers nations. He set three rods, the barks being stripped off, in the troughs of the waters, that by the beholding of them the sheep might be multiplied: and our Lord set before the faithful people, in the water of baptism, the names of the three persons, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost, that he who shall have looked upon this with a full heart may be made a sheep of God.

« And they seemed to him but a few days, » etc. It is to be asked: how was this said, since even a short time 156Cseems long to them that love? It was said therefore because of the labour of the servitude, which love made easy.

(AUG., lib. XXII contra Faustum.) Four wives are cast up against Jacob as a huge crime. But since it was then the custom, it was not a crime; now however it is a crime, because it is not the custom, etc., as far as save the burning of carnal concupiscence.

(ISID., in Gen., tom. V.) There are therefore two free wives of Jacob, for both are daughters of the remission of sins, etc., as far as and he saw in the beginning the Word with God, and wills to bring forth, and cannot, because « who shall declare his generation? » (Act. VIII.)

(ISID.) Two free wives of Jacob, because two lives are preached to us in the body of Jesus Christ, etc., as far as But this cannot be in the land of the dying, which 156Dis signified by these words.

VERS. 26.-- « It is not the custom in our place, » etc. (ID., ibid.) She is called the elder who is the earlier in time, etc., as far as to one thing therefore we must tend, but for this many things must be borne.

(ID., ibid.) Accordingly the life which suits the study of contemplation, etc., as far as whence below in XXX: « Rachel was jealous of her sister. »

(ID., ibid.) Accordingly, because a pure understanding of that divine substance, etc., as far as not because a divine power.

CAPUT XXX.

VERS. 14-31.-- « Egressus autem Ruben, » etc. (AUG., lib. XXII contra Faustum.) Quare rem gestam 157AScriptura non tacuit, etc., usque ad et quidquid de hac pie et sapienter dicitur, sine phantasmate carnalis cogitationis salubriter vel ex parte capiatur.

« Egressus autem Ruben, etc. » (AUG.) Historice. De hoc autem genere opinari quosdam scio quod acceptum in escam sterilibus feminis fecunditatem pariat, et ideo institisse Rachel ut hoc acciperet, quod ego non arbitror, nec sic tunc concepisset. Nunc vero cum post duos Liae alios ab illa nocte partus, Dominus eam prole donaverit, nihil est quod de mandragora tale aliquid suspicemur, quale in nulla femina experti sumus. Cum enim mala haec ipse vidissem, et propter hunc sacrae lectionis locum mihi obtigisse gratularer (rara enim res est), naturam eorum quantum potui perscrutatus sum, 157Bnon aliqua a communi sensu remotiore scientia, quae docet virtutes radicum et potestates herbarum, sed quantum renuntiat visus, olfactus et gustus, rem comperi pulchram, suave olentem, sapore autem insipido. Cur ergo eam mulier tantopere cupierit nescio, nisi forte propter pomi raritatem et odoris jucunditatem.

VERS. 32-43.-- « Gyra omnes greges tuos, » etc. (HIER., in Quaest. Hebr.) Multum apud Septuaginta confusus est sensus, etc., usque ad vicinus est finis.

(ALC.) Cur Jacob fraudis in hoc loco non arguitur, hoc sine dubio ut propheta fecit, nec credendum est eum fecisse sine spirituali revelatione.

CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 14-31.-- « And Ruben going out, » etc. (AUG., lib. XXII contra Faustum.) Why Scripture did not keep silence 157Aabout the thing done, etc., as far as and whatsoever is piously and wisely said of this may be wholesomely, or in part, received without the phantasm of carnal thought.

« And Ruben going out, etc. » (AUG.) Historically. But I know that some suppose of this kind, that, taken as food, it brings forth fruitfulness in barren women, and that Rachel therefore insisted that she should receive it, which I do not think, nor would she thus have conceived then. But now, since, after two other births of Lia from that night, the Lord has given her offspring, there is nothing wherefore we should suspect of the mandrake any such thing as we have experienced in no woman. For when I had myself seen these apples, and rejoiced that, because of this place of the sacred reading, they had fallen to me (for it is a rare thing), I searched out their nature as far as I could, 157Bnot by any knowledge further removed from the common sense, which teaches the powers of roots and the virtues of herbs, but so far as sight, smell and taste report, and I found the thing fair, sweet-smelling, but insipid in savour. Why then the woman desired it so greatly I know not, unless perhaps for the rarity of the fruit and the pleasantness of the odour.

VERS. 32-43.-- « Go round all thy flocks, » etc. (HIER., in Quaest. Hebr.) The sense is much confused among the Seventy, etc., as far as the end is near.

(ALC.) Why Jacob is not accused of fraud in this place, this without doubt he did as a prophet, nor is it to be believed that he did it without spiritual revelation.

CAPUT XXXI.

  • 157C

VERS. 1-11.-- « Postquam autem audivit, » etc. . . . . « Mutavit mercedem, » etc. (HIERON. in Quaest. Hebr.) Pro eo quod posuimus, « mutavit mercedem meam decem vicibus, » Septuaginta posuerunt « decem annis: » cum verbum Hebraicum monim מנים numerum magis quam annos sonat; et ex sequentibus is sensus probatur, quod per singulos fetus super Laban conditionem mutaverit. Si videbat varium nasci pecus, dicebat: Volo ut in futuro nascantur mihi varia; tunc Jacob virgas in canalibus non ponebat, ut fetus unicolor nasceretur: quo facto Laban ea pecora suae parti fieri debere dicebat; sic usque ad decem vices mutata est conditio: et quodcunque proposuerat Laban ut sibi nasceretur, 157Din contrarium colorem vertebatur. Nunc autem in sex annis decem pariendi vices incredibiles viderentur; sed lege Virgilium, in quo dicitur: « Bis gravidae pecudes. » Natura autem Italicarum ovium et Mesopotamiae eadem esse dicitur.

VERS. 12.-- « Vidi enim omnia, » etc. Ex hoc discimus quod Jacob nullo malo dolo fecit, sed divina dispositione, vel quia omnia in figura gerebantur. Unde angelica visione meruit confortari.

VERS. 13-31.-- « Nunc ergo surge. » (GREG., Moral. XXX, 16.) Post longam servitutem praecepit Dominus Jacob ut reverteretur in patriam: tunc, ignorante socero, cum uxoribus et comitatu properabat. Laban autem consecutus est eum in monte 158AGalaad cum furore idola requirens, nec reperit. Laban autem in hoc loco diaboli typum gerit. Interpretatur enim dealbatio. Diabolus vero, cum tenebrosus sit, « transfigurat se in angelum lucis. » Huic servivit Jacob, id est, Judaicus populus ex parte reproborum, de quo natus est Christus secundum carnem.

VERS. 32-39.--. . . . « Ignorabat quod Rachel furata esset idola. » (HIERON.) Ubi nos « idola » legimus, in Hebraeo theraphim תרפים scriptum est; quod Aquila « figuras, » vel « imagines » interpretatur. Hoc autem ideo dico ut sciamus quid in Judicum libro theraphim sonet.

« Contra montem Galaad, » etc. Non quod eo tempore mons Galaad diceretur, sed per anticipationem 158Billo nomine vocatur, quo postea nuncupatus est.

« Qui, assumptis fratribus suis, persecutus, » etc. (GREG.) Historice. Potest per Laban mundus exprimi: qui cum furore Jacob sequitur, quia electos, qui sunt membra Christi, opprimere conatur. Filiam mundi vel diaboli Jacob abstulit, cum sibi Christus Ecclesiam ex gentibus conjunxit, cui per prophetam dicitur psal. XLIV: « Obliviscere populum tuum, » etc. In idolis avaritia signatur, quae est idolorum servitus. Laban veniens apud Jacob idolum non reperit, quia, ostensis mundi divitiis Redemptori nostro, diabolus vestigia concupiscentiae non reperit. Sed quae Jacob non habuit, Rachel sedendo operuit, quae interpretatur ovis, et Ecclesiam 158Csignificat. Sedere autem est humilitatem poenitentiae appetere. Unde psal. CXXVI: « Surgite postquam sederitis. » Rachel idola sedendo operit, quia Ecclesia Christum sequens vitium terrenae concupiscentiae per humilitatem poenitentiae cooperuit. Unde psal. XIII: « Beati quorum remissae sunt iniquitates, et quorum tecta sunt peccata. » Nos igitur Rachel significat, qui idola sedendo premimus, si culpas avaritiae poenitendo damnamus; quae avaritiae immunditia non viriliter currentes impedit, sed effeminate gradientes per blandimenta saeculi resolvuntur, quod his verbis significatur: « Juxta consuetudinem feminarum accidit mihi. » Quasi enim muliebria pati se innotuit.

158D« Ut oscularer filios meos ac filias. » More Scripturae filios ac filias Jacob suos appellat, quae filios vel fratres cognatos appellat; unde in sequentibus: « Dixitque Jacob fratribus suis: Afferte lapides. » In Evangelio quoque filii Mariae, materterae Domini, fratres ejus vocantur Matthaei XII.

« Timui ne violenter auferres filias tuas, quod autem furti me arguis, » etc. Quasi ideo non tibi manifestavi, ne acerbitate persecutionis superares animas infirmas, noviter tibi ereptas.

VERS. 40-45.-- « Die noctuque aestu urebar, et gelu. Fugiebat somnus ab oculis meis. » Unus effectus est caloris et frigoris.

VERS. 46-52.-- « Afferte lapides, » etc. (HIERON. 159Ade Loc. Hebr. ) Acervus lingua Hebraea, etc., usque ad in qua habitabat sermone mutaverat.

(ISID.) Mystice. Inter fideles, tam Judaeos quam gentiles, testis est lapis eminens in similitudinem Christi; et acervus lapidum, qui est multitudo credentium.

VERS. 53, 54.-- « Juravit Jacob, » etc. Per timorem scilicet quo timebat Deum, quem commendavit supra, dicens, « Et timor, » etc.

VERS. 55.-- « Laban vero de nocte, etc. » Quia Laban et Jacob trans fluvium perrexerunt, significare potest duos populos per baptismum ad Ecclesiam venientes. Sed Jacob ultra progrediente, Laban reversus est, quia, filiis lucis in profectu virtutum post baptismum meantibus, reprobi de percepta 159Bdignitate post Satanam in apostasiam redeunt.

CHAPTER XXXI.

  • 157C

VERS. 1-11.-- « But after he had heard, » etc. . . . . « He changed the wages, » etc. (HIERON. in Quaest. Hebr.) In place of what we have set down, « he changed my wages ten times, » the Seventy have set « ten years: » whereas the Hebrew word monim sounds a number rather than years; and from what follows this sense is proved, that at every bringing forth he changed the condition upon Laban. If he saw a party-coloured beast born, he would say: I will that in time to come party-coloured ones be born to me; then Jacob did not put the rods in the channels, that the young might be born of one colour: which being done, Laban would say that those cattle ought to fall to his own share; thus up to ten times the condition was changed: and whatsoever Laban had proposed should be born to himself, 157Dwas turned into the contrary colour. But now in six years ten times of bringing forth would seem incredible; but read Virgil, in whom it is said: « Twice pregnant are the flocks. » But the nature of Italian sheep and of those of Mesopotamia is said to be the same.

VERS. 12.-- « For I have seen all things, » etc. From this we learn that Jacob did it by no evil guile, but by divine disposition, or because all things were being done in a figure. Whence he deserved to be strengthened by an angelic vision.

VERS. 13-31.-- « Now therefore arise. » (GREG., Moral. XXX, 16.) After a long servitude the Lord commanded Jacob to return into his own country: then, his father-in-law not knowing it, he hastened away with his wives and his company. But Laban followed after him in mount 158AGalaad, seeking the idols with fury, and found them not. But Laban in this place bears the type of the devil. For he is interpreted a whitening. But the devil, though he be dark, « transformeth himself into an angel of light. » Him Jacob served, that is, the Jewish people on the part of the reprobate, of whom Christ was born according to the flesh.

VERS. 32-39.--. . . . « He knew not that Rachel had stolen the idols. » (HIERON.) Where we read « idols, » in the Hebrew theraphim is written; which Aquila interprets « figures, » or « images ». But I say this for this cause, that we may know what theraphim sounds in the book of Judges.

« Over against mount Galaad, » etc. Not that at that time it was called mount Galaad, but by anticipation 158Bit is called by that name, by which it was afterwards named.

« Who, taking his brethren, pursued, » etc. (GREG.) Historically. By Laban the world can be expressed: which follows Jacob with fury, because it strives to oppress the elect, who are the members of Christ. Jacob took away the daughter of the world or of the devil, when Christ joined to himself the Church out of the Gentiles, to whom it is said through the prophet in psalm XLIV: « Forget thy people, » etc. In the idols avarice is signified, which is the service of idols. Laban, coming to Jacob, found not the idol, because, the riches of the world being shown to our Redeemer, the devil found no traces of concupiscence. But what Jacob had not, Rachel by sitting covered, who is interpreted a sheep, and signifies 158Cthe Church. But to sit is to seek the humility of penance. Whence in psalm CXXVI: « Rise up after you have sat. » Rachel by sitting covers the idols, because the Church, following Christ, has covered over the vice of earthly concupiscence by the humility of penance. Whence in psalm XIII: « Blessed are they whose iniquities are forgiven, and whose sins are covered. » We therefore Rachel signifies, who by sitting press down the idols, if by doing penance we condemn the faults of avarice; which uncleanness of avarice hinders not those that run manfully, but those that walk effeminately are loosed through the blandishments of the world, which is signified by these words: « It hath befallen me according to the custom of women. » For as it were she made known that she suffered the things of a woman.

158D« That I might kiss my sons and daughters. » After the manner of Scripture he calls the sons and daughters of Jacob his own, which calls kinsmen sons or brethren; whence in what follows: « And Jacob said to his brethren: Bring stones. » In the Gospel also the sons of Mary, the maternal aunt of the Lord, are called his brethren, in Matthew XII.

« I feared lest thou wouldst violently take away thy daughters, but as to thy accusing me of theft, » etc. As if to say, therefore I did not make it known to thee, lest by the bitterness of persecution thou shouldst overcome the weak souls, newly snatched from thee.

VERS. 40-45.-- « Day and night I was parched with heat, and with frost. Sleep fled from mine eyes. » The effect of heat and of cold is one.

VERS. 46-52.-- « Bring stones, » etc. (HIERON. 159Ade Loc. Hebr. ) A heap, in the Hebrew tongue, etc., as far as he had changed by the speech in which he dwelt.

(ISID.) Mystically. Among the faithful, both Jews and Gentiles, the witness is a stone standing out into the likeness of Christ; and the heap of stones, which is the multitude of believers.

VERS. 53, 54.-- « Jacob swore, » etc. That is, by the fear wherewith he feared God, whom he commended above, saying, « And the fear, » etc.

VERS. 55.-- « But Laban in the night, etc. » That Laban and Jacob went over the river can signify two peoples coming to the Church through baptism. But Jacob going further forward, Laban returned, because, while the sons of light pass on in the advancement of virtues after baptism, the reprobate, from the dignity they have received, 159Breturn after Satan into apostasy.

CAPUT XXXII.

VERS. 2-6.-- « Castra Dei sunt haec. » Ubi castra posita sunt, in Hebraeo mahanaim מהנים ; ut sciamus, si quando interpretatum in alio loco ponitur, quem locum significet. Et pulchre ad fratrem inimicum iturus, angelorum comitantium eum choris excipitur.

VERS. 7-9.-- « Timuit Jacob, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen. ) Quaeri potest quomodo fidem habuit promissis Dei, quod hoc dixit. Sed fieri potuit ut everteret castra ejus Esau, et tunc post illam afflictionem adesset, et quem promisit, impleret. Et admonemur hoc exemplo, ut quamvis credamus in Deum, 159Cfaciamus tamen quae facienda sunt in salutis praesidium. Verba quoque Jacob consideranda sunt, dicentis: « Deus patris mei Abraham, » etc., quia in his verbis et humana infirmitas et fides pietatis apparet.

VERS. 10.-- « Transivi Jordanem, » etc. Per baptismum conjunctus est paries de gentibus, ei qui ex Judaeis: primitivae tamen Ecclesiae non deest timor Judaeorum, quod significat Esau.

VERS. 11-19.-- « Erue me de manu, » etc. Sic orat ut spem timore moderetur, et timorem spe consoletur, significans ut vitae nostrae et domesticorum ratione consulamus, et tamen Dei auxilium invocemus.

VERS. 20-27.-- « Placabo illum, » etc. Haec interposita 159Dsunt a scriptore. Non est enim prudentis artem suam detegere, et sic eis cum quibus agitur suspectum se facere.

(GREG. hom. 14, in Evang., tom. II.) Mystice. Jacob cum ad parentes proprios redit, etc., usque ad ut suavitate intima Deum apprehendamus.

« Et ecce vir, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) In hoc principaliter sacramenti Dominici imago figurata est, etc., usque ad ne ultra jam generare possit.

VERS. 28, 29.-- « Nequaquam, inquit Jacob, appellabitur nomen tuum, sed, » etc. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr. ) Josephus in libro primo Antiquitatum, etc., usque ad nos magis Scripturae, et angeli, vel Dei, 160Aqui Israel ipsum vocavit, auctoritate ducimur, quam cujuslibet eloquentiae saecularis.

VERS. 30-32.-- « Vocavitque Jacob, » etc. Illud quoque quod sequitur, « et benedixit eum, vocavitque Jacob nomen loci illius Phanuel, dicens, » etc. In Hebraeo dicitur « Phanuel, » ut sciamus ipsum esse locum qui in caeteris Scripturae voluminibus secundum Hebraeos, Phanuel legitur.

(GREG., lib. IV Moral., cap. 6.) Quaeritur, cum Veritas dicat: « Nemo videbit faciem meam; » Et: « Deum nemo vidit unquam, » quomodo testatur Jacob: « Vidi Dominum facie ad faciem? » Humanae enim mentes oculo interiori purgato, etc., usque ad unde I Cor. XIII: « Tunc cognoscam sicut et cognitus sum. »

CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 2-6.-- « These are the camps of God. » Where castra is set, in the Hebrew is mahanaim ; that we may know, if at any time the interpreted word is set in another place, what place it signifies.
