And beautifully, being about to go to his enemy brother, he is received by the choirs of the angels accompanying him.

VERS. 7-9.-- « Jacob feared, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen. ) It can be asked how he had faith in the promises of God, in that he said this. But it could come to pass that Esau should overthrow his camps, and that then, after that affliction, he should be present, and should fulfil what he promised. And we are admonished by this example that, although we believe in God, 159Cwe should nevertheless do the things that are to be done for the safeguard of our salvation. The words also of Jacob are to be considered, when he says: « God of my father Abraham, » etc., because in these words both human infirmity and the faith of piety appear.

VERS. 10.-- « I passed over the Jordan, » etc. Through baptism the wall from the Gentiles was joined to that which is from the Jews: yet to the primitive Church the fear of the Jews is not lacking, which Esau signifies.

VERS. 11-19.-- « Deliver me from the hand, » etc. So he prays that he may temper hope by fear, and comfort fear by hope, signifying that we should take counsel by reason for our life and for our household, and nevertheless call upon the help of God.

VERS. 20-27.-- « I will appease him, » etc. These things are interposed 159Dby the writer. For it belongs not to a prudent man to uncover his art, and so to make himself suspected by those with whom he deals.

(GREG. hom. 14, in Evang., tom. II.) Mystically. When Jacob returns to his own parents, etc., as far as that with inward sweetness we may lay hold of God.

« And behold a man, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) In this chiefly the image of the Lord's sacrament is figured, etc., as far as that it may no more be able to beget.

VERS. 28, 29.-- « By no means, he says, shall thy name be called Jacob, but, » etc. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr. ) Josephus in the first book of the Antiquities, etc., as far as we are led rather by the authority of Scripture, and of the angel, or of God, 160Awho himself called him Israel, than by that of any secular eloquence.

VERS. 30-32.-- « And Jacob called, » etc. That also which follows, « and he blessed him, and Jacob called the name of that place Phanuel, saying, » etc. In the Hebrew it is called « Phanuel, » that we may know it to be the same place which in the other volumes of Scripture, according to the Hebrews, is read Phanuel.

(GREG., lib. IV Moral., cap. 6.) It is asked: since Truth says: « No man shall see my face; » And: « No man hath seen God at any time, » how does Jacob testify: « I saw the Lord face to face? » [Vulg. Gen. 32:30 Vidi Deum facie ad faciem, "I have seen God face to face"] For human minds, the inward eye being purged, etc., as far as whence I Cor. XIII: « Then shall I know even as I am known. »

CAPUT XXXIII.

  • 160B

VERS. 1-9.-- « Elevans autem Jacob oculos. . . . divisitque filios Liae et Rachel, » etc. Aquila dimidiavit, ut unum cuneum faceret ancillarum cum parvulis suis, et alterum liberarum cum filiis suis; primasque mitteret ancillas, secundas liberas: ipse ante omnes fratrem adoraturus occurreret.

VERS. 10-13.-- « Sic enim vidi, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen. tom. III.) Utrum paventis et perturbati animi verba, etc., usque ad qui Graecum audire et intelligere solent.

VERS. 14.-- « Et ego sequar, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeritur si mendacium fratri promiserit. Hoc enim, sicut Scriptura narrat, non fecit; sed eo perrexit 160Citinere quod dirigebat ad suos. An forte veraci animo promisit, sed aliud postea cogitando delegit?

« Socoth. » (HIERON.) Ubi nos tabernacula habemus, in Hebraeo legitur Socoth Est autem usque hodie civitas trans Jordanem hoc vocabulo, in parte Scytopoleos de qua in libro Locorum scripsimus.

VERS. 18-20. « Transivitque in Salem, » etc. (HIERON.) Quaestio oboritur quomodo Salem Sichen civitas appelletur, cum Hierusalem, in qua regnavit Melchisedech, Salem ante dicta sit. Aut igitur unius utraque urbs nominis est, quod de pluribus Judaeae locis invenimus, ut idem urbs et loci nomen in alia et alia tribu sit. Aut ista Salem, quae pro Sichem 160Dnunc nominatur, dicemus interpretari consummatam atque perfectam: et illam quae postea Hierusalem dicta est, pacificam. Utrumque accentu paululum declinato, hoc vocabulum sonat. Tradunt Hebraei quod femur claudicantis Jacob ibi sanatum sit: ideoque civitatem curati atque perfecti vocabulum consecutam.

CHAPTER XXXIII.

  • 160B

VERS. 1-9.-- « And Jacob lifting up his eyes. . . . and he divided the children of Lia and of Rachel, » etc. Aquila, he divided them in half, that he might make one company of the handmaids with their little ones, and another of the free women with their sons; and might send the handmaids first, the free women second: he himself running out before them all to adore his brother.

VERS. 10-13.-- « For so I have seen, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen. tom. III.) Whether they are the words of a fearful and troubled mind, etc., as far as those who are wont to hear and understand Greek.

VERS. 14.-- « And I will follow, » etc. (AUG.) It is asked whether he promised his brother a lie. For this, as Scripture relates, he did not do; but he went on by that 160Croad which led to his own people. Or did he perhaps promise with a truthful mind, but afterwards, on further thought, choose otherwise?

« Socoth. » (HIERON.) Where we have tabernacles, in the Hebrew is read Socoth [Migne prints a Hebrew word here, and the full stop that closes the sentence; our transcription of the Latin has lost both.] There is moreover to this day a city beyond the Jordan of this name, in the region of Scythopolis, of which we have written in the book of Places.

VERS. 18-20. « And he passed over into Salem, » etc. (HIERON.) A question arises how the city Sichen is called Salem, since Jerusalem, in which Melchisedech reigned, was formerly called Salem. Either then both cities are of one name, which we find of many places of Judaea, so that the same is the name of a city and of a place in one tribe and another. Or we shall say that this Salem, which 160Dis now named instead of Sichem, is interpreted finished and perfect: and that one which was afterwards called Jerusalem, peaceful. This word sounds each of the two, the accent being a little turned aside. The Hebrews hand down that the thigh of the limping Jacob was healed there: and that therefore the city obtained the name of the healed and the perfected.

CAPUT XXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Egressa est autem Dina, » etc. (GREG. in Pastor., c. 30.) Dina, ut mulieres extraneae regionis videat, egreditur, cum mens sua studia negligens, actiones alienas curans, extra ordinem proprium evagatur. Quam Sichem, princeps terrae, opprimit, quia inventam in curis exterioribus 161Adiabolus corrumpit. Et conglutinata est anima ejus cum ea, quia unitam sibi per iniquitatem respicit. Et quia mens a culpa resipiscens afficitur, et admissum flere conatur: corruptor spem ac securitatem vacuam ante oculos vocat, quatenus utilitatem tristitiae subtrahat. Unde additur: « Tristemque blanditus delinivit. » Modo enim, aliorum facta graviora; modo nihil esse quod factum est; modo misericordem Deum loquitur, et tempus ad poenitentiam pollicetur: ut dum per haec decepta mens ducitur, poenitentia differatur, ut tunc bona nulla percipiat, quam nunc mala contristant: et tunc plenius absorbeatur suppliciis, quae nunc gaudet in deliciis.

VERS. 2-32.-- « Quam cum vidisset Sichem. . . . 161Bet dormivit cum illa, » etc. (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Et intendit animae Dinae filiae Jacob et adamavit virginem, et locutus est secundum sensum virginis. Quomodo virgo dicitur, si jam dormivit cum ea? nisi forte virgo nomen aetatis est secundum Hebraeum eloquium, an potius per recapitulationem post commemoratur quod ante factum est. Prius enim potuit intendere animae ipsius, et amare virginem, et loqui secundum sensum virginis, deinde dormire cum illa.

« Pacifici. » Ubi Septuaginta interpretati sunt pacificos, Aquila transtulit consummatos, atque perfectos pro quo in Hebraeo legitur: Selemim. Ex quo apparet illud esse verum quod supra de Salem diximus.

161C« Assensi, » etc. (AUG.) Paulo ante Jacob loquens cum Esau, filios suos infantes esse significat, etc., usque ad sed filii ejus in hoc facto nominantur quasi principes et auctores.

« Arreptis, » etc. (STRAB.) Sic pastores Ecclesiae peccatum carnis vel animae ulcisci debent gladio spiritus, qui est verbum Dei: parati ulcisci omnem inobedientiam.

« Nos pauci sumus, » etc. (AUG.) Hoc dicit, quia plurimum bella poterant surgere, non quod multo minus haberet quam possent expugnationi civitatis sufficere.

« Nunquid ut scorto, » etc. (AUG.) Zelus filiorum Jacob in ultionem sororis moraliter commonet pastores fidelium, curam habere animarum sibi commissarum, 161Dne violenter corporali delicto, aut fornicatione spirituali succumbant. « Paratique sint ulcisci omnem inobedientiam, » et excommunicationis gladio feriant stupratorem, ne impunitus evadat.

CHAPTER XXXIV.

VERS. 1.-- « And Dina went out, » etc. (GREG. in Pastor., c. 30.) Dina goes out to see the women of a foreign country, when the mind, neglecting its own pursuits and busying itself with the doings of others, strays outside its proper order. Her Sichem, the prince of the land, oppresses, because the devil corrupts her 161Awhom he has found among outward cares. And his soul was knit to her, because he looks upon her as united to himself through iniquity. And because the mind, coming to itself from its fault, is stricken and strives to weep for what it has committed: the corrupter calls up before its eyes an empty hope and security, that he may take away the profit of its sorrow. Whence it is added: « And flattering he soothed her that was sad. » [Vulg. Gen. 34:3 tristemque delinivit blanditiis, "and he soothed her, being sad, with flatteries"] For now he speaks of the deeds of others as heavier; now that what has been done is nothing; now that God is merciful, and he promises time for penance: so that, while the mind is led on deceived by these things, penance may be put off, that then it may receive no good things, it whom evils now make sad: and that it may then be the more fully swallowed up in punishments, it which now rejoices in delights.

VERS. 2-32.-- « And when Sichem had seen her. . . . 161Band slept with her, » etc. (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) And he set his mind upon the soul of Dina the daughter of Jacob, and loved the virgin, and spoke according to the mind of the virgin. How is she called a virgin, if he had already slept with her? unless perhaps "virgin" is a name of age according to the Hebrew manner of speech, or rather, by recapitulation, that is recorded afterwards which was done before. For first he could set his mind upon her soul, and love the virgin, and speak according to the mind of the virgin, and then sleep with her.

« Peaceable. » Where the Seventy have rendered peaceable, Aquila has translated finished, and perfect for which is read in the Hebrew: Selemim. From which it appears that to be true which we said above of Salem.

161C« They consented, » etc. (AUG.) A little before, Jacob, speaking with Esau, signifies that his sons are infants, etc., as far as but his sons are named in this deed as though they were the chief men and the authors of it.

« Having taken up their swords, » etc. (STRAB.) So ought the pastors of the Church to avenge the sin of the flesh or of the soul with the sword of the spirit, which is the word of God: ready to avenge all disobedience.

« We are few, » etc. (AUG.) He says this because very many wars could arise, not because he had far fewer than would suffice for the storming of the city.

« Ought they to have abused our sister as a harlot, » etc. (AUG.) The zeal of the sons of Jacob for the avenging of their sister morally admonishes the pastors of the faithful to have care of the souls committed to them, 161Dlest they be violently overcome by a bodily offence, or by spiritual fornication. « And let them be ready to avenge all disobedience, » and let them strike the ravisher with the sword of excommunication, lest he escape unpunished.

CAPUT XXXV.

VERS. 4-5.-- « Dederunt ergo ei, » etc. (HIER. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Plane hoc ordine et his profectibus ascendendum est in Bethel, id est, in domum Dei, quae est Ecclesia Christi. Primum est, pristinos abdicare errores, et unius veri Dei profiteri fidem, quod est auferre deos alienos: deinde baptizari, quod est mundari; ac deinceps in novitate 162Avitae ambulare, quod est vestimenta mutare. Significant autem inaures falsae doctrinae phaleras, sermone nitidas, sed sensu veritatis vacuas.

« Et inaures, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeritur quale inaures, quae ornamenta erant ad idololatriam non pertinentia? sed intelligendum est phylacteria fuisse deorum alienorum.

VERS. 6-9.-- « Venit igitur Jacob Suzam, » etc. (AUG.) Notandum est tria nomina hujus civitatis esse commemorata, etc., usque ad multis de causis adduntur nomina vel mutantur.

(HIERON.) Ecce manifestissime comprobatur Bethel non Ulam, ut supra dictum est, sed Luzan, id est, amygdalum, antea esse vocatam.

VERS. 10.-- « Non vocaberis ultra, » etc. (AUG.) 162BQuaeritur cum semel dictum sit, « Non vocaberis ultra Jacob, » etc., cur legatur postea Jacob vocatus? Sed hoc nomen ad illam pertinet promissionem, in qua videtur Deus quomodo non est antea patribus visus, ubi non erit nomen vetus: quia nihil remanebit hic in ipso corpore vetustatis, et visio Dei summum praemium erit.

(HIERON.) « Non vocaberis ultra Jacob, » etc. Nondum enim ab angelo nomen ei imponitur, sed imponendum a Deo praedicitur. Quod igitur illuc futurum promittitur, hic expletum docetur.

VERS. 11-13.-- « Cresce et multiplicare. » Moraliter. Haec promissio ad spirituale semen Jacob pertinet, sicut jam ante de Abraham dictum est. 162COmnes enim qui supplantant vitia, et veterem hominem cum actibus suis deponunt, et mentis oculos ad Deum intendunt, recte ad Jacob pertinent, cujus fidem et actus imitantur. « Regesque de lumbis ejus egredientur, » id est, sancti qui secundum voluntatem Dei se et subditos vere regunt: de cujus semine spirituali, id est imitatione fidei, in bonis operibus fecundantur.

VERS. 14, 15.-- « Ille vero erexit titulum lapideum in loco quo locutus fuerat, » etc. (AUG.) Factum est iterum hoc in loco quod antea factum fuerat; vel memoratum hic quod ante fuerat factum. Sed quodlibet horum sit, super lapidem libavit Jacob, non lapidi. Non ergo sicut idololatrae solent aras ante lapidem constituere, et tanquam Deo lapidi 162Dlibare.

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Ephratam. » (HIERON.) Ephrata et Bethleem unius urbis vocabulum est, sub interpretatione consimili. Interpretatur enim frugifera et domus panis, propter panem qui de coelo descendit.

VERS. 18-20.-- « Egrediente autem, » etc. (ISID.) Quid sibi vult quod eumdem Bennoni Rachel, cum pareret, vocavit filium doloris mei? Nisi futurum ex ea tribu Paulum, qui affligeret filios Ecclesiae tempore persecutionis suae? Aliter per Benjamin terrestris Hierusalem figuratur, quae est in tribu Benjamin: cujus populus gravi dolore matrem afficit fundendo sanguinem prophetarum, et in necem 163AChristi clamando: « Sanguis ejus super nos, et super filios nostros (Matth. XXVII).

« Et factum est cum dimitteret animam. » (HIERON.) Siquidem moriebatur, « vocavit nomen ejus, filius doloris mei: pater vero ejus vocavit nomen ejus Benjamin. » In Hebraeo similitudo nominis resonat, filius enim doloris mei, quod nomen moriens mater imposuit, dicitur Bennoni. Filius vero dextrae, id est virtutis, quod Jacob mutavit, dicitur Benjamin. Unde errant qui putant Benjamin filium dierum interpretari: dextera enim dicitur iamin, et finitur in n; dies vero appellatur iamim, et terminatur in m.

VERS. 21.-- « Egressus inde, » etc. (HIERON.) Hunc locum volunt esse Hebraei, etc., usque ad vel 163Bquod verius est quodam vaticinio futurum jam tunc mysterium monstrabatur.

VERS. 22-26.-- « Abiit Ruben, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorice. Hoc crimen non scriberetur, nisi futura populi perversitas pronuntiaretur: quamvis in illo esset flagitium, in Scripturis est prophetia futurorum, quia per Ruben primogenitus populus Israel figuratur: qui thorum concubinae polluit, id est legem Veteris Testamenti praevaricando maculavit. Quod autem concubina Vetus Testamentum significaverit, Paulus ostendit dicens, Gal. IV: « Abraham duos filios habuit, unum de ancilla, et alterum de libera, » etc.

« Erant autem, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeritur quomodo hoc verum sit, etc., usque ad quam ut per synecdochen 163Caccipiatur.

VERS. 27-29.-- « Civitatem Arbee. » (HIERON.) Pro Arbee in LXX campum habetur, cum Hebron in monte sit. Eadem autem civitas antiquitus Mambre ab amico Abrahae dicta est.

« Civitatem Arbee, » etc. Angelorum vel sanctarum animarum, quibus est appositus, nulla remanente sollicitudine tentationum, vel periculo peccatorum.

CHAPTER XXXV.

VERS. 4-5.-- « They gave him therefore, » etc. (HIER. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Plainly it is by this order and by these advances that we must go up into Bethel, that is, into the house of God, which is the Church of Christ. The first thing is to renounce our former errors, and to profess the faith of the one true God, which is to put away strange gods: then to be baptized, which is to be cleansed; and thereafter to walk in newness 162Aof life, which is to change our garments. And the earrings signify the trappings of false doctrine, glittering in speech, but empty of the sense of truth.

« And the earrings, » etc. (AUG.) It is asked: of what sort were the earrings, which were ornaments not pertaining to idolatry? but it must be understood that they were phylacteries of strange gods.

VERS. 6-9.-- « Jacob therefore came to Suzam, » [Vulg. Gen. 35:6 Luzam] etc. (AUG.) It is to be noted that three names of this city are recorded, etc., as far as for many causes names are added or changed.

(HIERON.) Behold, it is most manifestly proved that Bethel was formerly called, not Ulam, as was said above, but Luzan, that is, an almond.

VERS. 10.-- « Thou shalt no more be called, » etc. (AUG.) 162BIt is asked: since it was once said, « Thou shalt no more be called Jacob, » etc., why is he afterwards read to have been called Jacob? But this name belongs to that promise in which God is seen as he was not seen before by the fathers, where the old name shall not be: because nothing of oldness shall remain here in the body itself, and the vision of God shall be the highest reward.

(HIERON.) « Thou shalt no more be called Jacob, » etc. For not yet is the name laid upon him by the angel, but it is foretold that it is to be laid upon him by God. What therefore is promised there as to come, is here taught to be fulfilled.

VERS. 11-13.-- « Increase and multiply. » Morally. This promise belongs to the spiritual seed of Jacob, as was already said before of Abraham. 162CFor all who supplant vices, and put off the old man with his deeds, and bend the eyes of the mind upon God, rightly belong to Jacob, whose faith and deeds they imitate. « And kings shall come forth from his loins, » [Vulg. Gen. 35:11 reges de lumbis tuis egredientur, "from thy loins"] that is, the saints who according to the will of God truly rule themselves and their subjects: of whose spiritual seed, that is, by the imitation of faith, they are made fruitful in good works.

VERS. 14, 15.-- « But he set up a stone pillar in the place where he had spoken, » etc. (AUG.) That was done again in this place which had been done before; or that is recorded here which had been done before. But whichever of these it be, Jacob poured his offering upon the stone, not to the stone. Not therefore as idolaters are wont to set altars before a stone, and 162Dto pour offering to the stone as to God.

VERS. 16, 17.-- « Ephrata. » (HIERON.) Ephrata and Bethlehem are the name of one city, under a like interpretation. For it is interpreted fruitful and the house of bread, because of the bread which came down from heaven.

VERS. 18-20.-- « And when she was going forth, » etc. (ISID.) What does it mean that Rachel, when she was in labour, called the same child Bennoni, the son of my sorrow? What but that Paul was to come of that tribe, who should afflict the sons of the Church in the time of his persecution? Otherwise by Benjamin the earthly Jerusalem is figured, which is in the tribe of Benjamin: whose people afflict their mother with grievous sorrow by shedding the blood of the prophets, and by crying out for the death 163Aof Christ: « His blood be upon us, and upon our children (Matth. XXVII).

« And it came to pass, when she was giving up her soul. » [Vulg. Gen. 35:18 Egrediente autem anima prae dolore, "and her soul departing for pain"] (HIERON.) Since indeed she was dying, « she called his name, the son of my sorrow: but his father called his name Benjamin. » [Vulg. Gen. 35:18 vocavit nomen filii sui Benoni, id est, Filius doloris mei : pater vero appellavit eum Benjamin] In the Hebrew a likeness of the name sounds, for son of my sorrow, which name the dying mother laid upon him, is said Bennoni. But son of the right hand, that is, of strength, which Jacob changed it into, is said Benjamin. Whence they err who think that Benjamin is interpreted son of days: for the right hand is called iamin, and ends in n; but a day is called iamim, and is terminated in m.

VERS. 21.-- « Going forth thence, » etc. (HIERON.) The Hebrews will have this place to be, etc., as far as or 163Bwhat is truer, that by a certain prophecy the mystery to come was even then being shown.

VERS. 22-26.-- « Ruben went, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorically. This crime would not be written, were not the coming perversity of the people declared: although in him there was an outrage, in the Scriptures there is a prophecy of things to come, because by Ruben the firstborn people of Israel is figured: who defiled the couch of the concubine, that is, stained the law of the Old Testament by transgressing. And that the concubine signified the Old Testament, Paul shows, saying, Gal. IV: « Abraham had two sons, one of a bondwoman, and the other of a free woman, » [Vulg. Gal. 4:22 unum de ancilla, et unum de libera, "one of a bondwoman, and one of a free woman"] etc.

« And there were, » etc. (AUG.) It is asked how this is true, etc., as far as than that it be taken by synecdoche 163C.

VERS. 27-29.-- « The city Arbee. » (HIERON.) For Arbee the Seventy have a plain, although Hebron is on a mountain. But the same city was anciently called Mambre, from the friend of Abraham.

« The city Arbee, » etc. Of the angels or of the holy souls, to whom he was gathered, no anxiety of temptations remaining, nor peril of sins.

CAPUT XXXVI.

VERS. 6-19.-- « Abiit in alteram regionem. » (STRAB) Quaeri potest cur Esau post mortem patris dicatur abiisse in Seir, et recessisse a fratre, cum jam quando venit Jacob, in Seir dicatur habitasse? 163DSed fieri potuit ut Jacob fugiente in Mesopotamiam, Esau, dolore praereptae benedictionis, a parente recesserit, et habitaverit in Seir viginti annis quibus Jacob abfuit. Cui reverso conciliatus, rursus de Seir reversus, cum fratre suo, donec pater viveret habitavit: quo mortuo, a fratre recessit, quia divites erant, et simul habitare non poterant.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « Isti filii Seir, » etc. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr. tom. III.) Postquam enumeravit filios Esau, altius repetit qui ante Esau in terra Edom principes exstiterunt ex genere Horraeorum, qui liberi interpretantur. In Deuteronomio manifestius scribitur quomodo venerunt filii Esau, et interfectis Horraeis terram eorum haereditaverunt.

164A« Filii Seir, » etc. (STRAB.) Id est liberi qui, sicut in Paralipomenis legitur, primo habitaverunt in monte Seir; quibus expulsis. Idumaei habitaverunt pro illis.

VERS. 22, 23.-- « Erat autem soror, » etc. (HIER.) Haec est Thamna, de qua supra dictum est: Et Thamna erat concubina Eliphaz, primogeniti Esau, etc. Ideo Horraeorum recordatur, quia primogenitus filiorum Esau ex filiabus eorum accepit concubinam. Quia autem dicitur Theman, et Cenez, et Amalec, et reliqua, sciamus postea regionibus et gentibus Idumaeorum ex his vocabula imposita.

VERS. 24-28.-- « Iste est Ana. » etc. (STRAB.) Quidam simpliciter aquas calidas hunc in deserto reperisse dicunt, quas vocamus thermas.

164B« Iste est Ana, » etc. (HIERON.) Varia apud Hebraeos de hoc capitulo disputantur, etc., usque ad ut muli contra naturam nascerentur.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « Isti duces. » De praecedentibus ait, qui de Seir; non de his quos incipit numerare, qui de Esau sunt.

VERS. 31-43.-- « Reges autem. » Sciendum quod non omnes reges commemorat qui fuerunt antequam haberent reges filii Ismael, quorum primus Saul, sed hos omnes tantum qui regnaverunt antequam moreretur Moses. « Reges autem. » Secundum tempus quo vivebat scriptor commemorat, cum autem Seir, qui istos genuit, ibi habitaret, nondum veniente Esau in terram illam, ab eo vocabatur terra Edom.

(AUG., Q. in Gen., t. III.) Non mirum quod numerantibus 164Cab Abraham per Esau patrem gentis Edom, etc., usque ad in illo enim ordine ubi plures numerantur, citius mortui sunt, quam hic ubi pauciores.

« Husan. » (HIER.) Quem quidam suspicantur esse Job, ut in fine voluminis ipsius additum est; sed Hebraei asserunt eum de stirpe Nachor generatum, ut jam supra dictum est.

CHAPTER XXXVI.

VERS. 6-19.-- « He went into another country. » (STRAB) It may be asked: why is Esau said to have gone away into Seir after his father's death, and to have withdrawn from his brother, when already, when Jacob came, he is said to have dwelt in Seir? 163DBut it could be that, while Jacob was fleeing into Mesopotamia, Esau, from grief at the blessing snatched away, withdrew from his parent, and dwelt in Seir the twenty years in which Jacob was away. Reconciled to him on his return, and having come back again from Seir, he dwelt with his brother so long as their father lived: and when he was dead, he withdrew from his brother, because they were rich, and could not dwell together.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « These are the sons of Seir, » etc. (HIERON. in Q. Hebr. tom. III.) After he had enumerated the sons of Esau, he goes further back to those who before Esau were chief men in the land of Edom, of the race of the Horrites, who are interpreted free men. In Deuteronomy it is written more plainly how the sons of Esau came, and, the Horrites being slain, inherited their land.

164A« The sons of Seir, » etc. (STRAB.) That is, the free men who, as is read in Paralipomenon, first dwelt in mount Seir; and when they were driven out. The Idumeans dwelt in their stead.

VERS. 22, 23.-- « And there was a sister, » etc. (HIER.) This is Thamna, of whom it was said above: And Thamna was the concubine of Eliphaz, the firstborn of Esau, etc. Therefore he makes mention of the Horrites, because the firstborn of the sons of Esau took a concubine of their daughters. And because Theman is named, and Cenez, and Amalec, and the rest, let us know that afterwards names were laid upon the regions and nations of the Idumeans from these.

VERS. 24-28.-- « This is Ana. » etc. (STRAB.) Some say simply that he found hot waters in the desert, which we call thermae.

164B« This is Ana, » etc. (HIERON.) Various things are disputed among the Hebrews concerning this chapter, etc., as far as that mules might be born against nature.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « These are the dukes. » He speaks of those going before, who are of Seir; not of these whom he begins to number, who are of Esau.

VERS. 31-43.-- « And the kings. » It is to be known that he does not record all the kings who were before the children of Ismael had kings, of whom the first was Saul, but only all those who reigned before Moses died. « And the kings. » He records according to the time in which the writer was living, but when Seir, who begot these men, dwelt there, Esau not yet having come into that land, it was called from him the land of Edom.

(AUG., Q. in Gen., t. III.) It is no wonder that to those numbering 164Cfrom Abraham through Esau the father of the nation of Edom, etc., as far as for in that order where more are numbered, they died sooner than here where fewer are.

« Husan. » (HIER.) Whom some suspect to be Job, as is added at the end of his own volume; but the Hebrews affirm that he was begotten of the stock of Nachor, as has already been said above.

CAPUT XXXVII.

VERS. 2.-- « Joseph cum sedecim esset annorum, » etc., (AUG. in Q. super Genesin. ) Historice. Quomodo poterit mors Isaac, etc., usque ad vivo tamen Isaac decem et septem annorum coepit esse Joseph.

« Et erat cum filiis Balae, » etc. Joseph unus ex 164Dduodecim filiis Jacob, prae caeteris patri dilectus, Christum significavit, quem Deus Pater secundum carnem natum caeteris fratribus ex Abrahae stirpe progenitis praetulit. Unde amabat eum Jacob, « eo quod in senectute genuisset eum. » Senescente enim mundo, Filius Dei de Virgine natus fuit, tanquam filius senectutis.

« Et erat cum, » etc. In primitiva Ecclesia nec abjiciebat sibi adhaerentes per praedicationem pseudoapostolorum, quos significant ancillae Jacob, quos et noluit prohibere, cum in nomine ejus miracula facerent.

VERS. 3.-- « Fecitque ei tunicam polymitam. » (HIER.) Pro tunica varia Aquila interpretatus est 165Aἀστραγάλον, id est talarem, Symmachus manicatam, sive quod ad talos usque descenderet et manibus artificis mira esset varietate distincta, sive quod haberet manicas: antiqui enim magis colobiis, id est vestibus sine manicis utebantur.

(GREG., hom. XXV in Evang.) Quia Joseph inter fratres usque ad finem vitae justus perseverasse describitur, solus talarem tunicam habuisse perhibetur. Quid est talaris tunica, nisi actio consummata? Quasi enim protensa tunica talum corporis operuit, cum bona actio ante oculos Dei usque ad terminos vitae nos regit. Unde Moyses caudam hostiae offerri praecepit, ut opus bonum quod incipimus perseveranti fine compleamus.

VERS. 4-9.-- « Videntes autem fratres ejus, » 165Betc. Fratres Christi secundum carnem Judaei, de quorum cognatione carnem suscepit. Sed quia videbant quod gratia virtutum in eo fiebat, quod testabatur Patris praecipuam dilectionem, invidebant, nec poterant ei quidquam pacifice loqui, et saepius insidias tentabant, et Patremfamilias in Beelzebub principe daemoniorum daemonia ejicere blasphemabant.

(ISID.) Hoc spiritualiter in Christo impletum est, etc., usque ad in stellarum figura.

VERS. 10, 11.-- « Num ego, » etc. Quaeri potest secundum litteram quando a patre adoratus sit, qui per solem; et a matre, quae per lunam designatur. Neque enim Jacob, quando descendit in Aegyptum, legitur adorasse eum: mater vero jam defuncta erat: 165Csed ad allegoriam recurramus, quae in Christo impletur.

VERS. 12-22.-- « Cumque fratres illius, » etc., « in pascendis gregibus, » usque « Renuntia mihi, » etc. (ISID.) Jacob mittit filium ut de fratribus curam gereret; et Deus Pater Filium unigenitum, ut genus humanum peccatis languidum visitaret. Unde Galat. capite quarto: « Misit Deus Filium suum in similitudinem carnis peccati. » Missus est Joseph ut videret si cuncta prospera essent erga oves: et Christus ait Matthaei decimo quinto: « Non veni nisi ad oves quae perierant domus Israel. »

« In Dothaim. » Defectionem. In grandi enim defectione erant, qui de fratricidio cogitabant. « Ruben. » Praevidens hoc sermo propheticus, quem 165DRuben significat (qui interpretatur visio filii ), propheticis comminationibus deterrebat, ut innocentes se servarent a sanguine Christi, fratris sui secundum carnem.

VERS. 23, 24.-- « Nudaverunt eum, » etc. (ISID.) Mystice. Et Judaei Christum per mortem exspoliaverunt tunica corporali et polimyta, id est, decorata omnium virtutum diversitate. Resperserunt autem tunicam sanguine haedi: quia falsis testimoniis eum accusantes, in invidiam peccati duxerunt. Joseph mittitur in cisternam, id est in lacum: et Christus exspolitus carne humana descendit ad infernum.

VERS. 25-27.-- « Viderunt viatores Ismaelitas. » (AUG.) Quaeritur quare Ismaelitas Scriptura, etc., 166Ausque ad qui dicunt Agar et Ceturam unam fuisse?

VERS. 28-33.-- « Extrahentes. » (ISID.) Allegorice. Joseph de cisterna levatus Ismaelitis venditis venditur: et Christus ab inferno regressus ab omnibus gentibus fidei commercio comparatur. Ille per Judae consilium triginta argenteis distrahitur, et hic per consilium Judae Iscariotae numero eodem venundatur.

« Vendiderunt. » (HIERON.) Hebraica veritas habet « argenteis. » neque enim viliori metallo Dominus vendi debuit quam Joseph.

VERS. 34.-- « Scissisque vestibus, » etc. (ISID.) Jacob, posteritatis suae plorans dispendia, quasi pater filium lugebat amissum, et quasi propheta interitum Judaeorum (Matth. XXVI). Scidit vestimentum: 166Bquod in passione Domini fecit princeps sacerdotum (Luc. XXIII). Sed et velum templi scissum est, ut prophetaret nudatum populum suum, et divisum ostenderet regnum.

VERS. 35, 36.-- « Descendam ad filium meum. » etc. Quaeritur quomodo hoc intelligatur, utrum mali tantum, an etiam boni in infernum descendant? Si tantum mali, quomodo iste ad filium suum dicit se lugentem descendere velle? Non enim in poenis inferni credibile est eum esse. An perturbati, et dolentis verba sunt, mala sua etiam hic exaggerantis? an credibile est apud infernum quaedam loca secreta, et minus poenalia ante passionem Domini fuisse, ad quae etiam sancti descendebant? quia nullus paradisum ingredi poterat, donec Christus rhomphaeam 166Cigneam amoveret.

(HIERON.) Madianei autem vendiderunt Joseph in Aegypto Putiphari eunucho, Pharaonis archimagiro. Plerumque sb;αρχιμαγείρους, id est, coquorum principes, pro magistris exercitus Scriptura ponit; μάγειρος enim Graece est coquus, et dicitur μαγειρειύεν, quod est occidere: eo quod coqui occidant coquendas pecudes. Venditus est igitur Joseph principi laniorum, non Petefre, ut in Latino scriptum est, sed Putiphari eunucho. Ubi quaeritur quomodo postea uxorem habere dicatur? Tradunt Hebraei emptum ab hoc Joseph ob nimiam pulchritudinem in turpe ministerium, et a domino, virilibus ejus arefactis, postea electum esse juxta morem hierophantarum, id est, sacra loquentium in pontificatu Heliopoleos: 166Det hujus filiam esse Aseneth, quam postea Joseph acceperit uxorem.

CHAPTER XXXVII.

VERS. 2.-- « Joseph, when he was sixteen years old, » etc., (AUG. in Q. super Genesin. ) Historically. How can the death of Isaac, etc., as far as yet in Isaac's lifetime Joseph began to be seventeen years old.

« And he was with the sons of Bala, » etc. Joseph, one of 164Dthe twelve sons of Jacob, beloved of his father beyond the rest, signified Christ, whom, born according to the flesh, God the Father preferred to the rest of the brethren begotten of the stock of Abraham. Whence Jacob loved him, « because he had begotten him in his old age. » For when the world was growing old, the Son of God was born of the Virgin, as it were a son of old age.

« And he was with, » etc. In the primitive Church he did not cast off those who clave to him through the preaching of false apostles, whom the handmaids of Jacob signify, whom also he would not forbid, when they wrought miracles in his name.

VERS. 3.-- « And he made him a coat of divers colours. » (HIER.) For a parti-coloured tunic Aquila has rendered 165Aἀστραγάλον, that is, reaching to the ankles, Symmachus sleeved, either because it came down as far as the ankles and was marked out with a wonderful variety by the craftsman's hands, or because it had sleeves: for the ancients used rather colobia, that is, garments without sleeves.

(GREG., hom. XXV in Evang.) Because Joseph is described as having persevered just among his brethren to the end of his life, he alone is said to have had a coat reaching to the ankles. What is a coat reaching to the ankles, but a completed action? For as it were a coat stretched down covered the ankle of the body, when a good action rules us before the eyes of God to the bounds of life. Whence Moses commanded the tail of the victim to be offered, that we may complete with a persevering end the good work which we begin.

VERS. 4-9.-- « And his brethren seeing, » 165Betc. The brethren of Christ according to the flesh are the Jews, of whose kindred he took flesh. But because they saw that the grace of virtues was wrought in him, which bore witness to the Father's especial love, they envied him, and could not speak anything peaceably to him, and again and again attempted snares, and blasphemously said that the Master of the household cast out demons by Beelzebub the prince of demons.

(ISID.) This was spiritually fulfilled in Christ, etc., as far as in the figure of the stars.

VERS. 10, 11.-- « Shall I, » etc. It may be asked according to the letter when he was adored by his father, who is designated by the sun; and by his mother, who is designated by the moon. For neither is Jacob read to have adored him when he went down into Egypt: and his mother was already dead: 165Cbut let us have recourse to the allegory, which is fulfilled in Christ.

VERS. 12-22.-- « And when his brethren, » etc., « in feeding the flocks, » as far as « Bring me word, » etc. (ISID.) Jacob sends his son to take care of his brethren; and God the Father his only-begotten Son, to visit the human race sick with sins. Whence Galatians, chapter four: « God sent his Son in the likeness of sinful flesh. » [Vulg. Rom. 8:3 Deus Filium suum mittens in similitudinem carnis peccati; the chapter cited, Gal. 4:4, has Misit Deus Filium suum, factum ex muliere] Joseph was sent to see whether all things were prosperous with the sheep: and Christ says, in Matthew the fifteenth: « I came not but to the sheep that had perished of the house of Israel. » [Vulg. Matt. 15:24 Non sum missus nisi ad oves, quae perierunt domus Israel, "I was not sent but to the sheep that are perished"]

« In Dothaim. » A falling away. For they were in a great falling away, who were thinking of fratricide. « Ruben. » Foreseeing this, the prophetic word, which 165DRuben signifies (who is interpreted the vision of the son ), deterred them by prophetic threatenings, that they might keep themselves innocent of the blood of Christ, their brother according to the flesh.

VERS. 23, 24.-- « They stripped him, » etc. (ISID.) Mystically. The Jews too stripped Christ by death of his bodily coat, and that of divers colours, that is, adorned with the variety of all the virtues. And they sprinkled the coat with the blood of a kid: because, accusing him by false testimonies, they brought him into the odium of sin. Joseph is sent into the cistern, that is, into the pit: and Christ, stripped of human flesh, went down to hell.

VERS. 25-27.-- « They saw travellers, Ismaelites. » (AUG.) It is asked why Scripture [names them] Ismaelites, etc., 166Aas far as who say that Agar and Cetura were one and the same?

VERS. 28-33.-- « Drawing him out. » (ISID.) Allegorically. Joseph, lifted out of the cistern, is sold to Ismaelites that had been sold: and Christ, returned from hell, is bought by all nations in the traffic of faith. The one is sold off for thirty pieces of silver by the counsel of Judas, and this one is sold for the same number by the counsel of Judas Iscariot.

« They sold. » (HIERON.) The Hebrew verity has « pieces of silver. » for neither ought the Lord to have been sold for a baser metal than Joseph.

VERS. 34.-- « And having rent his garments, » etc. (ISID.) Jacob, bewailing the losses of his posterity, mourned as a father for a son that was lost, and as a prophet for the destruction of the Jews (Matth. XXVI). He rent his garment: 166Bwhich in the Lord's passion the chief of the priests did (Luc. XXIII). But the veil of the temple also was rent, that he might prophesy his people stripped bare, and show the kingdom divided.

VERS. 35, 36.-- « I will go down to my son. » etc. It is asked: is this to be understood of the wicked only, or do the good also go down into hell? If the wicked only, how does this man say that he wishes to go down mourning to his son? For it is not credible that he is in the pains of hell. Or are they the words of a troubled and grieving man, magnifying his own evils here also? or is it credible that near hell there were certain hidden places, and less penal, before the Lord's passion, to which the saints also went down? because none could enter paradise, until Christ should take away the flaming 166Csword.

(HIERON.) But the Madianites sold Joseph in Egypt to Putiphar the eunuch, Pharaoh's chief cook. For the most part Scripture puts ἀρχιμαγείρους, that is, chiefs of the cooks, for masters of an army; for μάγειρος in Greek is a cook, and there is said μαγειρειύεν, which is to kill: because cooks kill the beasts that are to be cooked. Joseph therefore was sold to the chief of the butchers, not to Petefre, as it is written in the Latin, but to Putiphar the eunuch. Whereupon it is asked: how is he afterwards said to have a wife? The Hebrews hand down that Joseph was bought by this man for a foul service because of his exceeding beauty, and that afterwards, his manhood being withered by his master, he was chosen according to the custom of the hierophants, that is, of those who speak sacred things, into the pontificate of Heliopolis: 166Dand that Aseneth was this man's daughter, whom Joseph afterwards took to wife.

CAPUT XXXVIII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Eodem tempore, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen.) Quaeritur quomodo ista fieri potuerunt? Si enim posteaquam Joseph venit in Aegyptum, etc., usque ad incidisse eum in amorem ejus, quam duxit uxorem, nondum vendito Joseph.

« Judas, » etc. Hic, sicut caeteri patriarchae, Christum significat, qui descendit ab baptismum Joannis. Hiras enim interpretatur videns Deum: Odollamites, testimonium in aqua, quia Joannes videns Christum venientem ad baptismum, ait: « Ecce 167AAgnus Dei, » etc. (Joan. I.) Ad hoc testimonium adhaesit Christo Synagoga, quam significat Sue, filia Chananaei, quia ei danda erat terra humilis professionis, quod interpretatur Chanaan. Haec ergo Synagoga primo adhaesit Christo, quando Petrus et Andreas, et caeteri Joannis discipuli facti sunt discipuli Christi. Quo ordine haec completa sunt sequens significat historia, narrans quomodo Judas intravit ad Thamar, quae idem significat quod Sue.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « Quae concepit, » etc. Genuit Synagoga principes, qui ei tanquam mariti praefuerunt: quorum alii fuerunt nocentes, quos significat Her, qui interpretatur pelliceus (pellis enim mortem significat), quia primi parentes pellibus induti addicti sunt morti; alii inutiles, quos significat Onan, 167Bqui interpretatur moeror eorum, quibus scilicet non prosunt.

VERS. 5-12.-- « Tertium quoque peperit, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Tertius filius qui Thamar non jungitur, etc., usque ad et ideo tanquam Odollamites perhibuit testimonium in aqua.

« Judas, » etc. De Juda regia tribus processit, quae diu praefuit Judaicae plebi, quam significat Thamar, quae interpretatur amaritudo, quia amaram se in passione exibuit Christo.

(ISID.) Plebs Judaica, cui de tribu Juda reges, etc., usque ad Deus tamen utrosque occidit, quia regnum a talibus tollit.

VERS. 13.-- « Ad tondendas oves. » (STRAB.) Allegorice. Venit Dominus ad oves tondendas, id est 167Cpeccatis exonerandas, de quibus dicitur: « Dentes tui sicut greges tonsarum. » Thamar habitum mutat: nam et commutans interpretatur; mutat habitum et nomen, ut sit de Synagoga Ecclesia. Sed nomen amaritudinis manet: non in qua fel Domino ministravit, sed in qua Petrus amare flevit. Nam et Judas Confessio dicitur, Confessioni ergo amaritudo misceatur, ut vera poenitentia praesignetur. Haec poenitentia fecundatur, in omnibus gentibus Ecclesia constituta. Oportebat enim pati Christum, et resurgere, et praedicari in nomine ejus poenitentiam et remissionem peccatorum per omnes gentes. Habitus quoque meretricis confessio peccatorum est: Thamar quippe Ecclesiam de gentibus vocatam significat; sedens cum hoc habitu ad portam Enaiim 167D, quod interpretatur fons: quia currebat velut cervus ad fontes aquarum, pervenire ad semen Abrahae. Illo non cognoscente fecundatur, secundum illud Psalmi XVII. « Populus, quem non cognovi, servivit mihi. » Accepit in occulto annulum, monile, et virgam, quia vocatione signatur, justificatione decoratur, glorificatione exaltatur. « Quos autem praedestinavit, hos et vocavit; et quos vocavit, hos et justificavit (Rom. VIII); » sed in occulto, ubi conceptio sanctae ubertatis fit.

VERS. 14-19.-- « Quae depositis viduitatis vestibus, » etc. Notandum etiam in temporibus patriarcharum certa fuisse vestimenta viduarum, nec qualia conjugatarum.

168A« Sedit in bivio. » (HIERON.) « Et sedit ad portam Enaiim, quae est in transitu Thamna. » Sermo Hebraicus, Enaiim, transfertur in oculos. Non est igitur nomen loci, et est sensus, « Sedit in bivio, » sive in compito, ubi diligentius debet viator aspicere quo gradiatur.

VERS. 20-24.-- « Misit autem Judas. » Mittitur promissus haedus tanquam meretrici, id est exprobratio peccati, per Odolamitem, tanquam increpantem, « genimina viperarum, » etc. Sed eam non invenit peccati exprobratio, quam mutavit confessionis amaritudo. In hoc facto Judae non justitiae cernitur sinceritas, sed promissionis fidelitas. Si enim servaret justitiam servando castitatem, non introisset, ut opinabatur, ad meretricem; sed quod promisit, 168Bfideliter per pastorem transmisit.

VERS. 25.-- « Quae cum duceretur, etc. » (ISID.) Postea vero publicis signis annuli, monilis et virgae, vicit temere judicantes Judaeos, quos jam Judas significabat; qui hodie quoque dicunt non hunc esse populum Christi, nec Abrahae semen, sed prolatis documentis nostrae vocationis, justificationis, et glorificationis, confunduntur, et nos magis quam se justificatos esse confitebuntur. Pignora enim se habere refert Ecclesia, quae accusatur a Judaeis quasi adultera legis: sed ostendit virgam signum passionis, et monile legitima legis, et annulum pignus immortalitatis.

VERS. 26.--Qui agnitis, » etc. (HIERON.) « Cognovit autem Judas, etc. In Hebraeo habetur: « justificata est ex me; » non quod justa fuerit, sed quod 168Ccomparatione illius minus male fecerit, non vaga turpitudine, sed requirendo filios.

VERS. 27, 28.-- « Instante autem, » etc. Quod primus, qui dicitur Zara, manum emisit, et obstetrix coccinum ligavit et ipso manu retrahente, posterior, qui vocatur Phares, manum porrexit, et nascendo praecessit, significavit quod Israel in opere legis manum extenderet, et prophetarum et ipsius Christi cruore pollutam contraheret: populus vero gentium postea prorumperet, ut essent « primi novissimi, et novissimi primi. »

VERS. 29, 30.-- « Quare divisa, » etc. (HIERON.) Pro maceria Aquila et Symmachus divisionem transtulerunt, quod Hebraice dicitur Phares. Ab eo igitur quod diviserit membranulam secundinarum divisionis 168Dnomen accepit. Unde et Pharisaei, qui se quasi justos a populo separaverant, Pharisaei, id est divisi dicebantur.

Zara interpretatur oriens. Quia primus apparuit, vel quia plurimi justi ex eo nati sunt, sicut dicitur in Paralipomenis.

CHAPTER XXXVIII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « At the same time, » etc. (AUG., Q. in Gen.) It is asked: how could these things have come to pass? For if, after Joseph came into Egypt, etc., as far as that he fell into love of her whom he took to wife, Joseph not yet being sold.

« Judas, » etc. He, like the other patriarchs, signifies Christ, who went down from the baptism ab baptismum of John. For Hiras is interpreted seeing God: Odollamite, testimony in water, because John, seeing Christ coming to the baptism, said: « Behold 167Athe Lamb of God, » etc. (Joan. I.) To this testimony the Synagogue clave to Christ, which Sue, the daughter of the Chanaanite, signifies, because to her was to be given the land of humble profession, which is the interpretation of Chanaan. This Synagogue therefore first clave to Christ, when Peter and Andrew, and the rest of John's disciples, were made disciples of Christ. In what order these things were fulfilled the history following signifies, relating how Judas went in to Thamar, who signifies the same as Sue.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « And she conceived, » etc. The Synagogue bore princes, who were set over her as husbands: of whom some were harmful, whom Her signifies, who is interpreted made of skin (for a skin signifies death), because the first parents, clothed with skins, were made over to death; others useless, whom Onan signifies, 167Bwho is interpreted their sorrow, namely to those whom they profit not.

VERS. 5-12.-- « She bore a third also, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) The third son, who is not joined to Thamar, etc., as far as and therefore as an Odollamite he bore testimony in water.

« Judas, » etc. From Juda came forth the royal tribe, which long ruled the Jewish people, which Thamar signifies, who is interpreted bitterness, because she showed herself bitter to Christ in his passion.

(ISID.) The Jewish people, to whom kings from the tribe of Juda, etc., as far as yet God slew them both, because he takes away the kingdom from such men.

VERS. 13.-- « To shear the sheep. » (STRAB.) Allegorically. The Lord came to shear the sheep, that is, 167Cto unburden them of their sins, of which it is said: « Thy teeth as flocks of the shorn. » Thamar changes her habit: for she is also interpreted changing; she changes both habit and name, that of the Synagogue there may be a Church. But the name of bitterness remains: not that in which she ministered gall to the Lord, but that in which Peter wept bitterly. For Judas too is called Confession, let bitterness therefore be mingled with Confession, that true penance may be prefigured. This penance is made fruitful, the Church being established among all nations. For it behoved Christ to suffer, and to rise again, and that penance and remission of sins should be preached in his name among all nations. The habit of a harlot too is the confession of sins: for Thamar signifies the Church called out of the Gentiles; sitting in this habit at the gate of Enaiim 167D, which is interpreted a fountain: because she was running like a hart to the fountains of waters, to come to the seed of Abraham. She is made fruitful, he not knowing it, according to that of Psalm XVII. « The people which I knew not hath served me. » She received in secret the ring, the necklace, and the staff, because she is sealed by vocation, adorned by justification, exalted by glorification. « And whom he predestinated, them he also called; and whom he called, them he also justified (Rom. VIII); » but in secret, where the conception of holy fruitfulness is made.

VERS. 14-19.-- « Who, having laid aside the garments of her widowhood, » etc. It is to be noted also that in the times of the patriarchs there were fixed garments of widows, and not such as those of married women.

168A« She sat in the crossway. » (HIERON.) « And she sat at the gate of Enaiim, which is in the way to Thamna. » The Hebrew word Enaiim is rendered eyes. It is not therefore the name of a place, and the sense is, « She sat in the crossway, » or at the meeting of the roads, where a traveller ought to look the more carefully where he is going.

VERS. 20-24.-- « And Judas sent. » The promised kid is sent as to a harlot, that is, the reproach of sin, by the Odollamite, as by one rebuking, « brood of vipers, » etc. But the reproach of sin did not find her whom the bitterness of confession had changed. In this deed of Juda it is not the sincerity of justice that is discerned, but the faithfulness of his promise. For if he had kept justice by keeping chastity, he would not have gone in, as he supposed, to a harlot; but what he promised, 168Bhe faithfully sent by the shepherd.

VERS. 25.-- « And when she was led forth, etc. » (ISID.) But afterwards, by the public tokens of the ring, the necklace and the staff, she overcame the Jews who judged rashly, whom Judas already signified; who to this day also say that this is not the people of Christ, nor the seed of Abraham, but, when the proofs of our vocation, justification and glorification are brought forward, they are confounded, and will confess that we rather than they have been justified. For the Church, which is accused by the Jews as an adulteress against the law, declares that she has the pledges: but she shows the staff, the sign of the passion, and the necklace, the lawful things of the law, and the ring, the pledge of immortality.

VERS. 26.--Who, when they were acknowledged, » etc. (HIERON.) « And Judas acknowledged, etc. In the Hebrew it is found: « she is justified from me; » not that she was just, but that 168Cin comparison with him she had done less ill, not in wandering baseness, but in seeking children.

VERS. 27, 28.-- « And when she was in labour, » etc. That the first, who is called Zara, put forth his hand, and the midwife bound the scarlet, and, he himself drawing his hand back, the later, who is called Phares, stretched out his hand, and in being born went before him, signified that Israel would stretch out his hand in the work of the law, and would draw it back defiled with the blood of the prophets and of Christ himself: but that the people of the Gentiles would afterwards break forth, so that « the first should be last, and the last first. »

VERS. 29, 30.-- « Why is the wall divided, » etc. (HIERON.) For a wall Aquila and Symmachus have translated a division, which in Hebrew is said Phares. From this therefore, that he divided the membrane of the afterbirth, he took the name of division 168D. Whence also the Pharisees, who had separated themselves from the people as though they were just, were called Pharisees, that is, divided.

Zara is interpreted the rising. Because he appeared first, or because very many just men were born of him, as is said in Paralipomenon.

CAPUT XXXIX.

VERS. 1, 6.-- « Igitur Joseph ductus est in Aegyptum, » etc. (AUG. in Gen.) Ad ordinem tendit Scriptura unde recesserat, ut illa narret quae supra gesta sunt. « Fuitque, » etc. (GREG., lib. VI Moral., c. 11 seq.) Multi humana sapientia inflati, dum desideriis suis divina judicia contrarie aspiciunt, etc., usque ad ejusque pondera leviat, quae volens portat.

169AVERS. 7-11.-- « Post multos itaque dies injecit domina, » etc. (GREG. lib. XXX Moral., cap. 19.) Qui mundi successibus elevatus, etc., usque ad quia enim voluptas ex prosperitate nascitur, ejusdem consideratione feriatur.

« Injecit domina sua, » etc. (ISID.) Haec figura est Synagogae, etc., usque ad ubi enim veluti puniendus descenderat, inde alios liberavit.

VERS. 12-23.-- « Qui, relicto in manu ejus pallio, » etc. Sic nos tentant carnis illecebrae, et succutiunt vestem carneam: sed vir castus mavult omnia vincula dirumpere, quam se turpitudini subjugare.

Qui relicto, » etc. (GREG.) Allegorice. Quia dum Synagoga Christum, purum hominem credens, adulterino complexu constringere voluit, ipse tegmen 169Blitterae oculis ejus objecit, et ad cognoscendam divinitatis potentiam conspicuum se gentibus praebuit. Unde usque hodie dum legitur Moses, velamen est super oculos cordis eorum, quia scilicet adultera pallium retinuit, et quem male tenebat nudum amisit.

CHAPTER XXXIX.

VERS. 1, 6.-- « Joseph therefore was brought into Egypt, » etc. (AUG. in Gen.) Scripture returns to the order from which it had departed, that it may relate those things which were done above. « And he was, » etc. (GREG., lib. VI Moral., c. 11 seq.) Many, puffed up with human wisdom, while they look upon the divine judgments as contrary to their own desires, etc., as far as and lightens his burdens, which he bears willingly.

169AVERS. 7-11.-- « And after many days his mistress cast, » etc. (GREG. lib. XXX Moral., cap. 19.) He who is lifted up by the successes of the world, etc., as far as for because pleasure is born of prosperity, let it be struck down by the consideration of the same.

« His mistress cast her eyes, » etc. (ISID.) This is a figure of the Synagogue, etc., as far as for whence he had gone down as though to be punished, thence he set others free.

VERS. 12-23.-- « Who, leaving the cloak in her hand, » etc. So do the allurements of the flesh tempt us, and shake the garment of flesh: but the chaste man had rather break every bond than subject himself to baseness.

Who leaving, » etc. (GREG.) Allegorically. Because, while the Synagogue, believing Christ a mere man, wished to bind him in an adulterous embrace, he cast the covering 169Bof the letter before her eyes, and offered himself to be seen by the Gentiles, that the power of his godhead might be known. Whence to this day, while Moses is read, a veil is upon the eyes of their heart, because the adulteress kept the cloak, and lost naked him whom she was wrongfully holding.

CAPUT XL.

VERS. 1-8.-- « His ita gestis, accidit ut peccarent duo eunuchi, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Invenit Joseph duos eunuchos in carcere, etc., usque ad trangressionis ligno suspenditur.

« Pincerna regis Aegypti, et pistor, domino suo, » etc. (HIERON.) Ubi nos posuimus « principem vinariorum » in Hebraeo habetur masqueh , illud 169Cverbum quod in nomine servi Abrahae dudum legimus, quem nos possumus more vulgi vocare pincernam, et usque hodie apud barbaros maximae dignitatis est, regi poculum porrexisse.

VERS. 9-15.-- « Videbam coram me vitem, » etc. (HIERON.) « Et ecce vitis in conspectu meo, et in vite tres fundi, et ipsa germinans tres fundos. » Tria flagella, tres ramos, sive propagines, Hebraeus sermo significat, quae ab illis vocantur Sarigim .

VERS. 16-19.-- « Tria canistra. » (AUG. Quaest. in Gen.) Ubi in Latinis codicibus « tria canistra farinae » scriptum est, etc., usque ad et ex omni genere pistorii operis in eodem scilicet canistro superiore.

VERS. 20-23.-- « Natalitius Pharaonis. » Pharao 169Det Herodes diem nativitatis celebraverunt, quia ex hora nativitatis se reges fuisse putaverunt.

CHAPTER XL.

VERS. 1-8.-- « These things being thus done, it happened that two eunuchs sinned, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Joseph found two eunuchs in the prison, etc., as far as he is hanged upon the wood of transgression trangressionis.

« The butler of the king of Egypt, and the baker, against their lord, » etc. (HIERON.) Where we have put « the chief of the vintners » in the Hebrew is found masqueh [Migne prints a Hebrew word here; our transcription of the Latin has lost it, leaving the comma that follows standing free.], that 169Cword which we read long since in the name of Abraham's servant, whom we may in the common way call a butler, and to this day among the barbarians it is of the greatest dignity to have handed the cup to the king.

VERS. 9-15.-- « I saw before me a vine, » etc. (HIERON.) « And behold a vine in my sight, and on the vine three stems, and it budding forth three stems. » The Hebrew tongue signifies three shoots, three branches, or layers, which are called by them Sarigim [Migne prints a Hebrew word here; our transcription of the Latin has lost it, leaving the full stop that follows standing free.].

VERS. 16-19.-- « Three baskets. » (AUG. Quaest. in Gen.) Where in the Latin copies is written « three baskets of meal, » etc., as far as and of every kind of baker's work, namely in the same uppermost basket.

VERS. 20-23.-- « The birthday of Pharaoh. » Pharaoh 169Dand Herod kept the day of their birth, because they thought that from the hour of their birth they had been kings.

CAPUT XLI.

VERS. 1.-- « Post duos annos vidit Pharao somnium. Putabat se stare, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Sicut servus Abrahae dixit, etc., usque ad ubi habitant terrena animalia.

VERS. 2-8.-- « De quo ascendebant septem boves, » etc. (HIERON. Q. Hebr.) Ecce de fluvio ascendebant septem boves speciosae ad videndum, etc., usque ad χ Graecam copulaverunt.

VERS. 9-16.-- « Tunc demum reminiscens pincernarum magister, » etc. Quia gentilis populus tandem 170Aper exhibitionem praeteritorum se intulit in spem futurorum.

VERS. 17-32.-- « Putabam me stare, » etc. Septem anni, qui septem spicis plenis, vel septem vaccis pinguibus ostenduntur, spiritualia dona significant, quibus ubertas fidei larga pietate redundat. Septem vero steriles et jejuni, veritalis et justitiae famem in novissimo tempore.

VERS. 33.-- « Nunc ergo, » etc. Hoc tempore gratiae videmus impletum: quia abundante doctrina apostolica, septiformi Spiritu ministrata, divina dispositione per singulas urbes episcopi et doctores ordinati sunt, qui abundantia doctrinae divinitus sibi collatae libros sanctos scripserunt, quibus abundante fame verbi, et deficientibus doctoribus, indigentiam 170Bnostram reficeremus.

VERS. 34-37.-- « Et quintam partem fructuum, » etc. Notandum Aegyptii quod quintam partem frugum in horrea regis congregant: quia filii tenebrarum, quinque sensibus dediti, coelestia non curant: sed veri Israelitae decimas dant, quia coelestem patriam, cujus decima pars corruit, reformare laborant.

(ISID.) Congregavit Joseph per septem annos omnem frugum abundantiam: id est, frumenta fidei sanctorum horreis condens, per septem charismata, quasi per septem annos, ut cum septem anni inopiae coeperint, id est, cum iniquitas occurrerit septem capitalium criminum sub Antichristo, quando fames fidei fuerit et salutis, tunc habeant copiosam 170Cjustitiae frugem, ne fides eorum inopia sermonis deficiat.

VERS. 38-40.-- « Qui Spiritus Dei plenus sit, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen.) Tertio, nisi fallor, insinuatur nobis in hoc libro Spiritus sanctus. Primo ubi dictum est supra (cap. I): « Spiritus ferebatur super aquas, » secundo ubi dicit Deus supra (cap. VI): « Non permanebit Spiritus meus in hominibus istis, » etc. Tertio cum dicit Pharao in Joseph esse Spiritum Dei: nondum tamen legimus Spiritum sanctum.

VERS. 41.-- « Dixitque rursus Pharao, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorice. Joseph, qui typum Christi gerebat, currum meruit, et praeco praeconavit ante eum, et constituit eum Pharao super universam terram Aegypti: et Dominus noster, postquam distractus 170Dest a Juda, sicut Joseph a fratribus et de inferni carcere surrexit, ascendit currum coelestis regni, de quo dicitur psalmo LXVII: « Currus Dei decem millibus multiplex; » et accepit potestatem praedicandi et judicandi a Patre. Unde Phil. II: « Dedit ei nomen quod est super omne nomen, » etc. Accepit quoque annulum, id est, pontificatum fidei, quo credentium animae salutis signo signantur, frontibusque et cordibus nostris per signum crucis figura aeterni regis imprimitur. Induitur stola byssina, id est, carne sancta bysso splendidiore, et stola immortalitatis. Accepit quoque torquem auream, id est, intellectum bonum. Praeco antecedit eum, id est, Joannes Baptista, qui ait Matth. III: « Parate viam 171ADomini. » Habebit et alium praeconem, quia veniet in tuba angeli.

VERS. 41, 42.-- « Ecce constitui te super universam terram Aegypti, » etc. Joseph de carcere eductus, super Aegyptum constituitur; et Christus resurgens accepit « nomen quod est super omne nomen, ut in nomine Jesu omne genu flectatur (Philipp. II). »

VERS. 43, 44.-- « Clamante praecone, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr. ) « et clamavit ante eum praeco, et constituit eum super omnem terram Aegypti. » Pro quo Aquila transtulit: « Et clamavit in conspectu ejus ad geniculationem. » Symmachus, etc., usque ad quod juxta prudentiam omnium pater fuit, sed juxta aetatem tenerrimus adolescens 171Bet puer.

VERS. 45-49.-- « Et vocavit, » etc. (HIER., ibid. ) Et vocavit Pharao nomen Joseph, etc., usque ad ut vera illa Hebraeorum super eo quod ante diximus suspicio comprobetur.

« Deditque illi uxorem, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Quaeri solet cujus Putipharis, alias Petefre, ut plerique habent et corrupte, ut supra dictum est, etc., usque ad non est fidei periculosum, nec contrarium veritati Scripturarum.

VERS. 50-58.-- « Nati sunt autem Joseph filii duo, » etc. (HIERON.) Observa propter quaestionem quae post de filiis Joseph proponenda est, quod ante famis tempus, quo Jacob intravit in Aegyptum, duos tantum filios Joseph habuit, Manassem et 171CEphraim.

VERS. 56, 57.-- « Aperuitque Joseph universa horrea, » etc. Allegorice. Joseph a penuria frumenti salvat Aegyptum, et Christus a fame verbi liberat mundum. Aperuit horrea sua Christus in orbem terrarum, et erogatione frumenti sui omnia subjugavit. Nisi fratres Joseph vendidissent, defecerat Aegyptus: nisi Judaei Christum crucifixissent, perierat mundus. Joseph interpretatur augmentatio sive ampliatio: sed in illo Joseph ampliationem non habuit nisi sola Aegyptus; in nostro vero, universus mundus. Ille erogavit triticum; noster Dei verbum. « In omnem terram exivit sonus eorum (Psal. XVIII). »

CHAPTER XLI.

VERS. 1.-- « After two years Pharaoh saw a dream. He thought that he stood, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) As the servant of Abraham said, etc., as far as where earthly animals dwell.

VERS. 2-8.-- « Out of which came up seven oxen, » etc. (HIERON. Q. Hebr.) Behold, out of the river came up seven kine fair to look upon, etc., as far as they joined the Greek χ.

VERS. 9-16.-- « Then at length the chief of the butlers remembering, » etc. Because the Gentile people at last 170Aby the setting forth of things past brought itself into the hope of things to come.

VERS. 17-32.-- « I thought that I stood, » etc. The seven years, which are shown by the seven full ears, or by the seven fat kine, signify the spiritual gifts by which the plenty of faith overflows with a bountiful loving-kindness. But the seven barren and lean ones, the famine of truth and of justice in the last time.

VERS. 33.-- « Now therefore, » etc. We see this fulfilled in the time of grace: because, apostolic doctrine abounding and being ministered by the sevenfold Spirit, bishops and teachers were ordained by divine disposition through the several cities, who out of the abundance of doctrine divinely bestowed upon them wrote the holy books, wherewith, when famine of the word abounds and teachers fail, we may relieve our need 170B.

VERS. 34-37.-- « And the fifth part of the fruits, » etc. It is to be noted that the Egyptians gather the fifth part of the corn into the granaries of the king: because the sons of darkness, given over to the five senses, care not for heavenly things: but the true Israelites give tithes, because they labour to restore the heavenly country, of which a tenth part fell.

(ISID.) Joseph gathered through seven years all the abundance of the corn: that is, storing the grain of faith in the granaries of the saints, through the seven gifts, as it were through seven years, so that when the seven years of want shall have begun, that is, when the iniquity of the seven capital crimes shall have come under Antichrist, when there shall be a famine of faith and of salvation, then the saints may have a plentiful 170Charvest of justice, lest their faith fail through want of the word.

VERS. 38-40.-- « Who is full of the Spirit of God, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen.) For the third time, unless I am mistaken, the Holy Spirit is intimated to us in this book. First where it was said above (cap. I): « The Spirit of God moved over the waters, » secondly where God says above (cap. VI): « My Spirit shall not remain in these men, » [Vulg. Gen. 6:3 Non permanebit spiritus meus in homine in aeternum, quia caro est, "in man for ever, for he is flesh"] etc. Thirdly when Pharaoh says that the Spirit of God is in Joseph: yet we do not yet read of the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 41.-- « And Pharaoh said again, » etc. (ISID.) Allegorically. Joseph, who bore the type of Christ, earned a chariot, and a herald made proclamation before him, and Pharaoh set him over the whole land of Egypt: and our Lord, after he was sold away 170Dby Juda, as Joseph by his brethren, and rose from the prison of hell, ascended the chariot of the heavenly kingdom, of which it is said in psalm LXVII: « The chariot of God is manifold by ten thousands; » and he received from the Father the power of preaching and of judging. Whence Phil. II: « He gave him a name which is above every name, » [Vulg. Phil. 2:9 donavit illi nomen, quod est super omne nomen, "he bestowed on him a name"] etc. He received also a ring, that is, the pontificate of faith, wherewith the souls of believers are sealed with the sign of salvation, and upon our foreheads and hearts the image of the eternal king is stamped by the sign of the cross. He is clothed with a robe of fine linen, that is, with holy flesh more shining than fine linen, and with the robe of immortality. He received also a golden collar, that is, a good understanding. A herald goes before him, that is, John the Baptist, who says in Matth. III: « Prepare the way 171Aof the Lord. » He shall have another herald also, because he shall come at the trumpet of the angel.

VERS. 41, 42.-- « Behold, I have set thee over the whole land of Egypt, » etc. Joseph, brought out of the prison, is set over Egypt; and Christ rising again received « a name which is above every name, that in the name of Jesus every knee should bow (Philipp. II). »

VERS. 43, 44.-- « A herald proclaiming, » etc. (HIER. in Quaest. Hebr. ) « and a herald cried before him, and he set him over all the land of Egypt. » For which Aquila has translated: « And he cried in his sight, To the bending of the knee. » Symmachus, etc., as far as that in respect of the prudence of all he was a father, but in respect of age a most tender youth 171Band a boy.

VERS. 45-49.-- « And he called, » etc. (HIER., ibid. ) And Pharaoh called the name of Joseph, etc., as far as that that suspicion of the Hebrews concerning him, of which we spoke before, may be proved true.

« And he gave him a wife, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) It is wont to be asked of which Putiphar, otherwise Petefre, as very many have it, and corruptly, as was said above, etc., as far as it is not perilous to faith, nor contrary to the truth of the Scriptures.

VERS. 50-58.-- « And there were born to Joseph two sons, » etc. (HIERON.) Observe, because of the question which is to be put afterwards concerning the sons of Joseph, that before the time of the famine, in which Jacob entered into Egypt, Joseph had only two sons, Manasses and 171CEphraim.

VERS. 56, 57.-- « And Joseph opened all the granaries, » etc. Allegorically. Joseph saves Egypt from the want of corn, and Christ frees the world from the famine of the word. Christ opened his granaries into the whole earth, and by the dispensing of his corn subdued all things. Had the brethren not sold Joseph, Egypt had failed: had the Jews not crucified Christ, the world had perished. Joseph is interpreted increase or enlargement: but in that Joseph none had the enlargement save Egypt alone; in ours, the whole world. He dispensed wheat; ours the word of God. « Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth (Psal. XVIII). »

CAPUT XLII.

  • 171D

VERS. 1-15.-- « Audiens autem Jacob quod alimenta venderentur in Aegypto, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Est frumentum in Aegypto: dicit et Deus Pater, Osee XI: « Ex Aegypto vocavi Filium meum. » Descendunt decem provectiores, id est Judaei, sub decalogo constituti; quos ipse cognoscens, non est agnitus ab eis; cognoscuntur Hebraei a Christo, et ipsi non agnoscunt eum; dederunt illi pecuniam; sed Joseph, id est Christus, triticum dedit, et argentum reddidit, quia non pecunia emitur Christus, sed gratia.

VERS. 16-22.-- « Mititte ex vobis unum . . . . . alioquin per salutem Pharaonis exploratores estis. » 172A(AUG. Quaest. in Gen. ) Quid est quod Joseph, vir sapiens, etc., usque ad erit Deus, non enim erit, sed habebitur

VERS. 22-26.-- « Nesciebant autem, » etc. (Aug., ibid. ) Quid est quod cum inter se poenitentes loquerentur de Joseph, etc., usque ad sicut ipsum in exteras provincias vendiderant.

VERS. 27-34. « Apertoque, » etc. Fratres Joseph dant argentum, quia Judaei conversi dimittunt litteram occidentem, et accipiunt spiritum vivificantem: reperitur tamen argentum in ore saccorum, quia propter fructum spiritualis intelligentiae non deseritur nitor eloquentiae.

VERS. 35, 36.-- « Singuli repererunt, » etc. Fratres Joseph dederunt pecuniam in emptione frumenti, 172Bsed eam accepto frumento receperunt, quia noster Joseph non quaerit nostra, sed nos. Gratis enim dat sua munera, et in nostra emptione nos ditiores facit.

VERS. 37.-- « Cui respondit Ruben, » etc. (STRAB.) Ruben ait: « Duos filios meos interfice, si non reduxero illum tibi, » etc., nec tamen obtinuit. At Judas dicens: « Reus ero peccati in patrem omni tempore, » impetravit, quia ei commissus est Benjamin. Plus est ergo reum esse peccati, quam corporaliter occidi.

VERS. 38.-- « Deducetis canos meos, » etc. (AUG.) Quaeritur utrum ideo ad infernum, quia cum tristitia, an etiam si abesset tristitia, tanquam ad infernum moriendo descensurus haec loquatur? De inferno 172Cenim magna quaestio est, et quid inde Scriptura sentiat, ubicunque hoc memoratum fuerit, observandum est.

CHAPTER XLII.

  • 171D

VERS. 1-15.-- « And Jacob hearing that food was sold in Egypt, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) There is corn in Egypt: God the Father says also, Osee XI: « Out of Egypt I have called my Son. » Ten of the elder brethren go down, that is, the Jews, set under the decalogue; whom he himself knowing, was not acknowledged by them; the Hebrews are known by Christ, and they themselves do not acknowledge him; they gave him money; but Joseph, that is, Christ, gave wheat, and gave back the silver, because it is not with money that Christ is bought, but with grace.

VERS. 16-22.-- « Send one of you . . . . . otherwise, by the health of Pharaoh, you are spies. » 172A(AUG. Quaest. in Gen. ) What is it that Joseph, a wise man, etc., as far as he shall be God, for he shall not be, but shall be accounted

VERS. 22-26.-- « And they knew not, » etc. (Aug., ibid. ) What is it that, when they were speaking among themselves in penitence of Joseph, etc., as far as as they had sold him into foreign provinces.

VERS. 27-34. « And one having opened, » etc. The brethren of Joseph give silver, because the Jews when converted let go the letter that killeth, and receive the spirit that quickeneth: yet the silver is found in the mouth of the sacks, because for the sake of the fruit of spiritual understanding the beauty of eloquence is not forsaken.

VERS. 35, 36.-- « Each of them found, » etc. The brethren of Joseph gave money in the buying of corn, 172Bbut they received it back when they had received the corn, because our Joseph seeks not our goods, but us. For he gives his gifts freely, and in the buying of what is ours makes us richer.

VERS. 37.-- « To whom Ruben answered, » etc. (STRAB.) Ruben says: « Kill my two sons, if I bring him not back to thee, » etc., and yet he did not prevail. But Judas, saying: « I shall be guilty of sin against my father for ever, » [Vulg. Gen. 43:9 ero peccati reus in te omni tempore, "guilty of sin against thee"] obtained it, because Benjamin was committed to him. It is more, therefore, to be guilty of sin than to be slain in the body.

VERS. 38.-- « You shall bring down my grey hairs, » etc. (AUG.) It is asked: does he speak thus of going down to hell because it is with sorrow, or, even if sorrow were absent, as one about to go down to hell by dying? For concerning hell 172Cthere is a great question, and what Scripture holds of it, wherever this is mentioned, must be observed.

CAPUT XLIII.

VERS. 11-22.-- « Modicum resinae et mellis et storacis, » etc. (HIER. Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.) Aliquid resinae, et mellis, etc., usque ad eo quod in illo sunt aromata diversa condita.

VERS. 23-28.-- « Deus vester et Deus patris vestri dedit vobis thesauros, » etc. (AUG.) Mendacium videtur, sed aliquid significare credendum est. Argentum enim, quod datur et non minuitur, quod et probatum appellatum est, illud forte est de quo dicitur psalmo XI: « Eloquia Domini eloquia casta, argentum igne examinatum, probatum terrae, purgatum 172Dseptuplum, » id est probatum perfecte.

VERS. 29-31.-- « Attollens autem Joseph oculos vidit Benjamin, » etc. (ISID.) Act. IX Paulum quasi lux circumfulsit, qui parvulus, quia nondum maturam fidei aetatem gerebat. Unde etiam adolescens legitur, dum lapidantium Stephanum vestimenta servabat. Flevit Joseph; caecitas Pauli fletus est Christi; lavit faciem Christus, cum baptizatur Paulus, per quem a plurimis videndus est Christus.

(AUG.) Affectus boni animi semper proclivis est ad pietatem. Unde in Joseph virtutum possumus considerare copiam. Castus erat, cum sprevit impudicitiam dominae; sapiens, cum diligenti investigatione fratrum animos explorabat, qualiter sentirent 173Ade fratre suo uterino; justus erat in adhibitione disciplinae delinquentibus; pius, in conversione poenitentium.

VERS. 32, 33.-- « Quibus appositis, » etc. Seorsum appositi sunt cibi Joseph quasi advenae, et scorsum fratribus, quasi exteris natione: Aegyptiis quoque seorsum, quasi indigenis, qui putabant profanum esse cum peregrinis convivium.

VERS. 34.-- « Biberuntque et inebriati sunt, » etc. (AUG.) Solent ebriosi hoc testimonium adhibere sibi in patrocinium propter Joseph, qui valde sapiens commendatur. Sed hoc verbum pro saturitate ponitur in Scripturis, unde psalmo LXIV: « Visitasti terram et inebriasti eam, » quia in laude benedictionis hoc positum est, et donum Dei commemoratur; apparet 173Bhanc ebrietatem saturitatem significare: nam ita inebriari ut inebriantur ebriosi, nec ipsi terrae utile est; quoniam majore humore quam sufficit, corrumpitur; sicut vita ebriosi, qui non satietate se replet, sed mergit diluvio.

CHAPTER XLIII.

VERS. 11-22.-- « A little rosin and honey and storax, » etc. (HIER. Quaest. Hebr., tom. III.) Somewhat of rosin, and of honey, etc., as far as because in him are laid up divers spices.

VERS. 23-28.-- « Your God and the God of your father hath given you treasures, » etc. (AUG.) It seems a lie, but it must be believed to signify something. For the silver, which is given and is not diminished, which also has been called tried, is perhaps that of which it is said in psalm XI: « The words of the Lord are chaste words, silver tried by the fire, proved of the earth, purified 172Dseven times, » that is, tried perfectly.

VERS. 29-31.-- « And Joseph lifting up his eyes saw Benjamin, » etc. (ISID.) In Acts IX a light as it were shone round about Paul, who is the little one, because he did not yet bear the ripe age of faith. Whence he is also read of as a young man, while he kept the garments of those who stoned Stephen. Joseph wept; the blindness of Paul is the weeping of Christ; Christ washed his face, when Paul is baptized, through whom Christ is to be seen by very many.

(AUG.) The affection of a good mind is always prone to piety. Whence in Joseph we may consider an abundance of virtues. He was chaste, when he spurned the shamelessness of his mistress; wise, when with diligent search he explored the minds of his brethren, how they felt 173Atoward their brother by the same mother; he was just in applying discipline to offenders; loving, in the turning back of the penitent.

VERS. 32, 33.-- « And when these were set on, » etc. The dishes were set apart for Joseph as for a stranger, and apart for his brethren, as for men foreign by nation: and for the Egyptians also apart, as for natives, who thought a banquet with foreigners to be profane.

VERS. 34.-- « And they drank and were made drunk, » etc. (AUG.) Drunkards are wont to bring forward this testimony in their own defence, because of Joseph, who is commended as very wise. But this word is put in the Scriptures for fulness, whence in psalm LXIV: « Thou hast visited the earth and hast made it drunk, » because this is set down in praise of blessing, and the gift of God is recorded; it appears 173Bthat this drunkenness signifies fulness: for to be made drunk as drunkards are made drunk is not useful even to the earth itself; since by more moisture than suffices it is corrupted; like the life of a drunkard, who fills himself not with satiety, but drowns himself in a flood.

CAP. XLIV.

VERS. 5-14.-- « Scyphus quem furati estis, » etc. (ISID.)
