Quid sibi vult quod inventus est scyphus Joseph in sacculo Benjamin, nisi quia in corde Pauli coelestis doctrinae jam praefulgebat eloquium, dum esset eruditus in lege? Sed quia subjectus non erat Deo, intra saccum erat scyphus, doctrina intra legem, lucerna intra modium. Missus tamen Ananias manum imposuit, marsupium solvit, et argentum 173Cresplenduit, et decidentibus squamis, quasi vinculis, soluto sacco, id est deposito legis velamine, adeptus est gratiae libertatem, et revelata facie sermonis Evangelium praedicat.

VERS. 15.-- « An ignoratis quod non sit similis mei in augurandi scientia? » (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Quid hoc sibi velit quaeri solet. An quia non serio sedjoco dictum est, etc., usque ad cognito fratre, quem a se perditum existimabant.

VERS. 16-34.-- « Cui Judas, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Multa in narratione Judae aliter dicta sunt quam cum illis egerat Joseph, etc., usque ad sed qui noverat eum scire, ut eum flecteret ad misericordiam narrationi inserebat.

CHAP. XLIV.

VERS. 5-14.-- « The cup which you have stolen, » etc. (ISID.) What does it mean that Joseph's cup was found in Benjamin's sack, but that in Paul's heart the eloquence of heavenly doctrine already shone forth, while he was learned in the law? But because he was not subject to God, the cup was within the sack, doctrine within the law, the lamp within the bushel. Yet Ananias, being sent, laid his hand upon him, loosed the purse, and the silver 173Cshone out, and, the scales falling away as it were fetters, the sack being loosed, that is, the veil of the law laid aside, he obtained the liberty of grace, and with unveiled face of speech preaches the Gospel.

VERS. 15.-- « Know you not that there is none like me in the science of divining? » (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) What this may mean is wont to be asked. Whether because it was said not in earnest but in jest, etc., as far as the brother being recognized, whom they thought lost to them.

VERS. 16-34.-- « To whom Judas, » etc. (AUG., ubi supra. ) Many things in the narration of Judas are said otherwise than as Joseph had dealt with them, etc., as far as but he who knew that he knew it was inserting into the narration, that he might bend him to mercy.

CAPUT XLV.

  • 173D

VERS. 7-9.-- « Misit me Deus, » etc. (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Quid est quod juxta alia exemplaria ita legitur: « Misit enim me Deus ante vos, remanere reliquias vestras super terram, et enutrire semen reliquiarum vestrarum magnum? » Hoc enim non usquequaque consonat, etc., usque ad « et sic omnis Israel salvus fieret. »

VERS. 10-15.-- « Habitabis in terra Gessen, » etc. (HIERON.) In Hebraeo habetur Gosen ( ). Unde error increvit quod terra Gessen in Arabia sit. Porro, sicut in nostris codicibus est, per extremum m scribitur Gessem, quod mihi nequaquam placet terram significare complutam. Gessem enim in imbrem 174Avertitur, ubi et in plerisque Gessem reperitur.

VERS. 16-21.-- « Et gavisus est Pharao, » etc. Haec verba Pharaonis quae ad Joseph de Jacob et filiis ejus locutus est, significant gaudia gentilis populi de conversione Judaeorum. Magna enim devotio est Ecclesiae gentium quod Israeliticus populus ad fidem veniat, quibus, si convertantur et fidei participes exstiterint, promittit spirituales opes virtutum, et gratiam Spiritus sancti, quam qui accipit, indigentia boni non laborabit.

VERS. 22-28.-- « Benjamin vero dedit, » etc. Trecenti argentei dantur Benjamin, quos a Christo accepit qui praedicat Trinitatem, vel Christi crucem. Unde Paulus ait I ad Corinth. II: « Non judicavi me 174Baliquid scire inter vos, nisi Christum Jesum, et hunc crucifixum. » Quinque stolas accepit, id est sapientiam omnium sensuum. Praecellit ergo Paulus abundantia meritorum: sed et fratres, id est alii praedicatores, habent gratiam suam, id est binas stolas, ut confiteantur Christum Deum et hominem. Unde Prov. XXXI: « Omnes domestici ejus vestiti duplicibus, » id est mystica et morali intelligentia. Mittuntur et patri munera, et Christus promissis muneribus invitat populum suum. Munera portant asini, id est gentiles, inutiles et laboriosi: nunc autem portant utiles in typo Christi munera, in Evangelio munerum largitore.

(ISID.) Expavit Jacob, id est plebs incredula: sed postquam gesta Christi cognovit, revixit spiritus 174Cejus, et qui mortuus videbatur, fide resurrectionis Christi vivificatur. Vocatur ergo a filiis suis, id est, a Petro, Paulo et Joanne, populus Judaeorum ad gratiam. Occurrit illi Judas, qui interpretatur confessio, quia praecedit confessio quos ante perfidia possidebat: et sic Joseph, id est Christus, occurrit, qui senem jam aetate suscipiat ultimo tempore populum Judaeorum, non secundum merita, sed secundum electionem gratiae, et imponat manum super oculos ejus, et caecitatem auferat; quod distulit, ut postremus crederet, qui ante non putavit esse credendum. Unde ad Romanos undecimo capite: « Quia caecitas ex parte in Israel facta est, donec plenitudo gentium intraret, et sic omnis Israel salvus fieret. »

CHAPTER XLV.

  • 173D

VERS. 7-9.-- « God sent me, » etc. (AUG. Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) What is it that according to other copies is read thus: « For God sent me before you, that your remnant should remain upon the earth, and that he might nourish up a great seed of your remnant? » For this does not agree in every point, etc., as far as « and so all Israel should be saved. »

VERS. 10-15.-- « Thou shalt dwell in the land of Gessen, » etc. (HIERON.) In the Hebrew there stands Gosen ( )[Migne prints a Hebrew word within these parentheses; our digitized source has lost it and left them empty.]. Whence the error grew up that the land of Gessen is in Arabia. Further, as it stands in our codices, it is written with a final m, Gessem, which by no means pleases me, as signifying a land rained upon. For Gessem is turned into rain 174A, where also in very many copies Gessem is found.

VERS. 16-21.-- « And Pharao was glad, » etc. These words of Pharao, which he spoke to Joseph concerning Jacob and his sons, signify the joys of the Gentile people over the conversion of the Jews. For great is the devotion of the Church of the Gentiles that the people of Israel should come to the faith, to whom, if they be converted and become partakers of the faith, he promises the spiritual riches of the virtues, and the grace of the Holy Spirit, which he that receives shall not labour under want of good.

VERS. 22-28.-- « But to Benjamin he gave, » etc. Three hundred pieces of silver are given to Benjamin, which he received from Christ who preaches the Trinity, or the cross of Christ. Whence Paul says in the first to the Corinthians, II: « I judged not myself 174Bto know any thing among you, but Christ Jesus, and him crucified. » He received five robes, that is, the wisdom of all the senses. Paul therefore excels in the abundance of his merits: but his brethren also, that is, the other preachers, have their own grace, that is, two robes apiece, that they may confess Christ to be God and man. Whence Prov. XXXI: « All her household are clothed with double garments, » that is, with mystical and moral understanding. Gifts too are sent to the father, and Christ invites his people with gifts promised. Asses bear the gifts, that is, the Gentiles, useless and toilsome: but now, useful, they bear gifts in the type of Christ, in the Gospel the bestower of gifts.

(ISID.) Jacob was afraid, that is, the unbelieving people: but after he came to know the deeds of Christ, his spirit 174Crevived, and he who seemed dead is quickened by faith in the resurrection of Christ. The people of the Jews therefore is called by his sons, that is, by Peter, Paul and John, unto grace. Judas meets him, who is interpreted confession, because confession goes before those whom faithlessness possessed before: and so Joseph, that is, Christ, meets him, to take up at the last time the people of the Jews, now aged in years, not according to merits, but according to the election of grace, and to lay his hand upon his eyes, and take away his blindness; which he deferred, that he should believe last who before did not think that it was to be believed. Whence to the Romans in the eleventh chapter: « Because blindness in part is made in Israel, until the fulness of the Gentiles should come in, and so all Israel should be saved. »[Vulg. Rom 11:25 quia caecitas ex parte contigit in Israel, "because blindness in part hath befallen Israel."]

CAPUT XLVI.

  • 174D

VERS. 26.-- « Cunctae animae quae ingressae sunt cum Jacob in Aegyptum, » etc. (AUG.) Videndum est quid respondeatur eis qui hoc testimonio confirmant a parentibus animas cum corporibus propagari: animas enim dictas pro hominibus, a parte totum figurata locutione, nullus ambigit. Sed quomodo ipsam partem, id est animam cujus nomine totus homo significatus est, alienamus ab eo quod dictum est, « exierunt de femoribus ejus, » ut carnes ex illo tantum natas (quamvis solae animae nominentur) accipiamus, quaerendi sunt modi locutionum in Scripturis.

VERS. 27.-- « Filii autem Joseph, » etc. (HIERON. 175AQuaest. Hebr., tom. III.) Omnes animae domus Jacob, etc., usque ad licet plerique tradant Lucam, ut proselytum, Hebraeas litteras ignorasse.

VERS. 28-31.-- « Misit autem Judam, » etc. (HIER., ibid. ) Judam vero misit ante se ad Joseph, etc., usque ad pagum Arsenoyten sic olim vocatum autumant.

VERS. 32-34.-- « Et sunt viri pastores, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Commendatur in patriarchis, quod pecorum nutritores erant a pueritia sua, etc., usque ad in fine habituri ordinatissimam et sempiternam felicitatem.

CHAPTER XLVI.

  • 174D

VERS. 26.-- « All the souls that went in with Jacob into Egypt, » etc. (AUG.) It must be considered what answer is to be made to those who by this testimony maintain that souls are propagated from parents together with bodies: for that "souls" is said for "men," by a figurative manner of speech naming the whole from a part, no one doubts. But how we are to alienate that very part, that is, the soul by whose name the whole man is signified, from that which is said, « they came forth out of his thighs, »[Vulg. Gn 46:26 egressae sunt de femore illius, "went out of his thigh."] so as to take it that flesh only was born of him (although souls alone are named), the modes of speech in the Scriptures must be sought out.

VERS. 27.-- « And the sons of Joseph, » etc. (HIERON. 175AQuaest. Hebr., tom. III.) All the souls of the house of Jacob, etc., as far as although very many hand down that Luke, as a proselyte, was ignorant of Hebrew letters.

VERS. 28-31.-- « And he sent Judas, » etc. (HIER., ibid. ) But Judas he sent before him to Joseph, etc., as far as they suppose that the Arsenoite district was so called of old.

VERS. 32-34.-- « And they are men that are shepherds, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) It is commended in the patriarchs that they were feeders of cattle from their childhood, etc., as far as at the end they shall have a most ordered and everlasting happiness.

CAPUT XLVII.

VERS. 9, 10.-- « Dies peregrinationis, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) Quid est quod dicit Jacob 175BPharaoni, etc., usque ad non manent in domo in aeternum.

VERS. 11.-- « Joseph vero patri et fratribus suis . . . Ramesse. » Quaerendum est utrum terra Ramesses ipsa sit Gessen: hanc enim petierant, et Pharao praeceperat dari.

(ISID.) Allegorice. Tradidit Joseph patri et fratribus optimam terram Gessen, praebens eis cibaria, quia fames terram oppresserat: sic et Dominus parentibus, id est patriarchis et prophetis, ex quibus est secundum carnem, sive omnibus sanctis, de quibus in Evangelio dicit Matth. II: « Hi sunt fratres mei, qui faciunt voluntatem Patris mei, » his dat terram optimam, de qua dicitur psal. XXVI: « Credo videre bona Domini in terra viventium. »

175CVERS. 12-15.-- « Praebens cibaria, singulis, » etc. Metiebatur triticum patri suo Joseph, nec tamen eum pater quando vidit, nec quando triticum accepit, adoravit: quomodo ergo somnium impletum putamus, nisi majoris rei contineat prophetiam.

VERS. 16-19.-- « Adducite pecora vestra, et dabo vobis, » etc. Quaeritur, eum Joseph frumenta colligeret, unde homines viverent, pecora unde vivebant, cum tanta fames invaluisset, et fratres Joseph Pharaoni dixerunt: « Non sunt pascua pecoribus puerorum tuorum, » etc. Si ergo ea fame pascua defecerant in terra Chanaan, cur in Aegypto non defecerant, cum eadem fames esset ubique? An, sicut perhibetur ab eis qui loca sciunt, in multis Aegypti paludibus poterant pascua non deesse, etiam 175Dcum fames esset frumentorum, quae solent Nili inundatione provenire: dicuntur illae paludes feracius pascua gignere, quando aqua Nili minus excrescit.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « Emit igitur, » etc. Non injustitiae vel iniquitatis arguitur Joseph, cum etiam fidelitas ejus inde commendetur, quia nullius personae gratiam suscepit, sed juxta emptoris pretium aequo libramine repensavit.

Mystice autem significat omnes qui in spiritali Aegypto sunt et ejus opes ambiunt, servos esse. Nullus est enim liber Aegyptius, qui carnalis est, et venundatus sub peccato: Pharao enim eos subjecit; unde fortasse dicitur Exod. XX: « Ego sum Dominus Deus 176Atuus, qui eduxi te de Aegypto, de domo servitutis. » Unde Rom. VI: « Non regnet peccatum in vestro mortali corpore. » etc. Et ibidem: « Servi estis ejus cui obedistis, sive peccati in mortem, sive obeditionis ad justitiam. » Aegyptii autem violenter in servitutem redacti sunt, Hebraei vero violenter in servitium. Vendiderunt autem Aegyptii semetipsos, nec dispensatoris culpa est, ubi digna repensantur pro meritis. Hoc et Paulus fecit, cum illum qui indignus numero sanctorum vel consortio fuit, Satanae tradit (I Cor. V). Nemo ergo dicat Paulum durius egisse, qui hominem de Ecclesia ejecit, ut expulsus disceret non blasphemare: hominis enim voluntas est iniqua, cum amans terrena despicit coelestia, et propter avaritiam se subjicit diabolo. Dei autem 176Bpermissio est justa, cum eum qui spernens meliora, elegit pejora, dimittit arbitrio suo ut cadat. « Emit igitur, » etc. Allegorice emptio Joseph, qui frugibus emit terram Aegypti, significat Christum doctrina et sanguinis pretio redimere mundum. Quod autem dicit: « Accipite semina, et serite agros ut fruges habere possitis, » etc., semen est verbum Dei, agri corpora quae vomere evangelico exarata, et semine spirituali seminata, fructum virtutum germinant. Quinta pars, quae regi dabatur, quinque sensuum census, qui Christo regi solvendus. Quatuor reliquae in sementem et cibos possessoribus permittuntur, quia actualis vitae usus unicuique conceditur, ut seminet virtutum opera, et fructus justitiae metat in vitam aeternam, qui a Christo nobis tribuitur. Unde 176CJoseph dicitur: « Salus nostra in manu tua est, » quia salus mundi in potestate Christi.

VERS. 22-28.-- « Praeter terram sacerdotum, » etc. Hoc significat, Ecclesiae terram, in qua veri sunt sacerdotes, liberam esse a censu mundano, quae verbo divino indesinenter pascitur, nec damnum panis spiritualis sustinebit.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « Cumque appropinquare cerneret diem mortis suae, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) Ea filium juratione constringit, etc., usque ad qui in baptismo apparuit fere trigesimo anno.

VERS. 31.-- « Quo jurante. » (AUG., ubi supra. ) Quod habent Latini codices, « Adoravit super caput virgae ejus, » nonnulli emendatius habent: « Adoravit 176Dsuper caput virgae suae, » vel « in capite virgae suae, » vel « in cacumine, vel super cacumen virgae suae. » Et ideo merito quaeritur quid sit quod dictum est, etc., usque ad ubi figura rei futurae praesignabatur.

(HIERON.) « Et adoravit Israel contra summitatem virgae ejus. » Hoc etiam in loco quidam frustra simulant adorasse Jacob summitatem sceptri Joseph: quod scilicet honorans filium potestatem illius adoraverit, cum in Hebraeo multo aliter legatur: « Et adoravit Israel ad caput lectuli; » quia postquam juravit ei filius, securus de petitione, adoravit Deum conversus ad caput lectuli. Sanctus enim vir et Deo deditus senectute oppressus, sic habebat 177Alectulum positum, ut ipse jacentis habitus absque ulla difficultate ad orationem esset paratus.

CHAPTER XLVII.

VERS. 9, 10.-- « The days of my pilgrimage, » etc. (AUG. Q. in Gen., tom. III.) What is it that Jacob says 175Bto Pharao, etc., as far as they do not abide in the house for ever.

VERS. 11.-- « But Joseph to his father and his brethren . . . Ramesse. » It must be asked whether the land of Ramesses is itself Gessen: for this they had sought, and Pharao had commanded that it be given.

(ISID.) Allegorically. Joseph handed over to his father and his brethren the best land, Gessen, providing them with victuals, because famine had oppressed the land: so also the Lord, to his kindred, that is, to the patriarchs and prophets, of whom he is according to the flesh, or to all the saints, of whom he says in the Gospel, Matth. II: « These are my brethren, who do the will of my Father, »[Vulg. Mt 12:50 Quicumque enim fecerit voluntatem Patris mei, qui in caelis est, ipse meus frater, et soror, et mater est, "Whosoever shall do the will of my Father, who is in heaven, he is my brother, and sister, and mother."] to these he gives the best land, of which it is said, psal. XXVI: « I believe to see the good things of the Lord in the land of the living. »

175CVERS. 12-15.-- « Providing victuals, to every one, » etc. Joseph measured out wheat to his own father, and yet his father did not adore him, neither when he saw him nor when he received the wheat: how then do we think the dream fulfilled, unless it contain the prophecy of a greater thing.

VERS. 16-19.-- « Bring your cattle, and I will give you, » etc. It is asked, since Joseph was gathering the corn whereby men might live, whence the cattle were living, when so great a famine had grown strong, and the brethren of Joseph said to Pharao: « There is no pasture for the cattle of thy servants, »[Vulg. Gn 47:4 non est herba gregibus servorum tuorum, "there is no grass for the flocks of thy servants."] etc. If therefore in that famine the pastures had failed in the land of Chanaan, why had they not failed in Egypt, since the same famine was everywhere? Or could it be, as is reported by those who know the places, that in the many marshes of Egypt pastures might well not be wanting, even 175Dwhen there was a famine of the corn which is wont to come forth by the inundation of the Nile: those marshes are said to bring forth pasture more fruitfully when the water of the Nile rises less.

VERS. 20, 21.-- « He bought therefore, » etc. Joseph is not accused of injustice or of iniquity, since his fidelity is even commended from this, that he accepted the favour of no person, but weighed out with an even balance according to the buyer's price.

But mystically it signifies that all who are in the spiritual Egypt and court her riches are slaves. For no Egyptian is free, who is carnal, and sold under sin: for Pharao made them subject; whence perhaps it is said, Exod. XX: « I am the Lord thy God, 176Awho brought thee out of Egypt, out of the house of bondage. »[Vulg. Ex 20:2 qui eduxi te de terra Aegypti, "who brought thee out of the land of Egypt."] Whence Rom. VI: « Let not sin reign in your mortal body. » etc. And in the same place: « You are the servants of him whom you have obeyed, whether of sin unto death, or of obedience unto justice. »[Vulg. Rom 6:16 servi estis ejus, cui obeditis, sive peccati ad mortem, "you are the servants of him whom you obey, whether of sin unto death."] But the Egyptians were brought by violence into slavery, the Hebrews indeed by violence into service. The Egyptians, however, sold their own selves, and it is no fault of the steward where what is deserved is repaid according to merits. This Paul also did, when he delivers to Satan that man who was unworthy of the number or the fellowship of the saints (I Cor. V). Let no one therefore say that Paul acted too harshly, who cast the man out of the Church, that being expelled he might learn not to blaspheme: for the will of man is iniquitous, when, loving earthly things, it despises the heavenly, and for covetousness subjects itself to the devil. But God's 176Bpermission is just, when he leaves to his own choice, that he may fall, the man who, despising better things, has chosen worse. « He bought therefore, » etc. Allegorically the purchase of Joseph, who bought the land of Egypt with corn, signifies that Christ redeems the world by doctrine and by the price of his blood. And as to what he says: « Take seed, and sow the fields that you may have corn, » etc., the seed is the word of God, the fields are the bodies which, ploughed up by the evangelical share and sown with spiritual seed, bring forth the fruit of the virtues. The fifth part, which was given to the king, is the tribute of the five senses, which is to be paid to Christ the king. The four remaining are left to the possessors for seed and for food, because the use of the active life is granted to every one, that he may sow the works of the virtues and reap the fruit of justice unto life eternal, which is bestowed on us by Christ. Whence 176Cit is said to Joseph: « Our salvation is in thy hand, » because the salvation of the world is in the power of Christ.

VERS. 22-28.-- « Except the land of the priests, » etc. This signifies that the land of the Church, in which are the true priests, is free from worldly tribute, which is fed unceasingly by the divine word, and shall suffer no loss of the spiritual bread.

VERS. 29, 30.-- « And when he saw that the day of his death drew near, » etc. (AUG., Quaest. in Gen., tom. III.) He binds his son by that oath, etc., as far as who appeared in baptism about the thirtieth year.

VERS. 31.-- « He swearing. » (AUG., ubi supra. ) That which the Latin codices have, « He adored upon the head of his rod, » some have more correctly: « He adored 176Dupon the head of his own rod, » or « on the head of his own rod, » or « on the top, or upon the top of his own rod. » And therefore it is rightly asked what it is that has been said, etc., as far as where the figure of a thing to come was prefigured.

(HIERON.) « And Israel adored toward the top of his rod. » In this place also certain men vainly pretend that Jacob adored the top of Joseph's sceptre: namely, that honouring his son he adored his power, whereas in the Hebrew it is read far otherwise: « And Israel adored at the head of the bed; » because after his son had sworn to him, being secure of his request, he adored God, turned toward the head of the bed. For a holy man, and one given over to God, weighed down by old age, kept his little bed 177Aso placed that the very posture of one lying down was, without any difficulty, ready for prayer.

CAPUT XLVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « His ita transactis . . . . assumptis duobus filiis Manasse et Ephraim. » (HIERON.) Et tulit duos filios secum, Manassen et Ephraim. Ex hoc apparet quod supra diximus, quia Joseph ad id locorum duos tantum filios habuit, Ephraim et Manassen. Si autem post multos annos moriente Jacob duos tantum duxit ad benedictionem, illo utique tempore cum filii ejus generare nequiverant, parvuli atque lactentes, in ingressu patris et fratrum, nepotes ex his habere non potuit.

VERS. 2-4.-- « Qui confortatus, » etc. (HIERON.) 177BEt confortatus Israel sedit super lectulum. Cur LXX idem verbum aliter atque aliter ediderint nescio, sed audacter dico quod idem verbum mittha , quod hic lectulum transtulerunt, supra ubi dixit adorasse Jacob, virgam nominaverunt.

VERS. 5, 6.-- « Duo igitur filii qui nati, » etc. (HIERON., Quaest in Gen.) Patet quod septuaginta animae ingressae sunt in Aegyptum, etc., usque ad sed in tribus Ephraim et Manasse quasi appendices miscebuntur.

VERS. 7-11.-- « Mihi enim quando veniebam de Mesopotamia, mortua est Rachel et sepelivi eam, » etc. Joseph quasi nescienti indicat ubi et quando matrem sepelierit, forte prophetice commemorans ibi sepultam esse matrem ejus, ubi erat Christus nasciturus.

177CVERS. 12-15.-- « Cumque tulisset eos Joseph de gremio patris, » etc. (Isid. in Gen., tom. V.) Sicut factum est in Esau et Jacob, dicente Domino: « Major serviet minori, » ita et in filiis Joseph, etc., usque ad et juniorem de sinistro dextrum.

VERS. 16-21.-- « Invocetur super eos nomen meum. » (AUG.) Notandum ex hoc loco non solum exauditionem, sed invocationem etiam dici, quae non Dei, sed hominum sunt.

VERS. 22.-- « Do tibi partem unam, » etc. (HIERON. in Gen., tom. III.) « Ego dedi tibi Sichimam praecipuam super fratres tuos, quam accepi de manibus Amorrhaeorum in gladio meo et sagitta. » Sichima juxta Graecam et Latinam consuetudinem declinata 177Dest, etc., usque ad et Mausoleum ejus ibi usque hodie cernitur.

(AUG.) Quod Sichimam dicit Jacob praecipuam se dare filio suo Joseph, et addidit quod possederit eam in gladio et arcu, quaeritur quomodo ad litteram conveniat Gen. XXXIV. Emit enim centum agnis possessionem illam, non cepit jure belli. An quia Salem civitatem Sichimorum filii ejus expugnaverant, et jure belli potuit ejus fieri, ut justum bellum cum eis videatur, qui priores fecerunt injuriam, contaminando ejus filiam? Cur ergo non dedit eam majoribus filiis suis, qui hoc fecerunt? Deinde si modo de victoria glorians dat eam Joseph, cur ei tunc displicuerunt filii qui hoc commiserunt? Cur denique cum eos benediceret, id exprobrando memoravit? 178ALatet ergo hic propheticum sacramentum, quia Joseph quadam praecipua significatione Christum significavit: et ei datur illa terra ubi obruit Jacob deos alienos (Gen. XXXV), ut Christus intelligatur possessurus gentes diis patrum suorum renuntiantes et in Christum credentes.

CHAPTER XLVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « These things being thus done . . . . taking his two sons Manasses and Ephraim. » (HIERON.) And he took his two sons with him, Manasses and Ephraim. From this appears what we said above, that Joseph up to that time had only two sons, Ephraim and Manasses. But if, after many years, when Jacob was dying, he brought only two to the blessing, then assuredly at that time, when his sons could not yet beget, being little ones and at the breast, at the coming in of his father and his brethren, he could not have grandsons of them.

VERS. 2-4.-- « Who being strengthened, » etc. (HIERON.) 177BAnd Israel being strengthened sat upon the bed. Why the Seventy have given out the same word now one way and now another I do not know, but I say boldly that the same word mittha, which here they translated a bed, above, where he said that Jacob adored, they named a rod.

VERS. 5, 6.-- « Two sons therefore who were born, » etc. (HIERON., Quaest in Gen.) It is plain that seventy souls went into Egypt, etc., as far as but they shall be mingled into the tribes of Ephraim and Manasses as appendages.

VERS. 7-11.-- « For when I came from Mesopotamia, Rachel died and I buried her, » etc. He points out to Joseph, as to one who did not know, where and when he had buried his mother, perhaps prophetically recalling that his mother was buried in the place where Christ was to be born.

177CVERS. 12-15.-- « And when Joseph had taken them from his father's lap, » etc. (Isid. in Gen., tom. V.) As was done in Esau and Jacob, the Lord saying: « The elder shall serve the younger, » so also in the sons of Joseph, etc., as far as and the younger from the left hand to the right.

VERS. 16-21.-- « Let my name be called upon them. » (AUG.) It is to be noted from this place that not only a being heard, but an invoking also, is spoken of, which are not God's but men's.

VERS. 22.-- « I give thee one portion, » etc. (HIERON. in Gen., tom. III.) « I have given thee Sichem as a chief portion above thy brethren, which I received out of the hands of the Amorrhites with my sword and my arrow. »[Vulg. Gn 48:22 reads Do tibi partem unam extra fratres tuos, quam tuli de manu Amorrhaei in gladio et arcu meo — "I give thee one portion beyond thy brethren, which I took out of the hand of the Amorrhite with my sword and bow."] Sichima is declined 177Daccording to Greek and Latin usage, etc., as far as and his tomb is beheld there to this day.

(AUG.) As to Jacob's saying that he gives Sichem as a chief portion to his son Joseph, and his adding that he had possessed it with sword and bow, it is asked how this may agree to the letter with Gen. XXXIV. For he bought that possession with a hundred lambs, he did not take it by right of war. Or was it because his sons had stormed Salem, the city of the Sichemites, and it could become his by right of war, so that the war might seem just against those who first did the injury by defiling his daughter? Why then did he not give it to his elder sons, who did this? Then, if he now gives it to Joseph as glorying of the victory, why did the sons who committed it displease him at that time? Why, lastly, when he was blessing them, did he mention it by way of reproach? 178AThere lies hidden here therefore a prophetic sacrament, because Joseph by a certain special signification signified Christ: and to him is given that land where Jacob buried the strange gods (Gen. XXXV), that Christ may be understood as about to possess the Gentiles who renounce the gods of their fathers and believe in Christ.

CAPUT XLIX.

(ALBIN.) In benedictionibus Jacob historia tenenda, et allegoria investiganda: historia de divisione terrae promissionis, allegoria de Christo et Ecclesia, quae in novissimis temporibus revelanda; prius tamen historiae fundamenta sunt jacienda.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « Ruben primogenitus meus, » etc. (HIERON., Quaest. in Gen.) « Ruben primogenitus 178Bmeus, tu fortitudo mea, » et principium filiorum meorum, etc., usque ad primogeniti ordinem perdidisti.

VERS. 5.-- « Simeon et Levi, » etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) LXX: « Simeon et Levi fratres consummaverunt iniquitatem adinventionis suae, » etc., usque ad « in consilium eorum non veniat anima mea. »

VERS. 8.-- « Juda, te laudabunt, » etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Quia Juda confessio vel laus interpretatur, etc., usque ad juxta eumdem sensum aliis verbis Ecclesia monstratur, de qua dicitur Matth. V: « Non potest civitas abscondi super montem posita. »

VERS. 9.-- « Catulus leonis Juda: ad praedam, 178Cfili mi, ascendisti: requiescens accubuisti ut leo, » etc. (ISID.) Vel catulus leonis in somno requievit, quia non necessitate, sed potestate. Unde: « Potestatem habeo ponendi animam meam, et nemo tollit eam a me, sed ego pono eam (Joan. X). » Quod vero addit, « et ut catulus leonis, » inde enim mortuus unde natus. Aiunt physici quod catulus leonis, cum fuerit natus, tribus diebus dormit et tribus noctibus. Deinde patris fremitu tremefactus cubilis locus excitat dormientem: Christus quoque in cubili sepulcri tribus noctibus jacens, somnium mortis implevit. Bene ergo Christus ut leo requievit, qui non solum mortis acerbitatem, sed et imperium vicit, et ut catulus leonis, quia die tertia resurrexit. Unde additur.

178D« Quis suscitabit eum? » etc. Nullus, subaudi, nisi ipse qui ait Joan. II: « Solvite templum hoc et in triduo resuscitabo illud. »

VERS. 10.-- « Non auferetur sceptrum de Juda, » etc. Tandiu enim ex semine Judae reges apud Judaeos fuerunt, donec Christus ex Virgine nasceretur. Primus enim in gente Judaeorum rex alienigena fuit. Herodes, quando jam Christus natus est. Qui ergo negat venisse Christum, ostendat regem de tribu Juda in gente Judaeorum.

VERS. 11.-- « Ligans ad vineam pullum suum, » etc. Gentes quas sibi subjunxit. Unde Ezech. XVIII: « Vivo ego, dicit Dominus, nisi hos homines induam sicut vestimentum. » Nos enim Christus in sanguine 179Auvae mundavit quando sicut botrus in ligno crucis pependit, et ex latere ejus aqua in ablutionem, et sanguis exivit in redemptionem.

VERS. 12.-- « Pulchriores oculi, » etc. (ISID.) Quia doctrina eorum austeritatem veteris legis exsuperat, et evangelica praecepta clariora sunt quam legis mandata.

« Lacte, » id est doctrina legis, quae carnalem populum vel parvulum lacte alebat: sed « candidiores, » Ecclesiae doctores, qui fortem et validum verbi cibum manducant, et distribuunt. Unde Hebr. V: « Perfectorum est solidus cibus, » qui bene « candidiores lacte » dicuntur, quia candidi, puri et ab omni macula liberi.

VERS. 13.-- « Zabulon in littore, » etc. Ut credentibus 179Brefugium et periclitantibus demonstret fidei portum. Contra omnes terminos saeculi solidata exspectat naufragium Judaeorum et procellas haereticorum: qui circumferuntur omni vento doctrinae, inde etiam apostoli aliqui assumpti: ibi saepe Dominus docuit. Pertingit autem usque ad Sidonem, id est, usque ad gentes pervenit. Unde Isa. IX: « Terra Zabulon, et terra Nephthali, via maris trans Jordanem, » etc. Sidon, venatio: et ex eis locis sumpti sunt venatores, de quibus dicitur Jer. XVI: « Mittam venatores multos, et venabuntur vos in omni monte. »

VERS. 14, 15.-- « Issachar. » (HIERON. Quaest. Hebr. ) Bonum desideravit requiescens inter medios clericos, etc., usque ad et ideo ei omnes tribus serviant, 179Cquasi magistro dona portantes.

« Accubans inter terminos, » etc. Accubare, est exspectato mundi fine quiescere, nihilque de mundanis quaerere, sed ultima desiderare. Fortis asinus requiem vidit, et terram optimam: cum simplex gentilitas ad robur operis se erexit, quia ad vitae patriam tendit.

VERS. 16-18.-- « Dan judicabit populum suum, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Alii per haec verba putant praedici Antichristum de tribu Dan futurum, etc., usque ad et post beneficia fictae dulcedinis exercet cornua potestatis.

« Dan judicabit populum suum sicut unum de sceptris Israel. Fiat Dan coluber in via, regulus in 179Dsemita, mordens ungulas equi, et cadat ascensor ejus retrorsum. Salvatorem tuum exspectabo, Domine. » (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Samson judex Israel de tribu Dan fuit, etc., usque ad et ipse erit exspectatio gentium.

VERS. 19, 20.-- « Gad. » (HIERON.) Latrunculus latrocinabitur ante eum, etc., usque ad et victis hostibus fortiter dimicavit.

« Gad accinctus praeliabitur ante eum. » (ISID.) Christus ante et retro praeliabitur contra Antichristum: ante, occulto adventu humilitatis; post, manifestata gloria majestatis. Unde Moyses Deut. XXXIII: « Benedictus in latitudine Gad, quasi leo requievit: cepitque brachium et verticem, et vidit principatum suum. » Christus enim in sepulcro quievit, verticem 180Aet superbiam excelsorum humiliavit, et ei datus est principatus, et honor, et regnum.

VERS. 21.-- « Nephthali. » (HIERON.) Allegorice. Virgultum dissolutum, dans nationi pulchritudinem, etc., usque ad quia ibi vel maxime docuit, ut in Evangelio quoque scriptum est.

« Nephthali cervus emissus, » etc. (ISID.) Apostoli enim et praedicatores, velociter exsilientes, sicut cervi, transcendunt impedimenta saeculi, et excelsa meditantes, dant eloquia pulchritudinis, id est praedicant doctrinam Salvatoris.

VERS. 22-26.-- « Filius accrescens Joseph, » etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) « Filius accrescens super fontem, » etc., usque ad imperans quoque decem tribubus Israel.

180BVERS. 27. « Benjamin lupus rapax, » etc. (HIERON., ibid. ) « Mane comedet praedam et ad vesperam dividet escam. » Quanquam de Paulo manifestissime prophetia sit, quod in adolescentia sua persecutus Ecclesiam, in senectute praedicator Evangelii fuerit, etc., usque ad super sacerdotibus qui, servientes altari, vivant de altari (I Cor. IX).

« Benjamin lupus rapax, » etc. (ISID.) Quidam doctorum hoc ad Hierusalem retulit, etc., usque ad tunc dividet escam, intelligens a spiritu litteram esse dividendam, quia « littera occidit, spiritus autem vivificat (II Cor. III). »

VERS. 28-31.-- « Omnes hi in tribubus, » etc. Quaeritur cur Jacob omnes quos de liberis et ancillis 180Cgenuit, aequali honore filios et haeredes constituerit? quia sicut Christus omnibus qui per fidem corpori ejus conciliantur, pari honore et gloria coelestia praemia largitur. « Non est enim ibi discretio an Judaeus sit an Graecus, Barbarus an Scytha, servus an liber: quia per omnia et in omnibus » Christus, qui formam servi induit, et pro libero et pro servo servivit, ut omnibus aequale donum largiatur. Quicunque enim fidem Domini promeretur, nullis maculis carnalis nativitatis offuscatur. Unde Joel. II: « Effundam de spiritu meo super omnem carnem. »

VERS. 32.-- « Appositusque, » etc. (STRAB.) Quaerendum de quo populo dicatur? Nam de patribus nulla quaestio est. Notum est enim quod ad Abraham et Isaac appositus est, sed populus multitudo est. 180DIlli autem tam pauci non poterant populus dici. Intelligendum ergo hoc etiam de angelis dici, ad quorum societatem, de mundo transeuntes, apponuntur sancti. Unde Hebr. XII: « Accessistis ad montem Sion, et ad Hierusalem coelestem, et ad frequentiam angelorum, » etc.

CHAPTER XLIX.

(ALBIN.) In the blessings of Jacob the history is to be held and the allegory searched out: the history concerning the division of the land of promise, the allegory concerning Christ and the Church, which is to be revealed in the last times; yet first the foundations of the history must be laid.

VERS. 3, 4.-- « Ruben my firstborn, » etc. (HIERON., Quaest. in Gen.) « Ruben my firstborn 178B, thou art my strength, » and the beginning of my sons,[Vulg. Gn 49:3 et principium doloris mei, "and the beginning of my sorrow."] etc., as far as thou hast lost the order of the firstborn.

VERS. 5.-- « Simeon and Levi, » etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) LXX: « Simeon and Levi brethren have accomplished the iniquity of their own devising, » etc., as far as « let not my soul go into their counsel. »

VERS. 8.-- « Juda, they shall praise thee, » etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Because Juda is interpreted confession or praise, etc., as far as in other words the Church is shown according to the same sense, of which it is said, Matth. V: « A city cannot be hid, set upon a mountain. »

VERS. 9.-- « The whelp of a lion is Juda: to the prey, 178Cmy son, thou art gone up: resting thou hast couched as a lion, » etc. (ISID.) Or, the lion's whelp rested in sleep, because not of necessity but of power. Whence: « I have power to lay down my life, and no man taketh it from me, but I lay it down (Joan. X). »[Vulg. Jo 10:18 Nemo tollit eam a me : sed ego pono eam a meipso, et potestatem habeo ponendi eam, "No man taketh it away from me: but I lay it down of myself, and I have power to lay it down."] But as to what he adds, « and as a lion's whelp, »[Vulg. Gn 49:9 et quasi leaena, "and as a lioness."] for thence he died whence he was born. Naturalists say that the whelp of a lion, when it has been born, sleeps three days and three nights. Then the place of its lair, shaken by the roaring of its father, rouses the sleeper: Christ also, lying three nights in the lair of the sepulchre, fulfilled the sleep of death. Well therefore did Christ rest as a lion, who overcame not only the bitterness of death but its dominion also, and as a lion's whelp, because on the third day he rose again. Whence it is added.

178D« Who shall rouse him up? » etc. None, understand, save he himself who says, Joan. II: « Loose this temple, and in three days' space I will raise it again. »

VERS. 10.-- « The sceptre shall not be taken away from Juda, » etc. For so long were there kings of the seed of Juda among the Jews, until Christ should be born of the Virgin. For the first foreign king in the nation of the Jews was Herod, when Christ was already born. He therefore who denies that Christ has come, let him show a king of the tribe of Juda in the nation of the Jews.

VERS. 11.-- « Tying his colt to the vineyard, » etc. The Gentiles whom he joined to himself. Whence Ezech. XVIII: « As I live, saith the Lord, unless I put on these men as a garment. »[Vulg. Is 49:18, whose words these render, reads Vivo ego, dicit Dominus, quia omnibus his velut ornamento vestieris, "as I live, saith the Lord, thou shalt be clothed with all these as with an ornament"; Ez 18, the chapter Migne cites, has si erit ultra vobis parabola haec in proverbium in Israel.] For Christ cleansed us in the blood 179Aof the grape when he hung like a cluster on the wood of the cross, and out of his side there went forth water for washing, and blood for redemption.

VERS. 12.-- « Fairer the eyes, » etc. (ISID.) Because their doctrine surpasses the austerity of the old law, and the evangelical precepts are clearer than the commandments of the law.

« With milk, » that is, the doctrine of the law, which fed the carnal or little people with milk: but « whiter, » the doctors of the Church, who eat and distribute the strong and solid food of the word. Whence Hebr. V: « Strong meat is for the perfect, » who are well called « whiter than milk, » because they are white, pure and free from every stain.

VERS. 13.-- « Zabulon on the shore, » etc. That he may show a refuge 179Bto them that believe and a haven of faith to them that are in peril. Made firm against all the ends of the world, she awaits the shipwreck of the Jews and the storms of the heretics: who are carried about with every wind of doctrine, thence also were some of the apostles taken: there the Lord often taught. And he reaches as far as Sidon, that is, he comes as far as the Gentiles. Whence Isa. IX: « The land of Zabulon, and the land of Nephthali, the way of the sea beyond the Jordan, » etc.[Vulg. Is 9:1 Primo tempore alleviata est terra Zabulon et terra Nephthali : et novissimo aggravata est via maris trans Jordanem Galilaeae gentium, "at the first time the land of Zabulon and the land of Nephthali was lightened: and at the last the way of the sea beyond the Jordan of the Galilee of the Gentiles was heavily loaded"; the wording Migne prints is that of Mt 4:15.] Sidon, hunting: and out of those places were taken the hunters, of whom it is said Jer. XVI: « I will send many hunters, and they shall hunt you in every mountain. »[Vulg. Jr 16:16 mittam eis multos venatores, et venabuntur eos de omni monte, "I will send them many hunters, and they shall hunt them out of every mountain" — eos, "them," not vos, "you."]

VERS. 14, 15.-- « Issachar. » (HIERON. Quaest. Hebr. ) He desired the good, resting in the midst of the clergy, etc., as far as and therefore let all the tribes serve him, 179Cas bearing gifts to a master.

« Couching between the borders, » etc. To couch is to rest with the end of the world awaited, and to seek nothing of worldly things, but to long for the last things. The strong ass saw rest, and the best land: when the simple Gentile world raised itself up to the strength of the work, because it tends toward the country of life.

VERS. 16-18.-- « Dan shall judge his people, » etc. (ISID. in Gen., tom. V.) Others think that by these words Antichrist is foretold as to come of the tribe of Dan, etc., as far as and after the benefits of a feigned sweetness he plies the horns of power.

« Dan shall judge his people as one of the sceptres of Israel. Let Dan be a serpent in the way, a basilisk in 179Dthe path, biting the hoofs of the horse, and let his rider fall backward. I will look for thy Saviour, O Lord. »[Vulg. Gn 49:16-18 sicut et alia tribus in Israel … cerastes in semita … ut cadat ascensor ejus retro. Salutare tuum expectabo, Domine, "as another tribe in Israel … a horned serpent in the path … that his rider may fall backward. I will look for thy salvation, O Lord."] (HIERON., ubi supra. ) Samson, the judge of Israel, was of the tribe of Dan, etc., as far as and he himself shall be the expectation of the nations.

VERS. 19, 20.-- « Gad. » (HIERON.) A robber shall rob before him,[Vulg. Gn 49:19 Gad, accinctus praeliabatur ante eum, "Gad, being girded, fought before him."] etc., as far as and, his enemies overcome, he fought bravely.

« Gad girded shall fight before him. » (ISID.) Christ shall fight before and behind against Antichrist: before, by the hidden coming of his humility; after, by the manifested glory of his majesty. Whence Moses, Deut. XXXIII: « Blessed in breadth is Gad, he hath rested as a lion: and he took the arm and the crown, and he saw his principality. » For Christ rested in the sepulchre, and humbled the crown 180Aand pride of the lofty, and to him was given the principality, and honour, and a kingdom.

VERS. 21.-- « Nephthali. » (HIERON.) Allegorically. A shoot let loose, giving beauty to the nation,[Vulg. Gn 49:21 Nephthali, cervus emissus, et dans eloquia pulchritudinis, "Nephthali, a hart let loose, and giving words of beauty."] etc., as far as because there most of all he taught, as is written in the Gospel too.

« Nephthali a hart let loose, » etc. (ISID.) For the apostles and preachers, leaping forth swiftly like harts, overleap the hindrances of the world, and meditating on lofty things, give words of beauty, that is, they preach the doctrine of the Saviour.

VERS. 22-26.-- « Joseph a growing son, » etc. (HIERON., ubi supra. ) « A son growing above a fountain, »[Vulg. Gn 49:22 filius accrescens et decorus aspectu, "a growing son and comely to behold."] etc., as far as ruling also over the ten tribes of Israel.

180BVERS. 27. « Benjamin a ravening wolf, » etc. (HIERON., ibid. ) « In the morning he shall eat the prey, and at evening he shall divide the food. »[Vulg. Gn 49:27 mane comedat praedam, et vespere dividet spolia, "in the morning let him eat the prey, and in the evening he shall divide the spoil."] Although the prophecy is most manifestly of Paul, in that in his youth he persecuted the Church, and in his old age was a preacher of the Gospel, etc., as far as concerning the priests who, serving the altar, should live by the altar (I Cor. IX).

« Benjamin a ravening wolf, » etc. (ISID.) A certain one of the doctors referred this to Jerusalem, etc., as far as then shall he divide the food, understanding that the letter is to be divided from the spirit, because « the letter killeth, but the spirit quickeneth (II Cor. III). »

VERS. 28-31.-- « All these in the tribes, » etc. It is asked: why did Jacob appoint all whom he begot of free women and of handmaids 180Csons and heirs with equal honour? because, as Christ bestows heavenly rewards with like honour and glory upon all who are joined to his body through faith. « For there is no distinction there whether one be Jew or Greek, Barbarian or Scythian, bond or free: because through all things and in all things » is Christ,[Vulg. Col 3:11 ubi non est gentilis et Judaeus, circumcisio et praeputium, Barbarus et Scytha, servus et liber : sed omnia, et in omnibus Christus, "where there is neither Gentile nor Jew, circumcision nor uncircumcision, Barbarian nor Scythian, bond nor free: but Christ is all, and in all."] who put on the form of a servant, and served both for the free man and for the servant, that he might bestow an equal gift upon all. For whosoever earns the faith of the Lord is darkened by no stains of carnal birth. Whence Joel. II: « I will pour forth of my spirit upon all flesh. »[Vulg. Joel 2:28 effundam spiritum meum super omnem carnem, "I will pour out my spirit upon all flesh"; the wording Migne prints is that of Act 2:17.]

VERS. 32.-- « And being gathered, » etc. (STRAB.) It must be asked: of what people is this said? For concerning the fathers there is no question. For it is known that he was gathered to Abraham and Isaac, but a people is a multitude. 180DThose, however, being so few, could not be called a people. It is to be understood therefore that this is said of the angels also, to whose fellowship the saints, passing out of the world, are gathered. Whence Hebr. XII: « You are come to mount Sion, and to the heavenly Jerusalem, and to the company of angels, »[Vulg. Hbr 12:22 accessistis ad Sion montem, et civitatem Dei viventis, Jerusalem caelestem, et multorum millium angelorum frequentiam, "you are come to mount Sion, and to the city of the living God, the heavenly Jerusalem, and to the company of many thousands of angels."] etc.

CAPUT L.

VERS. 1-3.-- « Quod cernens Joseph, ruit. . . . transierunt quadraginta dies. » (STRAB.) Qui numerus poenitentiae et humilitati convenit. Unde in lege et in Evangelio a prophetis et ab ipso Domino observatus est.

(AUG.) Quadraginta dies sepulturae, quos memorat Scriptura, forte poenitentiam significant, qua sepeliuntur peccata. Non enim frustra quadraginta dies 181Ajejuniorum sunt constituti, quibus Moyses, et Elias, et ipse Dominus jejunavit, et Ecclesia praecipuam observationem jejuniorum Quadragesimam vocat. Unde et apud Jonam de Ninivitis scriptum est: « Adhuc quadraginta dies, et Ninive subvertetur (Jon. III), ut per tot dies, accommodatos scilicet humiliationi poenitentium, intelligantur in jejuniis sua flevisse peccata, et impetrasse misericordiam. Nec tantum luctui poenitentium hic numerus convenit. Quadraginta enim dies post resurrectionem fuit Dominus cum discipulis suis intrans et exiens, manducans et bibens, qui dies laetitiae fuerunt. Nec septuaginta Interpretes errasse credendi sunt qui dixerunt: « Triduum, et Ninive subvertetur: » majore enim auctoritate praediti quam interpretum 181Bofficium est, prophetico spiritu (quo etiam ore uno in suis interpretationibus, quod magnum miraculum fuit, consonuisse firmantur) « triduum, » posuerunt, quamvis non ignorarent quod « quadraginta dies » in Hebraeo legerentur; ut in Christi clarificatione intelligerentur dissolvi et aboleri peccata, « qui traditus est propter delicta nostra, et resurrexit propter justificationem nostram. » Clarificatio autem Domini in resurrectione et ascensione cognoscitur. Unde hic quamvis unum et eumdem spiritum dedit: primo postquam resurrexit, secundo postquam ascendit; et quia post triduum resurrexit, post quadraginta autem dies ascendit: unum horum, quod posterius factum est, per numerum quadraginta dierum Hebraei significant; alterum vero de triduo, quod ad 181Ceamdem rem pertinet, Septuaginta memorant, non interpretationis servitute, sed prophetiae auctoritate. Non ergo dicamus unum horum falsum esse, et pro aliis interpretibus contra alios litigemus; cum illi qui ex Hebraeo interpretantur, probent scriptum esse quod interpretantur; et Septuaginta auctoritas, quae tanto divinitus facto miraculo commendatur, tanta in Ecclesiis vetustate firmetur.

« Septuaginta diebus. » (STRAB.) Ethnici novem diebus mortuos suos flebant. Unde et novendialis nona dies vocabatur, quando sepeliebantur. Dei cultores septem diebus exsequias celebrabant. Unde Eccli. XXII: « Luctus mortui septem dies: luctus autem fatui omne tempus vitae ejus. » Septenarius enim requiem significat. Unde septima dies sabbatum, 181Did est requies dicitur. Celebrant ergo septimam diem mortuorum, optantes illos cum Domino requiescere a laboribus praesentis vitae. Hinc ergo dicitur: « Flevit eum Aegyptus septuaginta diebus, » qui est decuplatus septenarius. Septies enim deni, septuaginta sunt. Potest etiam dici quia sicut tertia dies mortui agitur a fidelibus, ut anima, quae est triplicis naturae, requiescat; ita septimus dies celebratur, ut anima corpori reconcilietur, quae carne illiciente peccavit. In septenario etiam tria sunt et 182Aquatuor; ternarius pertinet ad animam, quae irascibilis, et concupiscibilis, et rationalis est; quaternarius ad corpus, qui quatuor constat elementis.

VERS. 4-21.-- « Et expleto planctus tempore, » etc. (AUG.) Quod mandavit Joseph potentibus Aegypti, ut dicerent Pharaoni nomine ejus: « Pater meus adjuravit me dicens: In monumento quod ego feci mihi in terra Chanaan, ibi me sepelies; » quaeritur quomodo verum sit, cum haec verba patris ejus, cum de sepultura mandaret, non legantur. Sed ad sententiam verba sunt referenda, sicut supra alia similiter iterata. Voluntati enim enuntiandae et in notitiam perferendae, oportet verba servire. Fodisse autem sibi Jacob sepulcrum nusquam superius legitur, sed certe, nisi fodisset, non modo diceretur.

182BVERS. 10.-- « Veneruntque ad aream Atad, » etc. Hic locus trans Jordanem est, et distat ab Hebron septuaginta passuum millibus. Praetergressi sunt ergo locum in quo debebant sepelire eum, et postea transito Jordane redierunt ad locum duplicis speluncae. Unde quaeri potest quare hoc fecerint. Si dicit aliquis quia bella timebant, sicut filii Israel circumducti per desertum quadraginta annis, donec transmisso Jordane venerunt ad terram promissionis. Sed majoris significantiae causa factum est hoc. In loco enim quae area Arad dicitur, postea multitudo filiorum Israel murmurans contra Dominum, divino igne consumpta est. Tanto ergo luctu non tam mors Jacob, quam ruina populi futura lugebatur.

VERS. 22, 23.-- « Vixitque centum decem annis. » 182C(AUG., in Gen., tom. III.) Cum hos filios filiorum vel nepotes filiorum dicat Scriptura Joseph vivendo vidisse, quomodo jungit eos sexaginta quinque animabus cum quibus Jacob Aegyptum dicit intrasse, etc., usque ad quae illo vivo propagabatur, per quem factum est ut ingrederetur.

VERS. 24.-- « Asportate ossa, » etc. (STRAB.) Joseph, sicut pater ejus, terram promissionis tota mente desiderabat, et incolatum Aegypti detestabatur. Nos quosque moraliter instruit, ut quandiu sumus in hoc mundo, terram promissionis, id est viventium, semper desideremus, et ibi requiescere optemus: quod bene fit, si numerum annorum Joseph moribus imitemur. Centum enim et decem vitae annis expletis mortuus est: nos quoque studeamus 182Dper decalogi observantiam ad aeternam beatitudinem quam centenarius significat, pervenire.

VERS. 25.-- « Conditus aromatibus. » (STRAB.) Felix est anima quae, aromatibus virtutum condita in loculo corporis degens, quotidie proficiendo perenni vitae reservatur. Siquidem in fide, spe et charitate custodita fuerit, per gratiam Christi pervenire merebitur ad speciem divinae contemplationis, cujus adoptio perfecta est laetitia; unde psal. XV: « Adimplebis me laetitia cum vultu tuo. »

CHAPTER L.

VERS. 1-3.-- « Which when Joseph saw, he fell . . . . forty days passed. » (STRAB.) Which number befits penance and humility. Whence in the law and in the Gospel it was observed by the prophets and by the Lord himself.

(AUG.) The forty days of burial which the Scripture records perhaps signify the penance whereby sins are buried. For not in vain were forty days 181Aof fastings appointed, on which Moses, and Elias, and the Lord himself fasted, and the Church calls the chief observance of fastings Lent. Whence also concerning the Ninivites it is written in Jonas: « Yet forty days, and Ninive shall be destroyed (Jon. III), that through so many days, fitted, that is, to the humbling of the penitent, they may be understood to have wept for their own sins in fastings, and to have obtained mercy. Nor does this number befit the mourning of penitents only. For forty days after the resurrection the Lord was with his disciples, going in and going out, eating and drinking, and those were days of gladness. Nor are the seventy Interpreters to be believed to have erred, who said: « Three days, and Ninive shall be destroyed: » for, endowed with a greater authority than the office of interpreters is, by the prophetic spirit (by which also they are affirmed to have agreed with one mouth in their interpretations, which was a great miracle) they set down 181B« three days, » although they were not ignorant that « forty days » was read in the Hebrew; that in the glorification of Christ sins might be understood to be dissolved and abolished, « who was delivered up for our offences, and rose again for our justification. » But the glorification of the Lord is known in his resurrection and ascension. Whence here, although he gave one and the same Spirit: first after he rose again, secondly after he ascended; and because after three days he rose again, but after forty days ascended: the one of these, which was done later, the Hebrews signify by the number of forty days; but the other, of the three days, which 181Cbelongs to the same matter, the Seventy record, not by the servitude of interpretation, but by the authority of prophecy. Let us not therefore say that one of these is false, and let us not wrangle for some interpreters against others; since they who interpret out of the Hebrew prove that what they interpret is written; and the authority of the Seventy, which is commended by so great a miracle divinely wrought, is confirmed by so great an antiquity in the churches.

« Seventy days. » (STRAB.) The heathen bewailed their dead nine days. Whence also the ninth day was called the novendial, when they were buried. The worshippers of God celebrated the obsequies for seven days. Whence Eccli. XXII: « The mourning for the dead is seven days: but the mourning of a fool is all the time of his life. »[Vulg. Sir 22:13 Luctus mortui septem dies : fatui autem et impii omnes dies vitae illorum, "the mourning for the dead is seven days: but for the fool and the ungodly, all the days of their life."] For the number seven signifies rest. Whence the seventh day is called the sabbath, 181Dthat is, rest. They celebrate therefore the seventh day of the dead, wishing that they may rest with the Lord from the labours of the present life. Hence therefore it is said: « Egypt wept for him seventy days, » which is the sevenfold number multiplied by ten. For seven times ten are seventy. It may also be said that, as the third day of a dead man is kept by the faithful, that the soul, which is of a threefold nature, may rest; so the seventh day is celebrated, that the soul, which sinned at the enticement of the flesh, may be reconciled to the body. In the number seven also there are three and 182Afour; the number three belongs to the soul, which is irascible, and concupiscible, and rational; the number four to the body, which consists of four elements.

VERS. 4-21.-- « And when the time of the mourning was expired, » etc. (AUG.) As to what Joseph enjoined upon the great men of Egypt, that they should say to Pharao in his name: « My father made me swear, saying: In the monument which I made for myself in the land of Chanaan, there thou shalt bury me; »[Vulg. Gn 50:5 eo quod pater meus adjuraverit me dicens : En morior : in sepulchro meo, quod fodi mihi in terra Chanaan, sepelies me, "for that my father made me swear, saying: Behold I die: thou shalt bury me in my sepulchre which I dug for myself in the land of Chanaan" — fodi, "dug," which is the very word the gloss below reasons from, where Migne's lemma prints feci, "made."] it is asked how this may be true, since these words of his father, when he gave charge concerning his burial, are not read. But the words are to be referred to the sense, as other things above were likewise repeated. For words must serve a will that is to be uttered and brought to knowledge. But that Jacob dug himself a sepulchre is nowhere read above, yet assuredly, unless he had dug it, it would not now be said.

182BVERS. 10.-- « And they came to the threshing-floor of Atad, » etc. This place is beyond the Jordan, and is distant from Hebron seventy thousand paces. They passed by, therefore, the place in which they were to bury him, and afterwards, the Jordan being crossed, returned to the place of the double cave. Whence it may be asked why they did this. If any one says that they feared wars, as the children of Israel were led about through the desert for forty years, until, the Jordan being passed, they came to the land of promise. But this was done for the sake of a greater signification. For in the place which is called the threshing-floor of Arad, afterwards the multitude of the children of Israel, murmuring against the Lord, was consumed by divine fire. With so great a mourning, therefore, not so much the death of Jacob as the coming ruin of the people was bewailed.

VERS. 22, 23.-- « And he lived a hundred and ten years. » 182C(AUG., in Gen., tom. III.) Since the Scripture says that Joseph, while he lived, saw these sons of his sons or grandsons of his sons, how does it join them to the sixty-five souls with whom it says that Jacob entered Egypt, etc., as far as which was propagated while he lived, through whom it came about that he entered in.

VERS. 24.-- « Carry away my bones, » etc. (STRAB.) Joseph, like his father, desired with his whole mind the land of promise, and detested the sojourn in Egypt. He instructs us each morally also, that so long as we are in this world we should always desire the land of promise, that is, of the living, and should wish to rest there: which is well done, if we imitate in our manners the number of Joseph's years. For he died when a hundred and ten years of his life were completed: let us also study 182Dto come, through the keeping of the decalogue, to the eternal blessedness which the number one hundred signifies.

VERS. 25.-- « Embalmed with spices. » (STRAB.) Happy is the soul which, seasoned with the spices of the virtues while it dwells in the little coffer of the body, is kept by daily profiting for the everlasting life. For if it shall have been guarded in faith, hope and charity, it will deserve to come, by the grace of Christ, to the sight of the divine contemplation, whose adoption is perfect gladness; whence psal. XV: « Thou shalt fill me with joy with thy countenance. »

The Latin is Migne's printing of Liber Genesis (PL 113, coll. 67B–182D), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/113/67b resolves to the first.
